, Prop. 4.13]
)
.

The local model
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}
is stable under
L^{+}\mathcal{P}
-action. Moreover,
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}
is the
L^{+}\mathcal{P}
-orbit of
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}
under the fixed closed embedding
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P}}
.

Proof.

Choosing a faithful embedding
\rho:\mathcal{P}\to\operatorname{GL}_{n}
such that
\operatorname{GL}_{n}/\mathcal{P}
is quasi-affine, we have a locally closed embedding
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P}}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}
.
Let
X\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\operatorname{GL}_{n},\mathcal{O}_{E}}
be a quasi-compact and quasi-separated closed subsheaf that contains
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}
(for example, take
X=\operatorname{Gr}_{\operatorname{GL}_{n},\leq\rho(\mu),\mathcal{O}_{E}}
). Since
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}\to X
is a monomorphism, as in Lemma
1.4
,
a:L_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{+}\mathcal{P}\times\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{O}_{E}}
factors through
a_{N}:L_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{\leq N}\mathcal{P}\times\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{O}_{E}}
for some large
N
, where
N
is determined by
X
. Let
X_{1}=X\cap\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{O}_{E}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{O}_{E}}
, it is a closed sub-sheaf,
L_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{+}\mathcal{P}
acts on
X_{1}
and factors through
L_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{\leq N}\mathcal{P}
. Since
L_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{\leq N}\mathcal{P}\to\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}
is partially proper and open, by
[
1
, Cor. 2.9]
(which says that taking closure commutes with base change along partially proper and open morphisms), base change along the projection
L_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{\leq N}\mathcal{P}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}}X_{1}\to X_{1}
, we have

L_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{\leq N}\mathcal{P}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}=(L_{E}^{\leq N}\mathcal{P}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}E}\operatorname{Gr}_{P,\mu})^{\mathrm{cl}},

as a closed subsheaf of
L_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{\leq N}\mathcal{P}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}}X_{1}
. Since
L_{E}^{\leq N}\mathcal{P}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}E}\operatorname{Gr}_{P,\mu}\to X_{1,E}
factors through
\operatorname{Gr}_{P,\mu}
by construction, combining with the equality displayed, we see that
L_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{\leq N}\mathcal{P}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}\to X_{1}
factors through
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}
.

Let us show the second statement. Since
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}\subset\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}
,
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}
is stable under
L^{+}\mathcal{P}
-action implies that the morphism
a^{\prime}:L_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{+}\mathcal{P}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}}
factors through
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}
. We need to show it is surjective. Since
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}
is quasi-compact and quasi-separated, apply Lemma
1.4
,
a^{\prime}
factors through some
a^{\prime}_{N}
for large
N
. Apply
[
1
, Cor. 2.9]
again. Note that
\operatorname{Gr}_{G,\mu}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{P,\mu}
, as well as
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}\subset\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}
, is a closed embedding. Base-changing along the projection
L^{\leq N}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathcal{P}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}\to\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}
, we have

L^{\leq N}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathcal{P}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}=(L^{\leq N}_{E}\mathcal{P}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}E}\operatorname{Gr}_{G,\mu})^{\mathrm{cl}},

as a closed subsheaf of
L_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{\leq N}\mathcal{P}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}
. Since
L_{\mathcal{O}_{E}}^{\leq N}\mathcal{P}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}E}\operatorname{Gr}_{G,\mu}
surjects onto
\operatorname{Gr}_{P,\mu}
by construction, we apply Lemma
1.8
to the action
a_{N}^{\prime}
.
∎

1.2.2.
Over
\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{F}_{p}

Let
\mathcal{G}
be parahoric. Let
M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},k_{E}}^{W}
be the reduction of
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{O}_{E}}
. We have
M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}=\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},k_{E}}^{W}
(see
[
1
, Thm. 1.5]
). Here
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
is the
\mu
-admissible locus in the sense of Kottwitz–Rapoport, which is the
k_{E}
descent of

\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{G},\mu,k}=\bigcup_{w\in\operatorname{Adm}_{G}(\{\mu^{-1}\})_{\mathcal{G}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}}\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},w,k}^{W},

where
k=\bar{k}_{E}
,
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},w,k}^{W}
is the usual Schubert cell, it is the perfection of a quasi-projective scheme.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be parahoric and
\widetilde{W}
be the Iwahori-Weyl group of
G
. For any
w\in\widetilde{W}
, let
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},w}^{W}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},k}^{W}
be the orbit of
L^{+}_{k}\mathcal{P}
of
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},w}^{W}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},k}^{W}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},k}^{W}
. By calculating the stabilizer of the orbit of
L^{+}_{k}\mathcal{P}
at
w
, we see that
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},w}^{W}
is locally perfectly of finite type. Let us define the
\mu
-admissible locus
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},k_{E}}^{W}
as the
k_{E}
-descent of

(1.5)

\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mu,k}=\bigcup_{w\in\operatorname{Adm}_{P}(\{\mu^{-1}\})_{\mathcal{P}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}}\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},w}^{W},\quad\operatorname{Adm}_{P}(\{\mu^{-1}\})_{\mathcal{P}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}:=\operatorname{Adm}_{G}(\{\mu^{-1}\})_{\mathcal{G}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}.

Let
\breve{K}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and let
\prescript{}{\breve{K}}{\widetilde{W}}^{\breve{K}}\subset\widetilde{W}
be the index set introduced in
[
72
]
(cf.
[
78
, §1.2.6]
).

Lemma 1.28
.

Given
w\in\prescript{}{\breve{K}}{\widetilde{W}}^{\breve{K}}
, let
\overline{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},w}^{W}}
be the closure of
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},w}^{W}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},k}^{W}
, then

\overline{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},w}^{W}}=\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},\leq w}^{W}:=\bigcup_{w^{\prime}\leq w,w^{\prime}\in\prescript{}{\breve{K}}{\widetilde{W}}^{\breve{K}}}\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},w^{\prime}}^{W}.

In particular,
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},k_{E}}^{W}
is a closed subfunctor.

Proof.

Since the closure of
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},w}^{W}
in
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}}^{W}
is
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\leq w}^{W}
, the usual affine Schubert variety. Use the orbit map
L^{+}_{k}\mathcal{P}\times\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},k}^{W}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},k}^{W}
, we see that
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},\leq w}^{W}\subset\overline{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},w}^{W}}
. On the other hand, by Lemma
1.4
, the action
a:L^{+}_{k}\mathcal{P}\times\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\leq w}^{W}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},k}^{W}
factors through
a_{N}
for some large
N
, and
a_{N}
is proper. In particular,
L^{+}_{k}\mathcal{P}\times\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\leq w}^{W}
has closed image in
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P}}
that contains
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},w}
, thus
\overline{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},w}^{W}}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},\leq w}^{W}
.
∎

Lemma 1.29
.

Let
M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},k_{E}}^{W}
be the reduction of
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{O}_{E}}
. Then
M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},k_{E}}^{W}
is representable and closed.

Proof.

We need to show that
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}
is
\varpi
-adic formal (i.e. the structure morphism to
\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}
is formally adic in the sense of
[
25
, Def. 3.20]
); then
M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},k_{E}}^{W}
represents the base change of
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{O}_{E}}
along
\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{F}_{q}\to\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}
. To show the claim, we follow the proof in
[
1
, Prop. 4.14]
: We need to show that the special fiber of
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}
is represented by a perfect scheme. This follows from Lemma
1.27
and in particular (
1.5
).
∎

By checking the set of
k
-points of these two closed functors, we have

Corollary 1.30
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be parahoric. Then
M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}=\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}
.

Remark 1.31
.

Recall that,
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}
is a proper topologically normal rich kimberlite, due to results in
[
25
]
,
[
1
]
,
[
24
]
. One might expect that
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}
is also a topologically normal rich kimberlite, though not necessarily proper. Most arguments in the cited papers work here, except for one crucial ingredient, the purity of
\mathcal{G}
-torsor proved in
[
3
]
. This purity result simply fails for non-reductive group, in particular,
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P}}
might not be
v
-formalizing, thus might not be pre-kimberlite.

Let
\mathcal{G}_{0}
be the special fiber of
\mathcal{G}
. The
L^{+}_{k_{E}}\mathcal{G}
-action on
M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
factors through
\mathcal{G}_{0}
(see
[
66
, §4.9.1]
). In particular, we have
|\mathcal{G}_{0}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}|=\operatorname{Adm}_{G}(\{\mu^{-1}\})_{\mathcal{G}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}
. Similarly, let
\mathcal{P}_{0}
be the special fiber of
\mathcal{P}
. Assume
(\mathcal{P},\mu)
comes from the boundary, associated with
\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Use the monomorphisms
\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}\to\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mu}^{v}
, we have

Corollary 1.32
.

Assume
(\mathcal{P},\mu)
comes from the boundary. Then the
L^{+}_{k_{E}}\mathcal{P}
-action on
M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}
factors through
\mathcal{P}_{0}
. In particular,
|\mathcal{P}_{0}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}|=\operatorname{Adm}_{P}(\{\mu^{-1}\})_{\mathcal{P}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})}
.

1.2.3.
Functoriality

Definition 1.33
.

Let
P_{1}=U_{1}\rtimes G_{1}
,
P_{2}=U_{2}\rtimes G_{2}
. We say
P_{1}\to P_{2}
is compatible with
U_{1}\rtimes G_{1}\to U_{2}\rtimes G_{2}
if
P_{1}\to P_{2}
induces
U_{1}\to U_{2}
,
G_{1}\to G_{2}
, and there exist sections
G_{1}\to P_{1}
and
G_{2}\to P_{2}
such that
G_{1}\to P_{1}\to P_{2}
factors through
G_{2}
.

Remark 1.34
.

•

In this case,
P_{1}\to P_{2}
is uniquely determined by the maps
U_{1}\to U_{2}
and
G_{1}\to G_{2}
.

•

When
P_{1}\to P_{2}
induces
U_{1}\to U_{2}
,
G_{1}\to G_{2}
, then one can always find such a pair of sections
G_{1}\to P_{1}
and
G_{2}\to P_{2}
in characteristic
0
.

Let
P_{1}\to P_{2}
be compatible with
U_{1}\rtimes G_{1}\to U_{2}\rtimes G_{2}
, and let
\mathcal{P}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}
be a morphism of quasi-parahoric group schemes that extends
P_{1}\to P_{2}
; then
\mathcal{P}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}
induces
\mathcal{U}_{1}\to\mathcal{U}_{2}
and
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
.

Definition 1.35
.

We say
\mathcal{P}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}
is compatible with
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
if there exist sections
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{1}
,
\mathcal{G}_{2}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}
such that
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}
factors through
\mathcal{G}_{2}
.

In this subsection, we consider the following case. Let
P_{1}=U\rtimes G_{1}\to P_{2}=U\rtimes G_{2}
be compatible with
U\rtimes G_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mathrm{id},f)}}{{\to}}U\rtimes G_{2}
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{1}=\mathcal{U}\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}=\mathcal{U}\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{2}
be parahoric group schemes of
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
, respectively, such that
P_{1}\to P_{2}
induces
\mathcal{P}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}
compatible with
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
.

Recall that the sheafification functor commutes with fiber products. Since
\mathcal{P}_{2}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
is surjective with a section
\mathcal{G}_{2}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}
, we have:

Lemma 1.36
.

The following diagrams are Cartesian:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P}_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}^{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P}_{2}}^{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{2}},}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{2}},}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}^{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}_{2}}^{W}.}}

Assume that the cocharacter
\mu
of
P_{1}
is quotient-minuscule in both
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
.

Lemma 1.37
.

We have a Cartesian diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{1},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{2},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{1},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{2},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1},\mu}.}}

In particular,
\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{1},\mu}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{2},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1},\mu}
is proper.

Proof.

For
i=1,2
, let
\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{i},\mu,\operatorname{Spd}E_{1}}^{\prime}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{i},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1}}
be the base change of
\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{i},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1},\mu}\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{i},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1}}
. Consider the commutative diagram with vertical diagrams being Cartesian:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{1},\mu}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{2},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1},\mu}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{1},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{2},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{1},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{2},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{1},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{2},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1}}.}}

Both
\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{1},\mu}
and
\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{2},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1},\mu}
are proper over
\operatorname{Spd}E_{1}
, thus
\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{1},\mu}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{2},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1},\mu}
is proper. By Proposition
1.9
,
\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{i},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1},\mu}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{i},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1},\mu}^{\prime}
are closed embeddings for
i=1,2
. In particular,
\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{1},\mu}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{2},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1},\mu}
is proper. In order to show the proper morphism between diamonds

(1.6)

\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{1},\mu}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{2},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1},\mu}\times_{\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{2},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1},\mu}}\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{1},\mu}

is an isomorphism, it suffices to check it is an isomorphism over any
\operatorname{Spa}(C,C^{+})
-point in
\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{1},\mu}
. Let
\mathcal{O}=B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(C^{\sharp})
,
F=B_{\mathrm{dR}}(C^{\sharp})
,
g\xi^{\mu}G(\mathcal{O})\in\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{1},\mu}(C,C^{+})
, with
g\in G_{1}(\mathcal{O})
. Since
g
normalizes
U(\mathcal{O})\rtimes G_{i}(\mathcal{O})
, then the fibers of both sides of (
1.6
) over
g\xi^{\mu}G(\mathcal{O})
are given by the same set (
1.1
).
∎

Lemma 1.38
.

We have cartesian diagrams:

(1.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{P}_{1},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{P}_{2},\mu,k_{E_{1}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P}_{1},\mu}^{v}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P}_{2},\mu}^{v}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{2}}}\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G}_{1},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G}_{2},\mu,k_{E_{1}}},}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}_{1},\mu}^{v}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}_{2},\mu}^{v}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{2}}}\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}.}}

Proof.

Apply similar arguments appeared in the proof of Lemma
1.37
,
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P}_{1},\mu}^{v}\to\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P}_{2},\mu}^{v}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{2}}}\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}
is proper. In particular, to show the proper morphism

\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P}_{1},\mu}^{v}\to\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P}_{2},\mu,\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}^{v}\times_{\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}_{2},\mu,\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}^{v}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}_{1},\mu}^{v}

is an isomorphism, it suffices to check over geometric points (see
[
75
, Cor. 17.4.8]
). Over generic fiber, this is Lemma
1.37
. Over special fiber, this is the proper morphism

M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{P}_{1},\mu}\to M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{P}_{2},\mu,k_{E_{1}}}\times_{M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G}_{2},\mu,k_{E_{1}}}}M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G}_{1},\mu}

induced by the left diagram in (
1.7
). We apply the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma
1.37
and compute the fibers of both sides over
M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G}_{1},\mu}
, with the help of Corollary
1.30
(cf. Lemma
1.27
) and Lemma
1.25
.
∎

1.2.4.
Devissage

Let
\widetilde{P}\to P
be a surjective morphism with kernel a central multiplicative group
Z\subset\widetilde{P}
. Let
\widetilde{G}
be the Levi quotient of
\widetilde{P}
. Note that
Z
maps isomorphically into a central multiplicative group in
\widetilde{G}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}=\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}\rtimes\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
(resp.
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{U}\rtimes\mathcal{G}
) be a quasi-parahoric group scheme of
\widetilde{P}
(resp.
P
). Assume
\widetilde{P}\to P
induces
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}\to\mathcal{P}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}=\mathcal{U}
.

Lemma 1.39

(
[
75
, Prop. 21.5.2]
)
.

Let
\widetilde{\mu}
be a quotient-minuscule cocharacter of
\widetilde{P}
and
\mu
be its projection in
P
defined over
E\subset\widetilde{E}
, then the natural morphisms are isomorphisms:

\mathbb{M}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\widetilde{\mu}}^{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}\otimes_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{E}},\quad\mathbb{M}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{P}},\widetilde{\mu}}^{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}\otimes_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{E}}.

Proof.

See
[
75
, Prop. 21.5.2]
, Lemma
1.38
and
1.25
.
∎

1.3.
Shtukas with one leg bounded by
\mu

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a quasi-parahoric group scheme, and
\mu:\mathbb{G}_{m,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\to P_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
be a quotient-minuscule cocharacter.

Definition 1.40

(
[
66
, Def. 2.4.3]
)
.

Let
S
be a perfectoid space over
k=\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
, and let
S^{\sharp}
be an untilt of
S
over
\mathcal{O}_{\breve{E}}
. A
\mathcal{P}
-shtuka over
S
with one leg at
S^{\sharp}
is a pair
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
, where

(1)

\mathscr{P}
is a
\mathcal{P}
-torsor over the adic space
S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,

(2)

\phi_{\mathscr{P}}
is a
\mathcal{P}
-torsor isomorphism

\phi_{\mathscr{P}}:\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}(\mathscr{P})|_{S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\setminus S^{\sharp}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{P}|_{S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\setminus S^{\sharp}},

which is meromorphic along the closed Cartier divisor
S^{\sharp}\subset S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.

We say
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
is bounded by
\mu
if the relative position
\phi_{\mathscr{P}}(\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}(\mathscr{P}))
and
\mathscr{P}
at
S^{\sharp}
is bounded by
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}
(
1.4
).

Definition 1.41

(
[
66
, Def. 2.4.8]
)
.

Given a
v
-sheaf
\mathcal{F}
, a
\mathcal{P}
-shtuka over
\mathcal{F}
(resp. with one leg bounded by
\mu
) is a section of the
v
-stack (
[
75
, Prop. 19.5.3]
) given by the groupoid of
\mathcal{P}
-shtukas with one leg (resp. with one leg bounded by
\mu
) over
\mathcal{F}
.

1.3.1.
Functoriality

Let
P_{1}\to P_{2}
be a morphism,
\mathcal{P}_{1}
,
\mathcal{P}_{2}
be quasi-parahoric models of
P_{1}
,
P_{2}
respectively, such that
P_{1}\to P_{2}
induces a morphism
\mathcal{P}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}
. Let
\mu_{1}
be a cocharacter of
P_{1}
with reflex field
E_{1}
, and let
\mu_{2}
be the composition of
\mu_{1}
with
P_{1}\to P_{2}
, with reflex field
E_{2}
. Assume
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
are quotient-minuscule in
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
respectively. Then the natural functor
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P}_{2}}
induces

\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{1},\mu_{1}}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{2},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1},\mu_{2}},\quad\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P}_{1},\mu_{1}}^{v}\to\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P}_{2},\mu_{2}}^{v}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{2}}}\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}.

We also have

\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{2}},\quad\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{1},\mu_{1}}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{2},\mu_{2}},

which come from pushing out the
\mathcal{P}_{1}
-torsor to a
\mathcal{P}_{2}
-torsor and naturally extending the isomorphism of
\mathcal{P}_{1}
-torsors
\phi_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}
to an isomorphism of
\mathcal{P}_{2}
-torsors
\phi_{\mathcal{P}_{2}}
.

The projection
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
has a section
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}
. The substack
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}\subset\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}}
is contained in the preimage of
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\subset\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}}
, but they are not equal.

1.3.2.
Trivial cocharacter

Definition 1.42
.

We say
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
is a trivial
\mathcal{P}
-shtuka over
\mathcal{F}
if for each perfectoid space
S\to\mathcal{F}
,
\mathscr{P}_{S}
is a trivial
\mathcal{P}
-torsor over
S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and
\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{S}}
is the isomorphism induced by the Frobenius on the base
S
.

In the following, we assume that
X
is a normal scheme, flat and of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
. By
[
66
, Cor. 2.7.10]
, a
\mathcal{P}
-shtuka over
X^{\Diamond/}
is uniquely determined by its generic fiber over
X_{\eta}^{\Diamond}
. In particular, a
\mathcal{P}
-shtuka over
X^{\Diamond/}
is trivial if it is trivial over
X_{\eta}^{\Diamond}
.

Let
1\to P_{1}\to P_{2}\to P_{3}\to 1
be an exact sequence of linear algebraic groups which induces exact sequences

1\to U_{1}\to U_{2}\to U_{3}\to 1,\quad 1\to G_{1}\to G_{2}\to G_{3}\to 1.

Let
1\to\mathcal{P}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}\to\mathcal{P}_{3}\to 1
be an exact sequence of quasi-parahoric group schemes extending the generic fiber which induces

1\to\mathcal{U}_{1}\to\mathcal{U}_{2}\to\mathcal{U}_{3}\to 1,\quad 1\to\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}\to\mathcal{G}_{3}\to 1.

In other words, in the decomposition
\mathcal{P}_{i}=\mathcal{U}_{i}\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{i}
, we assume there exist compatible sections
\mathcal{G}_{i}\to\mathcal{P}_{i}
. Let
\mu
be a quotient-minuscule cocharacter of
P_{1}
, then it is automatically quotient-minuscule in
P_{2}
.

Proposition 1.43
.

In the above setting, let
(\mathscr{P}_{2},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{2}})
be a
\mathcal{P}_{2}
-shtuka over
X^{\Diamond/}
(resp. with one leg bounded by
\mu
). Assume that its base change over
X_{\eta}^{\Diamond}
reduces to a
\mathcal{P}_{1}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{1,\eta},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{1,\eta}})
over
X_{\eta}^{\Diamond}
(resp. with one leg bounded by
\mu
). Then
(\mathscr{P}_{2},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{2}})
reduces uniquely to a
\mathcal{P}_{1}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{1},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{1}})
(resp. with one leg bounded by
\mu
) over
X^{\Diamond/}
whose base change over
X_{\eta}^{\Diamond}
is
(\mathscr{P}_{1,\eta},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{1,\eta}})
.

Proof.

Consider the pushout
X^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{2}}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{3}}
. The pushout
\mathcal{P}_{3}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{3},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{3}})
is trivial over
X_{\eta}^{\Diamond}
, and thus is itself trivial over
X^{\Diamond/}
. In other words, given any perfectoid space
S\to X^{\Diamond/}
, the
\mathcal{P}_{2}
-torsor
\mathscr{P}_{2}
over
S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
reduces to a
\mathcal{P}_{1}
-torsor
\mathscr{P}_{1}
, and the isomorphism

\Phi_{\mathscr{P}_{2,S}}:\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathscr{P}_{2,S}|_{S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\backslash S^{\sharp}}\cong\mathscr{P}_{2,S}|_{S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\backslash S^{\sharp}}

reduces to an isomorphism of
\mathcal{P}_{1}
-torsor:

\Phi_{\mathscr{P}_{1,S}}:\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}\mathscr{P}_{1,S}|_{S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\backslash S^{\sharp}}\cong\mathscr{P}_{1,S}|_{S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\backslash S^{\sharp}}.

Let us check the boundedness condition.

(1)

When
P_{3}
is a reductive group, equivalently, we have that
U_{1}=U_{2}
. By
[
75
, Prop. 21.5.1]
,
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}_{1},\mu}^{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}_{2},\mu}^{v}\otimes\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}
is a canonical isomorphism. Lemma
1.38
gives
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P}_{1},\mu}^{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P}_{2},\mu}^{v}\otimes\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}
. Hence,
(\mathscr{P}_{1},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{1}})
is bounded by
\mu
.

(2)

When
P_{3}
is a unipotent group, equivalently, we have that
G_{1}=G_{2}
, we claim that the following commutative diagram of
v
-sheaves is Cartesian. In particular,
(\mathscr{P}_{1},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{1}})
is bounded by
\mu
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P}_{1},\mu}^{v}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P}_{2},\mu}^{v}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{2}}}\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P}_{1},\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P}_{2},\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}.}}

The bottom morphism is a closed embedding since
\mathcal{P}_{2}/\mathcal{P}_{1}=\mathcal{U}
is affine; then the top morphism is a closed embedding by construction. Then the induced morphism

(1.8)

\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P}_{1},\mu}^{v}\to\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P}_{2},\mu,\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}^{v}\times_{\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P}_{2},\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}}\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P}_{1},\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}

is also closed. To show that it is an isomorphism, it suffices to check the surjectivity on fibers over the
\operatorname{Spa}(C,C^{+})
-points in
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G},\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}
.

Over the generic fiber, (
1.8
) becomes

(1.9)

\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{1},\mu}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{2},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1},\mu}\times_{\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{2},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1}}}\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{1},\operatorname{Spd}E_{1}}.

Let
\mathcal{O}=B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}(C^{\sharp})
,
F=B_{\mathrm{dR}}(C^{\sharp})
, fix a point
g\xi^{\mu}G(\mathcal{O})\in\operatorname{Gr}_{G,\mu}(C,C^{+})
, where
g\in G(\mathcal{O})
, let
h=g\xi^{\mu}
, the fiber of the right hand side of (
1.9
) over
h
is

U_{2}(\mathcal{O})/(U_{2}(\mathcal{O})\cap\xi^{\mu}U_{2}(\mathcal{O})\xi^{-\mu})\times_{U_{2}(F)/\xi^{\mu}U_{2}(\mathcal{O})\xi^{-\mu}}U_{1}(F)/\xi^{\mu}U_{1}(\mathcal{O})\xi^{-\mu},

as subset of
U_{1}(F)/\xi^{\mu}U_{1}(\mathcal{O})\xi^{-\mu}
, equals the fiber of the left hand side of (
1.9
) over
h
:

U_{1}(\mathcal{O})/(U_{1}(\mathcal{O})\cap\xi^{\mu}U_{1}(\mathcal{O})\xi^{-\mu}),

since
U_{1}(F)\subset U_{2}(F)
(resp.
U_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{F})\subset U_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{F})
) is stabilized by the conjugation of
P_{1}(F)
(resp.
P_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{F})
). Over the special fiber, the isomorphism can be checked similarly, with the help of Corollary
1.30
(cf. Lemma
1.27
) and Lemma
1.25
.

(3)

In general, we can insert an auxiliary
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
into

\mathcal{P}_{1}=\mathcal{U}_{1}\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=\mathcal{U}_{2}\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}=\mathcal{U}_{2}\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{2},

where
\mathcal{G}_{1}
acts on
\mathcal{U}_{2}
through
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
, and apply the above two steps successively.

∎

1.3.3.
\operatorname{Bun}_{P}
and
P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}

Recall that
P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}
is a stack on
\operatorname{PCAlg^{op}}
that maps
S=\operatorname{Spec}R
to the groupoid of pairings
(\mathcal{E},\beta)
, where
\mathcal{E}
is a
P
-torsor over
\operatorname{Spec}W(R)[1/p]
, and
\beta:\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}
is an isomorphism of
P
-torsors. By
[
23
, Thm 7.14, Prop. 4.7, Prop. 6.3]
,
P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}
is a
v
-stack, and
P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}\cong\operatorname{Bun}_{P}^{\mathrm{red}}
, the proof of this part does not need
P
to be reductive. By definition there is a bijection of the underlying topological spaces
|P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}|\cong B(P)
.

Let
\mu
be a quotient-minuscule cocharacter of
P
, also denote by
\mu
its projection on
G
. Recall that one defines
G\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}_{\mu}\subset G\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}
as the closed substack corresponding to
B(G,\mu)\subset B(G)
, following the main result in
[
71
]
. Since the natural projection
B(P)\to B(G)
is a bijection (
[
50
, §3.6]
), we let
P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}_{\mu}\subset P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}
be the pullback of
G\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}_{\mu}\subset G\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}
, which is again a closed substack.

Let
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}}^{W}
be the functor sending any perfect algebra
R
to the groupoid of
(\mathcal{E},\beta)
, where
\mathcal{E}
is a
\mathcal{P}
-torsor on
\operatorname{Spec}W(R)
and
\beta:\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{E}
a modification. By
[
66
, Thm. 2.3.8]
and the Tannakian formalism, there is a natural isomorphism
\mathrm{Sht}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{P}}\cong\mathrm{Sht}^{W}_{\mathcal{P}}
; cf.
[
14
, Lem. 3.1.5]
. When
\mathcal{P}
is parahoric, one can define
\mathrm{Sht}^{W}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}
by requiring the relative position to be controlled by
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{W}
. Then, under
\mathrm{Sht}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{P}}\cong\mathrm{Sht}^{W}_{\mathcal{P}}
, we have
\mathrm{Sht}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}\cong\mathrm{Sht}^{W}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}
by
[
14
, Lem. 3.1.7]
(with the help of Corollary
1.30
). When
\mathcal{P}
is quasi-parahoric, we let
\mathrm{Sht}^{W}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}\subset\mathrm{Sht}^{W}_{\mathcal{P}}
be the reduction of
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}\subset\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}}
; this is compatible with the parahoric case.

Recall that the natural projection
\mathrm{Sht}^{W}_{\mathcal{P}}\to P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}
defined by
(\mathcal{E},\beta)\mapsto(\mathcal{E}|_{W(\ast)[1/p]},\beta)
is a
v
-cover. By construction,
\mathrm{Sht}^{W}_{\mathcal{P}}\to P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}\to G\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}
factors through
\mathrm{Sht}^{W}_{\mathcal{G}}
. By
[
14
, §3.2.2]
, the projection
\mathrm{Sht}^{W}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\to G\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}
factors through
G\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}_{\mu^{-1}}
when
\mathcal{G}
is parahoric, then
\mathrm{Sht}^{W}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}\to P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}
factors through
P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}_{\mu^{-1}}
when
\mathcal{P}
is parahoric.

Recall that one has the Beauville–Laszlo morphism
\operatorname{BL}:\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{P}
as follows: let
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
be a
\mathcal{P}
-shtuka on
S
with one leg at
S^{\sharp}
. Restrict
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
to
\mathcal{Y}_{[r,\infty)}(S)
for sufficiently large
r
so that it excludes the leg at
S^{\sharp}
, and descend it to
X_{\mathrm{FF},S}
; we obtain a
\mathcal{P}
-torsor
\mathcal{E}(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
on
X_{\mathrm{FF},S}
. By taking the reduction, we have a morphism
\operatorname{BL}^{\mathrm{red}}:\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\mathrm{red}}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{P}^{\mathrm{red}}
. This is compatible with the projection
\mathrm{Sht}^{W}_{\mathcal{P}}\to P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}
together with the identification
P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}\cong\operatorname{Bun}_{P}^{\mathrm{red}}
.

On the other hand, we have a functor
P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{P}
, defined by composing the exact
\otimes
-functor
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}P\to\operatorname{Isoc}
with the exact
\otimes
-functor
\operatorname{Isoc}\to\operatorname{Bun}
(see
[
21
, §III.2]
,
[
2
]
). By results in
[
22
]
and
[
2
]
, when
P=G
is a reductive group, such a functor induces a bijection on the underlying topological spaces (and moreover a homeomorphism when
B(G)
is endowed with the topology induced by the Bruhat order reversed from
[
71
]
; see
[
82
]
). In general, we also have

Lemma 1.44
.

The functor
P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{P}
induces a bijection
B(P)\to\operatorname{Bun}_{P}(C)/\cong
.

Proof.

Since
B(P)\to B(G)
and
B(G)\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}(C)/\cong
are bijections, it suffices to show that
H^{1}(X_{\mathrm{FF},C},U)=0
. In characteristic
0
, the unipotent group
U
is split and is a successive extension of
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{a}}
. Since
H^{1}(X_{\mathrm{FF},C},\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{a}})=H^{1}(X_{\mathrm{FF},C},\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathrm{FF},C}})=0
(see
[
21
, Prop. II.2.5(ii)]
), we have
H^{1}(X_{\mathrm{FF},C},U)=0
.
∎

1.3.4.
Shtukas and local systems

Using the Tannakian formalism and Beauville–Laszlo gluing, and following the proofs of
[
75
, Prop. 12.4.6]
and
[
66
, Prop. 2.5.1]
(
[
75
, Thm. 22.5.2, Prop. 22.6.1]
works for any smooth affine model
\mathcal{P}
with connected fibers), we have the following:

Proposition 1.45

(
[
66
, Prop. 2.5.1]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be parahoric. The construction

(1.10)

(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})\mapsto(\mathbb{P},\operatorname{DRT}(\mathscr{P}))

gives an equivalence of categories between

(1)

\mathcal{P}
-shtukas over
S/\operatorname{Spd}E
(resp. with one leg bounded by
\mu
),

(2)

pairs
(\mathbb{P},D)
, where
\mathbb{P}
is a pro-étale
\underline{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-torsor over
S
and
D:\mathbb{P}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{P,\operatorname{Spd}E}
(resp.
D:\mathbb{P}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{P,\mu^{-1}}
) is a
\underline{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-equivariant morphism over
\operatorname{Spd}E
.

This result can be generalized to the case where the base
S/\operatorname{Spd}E
is replaced by a
v
-sheaf
\mathcal{F}
.

Finally, as in
[
66
, §2.6]
, using the Tannakian formalism, given a de-Rham pro-étale torsor
\mathbb{P}
under
\underline{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
over
X^{\Diamond}/\operatorname{Spd}E
(resp. we moreover assume that for all classical points
x\in X
, the filtration
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{L}_{\rho,\bar{x}})
has constant conjugacy class), then
\mathbb{P}
is equipped with a canonical Hodge–Tate period map
D:\mathbb{P}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{P,\operatorname{Spd}E}
(resp.
D:\mathbb{P}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{P,\mu^{-1}}
), and one can attach to it a
\mathcal{P}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
over
X^{\Diamond}/\operatorname{Spd}E
(resp. with one leg bounded by
\mu
).

1.3.5.
Quasi-parahoric group schemes

Lemma 1.46
.

Let
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{U}\rtimes\mathcal{G}
be a quasi-parahoric group scheme. Then the following
2
-commutative diagrams are
2
-Cartesian:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}^{\circ}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}^{\circ},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{\circ}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}},}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{\circ},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}.}}

Proof.

Over
S\dot{\times}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, it is easy to show that the groupoid of
\mathcal{P}^{\circ}
-torsors is isomorphic to the groupoid of
\mathcal{P}
-torsors whose reduction to
\mathcal{G}
-torsors comes from
\mathcal{G}^{\circ}
-torsors. To check the boundedness condition, we use Lemma
1.25
.
∎

Consider the Kottwitz map
\kappa_{G}:|\operatorname{Bun}_{G}|\to\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma}
, where
\Gamma=\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}|\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
\pi_{1}(G)
is the algebraic fundamental group of
G
. Then
\kappa_{G}
is locally constant and maps
\operatorname{Bun}_{G,\mu^{-1}}
to
-\mu^{\natural}
. Let
\mathcal{G}
be a quasi-parahoric group scheme of
G
, and let
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{\kappa=-\mu^{\natural}}\subset\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
be the open and closed substack that maps to
-\mu^{\natural}
under the composition of the Beauville–Laszlo map
\operatorname{BL}^{\circ}:\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
with
\kappa_{G}
. The map
\operatorname{BL}^{\circ}
does not factor through
\operatorname{Bun}_{G,\mu^{-1}}
; nevertheless, the restriction
\operatorname{BL}^{\circ}:\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{\kappa=-\mu^{\natural}}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G}
does factor through
\operatorname{Bun}_{G,\mu^{-1}}
; see
[
15
, Prop. 3.1.10]
.

Recall that we have a short exact sequence

1\to\mathcal{G}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\pi_{0}(\mathcal{G})^{\phi}\to 1.

In
[
15
, §3.2]
, there is an action of
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{G})^{\phi}
on
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{\circ},\mu}
, and the pushout
\mathcal{E}\mapsto\mathcal{G}\times^{\mathcal{G}^{\circ}}\mathcal{E}
naturally induces an open and closed substack
[\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{\circ},\mu}/\pi_{0}(\mathcal{G})^{\phi}]\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
; see
[
15
, Thm. 3.3.5]
. Let
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu,\delta=1}
be the image of
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{\circ},\mu}
. Then
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{\circ},\mu}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu,\delta=1}
is a torsor under the finite abelian group
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{G})^{\phi}
.

Let
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{U}\rtimes\mathcal{G}
be a quasi-parahoric group scheme. We define

\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu,\delta=1}\subset\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{\kappa=-\mu^{\natural}}\subset\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}\quad(\textit{resp}.\ \mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu,\delta=1}^{W}\subset\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{W,\kappa=-\mu^{\natural}}\subset\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{W})

as the open and closed substacks that are the pullbacks of

\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu,\delta=1}\subset\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{\kappa=-\mu^{\natural}}\subset\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\quad(\textit{resp}.\ \mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu,\delta=1}^{W}\subset\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W,\kappa=-\mu^{\natural}}\subset\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W})

under the natural projection
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{W}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W}
). Then
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{W,\kappa=-\mu^{\natural}}\to P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}
factors through
P\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}_{\mu^{-1}}
.

Also, we have a short exact sequence

(1.11)

1\to\mathcal{P}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P})^{\phi}\to 1.

We define an action of
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P})^{\phi}
on
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}^{\circ},\mu}
as in
[
15
, §3.2]
, and the pushout functor naturally induces
[\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}^{\circ},\mu}/\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P})^{\phi}]\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}
.

Corollary 1.47

(cf.
[
15
, Cor. 3.3.8, 3.3.9, Rmk. 4.1.5]
)
.

(1)

[\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}^{\circ},\mu}/\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P})^{\phi}]
is open and closed in
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}
with image
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu,\delta=1}
. In particular,
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}^{\circ},\mu}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu,\delta=1}
is a torsor under the finite abelian group
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P})^{\phi}
.

(2)

There is a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu,\delta=1}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}}\operatorname{Spd}E\cong[\operatorname{Gr}_{P,\mu^{-1}}/\underline{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}].

(3)

Let
X
be a normal scheme of finite type and flat over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Assume that we have
X^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}
, and
X_{\eta}^{\Diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu,\operatorname{Spd}E}
factors through
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu,\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}
. Then
X^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}
factors through
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu,\delta=1}
.

Proof.

(1)

By definition,
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{G})=\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P})
. This corollary directly follows from Lemma
1.46
.

(2)

For parahoric subgroups, this follows from
[
89
, Prop. 11.16]
; note that
[
75
, Thm. 22.5.2, Prop. 22.6.1]
works for any smooth affine model
\mathcal{P}
with connected fibers. For quasi-parahoric subgroups, apply the results in Part
(1)
.

(3)

See
[
15
, Rmk. 4.1.5]
.

∎

Lemma 1.48
.

Let
P_{1}=U\rtimes G_{1}
,
P_{2}=U\rtimes G_{2}
,
P_{1}\to P_{2}
be a morphism compatible with
U\rtimes G_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\mathrm{id},f)}}{{\to}}U\rtimes G_{2}
in the sense of
1.33
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{i}=\mathcal{U}\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{i}
be parahoric group schemes of
P_{i}
. Assume
P_{1}\to P_{2}
induces
\mathcal{P}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}
that is compatible with
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
in the sense of
1.35
. Assume the cocharacter
\mu
of
P_{1}
is quotient-minuscule in both
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
. Then the following
2
-commutative diagrams are
2
-Cartesian:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{1},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{2},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{2}},}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{2},\mu}.}}

Proof.

The left diagram is Cartesian; see
[
16
, Cor. 2.17]
. Note that
\mathcal{P}_{1}=\mathcal{G}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{G}_{2}}\mathcal{P}_{2}
by assumption. The right diagram is Cartesian by Lemma
1.38
.
∎

Corollary 1.49
.

Keep the notation and assumptions from Lemma
1.48
, with the word
parahoric
replaced by
quasi-parahoric
. Then the following
2
-commutative diagram is
2
-Cartesian:

(1.12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{1},\mu,\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{2},\mu,\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1},\mu,\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{2},\mu,\delta=1}.}}

Proof.

First, assume
G_{1}=G_{2}
and
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
induces
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\circ}=\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\circ}
. By
[
15
, Cor. 3.3.11]
and the first part of Corollary
1.47
, the diagram (
1.12
) is
2
-Cartesian, where both horizontal morphisms are torsors under finite abelian groups

\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P}_{2})^{\phi}/\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P}_{1})^{\phi}=\pi_{0}(\mathcal{G}_{2})^{\phi}/\pi_{0}(\mathcal{G}_{1})^{\phi}.

In general, given a morphism of quasi-parahoric group schemes
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
, then
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\circ}\to\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
factors through
\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\circ}
, this can be seen by the equation
\mathcal{G}_{i}^{\circ}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\mathcal{G}_{i}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\cap\operatorname{ker}\tilde{\kappa}_{G_{i}}
, where
\tilde{\kappa}_{G_{i}}:G_{i}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\to\pi_{1}(G_{i})_{I}
is the functorial Kottwitz map. Consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\circ},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\circ},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{1},\mu,\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{2},\mu,\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\circ},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\circ},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1},\mu,\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{2},\mu,\delta=1}.}}

Since the left, right and the back diagrams are 2-Cartesian by above discussions and Lemma
1.48
, and since
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\circ},\mu}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1},\mu,\delta=1}
is a surjection of
v
-stacks, the front diagram (
1.12
) is 2-Cartesian.
∎

2.
Log diamonds and log shtukas

We discuss some theorems in
[
66
, Sec. 2]
in the log setting.
To start with, we also introduce some basic results about log diamonds defined in the
v
-topology for log adic spaces, schemes and formal schemes. Since we will deal with many slightly different cases simultaneously, the readers will be frequently reminded about the cases under consideration before seeing the results.

2.1.
Log diamonds associated with log schemes

Our goal is to develop results on log diamonds associated with fs log schemes over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. This may be viewed as a common generalization of some definitions and propositions in
[
75
, §18]
and
[
49
, §7]
.

2.1.1.

Let
X
be a scheme locally of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. We write
X^{\mathrm{ad}}
as the adic space that represents the fiber product

X\times_{\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\operatorname{Spa}\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

Let
\widehat{X}
be the formal scheme defined by the
p
-adic completion of
X
and
\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}
the adic space associated with it. We can also define
X^{\mathrm{ad}}
similarly when
X
is of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

When
X
is separated and of finite type, there is an open embedding
\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}\hookrightarrow X^{\mathrm{ad}}.

Now let
X
be a separated scheme of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
, where
E
is a finite field extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. We list some easy facts that will be used later:

(1)

Let
X_{\eta}
be the generic fiber of
X
, we can also define
(X_{\eta})^{\mathrm{ad}}=X_{\eta}\times_{\operatorname{Spec}E}\operatorname{Spa}(E,\mathcal{O}_{E})
in the sense of
[
35
, Proposition 3.8]
, then

(2.1)

(X_{\eta})^{\mathrm{ad}}=(X\times_{\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{E}}\operatorname{Spa}\mathcal{O}_{E})\times_{\operatorname{Spa}\mathcal{O}_{E}}\operatorname{Spa}(E,\mathcal{O}_{E})=(X^{\mathrm{ad}})_{\eta}.

(2)

Let
X\to Y\leftarrow Z
be schemes separated locally of finite type over
E
, then
X^{\mathrm{ad}}
,
Y^{\mathrm{ad}}
,
Z^{\mathrm{ad}}
are Tate. In particular,
X^{\mathrm{ad}}\to Y^{\mathrm{ad}}\leftarrow Z^{\mathrm{ad}}
are adic, the fiber product exists. Checking the functoriality in
[
35
, Proposition 3.8]
, we have

(2.2)

(X\times_{Y}Z)^{\mathrm{ad}}=X^{\mathrm{ad}}\times_{Y^{\mathrm{ad}}}Z^{\mathrm{ad}}

(3)

Let
X
be a smooth variety over
E
, then
X^{\mathrm{ad}}
is a sousperfectoid analytic adic space.

(4)

Let
\widehat{X}\to\widehat{Y}\leftarrow\widehat{Z}
be formal schemes separated locally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
, then (
2.2
) also holds:

(2.3)

(\widehat{X}\times_{\widehat{Y}}\widehat{Z})^{\mathrm{ad}}=\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}\times_{\widehat{Y}^{\mathrm{ad}}}\widehat{Z}^{\mathrm{ad}}.

(5)

The functor
X^{\Diamond}
(resp.
X^{\diamond}
) can be constructed via taking the diamond functor for adic spaces for
X^{\mathrm{ad}}
(resp.
\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}
); both
(-)^{\Diamond}
and
(-)^{\diamond}
commute with fiber products in the category of separated schemes of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
, and are compatible with (
2.2
) and (
2.3
) under (
2.1
).

2.1.2.

Let us recall some terminology in log geometry. For details, we refer the readers to
[
42
]
,
[
62
]
,
[
18
]
and
[
49
]
.

Let
\mathsf{P}
be a monoid. One can associate a group
\mathsf{P}^{\mathrm{gp}}
with a natural homomorphism between monoids
\mathrm{gp}_{\mathsf{P}}:\mathsf{P}\to\mathsf{P}^{\mathrm{gp}}
; in fact,
\mathsf{P}\mapsto\mathsf{P}^{\mathrm{gp}}
is the left adjoint functor of the natural inclusion of the category of groups into the category of monoids.

We say that
\mathsf{P}
is
integral
if
\mathrm{gp}_{\mathsf{P}}
is injective, say that
\mathsf{P}
is
saturated
, if
\mathsf{P}
is integral and any
x\in\mathsf{P}^{\mathrm{gp}}
such that
nx\in\mathsf{P}
for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
is in
\mathsf{P}
, and say that
\mathsf{P}
is
fine
if
\mathsf{P}
is integral and finitely generated. We say that
\mathsf{P}
is
fs
if
\mathsf{P}
is both fine and saturated. Denote
\overline{\mathsf{P}}:=\mathsf{P}/\mathsf{P}^{\times}
.

We say an adic space
X
is
étale sheafy
if
X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
is a site and if
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
is a sheaf. This is satisfied, for example, when
X
is analytic stably sheafy or is Noetherian affinoid (cf.
[
18
, Cor. A.11]
).

Definition 2.1

(
[
18
, Def. 2.2.2]
)
.

Let
X
be an étale sheafy adic space. A
prelog structure
on X is a pair
(\mathcal{M}_{X},\alpha)
, where
\mathcal{M}_{X}
is a sheaf of monoids over
X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
and
\alpha:\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
is a morphism of sheaves of monoids, and such a pair is a
log structure
if the induced morphism
\alpha^{-1}(\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}})\to\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
is an isomorphism.

A
log adic space
is a triple
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X},\alpha)
consisting of an étale sheafy adic space
X
and a log structure
(\mathcal{M}_{X},\alpha)
as above.

A
morphism

f:(X,\mathcal{M}_{X},\alpha_{X})\to(Y,\mathcal{M}_{Y},\alpha_{Y})
of log adic spaces is a morphism
f:X\to Y
of underlying adic spaces
X
and
Y
with a morphism of sheaves of monoids
f^{\sharp}:f^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{Y}\to\mathcal{M}_{X}
such that the diagram

(2.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\sharp}}

commutes.

We call such a log structure
(\mathcal{M}_{X},\alpha)
a log structure on
X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
(or simply on
X
). We omit
\alpha
if it is not important or is clear in the context.

Definition 2.2
.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a log structure on
X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
.
We say
\mathcal{M}_{X}
is
integral
(resp.
saturated
) if it is a sheaf of integral (resp. saturated) monoids. Let
f:X\to Y
be a morphism of adic spaces. The
inverse image

f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{Y}
of a log structure
\mathcal{M}_{Y}
of
Y
is defined to be the log structure associated with the prelog structure on
X
given by
f^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{Y}\to f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}\to\mathcal{O}_{X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
(cf.
[
42
, (1.4)]
).

(2)

For a sheaf of monoids
\mathcal{M}
on
X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
, denote by
\mathcal{M}^{\times}
its sheaf of invertible elements. Denote by
\mathcal{M}^{\flat}
its tilt
\varprojlim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathcal{M}
.
Following
[
49
, Def. 2.19]
, we say a sheaf of monoids
\mathcal{M}
on
X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
is
perfectoid
if
\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\flat}}:=\mathcal{M}^{\flat}/\mathcal{M}^{\flat,\times}\to\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{M}^{\times}=:\overline{\mathcal{M}}
is an isomorphism. In particular,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}
is
uniquely
p
-divisible
if
\mathcal{M}
is perfectoid (cf.
[
49
, Rmk. 2.23]
).

Definition 2.3

(
[
18
, Def. 2.3.1]
)
.

Let
\mathsf{P}
be a monoid. A
chart of
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
modeled on
\mathsf{P}
is a morphism
\theta:\mathsf{P}_{X}\to\mathcal{M}_{X}
of étale sheaves of monoids such that
\alpha\circ\theta
factors through
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
and the associated log structure of
\alpha\circ\theta
is canonically isomorphic to that of
\alpha
. We say the chart is fs if
\mathsf{P}
is fs.

A log adic space
X
is
fs
(resp.
fine
) if
X
étale locally admits fs (resp. fine) charts. Following the conventions in
[
42
]
, for a prelog ring/space
(A,\mathsf{P})
, one can associate a log ring/space denoted by
(A,\mathsf{P})^{a}
(see also
[
18
, Def. 2.2.2(6)]
); the functor
(-)^{a}
is the left adjoint of the natural inclusion of the category of log adic spaces into the category of prelog adic spaces.

2.1.3.
The cases we consider

From now on, the pair
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
can be the following cases:

(1)

an fs log adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
;

(2)

an fs log scheme over
\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
;

(3)

an fs log formal scheme over
\operatorname{Spf}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.

As a (pre-)adic space might not be étale sheafy in general, sometimes it is worth separating the last two cases.

Let
X
be a scheme over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
or
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.
In some situations, we assume that

(SF):

Let
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
be an fs log scheme over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
or
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
with
X
a separated and of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
or
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

We do not assume the properness of
X\to\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
here.

By
[
18
, Cor. A.11]
, for log schemes satisfying (SF),
X^{\mathrm{ad}}
and
\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}
are both étale sheafy.

There is a natural morphism of étale sites

\nu^{\mathrm{ad}}:=\nu^{\mathrm{ad}}_{X}:X^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}\to X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
(see
[
36
, 3.2.8, p.180]
). Also, there is a natural morphism of étale sites
\widehat{\nu}:=\widehat{\nu}_{X}:\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}\to X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
defined by composing with
\widehat{X}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}^{\mathrm{ad}}\to X^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
.

2.1.4.

To define log diamonds, it is crucial to study a certain class of log structures on perfectoid spaces.

Let
Y
be a perfectoid space. Denote by
Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
(resp.
Y_{v}
) the étale site (resp.
v
-site) of
Y
. There is a natural projection of sites

\nu:Y_{v}\to Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}.

By
[
77
, Thm. 8.7]
,
\mathcal{O}_{Y_{v}}
is a
v
-sheaf. So it makes sense to define log structures on
Y_{v}
.

Definition 2.4
.

A prelog structure on
Y_{v}
is a pair
(\mathcal{M}_{Y},\alpha)
consisting of a
v
-sheaf of monoids
\mathcal{M}_{Y}
in
Y_{v}^{\sim}
and a morphism between
v
-sheaves
\alpha:\mathcal{M}_{Y}\to\mathcal{O}_{Y_{v}}
. It is a log structure on
Y_{v}
if
\alpha^{-1}(\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{Y_{v}})\to\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{Y_{v}}
is an isomorphism.

The definitions of morphisms and charts are also similar to those defined for log structures on
Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
(cf. Definition
2.1
and
2.3
).

From now on, we let
Y_{\tau}
stand for
Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
(resp.
Y_{v}
) if
\tau={\text{{\'{e}}t}}
(resp.
\tau=v
).
We say a sheaf of monoids
\mathcal{M}
on
Y_{\tau}
is uniquely
p
-divisible (resp. perfectoid) if

\mathcal{M}\xrightarrow{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathcal{M}
(resp.

\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\flat}}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}
) is an isomorphism. That is, for any
U\in Y_{\tau}
,
\mathcal{M}(U)
is uniquely
p
-divisible (resp.
\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\flat}}(U)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}(U)
is an isomorphism).

The following property is weaker than being uniquely
n
-divisible but stronger than being
n
-torsion-free.

Definition 2.5
.

Let
n
be a positive integer and
\mathsf{P}
a monoid. We say that
\mathsf{P}
is
n
-unique
if
\mathsf{P}\xrightarrow{n\cdot}\mathsf{P}
is injective. Similarly, a sheaf of monoids
\mathcal{M}
on a site
\mathcal{C}
is
n
-unique if
\mathcal{M}(U)
is
n
-unique for any
U\in\ob\mathcal{C}
.

Definition 2.6
.

Let
Y
be a perfectoid space. Set
\tau=\text{{\'{e}}t}
or
v
. Let
\mathbb{N}[\frac{1}{p}]\subset\mathbb{Q}
be the monoid consisting of elements of the form
\frac{a}{b}
where
a\in\mathbb{N}
and
b\in\{p^{n}\}
for
n\geq 0
.

(1)

A uniquely
p
-divisible monoid
\mathsf{P}
is called
p
-finitely generated
if
\mathsf{P}
is
n
-unique for all positive integers
n
, and there is a finite set of elements
S\subset\mathsf{P}
such that the set of all
p^{i}
-th roots of elements of
S
generates
\mathsf{P}
for integers
i>0
. In other words, there is a surjection of monoids
\mathbb{N}[\frac{1}{p}]^{\oplus n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{P}
.

(2)

A uniquely
p
-divisible monoid
\mathsf{P}
is called
p
-weakly finitely generated
if
\mathsf{P}
is
n
-unique for all positive integers
n
, and there is a uniquely
p
-divisible,
p
-finitely generated submonoid
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}\subset\mathsf{P}
such that
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}\subset\mathsf{P}\subset\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}\mathsf{P^{\prime}}
. Note that the notation
\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}\mathsf{P}^{\prime}:=\varinjlim_{a\mapsto na,a\in\mathsf{P},n\geq 1}\mathsf{P}
and the inclusions make sense because of the
n
-uniqueness assumption.

(3)

A perfectoid log structure
(\mathcal{M}_{Y},\alpha)
on
Y_{\tau}
is
p
-coherent
(resp.
p
-weakly coherent
) if, for any geometric point
\overline{x}
of
Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
, there is an étale neighborhood
U_{\overline{x}}
such that
\mathcal{M}_{Y}|_{U_{\overline{x}}}
admits a
p
-finitely generated (resp.
p
-weakly finitely generated) chart
\mathsf{P}_{U_{\overline{x}}}
(of log structures on
(U_{\overline{x}})_{\tau}
).

(4)

A log structure
(\mathcal{M}_{Y},\alpha)
on
Y_{\tau}
is called
fine perfectoid
(resp. strongly fine perfectoid) if
\mathcal{M}_{Y}
is integral, perfectoid, and
p
-weakly coherent (resp.
p
-coherent).

Remark 2.7
.

A log structure
\mathcal{M}
on
Y_{v}
is fine perfectoid implies that
\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}
on
Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
is fine perfectoid. In fact, to see that
\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}
is perfectoid, note that
\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}\cong\nu_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}
and
\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\flat}}\cong\nu_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\flat}}
; this follows from Lemma
2.25
. Then the isomorphism of sheaves holds after pushforward.

Remark 2.8
.

We remark that the integrality is crucial in the proof of Theorem
2.18
, while perfectoidness and (weak)
p
-finite generation will be used in the proof of Theorem
2.49
.

We discuss some basic properties of the log structures defined above.

Lemma 2.9

(cf.
[
18
, Lem. 2.1.10]
)
.

Let
\lambda:\mathsf{M}\to\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
be a surjective morphism between integral monoids such that
\ker\lambda^{\mathrm{gp}}\subset\mathsf{M}
. Suppose that
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
is additionally saturated, sharp, uniquely
p
-divisible, and
p
-finitely generated monoid. Then
\lambda
admits a section.

Proof.

By integrality, there are injections
\mathsf{M}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{M}^{\mathrm{gp}}
and
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{P}^{\prime,\mathrm{gp}}
. Since
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
is saturated and sharp,
\mathsf{P}^{\prime,\mathrm{gp}}
is torsion-free. As there is a surjection
\mathbb{N}[\frac{1}{p}]^{N}\to\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
for some positive integer
N
, we know that there is a surjection
\mathbb{Z}[\frac{1}{p}]^{N}\to\mathsf{P}^{\prime,\mathrm{gp}}
. So
\mathsf{P}^{\prime,\mathrm{gp}}\cong\mathbb{Z}[\frac{1}{p}]^{n}
for some
n\leq N
as
\mathbb{Z}{[1/p]}
is a PID. So
\lambda^{\mathrm{gp}}:\mathsf{M}^{\mathrm{gp}}\to\mathsf{P}^{\prime,\mathrm{gp}}
admits a section. Since
\ker\lambda^{\mathrm{gp}}\subset\mathsf{M}
, we see this section restricted to
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
must be contained in
\mathsf{M}
. In fact, for any
s\in\mathsf{M}
,
\lambda^{\mathrm{gp},-1}(\lambda(s))=\lambda^{-1}(\lambda(s))\subset\mathsf{M}
.
∎

Lemma 2.10
.

Let
(\mathcal{M}_{Y},\alpha)
be a saturated and fine perfectoid log structure on
Y_{\tau}
. Then
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}
is saturated, uniquely
p
-divisible, and
p
-weakly finitely generated.

Proof.

We only show it when
\tau=\text{{\'{e}}t}
. The case when
\tau=v
is similar. Saturatedness and unique
p
-divisibility follow from the saturatedness and the fine-perfectoidness of
\mathcal{M}_{Y}
, respectively. The question is étale local, so we assume that
\mathcal{M}_{Y}
admits a global (uniquely
p
-divisible and)
p
-weakly finitely generated chart
\mathsf{P}
. From the definition above, there is a
p
-finitely generated submonoid
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
and inclusions
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}\subset\mathsf{P}\subset\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
. Fix a surjection
\mathbb{N}[\frac{1}{p}]^{n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
with a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{N}[\frac{1}{p}]^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{P}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}\mathsf{P}^{\prime},}

where
\mathsf{N}
is the pullback of
\mathsf{P}
via
\mathbb{Q}^{n}_{\geq 0}\to\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}^{\prime}
be the image of
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}_{Y}
via
\mathsf{P}_{Y}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}
. The diagram above extends to a commutative diagram

(2.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{N}[\frac{1}{p}]^{n}_{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{N}_{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0,Y}^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{P}^{\prime}_{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{P}_{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}\mathsf{P}^{\prime})_{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}.}

Fix
U\in Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
and
x_{1},x_{2}\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}(U)
such that
nx_{1}=nx_{2}
for some positive integer
n
. Replacing
U
with a cover, assume that
x_{1}
(resp.
x_{2}
) lifts to
y_{1}
(resp.
y_{2}
) in
\mathsf{N}
. Taking
(*)^{\mathrm{gp}}
to the left diagram, we have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}[\frac{1}{p}]^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{N}^{\mathrm{gp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}_{Y})^{\mathrm{gp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}^{\mathrm{gp}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}

Since
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}
is saturated and sharp, both
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}
and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}^{\mathrm{gp}}
are torsion-free. Hence, if
y\in\mathsf{N}^{\mathrm{gp}}
such that
ny\in\ker\beta_{p}
, then
y\in\ker\beta
. So
y_{1}-y_{2}\in\ker\beta
. This implies that
x_{1}=x_{2}
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}^{\mathrm{gp}}(U)
and,
a priori
, in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}(U)
by integrality. Now we have proved the
n
-uniqueness of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}
. Other properties follow from the diagram (
2.5
).
∎

Lemma 2.11
.

Let
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
be a saturated, sharp, uniquely
p
-divisible, and
p
-finitely generated monoid. Then there is an fs sharp submonoid
\mathsf{P}_{0}\subset\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
such that
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}=\mathsf{P}_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]
, where
\mathsf{P}_{0}[\frac{1}{p}]:=\varinjlim_{n}\frac{\mathsf{P}_{0}}{p^{n}}\subset\mathsf{P}^{\prime,\mathrm{gp}}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
is sharp and saturated, we embed
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
into
\mathsf{P}^{\prime,\mathrm{gp}}\cong\mathbb{Z}[\frac{1}{p}]^{n}
(see the proof of Lemma
2.9
). Then, by the definition of being
p
-finitely generated, there is a set
S_{0}
of
\mathbb{N}[\frac{1}{p}]
-generators of
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
. Let
\mathsf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{gp}}
be the
\mathbb{Z}
-module generated by
S_{0}
in
\mathsf{P}^{\prime,\mathrm{gp}}
, and
\mathsf{M}_{0}
be the (
\mathbb{N}
-)monoid generated by
S_{0}
in
\mathsf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{gp}}\cap\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
.
Let
\mathsf{P}_{0}
be the saturation of
\mathsf{M}_{0}
(in
\mathsf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{gp}}
); this monoid is saturated, finitely generated by
[
62
, I. Cor. 2.1.11]
, and integral by construction.

Note that the saturation
\mathsf{P}_{0}
of
\mathsf{M}_{0}\subset\mathsf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{gp}}\cap\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
is in
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
, as
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
is saturated.
Hence,
\mathsf{P}_{0}
is fine by the above paragraph, and is sharp since it is included in a sharp monoid
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 2.12
.

Let
(\mathcal{M}_{Y},\alpha)
be a saturated and strongly fine perfectoid log structure on
Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
.

Then, for any geometric point
\overline{x}
of
Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
, there is an étale neighborhood
U_{\overline{x}}
such that
\mathcal{M}_{Y}
admits a chart modeled on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,\overline{x}}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of
[
18
, Prop. 2.3.12-2.3.13]
, but it is much more involved here, as we do not assume that the log structure is fs. Since this is a local question, we assume that there is an
p
-finitely generated
\mathsf{P}
together with
\theta:\mathsf{P}\to\mathcal{M}_{Y}
factoring through
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
, such that
\theta^{a}:(\mathsf{P}_{X})^{a}\to\mathcal{M}_{Y}
is an isomorphism. Note that this implies that
\overline{\theta}_{\overline{x}}:\mathsf{P}\to(\mathsf{P}_{X})^{a}/(\alpha\circ\theta)^{-1}(\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}},\overline{x}})\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,\overline{x}}
is surjective (see
[
18
, Rmk. 2.3.4]
).
Our goal is reduced to finding a
p
-finitely generated chart
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}:=\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,\overline{x}}
in an étale neighborhood of
\overline{x}
.

Firstly, we show that there is a section at the geometric point
\overline{x}
.
There is a natural surjective map between integral monoids

\mathcal{M}^{\flat}_{Y,\overline{x}}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\flat}_{Y,\overline{x}}}=\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,\overline{x}}.

The last equality follows from the definition of perfectoid log structures.
By Lemma
2.9
above, there is a section from
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,\overline{x}}
to
\mathcal{M}^{\flat}_{Y,\overline{x}}
.
Let
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}:=\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,\overline{x}}
and fix a section
\mathsf{s}^{\flat}:\mathsf{P}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}^{\flat}_{Y,\overline{x}}
. Composing
\mathsf{s}^{\flat}
with the natural projection
p_{0}:\mathcal{M}^{\flat}_{Y,\overline{x}}\to\mathcal{M}_{Y,\overline{x}}
, we obtain a section

\mathsf{s}:\mathsf{P}^{\prime}\cong(\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\flat}_{Y}})_{\overline{x}}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{s}^{\flat}}\mathcal{M}^{\flat}_{Y,\overline{x}}\xrightarrow{p_{0}}\mathcal{M}_{Y,\overline{x}}.

Denote by
\mathsf{P}_{0}
the fs sharp submonoid in
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
as constructed in Lemma
2.11
, and denote by
S_{0}^{\prime}
the finite set of generators of
\mathsf{P}_{0}
. By the proof of both Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
2.11
, there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{P}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{P}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{gp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{P}^{\prime,\mathrm{gp}},}

where
\mathsf{P}_{0}^{\mathrm{gp}}
(resp.
\mathsf{P}^{\prime,\mathrm{gp}}
) is a finite free
\mathbb{Z}
- (resp.
\mathbb{Z}[\frac{1}{p}]
-) module.
Since
\mathcal{M}_{Y}
is perfectoid,
\mathcal{M}_{Y,\overline{x}}
is also perfectoid. Since
\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\flat}_{Y}}_{\overline{x}}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,\overline{x}}
is an isomorphism, we can lift any
s\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y,\overline{x}}
to
\widetilde{s}=(s_{0},s_{1},\dots)\in\mathcal{M}^{\flat}_{Y,\overline{x}}
via
\mathsf{s}
. The
p^{n}
-th root of
\widetilde{s}
taken in
\mathcal{M}^{\flat}_{Y,\overline{x}}
is given by shifting the limit representing
\widetilde{s}
to the left by
n
.

Consider the map
\alpha\circ\mathsf{s}:\mathsf{P}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}^{\flat}_{Y,\overline{x}}\to\mathcal{M}_{Y,\overline{x}}\xrightarrow{\alpha}\mathcal{O}_{Y,\overline{x}}
. We can make this composition factor through
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y,\overline{x}}
by adjusting
\mathsf{s}
, which will be explained now.
Since
Y
is perfectoid (and Tate), we have that
\{f\in\mathcal{O}_{Y,\overline{x}}||f(\overline{x})|>1\}\subset\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{Y,\overline{x}}
and
\{f\in\mathcal{O}_{Y,\overline{x}}||f(\overline{x})|\leq 1\}=\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y,\overline{x}}
. If there is
c\in S_{0}^{\prime}
such that
|\alpha\circ\mathsf{s}(c)(\overline{x})|>1
, then
f_{c}:=\alpha\circ\mathsf{s}(c)\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{Y,\overline{x}}
.
Assume that
|f_{c}(\overline{x})|
is maximal among all
c_{i}\in S_{0}^{\prime}
(noting that
|S_{0}^{\prime}|
is finite). Write
S_{0}^{\prime}=\{c_{i}\}_{i\in I}
.
By
[
62
, I. Cor. 2.2.7]
(see also
[
18
, Lem. 2.3.12]
),
\mathsf{P}_{0}
is embedded in some
\mathbb{N}^{m}
, where
c_{i}
can be uniquely represented by a tuple
\underline{c}_{i}=(n^{i}_{1},\cdots,n^{i}_{m})\neq(0,0,\cdots,0)
of natural numbers. Denote by
||\underline{c}_{i}||
the sum of the coefficients of
\underline{c}_{i}
representing
c_{i}
; it is not less than
1
.
Define a map

\mathsf{s}^{\flat}_{/f_{c}}:\sum_{i\in I}a_{i}c_{i}\mapsto\prod_{i\in I}(\mathsf{s}^{\flat}(c_{i})\cdot p_{0}^{-1}[(\mathsf{s}(c))^{-||\underline{c}_{i}||}])^{a_{i}},

where
a_{i}\in\mathbb{N}[\frac{1}{p}]
. The function
(-)^{a_{i}}
makes sense, as explained in the last paragraph. Note that
\mathsf{s}^{\flat}_{/f_{c}}
is well-defined: On
\mathsf{P}_{0}
, it is well-defined because
\underline{c}_{i}
is uniquely defined; on
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
, note that any element lies in
\mathsf{P}_{0}
by multiplying some
p^{k}
and that
\mathcal{M}_{Y,\overline{x}}^{\flat}
is uniquely
p
-divisible by the explanation in the last paragraph.

Then
\mathsf{s}^{\flat}_{/f_{c}}:\mathsf{P}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}^{\flat}_{Y,\overline{x}}
is a
\mathbb{N}[\frac{1}{p}]
-equivariant morphism. Set
\mathsf{s}_{/f_{c}}:=p_{0}\circ\mathsf{s}^{\flat}_{/f_{c}}
. Then
\alpha\circ\mathsf{s}_{/f_{c}}
factors through
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{Y,\overline{x}}
, as desired.

Set
\theta^{\prime}_{\overline{x}}:=\mathsf{s}_{/f_{c}}
and
\theta^{\prime,a}_{\overline{x}}:(\mathsf{P}^{\prime})^{a}\to\mathcal{M}_{Y,\overline{x}}
.
There is an étale neighborhood
U
of
\overline{x}
such that
\theta^{\prime}_{\overline{x}}
extends to
\theta^{\prime}:\mathsf{P}^{\prime}_{U}\to\mathcal{M}_{Y}|_{U}
, since
\mathsf{s}^{\flat}:\mathsf{P}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{M}^{\flat}_{Y,\overline{x}}
extends to an étale neighborhood. Consequently, there is an extension
\theta^{\prime,a}:=(\theta^{\prime})^{a}
for
\theta^{\prime,a}_{\overline{x}}
on
U
. We only need to show that
\theta^{\prime,a}
is an isomorphism up to shrinking the étale neighborhood
U
.

Indeed,
\theta^{\prime,a}
is surjective over some
U_{1}\in Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}\to U
, since
\mathcal{M}_{Y}|_{U}/\theta^{\prime,a}(\mathsf{P}^{\prime,a}_{U})=\theta^{a}(\mathsf{P}^{a}_{U})/\theta^{\prime,a}(\mathsf{P}^{\prime,a}_{U})
is trivial at
\overline{x}
, and both
\mathsf{P}
and
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
are uniquely
p
-divisible and
p
-finitely generated.

Moreover, this map is injective. The congruence relation
\mathsf{R}_{0}
in
\mathsf{P}_{0}\times\mathsf{P}_{0}
induced by
\mathsf{P}_{0}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}
is finitely generated by
[
62
, I. Lem. 2.1.9]
and is trivial at
\overline{x}
. Hence, it is trivial over some étale neighborhood
U_{2}
of
\overline{x}
, which we assume there is an étale morphism
U_{2}\to U_{1}
. As both
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}
are integral and uniquely
p
-divisible, the congruence relation
\mathsf{R}\subset\mathsf{P}^{\prime}\times\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
induced by
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}
is trivial over
U_{2,\text{{\'{e}}t}}
. Indeed, for
a,b\in\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
, if
a\sim b
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}
over
U_{2,\text{{\'{e}}t}}
, so is
p^{i}a
and
p^{i}b
. Conversely, we can divide relations in
\mathsf{R}_{0}
by
p
: if
a\sim b
, let
a^{\prime}=\frac{a}{p},b^{\prime}=\frac{b}{p}
in
\mathsf{P}^{\prime}
, then the images
c_{1},c_{2}
of
a^{\prime}
and
b^{\prime}
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}
are equal since
pc_{1}=pc_{2}
by the unique
p
-divisibility of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{Y}
. So
\mathsf{R}
is trivial if and only if
\mathsf{R}_{0}
is trivial.
This completes the proof.
∎

2.1.5.

We define log diamonds for the cases in §
2.1.3
.

In Case
1
,

Definition 2.13
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X},\alpha)
be an (étale sheafy) fs log adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Then the log diamond
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond}
(or
X^{\log\Diamond}
) is a functor sending
S=\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\in\ob\operatorname{Perf}
to the isomorphism classes
\{(S^{\sharp},\iota,\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f)\}/\simeq
, where
(S^{\sharp},\iota)
denotes an untilt as in §
2.1.1
,
\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}
is a saturated and fine perfectoid log structure, and
f:S^{\sharp}\to X
is a morphism between adic spaces that induces a morphism between log structures
f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}
.

In Case
2
, we can also define small and big log diamonds.

Definition 2.14
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
be an fs log scheme over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. We define a
log big diamond
functor
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond}
(or
X^{\log\Diamond}
) as

S=\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\in\operatorname{Perf}\mapsto\{(S^{\sharp}=\operatorname{Spa}(A^{\sharp},A^{\sharp,+}),\iota,\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f)\}/\simeq,

where all but the last term in the tuple are as above, and
f:\operatorname{Spa}(A^{\sharp},A^{\sharp,+})\to\operatorname{Spec}A^{\sharp}\to X
induces a morphism
f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}
; note that
f
induces a morphism between étale sites
S^{\sharp}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}\to(\operatorname{Spec}A^{\sharp})_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}\to X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
, and that all perfectoid spaces are étale sheafy by
[
18
, Cor. A.11]
.

We define a
log small diamond
functor
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\diamond}
(or
X^{\log\diamond}
) in a similarly-written form
S\in\operatorname{Perf}\mapsto\{(S^{\sharp}=\operatorname{Spa}(A^{\sharp},A^{\sharp,+}),\iota,\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f)\}/\simeq
, but here
f
is a morphism
f:\operatorname{Spa}(A^{\sharp},A^{\sharp,+})\to\operatorname{Spec}A^{\sharp,+}\to X
.

In Case
3
, we can associate a log diamond functor; we denote it by
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\diamond}
or
X^{\log\diamond}
.

Definition 2.15
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
be an fs log formal scheme over
\operatorname{Spf}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. The functor
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond}
sends
S=\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\in\operatorname{Perf}
to

\{(S^{\sharp}=\operatorname{Spa}(A^{\sharp},A^{\sharp,+}),\iota,\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f)\}/\simeq,

where
f:S^{\sharp}\to X
is a morphism that induces
f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}
and other components are the same as above.

This symbol is compatible with the log small diamond for Case
2
: Let
\operatorname{Spf}(A,I)
be an affine formally of finite type formal scheme. Suppose that
A
is also
p
-adic complete. Let
X=\operatorname{Spec}A
; and we equip
X
with an fs log structure
\mathcal{M}
. Then
X^{\log\diamond}
is by definition the same as
(\operatorname{Spf}(A,(p)),\mathcal{M})^{\Diamond}
; here the latter
\mathcal{M}
is the log structure pulled back from
X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
.

In the case of (bounded) fs log
p
-adic formal schemes, Definition
2.15
essentially comes from the category
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{\textrm{perf}}
in
[
49
, Def. 7.34 and Thm. 7.35]
.

Remark 2.16

(cf. Remark
2.8
)
.

As we can see later from Lemma
2.37
, one might remove the
p
-finite generation condition in the definition of log diamonds given above. But it is this more restrictive class of log structures that is useful to our purpose.

Assume that
X
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme satisfying (SF). The diamond functors in Case
2
also admit constructions via the diamond functor in the log adic space case.

Lemma 2.17
.

Under the assumption above, the log big diamond functor can be constructed as a functor
sending any affinoid perfectoid
S=\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\in\ob\operatorname{Perf}
to the isomorphism classes

\{(S^{\sharp},\iota,\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f)\}/\simeq,

where
f:(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}})\to(X^{\mathrm{ad}},\nu^{\mathrm{ad},-1}\mathcal{M}_{X})
a morphism. More precisely,
f
is a morphism between adic spaces
f:S^{\sharp}\to X^{\mathrm{ad}}
that induces a morphism of sites

S^{\sharp}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}\xrightarrow{f}X^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}\xrightarrow{\nu^{\mathrm{ad}}}X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}

together with a morphism
f^{-1}\circ\nu^{\mathrm{ad},-1}\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}
.

Similarly, the log small diamond functor can be constructed as a functor the same as above, but changing
f
to
f:(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}})\to(\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}},\widehat{\nu}^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{X})
, which, more precisely, is a morphism of adic spaces
f:S^{\sharp}\to\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}
that induces a morphism of sites:

S^{\sharp}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}\xrightarrow{f}\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}\xrightarrow{\widehat{\nu}}X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}

together with a morphism
f^{-1}\circ\widehat{\nu}^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}
.

Proof.

The fact that one can construct diamonds via
X^{\mathrm{ad}}
and
\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}
is in
[
1
, §2.2]
. By
[
18
, Cor. A.11]
,
X^{\mathrm{ad}}
and
\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}
are étale sheafy.
∎

The main theorem of §
2.1
is the following:

Theorem 2.18
.

The functors
X^{\log\Diamond}
and
X^{\log\diamond}
in Definition
2.13
, Definition
2.14
and Definition
2.15
are
v
-sheaves over
\operatorname{Perf}
.

We will complete the proof of this theorem in §
2.1.6
and §
2.1.7
.

2.1.6.

Let
Y
be a perfectoid space. Let
\nu:Y_{v}\to Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
be the natural projection from the
v
-site to the étale site of
Y
. There is a pullback functor
\nu^{-1}:Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}^{\sim}\to Y_{v}^{\sim}
and a pushforward functor
\nu_{*}:Y_{v}^{\sim}\to Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}^{\sim}
between topoi.

Let
\mathcal{LOG}_{?}
(resp.
\mathcal{LOG}_{?}^{\mathrm{int}}
) be the category (resp. the full subcategory) of log structures (resp. integral log structures) on
Y_{?}
for
?=\text{{\'{e}}t}
and
v
. Let
\mathcal{M}
be a log structure on
Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
. Define the pullback of
\mathcal{M}
to
Y_{v}
as
\nu^{*}\mathcal{M}:=(\nu^{-1}\mathcal{M})^{a}
.

The following statement can be viewed as an analogue of
[
63
, App. A]
and
[
62
, III. Prop. 1.4.1]
for perfectoid spaces.

Lemma 2.19
.

With the conventions above, the pullback
\nu^{*}:\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}\to\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{v}}
restricts to a functor from
\mathcal{LOG}^{\mathrm{int}}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
to
\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{v}}^{\mathrm{int}}
, and this restriction induces an equivalence from the category of fine perfectoid log structures on
Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
to the category of fine perfectoid log structures on
Y_{v}
.

Moreover, it induces an equivalence between the category of saturated and fine perfectoid log structures on
Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
and the category of saturated and fine perfectoid log structures on
Y_{v}
.

We will show Lemma
2.19
in the rest of §
2.1.6
. The procedures are similar to the two references above.

We first show the full faithfulness of
\nu^{*}|_{\mathcal{LOG}^{\mathrm{int}}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}}
and show that the essential image of this functor lies in
\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{v}}^{\mathrm{int}}
.

Lemma 2.20
.

The functor
\nu^{*}:\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}^{\mathrm{int}}\to\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{v}}
is fully faithful. That is, we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{v}}}(\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1},\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}_{2})

for integral log structures
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2}\in\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}^{\mathrm{int}}
.

Proof.

Denote by
\mathcal{LOG}^{\mathrm{pre}}_{Y_{?}}
the category of prelog structures on the
?
topology of
Y
. We have
