\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{v}}}(\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1},\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}_{2})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{v}}^{\mathrm{pre}}}(\nu^{-1}\mathcal{L}_{1},\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}_{2})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}^{\mathrm{pre}}}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\nu_{*}\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}_{2})
by adjunctions. The last one is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\nu_{*}\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}_{2})
. It suffices to show that the last term is canonically isomorphic to
\mathcal{L}_{2}
, which we will do in Lemma
2.21
.
∎

Lemma 2.21
.

The canonical morphism between log structures
\mathcal{L}\to\nu_{*}\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}
is an isomorphism for any
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{LOG}^{\mathrm{int}}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
.

Proof.

There is a functor defined on
Y_{v}
by

(f:Y^{\prime}\to Y)\in Y_{v}\mapsto f^{*}\mathcal{L}:=(f^{-1}\mathcal{L})^{a}\in\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}^{\prime}}.

We claim that the presheaf

(f:Y^{\prime}\to Y)\mapsto f^{*}\mathcal{L}(Y^{\prime})

is a sheaf on
Y_{v}
.
If we know this, then
\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}
is this (pre)sheaf, and the desired result follows from the definition of
\nu_{*}
.

We now show the claim in the last paragraph. Let
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}:=f^{*}\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}=p_{1}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{L}=p_{2}^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{L}
.
We show that the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{eq}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime})).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}^{*}}

is an isomorphism.

First of all,
f^{*}\mathcal{L}
is integral: By
[
18
, Lem. 2.2.4]
and
[
62
, I. Prop. 1.3.4]
, we check pointwise that
f^{-1}\mathcal{L}
is integral and
f^{*}\mathcal{L}
is integral.

Consider the commutative diagram

(2.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}^{\times}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{eq}(\mathcal{O}^{\times}(Y^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}^{\times}(Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{eq}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{L}}(Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{eq}(\overline{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime\prime}(Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime})).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}^{*}}

It follows from
[
77
, Thm. 8.7]
that
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
is a
v
-sheaf, so is
\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{Y}
. We have the isomorphism of the first row of (
2.6
).
The last row is also an isomorphism. This follows from
[
77
, Prop. 14.7 and 14.8]
.

We now examine the middle row of (
2.6
).
Then it follows from a diagram chasing that
\mathcal{L}(V)\to\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(V\times_{Y}Y^{\prime})
is injective for any
V\in Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
: Indeed, suppose that there are sections
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathcal{L}(V)
mapping to the same element
s\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(V\times_{Y}Y^{\prime})
. We have that
s_{1}=s_{2}+u
for
u\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}(V)
. Then
s=s+u
in
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(V\times_{Y}Y^{\prime})
, and therefore
u=0
in
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(V\times_{Y}Y^{\prime})
by integrality. Then
u=0
in
\mathcal{O}^{\times}(V)\to\mathcal{L}(V)
by the paragraph above, as desired.

On the other hand, for any
s\in\mathrm{eq}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(Y^{\prime})\rightrightarrows\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}(Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}Y^{\prime}))
, there is étale locally
s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(U)
for some
U\in Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
mapping to
s\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}U)
by diagram chasing.
The element
s^{\prime}
on the étale cover
U
of
Y
glues to a section on
Y
since we can check it over the cover
Y^{\prime}\times_{Y}U
by the injectivity we just proved. We now have the isomorphism of the middle arrow, as desired.
∎

In the proof, we have shown that

Corollary 2.22
.

The pullback functor
\nu^{*}
restricts to a fully faithful functor

\nu^{*}:\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}^{\mathrm{int}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{v}}^{\mathrm{int}}.

Corollary 2.23
.

The functor
\nu^{*}:\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}^{\mathrm{int}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{v}}^{\mathrm{int}}
sends fine perfectoid objects to fine perfectoid objects.

Proof.

All conditions we need follow from the construction in Lemma
2.21
.
∎

We then show the essential surjectivity. Fix
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{LOG}_{Y_{v}}
that is fine perfectoid. We show that

Lemma 2.24
.

The adjunction morphism
\nu^{*}\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

There is an exact sequence
1\to\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}\to\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}\to\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}\to 0
. Applying
\nu^{-1}
, we get

1\to\nu^{-1}\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}\to\nu^{-1}\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}\to\nu^{-1}\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}\to 0
. Moreover, we have a commutative diagram of exact sequences

(2.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{*}\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{*}\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{-1}\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{Y_{v}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

We first show that the composition

(2.8)

\nu^{-1}\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}\to\nu^{-1}\nu_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}

is injective.
By the proof of Lemma
2.21
, for any
v
-cover
(f:X\to Y)\in Y_{v}
,
\nu^{*}\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}(X)
(resp.
\nu^{-1}\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}(X)
) is given by
f^{*}\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}(X)
(resp.
f^{-1}\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}(X)
) with a surjection
f^{*}\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}\to f^{-1}\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}
obtained by pulling back
\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}\to\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}
.
Fix such a morphism
f
. Suppose that there are
\overline{s}_{1},\overline{s}_{2}\in f^{-1}\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}(X)
mapping to the same element
\overline{s}\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}(X)
.
Up to replacing
X
with an étale cover, there is a cover
U\in X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
that is the pullback of an étale cover
U_{Y}
of
Y
via
f
, such that
\overline{s}_{1},\overline{s}_{2}\in\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}(U_{Y})=\nu_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}(U_{Y})=\overline{\mathcal{M}}(U_{Y})
. The equation
\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}=\nu_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}
is proved in Lemma
2.25
below.
Moreover, they lift to
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathcal{M}(U_{Y}^{\prime})
for some cover
U_{Y}^{\prime}\in U_{Y,\text{{\'{e}}t}}
, respectively.
On the other hand, there is a
v
-cover
V\to U^{\prime}:=U_{Y}^{\prime}\times_{Y}X
with
u\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}(V)
such that
s_{1}=s_{2}+u
in
\mathcal{M}(V)
. The descent data of
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
from
V
to
U^{\prime}
, along with the integrality of
\mathcal{M}
, induces a descent datum of
u
from
V
to
U^{\prime}
. So
u\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}(U^{\prime})
and
\overline{s}_{1}=\overline{s}_{2}
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}(U^{\prime})
. We have shown the desired claim since
U^{\prime}\to U_{Y}
is a
v
-cover.

A similar argument as above implies that
\nu^{*}\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}
is injective. For
Y^{\prime}\in Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
and
s_{1},s_{2}\in\nu^{*}\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}(Y^{\prime})
mapping to
s\in\mathcal{M}(Y^{\prime})
. By diagram chasing,
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
project to the same
\overline{s}\in\nu^{-1}\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}(Y^{\prime})
and
s_{1}=s_{2}+u^{\prime}
in
\nu^{*}\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}(Y^{\prime\prime})
, where
Y^{\prime\prime}
is a
v
-cover of
Y^{\prime}
and
u\in\mathcal{O}^{\times}(Y^{\prime\prime})
. By the integrality of
\nu^{*}\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}
,
u
descends to
\mathcal{O}^{\times}(Y^{\prime})
. Mapping to
\mathcal{M}(Y^{\prime})
, by the integrality of
\mathcal{M}
and
\nu^{*}\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}=\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{Y_{v}}
, we have that
u=0
, as desired.

We next show that (
2.8
) is an isomorphism. In fact, by assumption, for any geometric point
\overline{x}\in X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
, there is an étale neighborhood
U_{\overline{x}}
of it such that
\mathcal{M}
admits a chart modeled on a weakly
p
-finitely generated
\mathsf{P}
denoted by
\theta:\mathsf{P}_{U_{\overline{x}}}\to\mathcal{M}|_{U_{\overline{x}}}
, and such that
\theta
factors through
\nu^{*}\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}|_{U_{\overline{x}}}
.
Then there is a morphism
\mathsf{P}\to\nu^{-1}\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}\to\nu^{-1}\nu_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}
over
U_{\overline{x}}
whose composition is surjective. Then (
2.8
) is surjective, as desired.

Finally, the remainder of the lemma follows from a diagram chasing for (
2.7
).
∎

In fact, we have

Lemma 2.25
.

The natural morphism
\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}\to\nu_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

It is an injection, as there is a commutative diagram of exact sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{*}\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{Y_{v}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

and the injectivity is proved by diagram chasing. We show that this is a surjection. It can be checked that, for
U\in Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
,
\overline{\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}}(U)=

\{s\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}(U)|s\text{ can be lifted to }s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}(V)\text{ for some {\'{e}}tale cover }V\to U\}.

Fix
s\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}(U)
for
U\in Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
. The functor
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{s}
sending
V\in U_{v}
to
\{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}(V)|s^{\prime}\mapsto s|_{V}\}
is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-torsor on
U_{v}
. By
[
75
, Lem. 17.1.8]
and
[
44
, Thm. 2.5.8]
,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{s}
is the pullback of an étale
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-torsor
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{s}=\nu_{*}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{s}
. So
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{s}
étale locally has a section, and
s
étale locally admits a lifting.
∎

We now show the second paragraph of Lemma
2.19
.

Lemma 2.26
.

Let
\mathcal{L}\in\mathcal{LOG}^{\mathrm{int}}_{Y_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
. Then
\mathcal{L}
is fine perfectoid if and only if
\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}
is. In this case,
\mathcal{L}
is saturated if and only if
\nu^{*}\mathcal{L}
is so.

Proof.

For fine perfectoidness, the “only if” part is Corollary
2.23
.
For the “only if” part of saturatedness, if
\mathcal{L}
is saturated, then, by the construction in Lemma
2.21
, we see that
f^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{L}}
is saturated by
[
18
, Lem. 2.2.4]
, and
f^{*}\mathcal{L}
is saturated by
[
62
, I. Prop. 1.3.5]
and
[
18
, Lem. 2.2.4]
.

The “if” parts are immediate from Lemma
2.21
and the definitions.
∎

This completes the proof of Lemma
2.19
.

2.1.7.
End of the proof

From Lemma
2.19
, we obtain the following proposition:

Proposition 2.27
.

Let
(Y,\mathcal{M}_{Y})
be a log perfectoid space (cf.
[
18
, Def. 2.2.2(9)]
) that is fs. Then the
v
-descent data of the functor sending
(Y^{\prime},f:Y^{\prime}\to Y)\in\operatorname{Perfd}_{/Y}
to the groupoid

\{\text{saturated and fine perfectoid log structures }\mathcal{M}\text{ on }Y^{\prime}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}\text{ with }f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{Y}\to\mathcal{M}\}

are effective.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.19
, the functor in the proposition is isomorphic to the functor sending
(Y^{\prime},f:Y^{\prime}\to Y)\in\operatorname{Perfd}_{/Y}
to the groupoid

\{\text{saturated and fine perfectoid log structures }\mathcal{M}\text{ on }Y_{v}^{\prime}\text{ with }\nu^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{Y}\to\mathcal{M}\}.

Let
h:Y^{\prime\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
be a
v
-cover. Let
(\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime})
be a saturated and fine perfectoid log structure on
Y^{\prime\prime}_{v}
equipped with a descent datum
\sigma:p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\simeq p_{2}^{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
on
Y^{\prime\prime}\times_{Y^{\prime}}Y^{\prime\prime}
which satisfies the following conditions:

•

p_{2}^{*}\alpha^{\prime\prime}\circ\sigma=\sigma_{0}\circ p_{1}^{*}\alpha^{\prime\prime}
, where
\sigma_{0}
is the canonical descent datum associated with pulling back
\mathcal{O}_{Y^{\prime}}
to
Y^{\prime\prime}
via
h
.

•

There is a morphism
h^{\sharp}:\nu^{*}h^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{Y}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
such that
(p_{2}^{*}h^{\sharp})\circ\sigma_{Y}=\sigma\circ(p_{1}^{*}h^{\sharp})
, where
\sigma_{Y}
is the canonical descent datum of
\nu^{*}h^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{Y}=h^{*}\nu^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{Y}
induced by pulling back
\nu^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{Y}
via
h
.

This descent datum determines and is determined by a log structure
(\mathcal{M}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})
on
Y^{\prime}_{v}
together with a morphism
\nu^{*}f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{Y}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
.
This log structure
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
is saturated and fine perfectoid. Taking
\nu_{*}
, we get the desired morphism
f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{Y}\to\nu_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
(cf. Lemma
2.21
). Then the Proposition follows from Lemma
2.19
.
∎

We then have the following corollary:

Corollary 2.28
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
be one of the Cases
1
-
3
.

Then the functor “
\mathrm{Hom}(-,(X,\mathcal{M}_{X}))
” on
\operatorname{Perfd}
defined as follows is a
v
-sheaf:

Y\in\operatorname{Perfd}\longmapsto\{f:(Y,\mathcal{M})\to(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})|\mathcal{M}\text{ is saturated and fine perfectoid}\}/\simeq.

Proof.

By
[
75
, Thm. 17.1.3 and Thm. 18.1.1]
,
\mathrm{Hom}(-,X)
is a
v
-sheaf on
\operatorname{Perfd}
. We then get the desired result by applying Proposition
2.27
where
\mathcal{M}_{Y}=f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{X}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.18
.

Now we can show Theorem
2.18
with these facts.
Indeed, since
\mathrm{Untilt}
is a
v
-sheaf by
[
77
, Lem. 15.1]
, we only have to show that
\mathrm{Hom}(-,(X,\mathcal{M}_{X}))
is a
v
-sheaf on
\operatorname{Perfd}
. But this is Corollary
2.28
.

\square

2.1.8.

Suppose that
X
is of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and satisfies (SF).
It follows from the definition that there is a natural “structural morphism”
X^{\log\diamond}\to\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
Moreover,

Lemma 2.29
.

There is a natural injective morphism between
v
-sheaves

X^{\log\diamond}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Q}_{p}\longrightarrow(X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})^{\log\Diamond}.

Proof.

For any
S:=\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\in\operatorname{Perf}
,
X^{\log\diamond}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Q}_{p}(S)
parametrizes isomorphism classes of
\{S^{\sharp}=\operatorname{Spa}(A^{\sharp},A^{\sharp,+}),f:(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}})\to(\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}},\widehat{\nu}^{-1}_{X}\mathcal{M}_{X})\}
where
A^{\sharp}
’s are
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebras. To define a class in
(X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})^{\log\Diamond}(S)
, it remains to define a morphism

f_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}:(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}})\to((X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})^{\mathrm{ad}},\nu^{\mathrm{ad},-1}_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\mathcal{M}_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}})
.
For the morphism
f_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}:S^{\sharp}\to(X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})^{\mathrm{ad}}
, it is defined as
S^{\sharp}\xrightarrow{f}\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\eta}\hookrightarrow(X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})^{\mathrm{ad}}
; for the morphism between sheaves
\nu^{\mathrm{ad},-1}_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\mathcal{M}_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\to\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}
, note that there is a commutative diagram of sites

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\eta})_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}})_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})^{\mathrm{ad}})_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X^{\mathrm{ad}})_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p},\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\nu}_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\nu}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{\mathrm{ad}}_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu^{\mathrm{ad}}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{3}}

For a fixed
f:S^{\sharp}\to\widehat{X}_{\eta}^{\mathrm{ad}}
, a morphism
f^{-1}i_{1}^{-1}\widehat{\nu}_{X}^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}
corresponds to a morphism
\alpha_{0}:f^{-1}j_{1}^{-1}\nu^{\mathrm{ad},-1}_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}i_{3}^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}
.
The latter one induces a morphism
\alpha:f^{-1}j_{1}^{-1}\nu^{\mathrm{ad},-1}_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}i_{3}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}
, where
i_{3}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{X}=\mathcal{M}_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
. Conversely,
\alpha
also uniquely determines
\alpha_{0}
. So the injectivity follows from the injectivity of
X^{\diamond}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Q}_{p}\to X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\Diamond}.

∎

Definition 2.30
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
be an fs log scheme over
\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
Define the slashed log diamond
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond/}
(or denoted by
X^{\log\Diamond/}
) as the quotient
v
-sheaf

(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond/}:=X^{\log\diamond}\coprod_{X^{\log\diamond}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})^{\log\Diamond}.

Note that the definition makes sense by Theorem
2.18
.

Lemma 2.31
.

When
X
is proper over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, the natural injections
X^{\log\diamond}\hookrightarrow X^{\log\Diamond/}\hookrightarrow X^{\log\Diamond}
are isomorphisms.

Proof.

This follows from the construction and the fact that
X^{\mathrm{ad}}\cong\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}
when
X
is proper over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
∎

Let us also remark that there is another way of defining a log diamond, which is a generalization of
[
75
, Def. 8.3.1]
.

Remark 2.32
.

Let
\mathrm{FSPerf}
be the category of saturated and fine perfectoid log perfectoid spaces of characteristic
p
. We then define a log diamond
X
as a pro-étale sheaf on
\mathrm{FSPerf}
such that: (1) There is a surjective map between sheaves
Y\to X
, where
Y
is representable by an object in
\mathrm{FSPerf}
; (2) the fiber product of sheaves
Y\times_{X}Y
is also representable by an object in
\mathrm{FSPerf}
, denoted by
Y\times_{X}^{sat}Y
, such that the two projections
p_{1}
,
p_{2}:Y\times_{X}^{sat}Y\to Y
are (non-log) pro-étale.

We expect that Definition
2.13
should satisfy this definition when
X
is analytic over
\operatorname{Spa}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
(cf. Lemma
2.47
). We do not explore this direction in the current paper.

One can also change the definition of log diamonds by changing the category
\mathrm{FSPerf}
to other categories with a different class of log structures. This definition may also be viewed as an analogue of the definition of a log algebraic space in the second sense due to Kajiwara-Kato-Nakayama (see
[
41
, 10.1]
) in perfectoid geometry.
The definition of log diamonds in
[
49
, Def. 7.3]
may also be viewed as an analogue of log algebraic spaces in the first sense in
[
41
]
for some category of log perfectoid spaces.

2.2.
Log shtukas

We define a notion of
p
-adic shtukas in log geometry.

Let
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
be one of the cases in §
2.1.3
.

2.2.1.

As §
1.3
, let
G
be a connected linear algebraic group over
\mathbb{Q}
that has a quasi-parahoric model
\mathcal{G}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
in the sense of Definition
1.18
. Note that we still use the symbol
G
and
\mathcal{G}
here, but
G
might not be reductive.

Let
\mu:\mathbb{G}_{m,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}\to G_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
be a minuscule cocharacter in the sense of Definition
1.11
(2), and denote by
\{\mu\}
the
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-conjugacy class of it. Let
E
be the field of definition of
\{\mu\}
.

In what follows,
?
denotes
\Diamond
or
\diamond
. Since
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?}
is a functor from
\operatorname{Perf}
to isomorphism classes, we can and will regard
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?}
as a functor
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?}:\operatorname{Perf}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathsf{Categories}
that sends
S\in\operatorname{Perf}
to the collection of tuples
(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},\alpha_{S^{\sharp}},f)
viewed as a category, which is still denoted by
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?}(S)
. More precisely, the objects of
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?}(S)
are those
(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},\alpha_{S^{\sharp}},f)
, while the morphisms between
(S^{\sharp}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}_{1}},\alpha_{S^{\sharp}_{1}},f_{1})
and
(S^{\sharp}_{2},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}_{2}},\alpha_{S^{\sharp}_{2}},f_{2})
are the morphisms between log adic spaces

g:(S^{\sharp}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}_{1}},\alpha_{S^{\sharp}_{1}})\to(S^{\sharp}_{2},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}_{2}},\alpha_{S^{\sharp}_{2}})

such that
f_{2}\circ g=f_{1}
.

By Lemma
2.19
, Theorem
2.18
and Proposition
2.27
, this functor is a
v
-stack, which we abusively denote by

p^{?}:(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?}\to\operatorname{Perf}.

2.2.2.

On the other hand, let

\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}:\operatorname{Perf}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathsf{Groupoids}

be the
v
-stack over
\operatorname{Perf}
sending
S\in\operatorname{Perf}
to the groupoid with objects
(S^{\sharp};(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}}))
, where
S^{\sharp}
is an untilt of
S
and
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
is a
\mathcal{G}
-shtuka over
S
with one leg at
S^{\sharp}
bounded by
\mu
(see
[
66
, Def. 2.4.3]
). For a fixed
S^{\sharp}
, let
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(S^{\sharp})
be the groupoid of
\mathcal{G}
-shtukas over
S
with one leg at
S^{\sharp}
bounded by
\mu
.

Write the fibered category in groupoids corresponding to
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
by
p_{\mathrm{Sht}}:\mathcal{SHT}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\to\operatorname{Perf}
.

Note that there is a commutative diagram of fibered categories

(2.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{SHT}_{\mathcal{G},\mu/(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{SHT}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Perf}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Untilt}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\sharp}((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\mathrm{Sht}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\flat}

In the diagram above,
p^{\sharp}
(resp.
p_{2}
) is a functor sending
(S^{\sharp};(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}}))
(resp.
(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f)
) to
S^{\sharp}
with morphisms also projected to the morphisms of the first factor.
The fibered category
p^{\sharp}((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?}):\mathcal{SHT}_{\mathcal{G},\mu/(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?}}\to(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?}
is defined by a functor
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu/(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?}}
sending
(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f)
to
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(S^{\sharp})
. The functor
p_{1}
is the natural projection.
In conclusion, this is the situation to which Appendix
A.2
applies. When
?=\Diamond/
, by Definition
2.30
,
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond/}
also satisfies the diagram above because it is true for both small and big diamonds.

Definition 2.33
.

Suppose that
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
is in one of the Cases
1
-
3
in §
2.1.3
.
Define the groupoid of
log
\mathcal{G}
-shtukas bounded by
\mu
on
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?}
as (see Definition
A.3
)

\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{?}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X}):=\operatornamewithlimits{2-lim}\limits_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f_{S^{\sharp}})\in((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(S^{\sharp}),

where
?=\Diamond
or
\diamond
.

Similarly, we define the groupoid of
p
-adic
\mathcal{G}
-shtukas
on
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?}
as

\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}}^{?}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X}):=\operatornamewithlimits{2-lim}\limits_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f_{S^{\sharp}})\in((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}}(S^{\sharp}).

The following statement follows immediately from Lemma
A.4
.

Proposition 2.34
.

With the definitions above, the following two groupoids are canonically
isomorphic
:

(1)

The groupoid
\mathrm{Sht}^{?}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
;

(2)

The groupoid of
1
-morphisms between
v
-stacks on
\operatorname{Perf}

\mathscr{P}^{\log?}:(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}

that are sections of
p^{\sharp}((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{?})
.

We call an object in either of the two groupoids a
log shtuka
.

Definition 2.35
.

In general, we can also define
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(Y)
for any
v
-stack
Y
mapping to
\mathrm{Untilt}
satisfying the diagram (
2.9
) as the groupoid of
1
-morphisms
Y\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
.

Denote
\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond/}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X}):=\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond/})
.

For example, we can define
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\diamond}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Q}_{p})
as a
2
-limit over
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\diamond}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
as in Definition
2.33
or the groupoid of
1
-morphisms from
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\diamond}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
to
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
.

The following lemma is immediate from definition:

Lemma 2.36
.

In all cases for
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
in §
2.1.3
, (strict) étale descent for log shtukas is effective.

Proof.

Let
X^{\prime}\to X
be an étale cover. Denote by
S^{\sharp,\prime}\to S^{\sharp}
the pullback of
X^{\prime}\to X
via
f:S^{\sharp}\to X
. Since the descent for the étale cover
S^{\sharp,\prime}\to S^{\sharp}
is effective for
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
(see
[
75
, Prop. 19.5.3]
), we have the desired assertion by taking limits.
∎

2.2.3.

Lemma 2.37
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
be defined as in §
2.1.3
. Let
S\in\operatorname{Perf}
. Suppose that
X^{\log\Diamond}(S)
is nonempty. Let
(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},\alpha_{S^{\sharp}},f)\in X^{\log\Diamond}(S)
. Then there is a saturated and strongly fine perfectoid log structure
(\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}},\alpha^{\mathrm{can}}_{S^{\sharp}})
that satisfies the following universal property:

For any object
(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}_{S^{\sharp}},f)\in X^{\log\Diamond}
(for the same
S^{\sharp}
but varying saturated and fine perfectoid log structures), there is a uniquely determined morphism between log perfectoid spaces
c_{\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\prime}}:(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}_{S^{\sharp}})\to(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}},\alpha^{\mathrm{can}})
extending the identity morphism of the underlying perfectoid spaces.

Moreover, for any morphism
g:(S^{\sharp}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}_{1}},\alpha_{S^{\sharp}_{1}})\to(S^{\sharp}_{2},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}_{2}},\alpha_{S^{\sharp}_{2}})
in the fibered category
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond}
over
\operatorname{Perf}
, there is a unique morphism

g^{\mathrm{can}}:(S^{\sharp}_{1},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}_{1}}^{\mathrm{can}},\alpha_{S^{\sharp}_{1}}^{\mathrm{can}})\to(S^{\sharp}_{2},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}_{2}}^{\mathrm{can}},\alpha_{S^{\sharp}_{2}}^{\mathrm{can}})

such that
g^{\mathrm{can}}\circ c_{\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}_{1}}}=c_{\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}_{2}}}\circ g
.

Similar results hold if we replace “
\log\Diamond
” with “
\log\diamond
”.

Proof.

We only show the assertions in Case
2
for “
\log\Diamond
”; other cases are proved in the same way. Let us fix
(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},\alpha_{S^{\sharp}},f)
.

The morphism
f^{\sharp}:(\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},\alpha_{S^{\sharp}})\to(\mathcal{M}_{X},\alpha_{X})
gives a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{S^{\sharp}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathcal{M}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{S^{\sharp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{S^{\sharp}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}/\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{S^{\sharp}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}/\mathcal{O}_{X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}^{\times}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{S^{\sharp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\alpha}_{S^{\sharp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\alpha}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{f}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{f}^{\sharp}}

Let
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{canperf}}_{S^{\sharp}}:=(f^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X})^{\mathrm{perf}}=(\overline{f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{X}})^{\mathrm{perf}}
.
As
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{S^{\sharp}}
is uniquely
p
-divisible, there is a unique homomorphism
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{can}}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{canperf}}_{S^{\sharp}}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{S^{\sharp}}
.
Denote by
\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{canperf}}
the pullback of
\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}
via
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{can}}
.
That is,
\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}}
fits into the following commutative diagram as an extension of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{canperf}}
by
\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{S^{\sharp}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{S^{\sharp}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{can}}_{S^{\sharp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{canperf}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{S^{\sharp}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{S^{\sharp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{\alpha}}^{\mathrm{can}}}

Let
\alpha^{\mathrm{can}}_{S^{\sharp}}:=\alpha_{S^{\sharp}}\circ c_{S^{\sharp}}
. We claim that
(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{can}}_{S^{\sharp}},\alpha^{\mathrm{can}})
is the desired pair of log structure.

To check the universal property, it suffices to show that the construction is independent of the choice of
f^{\sharp}:(\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},\alpha_{S^{\sharp}})\to(\mathcal{M}_{X},\alpha_{X})
for a fixed
f:S^{\sharp}\to X
.
Given two such morphisms
f_{1}^{\sharp}:(\mathcal{M}^{1}_{S^{\sharp}},\alpha^{1}_{S^{\sharp}})\to(\mathcal{M}_{X},\alpha_{X})
and
f_{2}^{\sharp}:(\mathcal{M}^{2}_{S^{\sharp}},\alpha^{2}_{S^{\sharp}})\to(\mathcal{M}_{X},\alpha_{X})
that are compatible with a fixed
f:S^{\sharp}\to X
, we show the definition of
c_{S^{\sharp}}
is independent of this choice.
Indeed, there is a commutative diagram

(2.10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{1}_{S^{\sharp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{-1}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{S^{\sharp}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}/\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{S^{\sharp}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{2}_{S^{\sharp}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\alpha}^{1}_{S^{\sharp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{f}^{1,\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{f}^{2,\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\alpha}^{2}_{S^{\sharp}}}

The map
\overline{f}^{i,\sharp}
uniquely determines a homomorphism
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{can},i}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{canperf}}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{i}_{S^{\sharp}}
for
i=1,2
and

\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}}
is constructed by pulling back
\mathcal{M}^{i}_{S^{\sharp}}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{i}_{S^{\sharp}}
via
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{can},i}
.
We define
(\mathcal{M}^{3}_{S^{\sharp}},\alpha^{3}_{S^{\sharp}})
as
((\mathcal{M}^{1}_{S^{\sharp}},\alpha^{1}_{S^{\sharp}})\oplus(\mathcal{M}^{2}_{S^{\sharp}},\alpha^{2}_{S^{\sharp}}))/\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{S^{\sharp}}
, which is saturated by
[
62
, I. Prop. 1.3.4]
and
[
62
, I. Prop. 1.3.5]
. Moreover,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{3}_{S^{\sharp}}\cong\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{1}_{S^{\sharp}}\oplus\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{2}_{S^{\sharp}}
is uniquely
p
-divisible. We have the following commutative diagram

(2.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{1}_{S^{\sharp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{can}}_{S^{\sharp}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{S^{\sharp}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{2}_{S^{\sharp}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{1}_{S^{\sharp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{2}_{S^{\sharp}}}

as desired. The functoriality in the third paragraph follows from the construction of
(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{can}}_{S^{\sharp}},\alpha^{\mathrm{can}}_{S^{\sharp}})
,
c_{\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}}
and the unique
p
-divisibility of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{S_{1}^{\sharp}}
and
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{S_{2}^{\sharp}}
.

The resulting
\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}}
is saturated and strongly fine perfectoid. Indeed, since
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
is fs, by
[
18
, Prop. 2.3.13]
and
[
62
, I. 1.3.6]
,
\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}}
admits saturated, uniquely
p
-divisible, and
p
-finitely generated charts. By
[
62
, I. Prop. 1.3.4, Prop. 1.3.5]
,
\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}}
is saturated; it suffices to prove perfectoidness. This follows again from the construction of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{can}}_{S^{\sharp}}
as a pullback of
\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}
via
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{can}}
and the perfectoidness of
\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}
.
∎

Proposition 2.38
.

Denote by
\mathfrak{f}:X^{\log\Diamond}\to X^{\Diamond}
the natural projection. With the conventions as in Lemma
2.37
, the object
(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}},\alpha^{\mathrm{can}},f)
is the final object of the fiber of
\mathfrak{f}
at
(S^{\sharp},f)
(if the fiber is non-empty). Then a log shtuka

\mathscr{P}^{\log\Diamond}:X^{\log\Diamond}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}

is uniquely determined by its restriction to the objects
(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}},\alpha^{\mathrm{can}})
up to isomorphisms. The same is true for “
\diamond
”.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
2.37
and Lemma
A.6
.
∎

Corollary 2.39
.

If
X
is equipped with the trivial log structure
\mathcal{M}_{X}=\mathcal{O}^{*}_{X_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
, then a log shtuka

\mathscr{P}^{\log\Diamond}:X^{\log\Diamond}\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}

determines and is determined by its restriction to the objects
(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{O}^{*}_{S^{\sharp}})
, i.e., a nonlog shtuka: The projection
X^{\log\Diamond}\to X^{\Diamond}
admits a canonical section, and therefore determines a nonlog shtuka. The same is true for “
\diamond
” and “
\Diamond/
”.

Proof.

This follows from the same argument as that of Proposition
2.38
. Note that, in this case, the canonical log structures are all trivial. The case of “
\Diamond/
” follows from Definition
2.30
as the corollary is true for both small and big diamonds.
∎

Lemma 2.40
.

Continuing with the conventions in Proposition
2.38
with
X
satisfying (SF), we consider the small diamond
\mathfrak{f}:X^{\log\diamond}\to X^{\diamond}
.
The map
f:(S^{\sharp}:=\operatorname{Spa}(R^{\sharp},R^{\sharp,+}),\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}},\alpha^{\mathrm{can}})\to(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
by Definition
2.14
factors through a map
f^{+}:(\operatorname{Spec}R^{\sharp,+},\mathcal{M}_{R^{\sharp,+}},\alpha_{R^{\sharp,+}})\to(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
such that the pullback log structure
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{R^{\sharp,+}}
of
\mathcal{M}_{R^{\sharp,+}}
to
\operatorname{Spa}(R^{\sharp,+},R^{\sharp,+})
via
\operatorname{Spa}(R^{\sharp,+},R^{\sharp,+})\to\operatorname{Spec}R^{\sharp,+}
induces the log structure
\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}}
on
S^{\sharp}
via the pullback through
S^{\sharp}\to\operatorname{Spa}(R^{\sharp,+},R^{\sharp,+})
.
Moreover,
f
étale locally admits a chart of the form
\mathsf{P}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{P}[\frac{1}{p}]
.

Proof.

The proof of the first assertion follows from the same argument as Lemma
2.37
after the following changes: Here
X
is a scheme; we replace
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{canperf}}
with the log structure induced by
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{R^{\sharp,+}}:=(f^{+,-1}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X})^{\textrm{perf}}
by pulling back via
S^{\sharp}\to\operatorname{Spec}R^{\sharp,+}
; the map
\overline{\alpha}^{\mathrm{can}}
still exists by Lemma
2.12
, since the chart, by definition, factors through
R^{\sharp,+}
. The rest of the proof remains unchanged.

For the second assertion, assume that
X
is affine and admits a global fs sharp chart
\mathsf{P}
. By construction, there is a map
\theta:\mathsf{P}\to\mathcal{M}_{R^{\sharp,+}}\to R^{\sharp,+}
. Since
\mathcal{M}_{R^{\sharp,+}}
is integral and perfectoid, by
[
49
, Rmk. 2.25 and Rmk. 2.26]
, the induced log structure
\mathcal{M}_{R^{\sharp,+}/p}
on the mod-
p
log ring is uniquely
p
-divisible, and
\theta/p:\mathsf{P}\to\mathcal{M}_{R^{\sharp,+}/p}\to R^{\sharp,+}/p
can be extended to
\widetilde{\theta/p}:\mathsf{P}[\frac{1}{p}]\to\mathcal{M}_{R^{\sharp,+}/p}\to R^{\sharp,+}/p
. This, in turn, induces a map

\xi\circ[\widetilde{\theta/p}^{\flat}]:\mathsf{P}[\frac{1}{p}]\to W(R^{+})\to R^{\sharp,+},

which induces the log structure
\mathcal{M}_{R^{\sharp,+}}
. The rest of the assertion follows from the construction in the last paragraph.
∎

2.3.
Equivalence of categories on generic fiber

We discuss a generalization of
[
66
, §2.5]
in log geometry.

2.3.1.

To formulate the equivalence of categories, we need a notion of
(pro-)
p
-Kummer étale local systems
(see
[
39
]
)
2
2

2

We are grateful to Inoue and Koshikawa for suggesting the name “(pro-)
p
-Kummer”; a similar one “
p
-primary Kummer” was also suggested to one of us (PW) during his visit to ZJU. In an update
[
39
]
to
[
49
]
, Inoue, Koshikawa, and Yao have discussed various equivalent definitions for this notion in more detail. Therefore, the definition used here can be viewed as an adaptation of their work to the context of
[
18
]
.
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
be a locally Noetherian fs log adic space. Denote by
\mathrm{Loc}(X_{\text{k{\'{e}}t}})
the category of Kummer étale local systems on
X
with values in finite sets. If we fix a finite set or finite abelian group
\Lambda
, we denote by
\Lambda
-
\mathrm{Loc}(X_{\text{k{\'{e}}t}})
the category of Kummer étale local systems on
X
with values in
\Lambda
. Let
\zeta
be a log geometric point on
X
defined as in
[
18
, 4.4.2-4.4.3]
. Let
\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(X,\zeta)
be the Kummer étale fundamental group at
\zeta
. The following result is a logarithmic analogue of the classical theory that étale local systems correspond to finite étale covers.

Proposition 2.41

(
[
18
, Thm. 4.4.15-Cor. 4.4.18]
)
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
be a connected locally Noetherian fs log adic space. There is a natural equivalence of categories:

X_{\text{fk{\'{e}}t}}\cong\mathrm{Loc}(X_{\text{k{\'{e}}t}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(X,\zeta)\text{-}\mathrm{Fsets}.

Let
\Lambda
be a finite discrete abelian group. Then the equivalence above restricts to an equivalence

\Lambda\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}(X_{\text{k{\'{e}}t}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Rep}_{\Lambda}(\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(X,\zeta)).

The category
\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(X,\zeta)\text{-}\mathrm{Fsets}
(resp.
\mathrm{Rep}_{\Lambda}(\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(X,\zeta))
) consists of finite discrete sets with continuous
\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(X,\zeta)
-actions (resp. continuous
\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(X,\zeta)
-representations of
\Lambda
).

Proof.

Note that the first statement is exactly
loc. cit.
. For any local system
L\in\Lambda
-
\mathrm{Loc}(X_{\text{k{\'{e}}t}})
, the corresponding finite Kummer étale cover
Y
is Kummer étale locally represented by
X^{\Lambda}
, and the corresponding
\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(X,\zeta)
-finite set is given by
\mathrm{Hom}_{X}(\zeta,Y)
. So the fundamental group
\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(X,\zeta)
acts through
\mathrm{Aut}(\Lambda)
.
∎

The log geometric point
\zeta
is constructed from a geometric point
\xi=\operatorname{Spec}l
of
X
. By
[
18
, Cor. 4.4.22]
, we have that
\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(\xi,\zeta)\cong\mathrm{Hom}(\overline{M}^{\mathrm{gp}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\prime}(1)(l))
, where
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\prime}(1)(l)=\varprojlim_{n}\mu_{n}(l)
, in which
\mu_{n}(l)
is the group of
n
-th roots of unity in
l
; the limit runs over all
n
invertible in
l
.

Now, assume that
X
is defined over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Then, in this case, we have
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\prime}(1)(l)=\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)
.

Definition 2.42
.

Let
L
be a Kummer étale local system on
X
with values in
\Lambda
. For any log geometric point
\zeta
constructed from a geometric point
\xi
,
L
corresponds to a representation
\rho_{L}\in\mathrm{Rep}_{\Lambda}(\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(X,\zeta))
, and the action of
\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(\xi,\zeta)
on
L
is given by composing this representation with the natural map
p_{\xi}^{X}:\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(\xi,\zeta)\to\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(X,\zeta)
. Set
\overline{M}:=\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{X,\xi}
.
In this setting, we say that
L
is
p
-Kummer at
\xi
if
\rho\circ p^{X}_{\xi}
is trivial when restricted to
\mathrm{Hom}(\overline{M}^{\mathrm{gp}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}(1))
. We say that
L
is
p
-Kummer
if it is
p
-Kummer at every geometric point.

Next, we consider a pro-Kummer-étale
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-local system
\widehat{L}
on
X
. By
[
18
, Lem. 6.3.3]
,
\widehat{L}=\varprojlim_{n}L_{n}
, where
L_{n}
is a
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}
-local system on
X_{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}
such that
L_{n+1}/p^{n}L_{n+1}\cong L_{n}
. Then, at a log geometric point
\zeta
over a geometric point
\xi
, the Kummer étale fundamental group
\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(X,\zeta)
has a compatible action on
\{L_{n,\zeta}\}_{n}
. We obtain a continuous
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representation
\rho_{\widehat{L}}:=\varprojlim_{n}\rho_{L_{n}}
.

Definition 2.43
.

Let
\widehat{L}
be a pro-Kummer étale
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-local system on
X
.
In the setting of Definition
2.42
,
we say that
\widehat{L}
is
pro-
p
-Kummer at
\xi
if
\rho_{\widehat{L}}\circ p^{X}_{\xi}
is trivial when restricted to
\mathrm{Hom}(\overline{M}^{\mathrm{gp}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}(1))
. We say that
\widehat{L}
is
pro-
p
-Kummer
if it is pro-
p
-Kummer at every geometric point. Denote by
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}_{p}(X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})
the category of pro-
p
-Kummer étale
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-local systems on
X
.

Remark 2.44
.

Suppose that
\widehat{L}
is torsion free.
Note that the image of
\mathrm{Hom}(\overline{M}^{\mathrm{gp}},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}(1))
in
\mathrm{Aut}(\widehat{L}_{\zeta})
is always finite because a continuous map from the first term (which is a formed by copies of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}
) to
\mathrm{Aut}(\widehat{L}_{\zeta})\cong\mathrm{GL}_{N}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
always has a finite image. If
\widehat{L}
has unipotent boundary monodromy (i.e., the image of
\rho_{\widehat{L}}\circ p_{\xi}^{X}
is contained in some
U(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\subset GL_{N}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
for a unipotent subgroup
U\subset\mathrm{GL}_{N,\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
), it is always pro-
p
-Kummer.

Finally, let
\mathcal{G}
be the quasi-parahoric
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-model of a connected linear algebraic group
G
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
in the sense of Definition
1.18
.

Definition 2.45
.

We say a pro-Kummer étale
\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-torsor
\mathcal{E}
is
pro-
p
-Kummer
if, for any continuous torsion-free
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-representation
V
, the corresponding pro-Kummer étale local system
\underline{V}:=\mathcal{E}\times^{\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}}V
is pro-
p
-Kummer. Denote by
\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-
\mathrm{Loc}_{p}(X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})
the category of pro-
p
-Kummer étale
\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-torsors on
X
.

2.3.2.

Assume that
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
is either an fs log scheme locally of finite type over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, or a locally Noetherian fs log adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.
Let
G
,
\mu
,
E
, and
\mathcal{G}
be as in §
2.2
.

Proposition 2.46
.

In the case of locally Noetherian fs log adic spaces over
\operatorname{Spa}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we have a natural bi-exact tensor equivalence of categories

(2.12)

\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}_{p}(X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})\cong\operatornamewithlimits{2-lim}\limits_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}},f_{S^{\sharp}})\in((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond})^{\mathrm{op}}}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}(S^{\sharp}_{\text{pro{\'{e}}t}}).

By the Tannakian formalism, we have a natural equivalence of categories

(2.13)

\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}_{p}(X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})\cong\operatornamewithlimits{2-lim}\limits_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}},f_{S^{\sharp}})\in((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond})^{\mathrm{op}}}\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}(S^{\sharp}_{\text{pro{\'{e}}t}}).

In the case of locally of finite type schemes, replacing the categories on the left by
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}_{p}(X^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})
and
\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}_{p}(X^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})
, we have the same results.

Lemma 2.47
.

Let
(U,\mathcal{M}_{U})
be an affinoid Tate fs log adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
that admits a global sharp fs chart
\mathsf{P}
.

(1)

There is an inverse limit
\varprojlim_{i}(U_{i},\mathcal{M}_{i})
of affinoid finite Kummer étale covers
(U_{i},\mathcal{M}_{i})
of
(U,\mathcal{M}_{U})
that is associated with an affinoid perfectoid log adic space
(\widehat{U}_{\infty},\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty})
, with
\overline{\widehat{\mathcal{M}}}_{\infty}
being uniquely divisible. Moreover,
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}
is perfectoid.

(2)

Let
\mathsf{P}[1/p]:=\varinjlim_{n}\frac{1}{p^{n}}\mathsf{P}
.
One can define another perfectoid cover
(\widehat{U}_{p^{-\infty}},\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{p^{-\infty}})
in
U_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}}
.
In fact, there is an inverse limit
U_{p^{-\infty}}:=\varprojlim_{J}\operatorname{Spa}(R_{j},R_{j}^{+})
of affinoid finite étale covers of
U\times_{U\langle\mathsf{P}\rangle}U\langle\mathsf{P}[1/p]\rangle
, which is associated with a log perfectoid space
\widehat{U}_{p^{-\infty}}
equipped with the log structure
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{p^{-\infty}}
induced by
\mathsf{P}[1/p]
.

Proof.

We prove Part
1
.
This is essentially
[
18
, Prop. 5.3.12]
and
[
77
, Lem. 15.3]
. The argument in
[
18
, Prop. 5.3.12]
works for both two parts; let us explain more.

In fact, by
[
76
, Prop. 4.8]
and
[
77
, Lem. 15.3]
, for any
U=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
as in the Lemma, there is an affinoid perfectoid object
\varprojlim_{I}U_{i}=\operatorname{Spa}(R_{i},R_{i}^{+})
in
U_{\text{pro{\'{e}}t}}
where
R_{i}
are finite étale algebras of
R
, and
\varprojlim_{I}U_{i}\sim\widehat{U}
, that is, is associated with a perfectoid space
\widehat{U}
.
Then, by the argument in
[
18
, Prop. 5.3.12]
, the inverse limits

\varprojlim_{(i,n)\in I\times\mathbb{N}}U_{i}\langle\frac{1}{n}\mathsf{P}\rangle

and
\varprojlim_{(i,n)\in I\times\mathbb{N}}U_{i}\langle\frac{1}{p^{n}}\mathsf{P}\rangle

are associated with affinoid log perfectoid spaces
\widetilde{U}_{\infty}
and
\widetilde{U}_{p^{-\infty}}
.
Since
U\to U\langle\mathsf{P}\rangle
is a strict closed immersion, the pullback of
\widetilde{U}_{\infty}\to U\langle\mathsf{P}\rangle
(resp.
\widetilde{U}_{p^{-\infty}}\to U\langle\mathsf{P}\rangle
) via
U\to U\langle\mathsf{P}\rangle
is an affinoid perfectoid log adic space
\widehat{U}_{\infty}
(resp.
\widehat{U}_{p^{-\infty}}
) in
U_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}}
. The log structures are as desired by construction.
∎

Lemma 2.48

(cf.
[
39
, Prop. 5]
)
.

Let
(U,\mathcal{M}_{U})
be a Noetherian affinoid fs log adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
that admits a global sharp fs chart
\mathsf{P}
.

Let
\widehat{L}\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}(U_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})
. Then
\widehat{L}
is in
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}_{p}(U_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})
if and only if
\widehat{L}
splits over a strict pro-finite étale cover
\widehat{V}_{p^{-\infty}}
of
\widehat{U}_{p^{-\infty}}
in the notation of Lemma
2.47
(2), such that
\widehat{V}_{p^{-\infty}}\to\widehat{U}_{p^{-\infty}}
is associated with a pro-finite étale
V_{p^{-\infty}}\to U_{p^{-\infty}}
in
U_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}}
. In fact, in this case,
\widehat{L}
splits over
\widehat{V}_{p^{-\infty}}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, assume that
U
is connected.
Since
\mathsf{P}
is fs and sharp, for any geometric point
\xi\to U
of
U
, there is a surjective map
\mathsf{P}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{U,\xi}
between finitely generated, torsion-free and sharp monoids. Then this induces a surjective map
\pi:\mathsf{P}[1/p]^{\mathrm{gp}}/\mathsf{P}^{\mathrm{gp}}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{gp}}_{U,\xi,p^{-\infty}}/\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{U,\xi}^{\mathrm{gp}}
. Note that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{gp}}_{U,\xi,p^{-\infty}}/\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{U,\xi}^{\mathrm{gp}}\cong\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{U,\xi}^{\mathrm{gp}}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}\cong\mathrm{Hom}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{U,\xi}^{\mathrm{gp}},\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1))
is a quotient of
\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(\xi,\zeta)\cong\mathrm{Hom}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{gp}}_{U,\xi},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}(1))
.
Furthermore, we have the following commutative diagram

(2.14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{P}^{\mathrm{gp}}_{\infty}/\mathsf{P}^{\mathrm{gp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{gp}}_{U,\xi,\infty}/\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{gp}}_{U,\xi}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{P}[1/p]^{\mathrm{gp}}/\mathsf{P}^{\mathrm{gp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{gp}}_{U,\xi,p^{-\infty}}/\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{U,\xi}^{\mathrm{gp}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

Take a log geometric point
\zeta
over
\xi
. By
[
18
, Cor. 4.4.18]
, the fiber
\widehat{L}_{\zeta}
of
\widehat{L}
at
\zeta
is a continuous representation of
\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(U,\zeta)
. There is an exact sequence

1\to\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(\xi,\zeta)\to\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(U,\zeta)\to\pi_{1}^{\text{{\'{e}}t}}(U,\xi)\to 1,

where
\pi_{1}^{\text{{\'{e}}t}}(U,\xi)
corresponds to the inverse limit of all finite étale covers of
U
via the equivalence of categories (4.4.19) in
[
18
, Cor. 4.4.18]
. Furthermore, the quotient of
\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(U,\zeta)
by the subgroup
\mathrm{Hom}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{gp}}_{U,\xi},\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}(1))\subset\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(\xi,\zeta)
corresponds to the cover
\xi_{p^{-\infty}}:=U(\xi)\times_{U(\xi)\langle\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{U,\xi}\rangle}U(\xi)\langle\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{U,\xi,p^{-\infty}}\rangle
.

Then
\widehat{L}
splits over
\widehat{V}_{p^{-\infty}}
if and only if
\widehat{L}|_{\xi}
splits over
\xi\times_{U}V_{p^{-\infty}}
for any
\xi
.
Since
\xi_{p^{-\infty}}\to\xi
factors through
\xi\times_{U}V_{p^{-\infty}}
, we have the “
\impliedby
” direction.

For the other direction,
the action of
\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(\xi,\zeta)
factors through the image of
\pi
. By (
2.14
), the action of
\mathsf{P}^{\mathrm{gp}}_{\infty}/\mathsf{P}^{\mathrm{gp}}_{p^{-\infty}}
on
\widehat{L}|_{U(\xi)\times_{U(\xi)\langle\mathsf{P}\rangle}U(\xi)\langle\mathsf{P}_{\infty}\rangle}
is trivial by the pro-
p
-Kummer étale condition at
(\xi,\zeta)
. Thus,
\widehat{L}|_{U(\xi)}
becomes trivial over
U(\xi)\times_{U(\xi)\langle\mathsf{P}\rangle}U(\xi)\langle\mathsf{P}[1/p]\rangle
by
[
18
, Prop. 4.4.9]
. By writing
\widehat{L}
as an inverse limit of
\mathbb{L}_{n}
, we find a pro-finite étale cover
V
of
U
such that
\widehat{L}
is trivialized over
V\times_{V\langle\mathsf{P}\rangle}V\langle\mathsf{P}[1/p]\rangle
. Hence, the pullback of
\widehat{L}
to the perfectoid cover
\widehat{U}_{p^{-\infty}}
is trivialized over a pro-finite étale cover of
\widehat{U}_{p^{-\infty}}
.

The last sentence can be seen from the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.46
.
We only need to show the first equivalence (
2.12
) as the bi-exactness is clear in the construction.
Without loss of generality, we assume that we are in the case of log adic spaces since
(X^{\mathrm{ad}},\nu^{\mathrm{ad},*}\mathcal{M}_{X})
is locally Noetherian and fs by construction in the case of log schemes. We also assume the connectedness of
X
.

Since both sides satisfy étale descent, we assume that
X
is affinoid and that
\mathcal{M}_{X}
admits a global chart
\mathsf{P}
.

The RHS of (
2.12
) is equivalent to
\operatornamewithlimits{2-lim}\limits_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f_{S^{\sharp}})\in((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond})^{\mathrm{op}}}\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}(S^{\sharp}_{\text{pro{\'{e}}t}})
. Let
\{L_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f)}\}
be an object in this limit. This limit gives an object
L_{p^{-\infty}}:=L_{(\widehat{X}_{p^{-\infty}},\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{p^{-\infty}})}
by evaluating on the log perfectoid space with a perfectoid log structure
(\widehat{X}_{p^{-\infty}},\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{p^{-\infty}})
formed by Lemma
2.47
(2). After replacing
\widehat{X}_{p^{-\infty}}
with a pro-finite étale cover of it, we can assume that
L_{p^{-\infty}}
is trivialized.
The perfectoid space
\widehat{X}_{p^{-\infty}}
is, by construction, associated with a perfectoid object
X_{p^{-\infty}}
in
X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}}
.
Note that
X_{p^{-\infty}}\to X
is a pro-Kummer étale cover by construction.
Then the Kummer étale fundamental group of
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
acts on
X_{p^{-\infty}}
, on its associated perfectoid space, and on the trivial local system
L_{p^{-\infty}}
(which is encoded in the
2
-limit).
This induces a pro-Kummer étale
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-local system
\widehat{L}
in
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}(X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})
. Furthermore,
\widehat{L}
is in
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}_{p}(X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})
by Lemma
2.48
.

Let us show the other direction. By Lemma
2.48
, an object
\widehat{L}\in\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}_{p}(X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})
is trivialized over a perfectoid object
X_{p^{-\infty}}
in
X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}}
. Moreover,
\widehat{L}
is equipped with an action of
\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(X,\zeta)
that factors through
\mathrm{Aut}(X_{p^{-\infty}}/X)
. By applying
[
18
, Lem. 5.3.8]
and taking an inverse limit, the saturated product
X_{p^{-\infty}}\times_{X}^{sat}(X\times_{X\langle\mathsf{P}\rangle}X\langle\mathsf{P}[1/p]\rangle)
is pro-finite étale over
X\times_{X\langle\mathsf{P}\rangle}X\langle\mathsf{P}[1/p]\rangle
. Since, for every object
(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{can}}_{S^{\sharp}},f)\in(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond}
, the morphism
f:S^{\sharp}\to X
factors through
X\times_{X\langle\mathsf{P}\rangle}X\langle\mathsf{P}[1/p]\rangle
, the saturated product
S^{\sharp}_{p^{-\infty}}:=X_{p^{-\infty}}\times_{X}S^{\sharp}
is pro-finite étale and surjective over
S^{\sharp}
. The
\pi_{1}^{\text{k{\'{e}}t}}(X,\zeta)
-action on
\widehat{L}
is pulled back to the action of
\mathrm{Aut}(S^{\sharp}_{p^{-\infty}}/S^{\sharp})
on
(f^{-1}\widehat{L})(S^{\sharp}_{p^{-\infty}})
. Hence,
f^{-1}\widehat{L}
is a pro-étale local system on
S^{\sharp}
. For any morphism
g:(S_{1}^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}_{1}},f_{1})\to(S_{2}^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}_{2}},f_{2})
, it is easy to check that the pullbacks satisfy the desired functoriality required by a
2
-limit.

\square

2.3.3.

In this subsection, we show the following theorem:

Theorem 2.49

(cf.
[
66
, Prop. 2.5.3]
)
.

Let
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
be a locally Noetherian fs log adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
(resp. an fs log scheme of finite type over
\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
).
With this assumption, there is a natural equivalence between the following two categories:

(1)

The category of log shtukas
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{\Diamond}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
;

(2)

The category
HT_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
of pairs
(\mathbb{P},\pi_{HT})
, where
\mathbb{P}
is a pro-
p
-Kummer-étale
\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-torsor defined over
X
(resp.
X^{\mathrm{ad}}
) and a Hodge-Tate period map
\pi_{HT}:\mathbb{P}\to\mathcal{F}_{G,\mu^{-1}}^{\Diamond}
.

The meaning of the last morphism is the following: The torsor
\mathbb{P}
can be viewed as a limit over
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond}
of pro-étale
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-torsors
\mathcal{E}_{S^{\sharp}}
by Proposition
2.46
. The last morphism is given by a functorial assignment of
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-equivariant maps between
v
-sheaves
HT(\mathcal{E}_{S^{\sharp}}):\mathcal{E}_{S^{\sharp}}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\operatorname{Spd}E,\mu^{-1}}
for objects in
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond}
followed by the Bialynicki-Birula isomorphism (see Lemma
1.12
)
\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\operatorname{Spd}E,\mu^{-1}}\to\mathcal{F}^{\Diamond}_{G,\mu^{-1}}
.

Remark 2.50
.

See
[
39
, Thm. 7]
(and also
[
49
, Thm. 7.36]
) for an analogous theorem in the log prismatic theory. In fact, our proof of Theorem
2.49
is also similar to the one presented there.

Remark 2.51
.

The following statement is included in the case of log adic spaces in Theorem
2.49
:
Let
X
be a separated
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme that is flat and of finite type. Let
\widehat{X}
be the
p
-adic completion and
Y:=\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\eta}
be the adic generic fiber. If
Y
is equipped with an fs log structure
\mathcal{M}
, then applying the theorem above gives an equivalence over
(Y,\mathcal{M})^{\Diamond}
.

Proof of Theorem
2.49
.

By definition, we have
\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})=

\operatornamewithlimits{2-lim}\limits_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f_{S^{\sharp}})\in((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(S^{\sharp}).

By
[
66
, Prop. 2.5.2]
, this expression is equivalent to

\operatornamewithlimits{2-lim}\limits_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f_{S^{\sharp}})\in((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond})^{\mathrm{op}}}HT_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(S^{\sharp}).

The last
2
-limit is equivalent to
\operatornamewithlimits{2-lim}\limits_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}},f_{S^{\sharp}})\in((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond})^{\mathrm{op}}}HT_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(S^{\sharp})
by Lemma
2.37
and Lemma
A.6
. Finally, combining Proposition
2.46
(and the explanation in Theorem
2.49
), we have the desired equivalence.

\square

Corollary 2.52
.

For an fs log scheme
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
in §
2.1.3
Case
2
, we have an equivalence between
\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
and
HT_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{\Diamond}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
, the category of pairs
(\mathbb{P},\pi_{HT})
where
\mathbb{P}
is an object in

\operatornamewithlimits{2-lim}\limits_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f_{S^{\sharp}})\in((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond})^{\mathrm{op}}}\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}(S^{\sharp}_{\text{pro{\'{e}}t}}),

and
\pi_{HT}
is as above.

Proof.

Write as
2
-limits and apply
[
66
, Prop. 2.5.2]
.
∎

Corollary 2.53

(Canonical extension of shtukas in characteristic zero)
.

(1)

Let
E
be a
p
-adic field. Assume that
X
is a smooth rigid analytic variety over
\operatorname{Spa}E
with a normal crossings divisor
D
such that
U:=X\backslash D
is open and dense in
X
. Denote by
\mathcal{M}_{X}
the log structure induced by
D\hookrightarrow X\xhookleftarrow{j}U
. Then the restriction functor

\operatorname{Res}^{X}_{U}:\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})\to\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(U)

is fully faithful.

(2)

With the same assumptions as in Part
1
, for any object
\mathscr{P}_{U}\in\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{\Diamond}(U)
,
\mathscr{P}_{U}
corresponds to a
\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-torsor
\mathbb{P}_{U}
and a Hodge-Tate map
\pi_{HT}:\mathbb{P}_{U}\to\mathcal{F}_{G,\mu^{-1}}^{\Diamond}
.
Then
\mathscr{P}_{U}
is in the essential image of
\operatorname{Res}^{X}_{U}

only if

\mathbb{P}:=j_{*}\mathbb{P}_{U}
is pro-
p
-Kummer étale.

(3)

(Diao-Lan-Liu-Zhu) With the same assumptions as in Parts
1
and
2
, we further assume that
\mathbb{P}_{U}
is
de Rham
in the sense that, for any
V\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
,
\underline{V}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}:=(\mathbb{P}_{U}\times^{\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}}V)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is de Rham.
If
\underline{V}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
has
unipotent
geometric monodromy along the boundary in the sense of
[
18
, Def. 6.3.7]
, then there is a canonical way of associating a Hodge-Tate map
\pi_{HT,X}:\mathbb{P}\to\mathcal{F}_{G,\mu^{-1}}^{\Diamond}
to
\mathbb{P}
, and therefore defining a log shtuka
\mathscr{P}\in\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{\Diamond}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
via Theorem
2.49
. This association is compatible with the one in
[
66
, Prop. 2.6.3]
after the restriction back to
U^{\Diamond}
.

Proof.

Consider the diagram

(2.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces HT_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces HT_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(U).}

This diagram is commutative. The vertical maps are equivalences of categories by Theorem
2.49
and
[
66
, Prop. 2.5.3]
. Hence, for Part
1
, it suffices to show that the bottom arrow is fully faithful.

By rigid Abhyankar’s Lemma
[
18
, Prop. 4.2.1]
and by
[
18
, Cor. 6.3.4]
, there is an equivalence

(2.16)

\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}(X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})\cong\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}(U_{\text{pro{\'{e}}t}})

induced by
\widehat{L}\mapsto\widehat{L}|_{U_{\text{pro{\'{e}}t}}}
; the quasi-inverse is defined by the pushforward of
\widehat{L}|_{U_{\text{pro{\'{e}}t}}}
from
U
to
X
. By the Tannakian formalism, there is an equivalence

\operatorname{Res}^{\mathcal{G}-\mathrm{Loc}}:\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}(X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})\cong\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}(U_{\text{pro{\'{e}}t}})

induced by restricting the pro-Kummer étale
\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-torsor to
U
.
Since the functor
\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-
\mathrm{Loc}_{p}(X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})\to\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-
\mathrm{Loc}(X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})
is fully faithful, there is a fully faithful functor induced by restriction to
U
:

i:\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}_{p}(X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})\to\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}(U_{\text{pro{\'{e}}t}}).

The rest of Part
1
follows from Lemma
2.54
below.

The quasi-inverse of (
2.16
) is given by
\widehat{L}\mapsto j_{*}\widehat{L}
. By the construction above,
j_{*}\mathbb{P}_{U}
in the statement of Part
2
is pro-
p
-Kummer if and only if
\mathbb{P}_{U}
is in the essential image of
\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\text{-}\mathrm{Loc}_{p}(X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}})
under the composition
i\circ\operatorname{Res}^{\mathcal{G}-\mathrm{Loc}}
.
So Part
2
follows.

Part
3
follows from the main theorems of
[
19
]
(see also
[
74
, Thm. 4.2.1]
).
In fact, by the proof of
[
19
, Thm. 3.2.12]
written above
[
19
, §3.5]
, there is a canonical isomorphism

\mu^{*}D_{\mathrm{dR},\log}(\underline{V}){\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}}}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},\log}\xrightarrow{\sim}\underline{V}\otimes_{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},\log},

where
\mu:X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}}\to X_{an}
,
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},\log}
is a peroid sheaf with filtration and log connection extending
\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}
, and
D_{\mathrm{dR},\log}
is the arithmetic log Riemann-Hilbert functor (see
[
19
, 2.2 and 3.2.6]
). This isomorphism is compatible with filtrations and log connections on both sides.

We then define
\mathbb{M}_{0,\log}:=(D_{\mathrm{dR},\log}(\underline{V})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},\log}^{+})^{\nabla^{\log}=0}
, and
\mathbb{M}_{\log}:=(\mathrm{Fil}^{0}(D_{\mathrm{dR},\log}(\underline{V})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR},\log}))^{\nabla^{\log}=0}
; they are
B_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}
-modules due to log Poincaré’s lemma
[
19
, Cor. 2.4.2]
. Moreover, the filtrations on them are finite projective modules by
[
19
, Cor. 3.4.22]
. Then, using the same argument as in
[
66
, §2.6.1]
, we obtain a Hodge-Tate map
\pi_{HT,X}:\mathbb{P}\to\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mu^{-1}}
. It is clear that this construction is compatible with and extends the one in
loc. cit.
.
∎

Lemma 2.54
.

With the conventions and assumptions as in Corollary
2.53
, every Hodge-Tate map
\pi_{HT}:\mathbb{P}_{U}\to\mathcal{F}^{\Diamond}_{G,\mu^{-1}}
on
U^{\Diamond}
for a fixed
\underline{\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-torsor
\mathbb{P}_{U}
admits
at most one
extension to
\pi_{HT,X}:\mathbb{P}:=j_{*}\mathbb{P}_{U}\to\mathcal{F}^{\Diamond}_{G,\mu^{-1}}
on
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond}
.

Proof.

Denote by
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
two extensions of
\pi_{HT}
. As in the proof of
[
18
, Prop. 4.2.1]
, let us assume that both
X=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
and
U=\operatorname{Spa}(R_{0},R_{0}^{+})
are affinoid.
By rigid Abhyankar
[
18
, Prop. 4.2.1]
and by Lemma
2.47
(1), we can choose an affinoid perfectoid object
\widetilde{U}:=\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})
such that
\widetilde{U}\to U
extends to an affinoid perfectoid object
\widetilde{X}:=\operatorname{Spa}(B,B^{+})\to X
in
X_{\text{prok{\'{e}}t}}
. We then take the associated perfectoid spaces
\widehat{X}\sim\widetilde{X}
and
\widehat{U}\sim\widetilde{U}
, and take a strictly totally disconnected cover
\widehat{X}^{str}\to\widehat{X}\to X
where
\widehat{X}^{str}\to\widehat{X}
is universally open by
[
77
, Lem. 7.18]
. Let
\widehat{U}^{str}:=\widehat{X}^{str}\times_{\widehat{X}}\widehat{U}
; this is also a strictly totally disconnected space, since it is an open subspace of
\widehat{X}^{str}
. Note that the log structure on
\widehat{X}^{str}
is induced by the pullback of the log structure on
\widehat{X}
. Thus,
\widehat{X}^{str}
with its log structure is an object in
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond}
.

By assumption,
|U|
is open dense in
|X|
. In fact,
|\widehat{U}|
is dense in
|\widehat{X}|
.
Indeed, since
|\widehat{X}|\cong\varprojlim_{j\in J}|X_{j}|
by
[
77
, Prop. 6.4]
, it suffices to show that each
U\times_{X}X_{j}
is open dense in
X_{j}
. By
[
30
, Prop. 2.8]
, and since the constructions in
[
30
]
and
[
18
, Prop. 4.2.1]
are compatible, the pullback of
D
through
X_{j}\to X
is nowhere-dense. This implies that
U\times_{X}X_{j}
is dense in
X_{j}
. Furthermore, we deduce from this fact that
\widehat{U}^{str}
is also dense in
\widehat{X}^{str}
since
\widehat{X}^{str}\to\widehat{X}
is universally open.

If there are two maps
\pi_{1}(\widehat{X}^{str})
and
\pi_{2}(\widehat{X}^{str}):\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{X}^{str}}\to\mathcal{F}_{G,\mu^{-1},\widehat{X}^{str}}
extending
\pi_{HT,\widehat{U}^{str}}
, since
|\widehat{U}^{str}|
is dense in
|\widehat{X}^{str}|
and
\mathcal{F}_{G,\mu^{-1}}
is separated over
E
, we have
\pi_{1}=\pi_{2}
.

Pick an object
(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f)\in(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond}
.
Let
\pi_{1}(S^{\sharp})
and
\pi_{2}(S^{\sharp}):\mathbb{P}_{S^{\sharp}}\to\mathcal{F}_{G,\mu^{-1},S^{\sharp}}
be two Hodge-Tate maps. We now pull them back to the perfectoid space
\widehat{S}^{\sharp}_{p^{-\infty}}
associated with the saturated product
S^{\sharp}\times^{sat}_{X}\widetilde{X}
, and to
\widehat{S}_{1}^{\sharp}:=\widehat{S}^{\sharp}_{p^{-\infty}}\times_{\widehat{X}}\widehat{X}^{str}
. By functoriality of
2
-limits, the two pullbacks
\pi_{1}(\widehat{S}^{\sharp}_{1})
and
\pi_{2}(\widehat{S}_{1}^{\sharp})
are equal. For the same reason, the
v
-descent data of the two maps from
\widehat{S}^{\sharp}_{1}
to
S^{\sharp}
are canonically identified. Hence, we have
\pi_{1}(S^{\sharp})=\pi_{2}(S^{\sharp})
by descent.
∎

2.4.
Extending log shtukas

Let
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
be an fs log scheme, where
X
is a normal scheme that is separated, flat and of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
or
X=\operatorname{Spec}A
for an excellent Noetherian normal domain
A
that is flat over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
Denote by
X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
the generic fiber of
X
.
Let
\mathcal{M}_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}=i^{*}\mathcal{M}_{X}
be the log structure induced by the natural inclusion
i:X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\hookrightarrow X
.

We show that

Theorem 2.55

(cf.
[
66
, Thm. 2.7.7]
)
.

In the situation above, the restriction functor

\operatorname{Res}:=\operatorname{Res}^{X}_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}:\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond/}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\mathcal{M}_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}})

is fully faithful.

Moreover, the restriction

\operatorname{Res}:\mathrm{Sht}^{\diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\diamond}\times_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\operatorname{Spd}\mathbb{Q}_{p})

is fully faithful.

2.4.1.

Let us introduce some conventions.

Let
\mathscr{P}_{1}
and
\mathscr{P}_{2}
be two objects in
\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond/}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
. It follows from the construction in Definition
2.33
and Definition
A.3
that
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond/}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})}(\mathscr{P}_{1},\mathscr{P}_{2})=

\lim\limits_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}},f_{S^{\sharp}})\in((X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\Diamond/})^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(S^{\sharp})}(\mathscr{P}_{1,(S^{\sharp},f)},\mathscr{P}_{2,(S^{\sharp},f)}).

The morphisms in
\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\mathcal{M}_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}})
are formed in a similar way.

Writing
\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{i}:=\mathscr{P}_{i}|_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\log\Diamond}}
the restriction of
\mathscr{P}_{i}
to
X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\log\Diamond}
for
i=1,2
, a morphism
\mathbf{H}\in\ob\mathrm{Hom}(\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{1},\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{2})
is then represented by
\mathbf{H}=\{\mathbf{H}_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{can}}_{S^{\sharp}},f)}\}
, where
\mathbf{H}_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{can}}_{S^{\sharp}},f)}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(S^{\sharp})}(\mathscr{P}_{1,(S^{\sharp},f)},\mathscr{P}_{2,(S^{\sharp},f)})
and the collection runs over the objects
(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}},f)
in
X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\log\Diamond}
that satisfy the condition in Definition
A.3
(2).

Similarly, let
\mathcal{H}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond/}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})}(\mathscr{P}_{1},\mathscr{P}_{2})
be a morphism. Denote by
\mathcal{H}_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{can}}_{S^{\sharp}},f)}
the morphism between
\mathscr{P}_{1,(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{can}}_{S^{\sharp}},f)}
and
\mathscr{P}_{2,(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}},f)}
assigned to
\mathcal{H}
.

In what follows, we will frequently use the abbreviations
\mathcal{H}_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{can}}_{S^{\sharp}},f)}=\mathcal{H}_{S^{\sharp}}
and
\mathbf{H}_{(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{can}}_{S^{\sharp}},f)}=\mathbf{H}_{S^{\sharp}}
if there is nothing confusing.

Proof of Theorem
2.55
.
The proof can be adapted from the proofs of
[
66
, Prop. 2.7.6]
and
[
66
, Thm. 2.7.7]
. First, note that the argument in
loc. cit.
did not use that the scheme is of finite type after restricting the question locally to a normal domain; moreover, the second claim will follow from the same proof as the first. We assume that we are in the first situation for
X
.

With the conventions as above, given
\mathbf{H}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{1},\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{2})
, we will show that
\mathbf{H}
extends uniquely to a morphism
\mathcal{H}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond/}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})}(\mathscr{P}_{1},\mathscr{P}_{2})
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
X=\operatorname{Spec}A^{+}
is affine and admits a global fs sharp chart
\mathsf{P}
. (Indeed, we can do this by taking an étale neighborhood around each geometric point of
X
and by taking the log structure on the étale neighborhood the pullback of
\mathcal{M}_{X}
. After proving this case, one can pass to a Cech nerve of an étale cover.) We write
\widehat{A}^{+}
the (
p
-adic) completion and write
\widehat{A}:=\widehat{A}^{+}[\frac{1}{p}]
. Write
\widehat{X}:=\mathrm{Spf}\widehat{A}^{+}
and
\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}:=\operatorname{Spa}(\widehat{A}^{+},\widehat{A}^{+})
.

Let
f:(Y:=\operatorname{Spa}(B,B^{+}),\mathcal{M}_{Y})\to(\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}},\mathcal{M}_{\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}})
be an object in
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\diamond}
. Here, we also assume that
Y=\operatorname{Spa}(B,B^{+})
is affinoid perfectoid and assume that
\mathcal{M}_{Y}=\mathcal{M}_{Y}^{\mathrm{can}}
.

Firstly, we consider the case where
(B,B^{+})=(C,C^{+})
is an algebraically closed, (nonarchimedean, complete) perfectoid field. As in the proof of
[
66
, Prop. 2.7.6]
, if the morphism
f
is adic, then
C
is of characteristic zero, but, in this case, we have that
f
is already an object in
(X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\mathcal{M}_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}})^{\Diamond}
, so we set
\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{Spa}(C,C^{+})}=\mathbf{H}_{\operatorname{Spa}(C,C^{+})}
.

If the morphism
f
is not adic, then
C
is of characteristic
p
.
In this case, we claim that
f:\operatorname{Spa}(C,C^{+})\to\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}
factors through an integral perfectoid space
X_{\infty}
over
\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}
. In fact, let
\widehat{X}_{0}:=\operatorname{Spa}(\widehat{A},\widehat{A}^{+})
. Then
\widehat{X}_{0}
is an affinoid Tate adic space equipped with a morphism to the adic generic fiber of
\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}
and to
\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}
,
i:\widehat{X}_{0}\to\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\eta}\to\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}
.
Let
R_{p^{-\infty}}
be the inverse limit of finite étale algebras over
\widehat{A}_{p^{-\infty}}:=\widehat{A}\otimes_{\widehat{A}\langle\mathsf{P}\rangle}\widehat{A}\langle\mathsf{P}[1/p]\rangle
.
Let
R^{+}_{p^{-\infty}}
be the integral closure in
R_{p^{-\infty}}
of
\widehat{A}^{+}
. By Lemma
2.47
(2), the
p
-adic completion
(\widehat{R}_{p^{-\infty}},\widehat{R}^{+}_{p^{-\infty}})
is a complete affinoid perfectoid Huber pair. Now, since
(C,C^{+})
is an algebraically closed perfectoid field,
C^{+}
is a valuation ring and
R^{+}_{p^{-\infty}}
is formed by an inverse limit of normalizations in finite ring extensions, the map
\widehat{A}^{+}\to C^{+}
factors through
\widehat{A}^{+}\to\widehat{R}^{+}_{p^{-\infty}}\to C^{+}
. Set
X_{\infty}:=\operatorname{Spa}(\widehat{R}^{+}_{p^{-\infty}})
. The claim is shown.

By Lemma
2.47
(2),
X_{\infty}
is equipped with the log structure
\mathsf{P}[1/p]^{a}
induced by
\mathsf{P}[1/p]
; this is a saturated and fine perfectoid log structure by construction (see also
[
49
, Rmk. 2.26]
). So the associated log perfectoid space
X_{\infty,\eta}:=\operatorname{Spa}(\widehat{R}_{p^{-\infty}},\widehat{R}^{+}_{p^{-\infty}})
with its natural morphism to
\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}
is an object in
X^{\log\diamond}
. By
[
66
, Prop. 2.7.6]
,
\mathbf{H}_{X_{\infty,\eta}}
extends uniquely to a morphism
\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{Spd}(\widehat{R}^{+}_{p^{-\infty}})}:\mathscr{P}_{1}|_{\operatorname{Spd}(\widehat{R}^{+}_{p^{-\infty}})}\to\mathscr{P}_{2}|_{\operatorname{Spd}(\widehat{R}^{+}_{p^{-\infty}})}
, and
\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{Spa}C}
is determined by pulling back
\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{Spd}(\widehat{R}^{+}_{p^{-\infty}})}
to
\operatorname{Spd}C
. (To apply the proposition there, one needs that
\widehat{R}^{+}_{p^{-\infty}}=\widehat{R}^{\circ}_{p^{-\infty}}
. This follows from the normalization construction.)

Finally, we consider general
Y=\operatorname{Spa}(B,B^{+})
. This follows from a
v
-descent argument.
As in the proof of
loc. cit.
, we choose a collection of maps
\{\pi_{i}:\operatorname{Spa}(C_{i},C_{i}^{+})\to\operatorname{Spa}(B,B^{+})\}
covering
|Y|
, where
\operatorname{Spa}(C_{i},C_{i}^{+})
are algebraically closed perfectoid fields. We then form a product
Z:=\operatorname{Spa}(\prod_{i}C_{i},\prod_{i}C_{i}^{+})
with a
v
-cover
\pi:=\prod_{i}\pi_{i}:Z\to Y
.
Then
Z
maps to
\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}
via
Z\xrightarrow{\ \pi\ }Y\xrightarrow{\ f\ }\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}
, and the log structure on
Z
is defined by pulling back
\mathcal{M}_{Y}
.
We can construct
\mathcal{H}_{Z}
: By the proof of
[
66
, Prop. 2.7.6]
, the morphisms on
W(C_{i}^{+})
for the corresponding BKF modules. We then take the product of these morphisms on
\prod_{i}W(C_{i}^{+})
and restrict to get a morphism
\mathcal{H}_{Z}
between shtukas.

To show that the morphism
\mathcal{H}_{Z}
descends to a unique morphism
\mathcal{H}_{Y}
, it suffices to check that there is a descent datum
p^{*}_{1}\mathcal{H}_{Z}\cong p^{*}_{2}\mathcal{H}_{Z}
on
\widetilde{Z}:=Z\times_{Y}Z
.
As explained in the proof of
loc. cit.
, it suffices to check this pointwisely.
For any point
s:\operatorname{Spa}(C,C^{+})\to\widetilde{Z}
, we have
\pi\circ p_{1}\circ s=\pi\circ p_{2}\circ s
.
So the two sides are the equal after post-composing
f
. It suffices to deal with the case that
C
is of characteristic
p
. By the last three paragraphs, we know that
f\circ\pi\circ p_{i}\circ s
can also be factored as
x:\operatorname{Spa}C\to\operatorname{Spd}C^{+}\to\operatorname{Spd}\widehat{R}^{+}_{p^{-\infty}}\to X^{\log\diamond}
, and this factorization is
unique
by the first paragraph of the proof of
loc. cit.
(see
[
66
, 2.7.2]
and
[
25
]
). Thus the last two paragraphs gives a unique way of defining
\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{Spa}C,x}
, as desired.

We now check the uniqueness (as there might be a different choice of
v
-cover) and functoriality of this assignment. Given a morphism
g:(Y,\mathcal{M}_{Y}^{\mathrm{can}},f)\to(Y^{\prime},\mathcal{M}_{Y^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{can}},f^{\prime})
such that
f^{\prime}\circ g=f
, we check that, taking products of points
Z\to Y
and
Z^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime}
and constructing
\mathcal{H}_{Y}
and
\mathcal{H}_{Y^{\prime}}
