using them respectively, we have
g^{*}\mathcal{H}_{Y^{\prime}}=\mathcal{H}_{Y}
.
Upon replacing
Z
with the disjoint union of it with a
v
-cover of
Y\times_{Y^{\prime}}Z^{\prime}
, we assume that there is a morphism
\widetilde{g}:Z\to Z^{\prime}
covering
g
. Then, by the same argument as the last paragraph, the
\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{Spa}C,x}
at any point of
Z
constructed by either pulling back from
Z
or pulling back from
Z\xrightarrow{\widetilde{g}}Z^{\prime}
are the same as the one factoring through
\operatorname{Spd}\widehat{R}^{+}_{p^{-\infty}}
. So
\widetilde{g}^{*}\mathcal{H}_{Z^{\prime}}=\mathcal{H}_{Z}
. Next, we consider the fiber product
\widetilde{Z}
as the last paragraph, and the same argument shows that the morphisms descend and are equal on
Y
.

For the case of affine excellent normal flat domains, all arguments above work; note that the normalization argument goes through by
[
26
, part2, 7.8.3(5)]
.

\square

2.4.2.

Let us state an immediate corollary of Theorem
2.55
.

Corollary 2.56

(cf.
[
66
, Rmk. 2.7.9]
)
.

Let
X
be a normal scheme that is flat, separated and of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Let
D\subset X
be a relative Cartier divisor such that
U:=X\backslash D
is open dense in
X
.
Let
\mathcal{M}_{X}
be the log structure defined by
D\hookrightarrow X\hookleftarrow U
.

(1)

Suppose that the generic fiber
X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is a smooth variety with the normal crossings divisor
D_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. Then the restriction functor

\operatorname{Res}^{X}_{U}:\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond/}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})\to\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond/}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(U)

is fully faithful.

(2)

Suppose that
X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
is a smooth variety, and that
\widehat{U}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\eta}\hookrightarrow\widehat{X}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\eta}
has a normal crossings complement. Then the restriction functor

\operatorname{Res}^{X}_{U}:\mathrm{Sht}^{\diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{\diamond}(U)

is fully faithful.

Proof.

We have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond/}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond/}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}},\mathcal{M}_{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sht}^{\Diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(U_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}^{X}_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}^{X_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}_{U_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}}

Then the assertion follows from combining Theorem
2.55
,
[
66
, Thm. 2.7.7]
and Corollary
2.53
. Indeed, we have known that the functors represented by the bottom arrow and the vertical arrows are fully faithful.

The second part follows similarly from Remark
2.51
, Corollary
2.53
and Theorem
2.55
.
∎

2.5.
Restriction to special fiber

Suppose that
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})
is in Case
2
of log schemes.

2.5.1.

Assume that
X
is defined over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
or just
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.

Proposition 2.57
.

The projection
F^{\log}:(X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}},\mathcal{M}_{X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}})^{\diamond}\to X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{\diamond}
admits a canonical section
i^{\log}
.
This section is given by assigning to any
(S^{\sharp},f)\in X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{\diamond}(S)
an object in log diamond
(S^{\sharp},(f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}})^{\textrm{perf}},f)\in X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{\log\diamond}.

The same holds after replacing
\diamond
with
\Diamond
.

Proof.

When
S^{\sharp}
is an affinoid perfectoid space of characteristic
p
, it is perfect. Then the morphism between sheaves of monoids
f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}}\to\mathcal{O}_{S^{\sharp}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
induces a morphism
(f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}})^{\textrm{perf}}\to\mathcal{O}_{S^{\sharp}_{\text{{\'{e}}t}}}
.
∎

Corollary 2.58
.

Suppose that there is a log
\mathcal{G}
-shtuka bounded by
\mu
,

\mathscr{P}:(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}.

Then the restriction of
\mathscr{P}
to
(X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}},\mathcal{M}_{X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}})^{\diamond}
gives a morphism

\mathscr{P}|_{X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}}:(X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}},\mathcal{M}_{X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}})^{\diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}.

This morphism is equivalent to a morphism

\mathscr{P}|_{X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}}:X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{\diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}.

The same holds if we replace
\diamond
with
\Diamond
.

Moreover, there is a morphism
\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{red}}:X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{\textrm{perf}}\to\mathrm{Sht}^{W}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
.

Proof.

The construction of
\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}}
in Lemma
2.37
coincides with the perfection of
f^{*}\mathcal{M}_{X}
in Proposition
2.57
. By Proposition
2.38
, we have a morphism
X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{\diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
. Applying the reduction functor in the sense of
[
25
]
, and noting that
(\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu})_{\mathrm{red}}
is represented by
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W}
,
X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{\diamond}=(X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{\textrm{perf}})^{\diamond}
by
[
75
, Prop. 18.3.1]

and that
((X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{\textrm{perf}})^{\diamond})_{\mathrm{red}}
is represented by
X_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}^{\textrm{perf}}
by
[
25
, Prop. 3.16]
, we get the desired morphism.
∎

For simplicity, we will use charts to refer to log structures in the remaining part of §
2.5
.

Remark 2.59
.

Let
K
be a finite field extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and let
\varpi\in K
be a uniformizer. One can consider the absolute log prismatic site
(\mathcal{O}_{K},\varpi^{\mathbb{N}})_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
. A log prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{E}
on
(\mathcal{O}_{K},\varpi^{\mathbb{N}})_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
is equivalent to a semistable
G_{K}
-representation
\mathbb{L}
in
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{st}}(G_{K})
by
[
87
]
. One can read off the monodromy action of the semistable representation
\mathbb{L}[1/p]
by evaluating the associated log prismatic
F
-crystal
\mathcal{E}
on Breuil-Kisin/Hyodo–Kato log prisms. If we evaluate
\mathcal{E}
at the cover
(\mathcal{O}_{K},\varpi^{\mathbb{N}})\to(\mathcal{O}_{C},\varpi^{\mathbb{N}[\frac{1}{p}]})
, one obtains a Breuil-Kisin-Fargues module (see
[
87
, Ex. 3.1]
). Let
S=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})\in\operatorname{Perf}
. The objects
(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f)\in(\mathcal{O}_{K},\varpi^{\mathbb{N}})^{\diamond}(S)
require
S^{\sharp}
to be perfectoid and
\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}
to be saturated and fine perfectoid; thus
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{S^{\sharp}}
is uniquely
p
-divisible (see Lemma
2.10
). In particular, under the equivalence of categories between perfect log prisms and perfectoid log rings in
[
49
, Prop. 2.39]
, one only sees the
perfect
objects in
(\mathcal{O}_{K},\varpi^{\mathbb{N}})^{\diamond}
. Therefore, the realization functor from log prismatic
F
-crystals to log shtukas forgets the monodromy action when we consider the specialization map as in
[
66
, Prop. 2.4.6]
.

2.5.2.
Log
p
-divisible groups

In this subsection, we give an example that might be relevant to Corollary
2.58
. Proposition
2.60
will not be used in the remaining of this paper.

Let
K
be a finite field extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, let
\mathcal{O}_{K}
be its ring of integers, and let
k
be its residue field. Let
\varpi\in K
be a uniformizer, and set
S=(\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{K},\varpi^{\mathbb{N}})
,
S_{0}=(\operatorname{Spec}k,0^{\mathbb{N}})
. By the main theorem of
[
8
]
, taking the generic fiber
(\cdot)_{K}
gives an equivalence of categories between the category
\mathbf{BT}^{\log}_{S,d}
of dual-representable log
p
-divisible groups over
S
and the category
\mathbf{BT}_{K}^{\mathrm{st}}
of
p
-divisible groups over
K
with semistable reduction. Moreover, taking the Tate module
T_{p}
gives an equivalence of categories between
\mathbf{BT}_{K}^{\mathrm{st}}
and the category
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\mathrm{st},\{0,1\}}(G_{K})
of semistable
G_{K}
-representations with Hodge–Tate weights in
\{0,1\}
.

Let
\mathcal{H}\in\mathbf{BT}^{\log}_{S,d}
. On the one hand, as in Remark
2.59
, one can attach to it a log prismatic
F
-crystal and then a log shtuka in
(\mathcal{O}_{K},\varpi^{\mathbb{N}})^{\diamond}
; by restricting to the special fiber and by Corollary
2.58
, we obtain a usual shtuka over
\operatorname{Spec}k
. On the other hand, the special fiber
\mathcal{H}_{k}
of
\mathcal{H}
is a log
p
-divisible group over
S_{0}
, which need not be a classical
p
-divisible group. Nevertheless, one can still produce a usual shtuka from
\mathcal{H}_{k}
that is compatible with the above one, by Lemma
2.37
and the following result:

Proposition 2.60
.

Let
\mathcal{H}
be a dualizable log
p
-divisible group over
(\operatorname{Spec}k,0^{\mathbb{N}})
. Then its pullback to
(\operatorname{Spec}k,0^{\mathbb{N}[\frac{1}{p}]})
becomes a classical
p
-divisible group.

Proof.

This follows from Kato’s classification of log
p
-divisible groups
[
43
, Thm. 3.1]
; cf.
[
84
, Thm. 3.8, Cor. 3.10]
. We briefly recall some notation and results from Kato’s work on log
p
-divisible groups, following
[
84
, §3]
. Let
T=\operatorname{Spec}A
, where
A
is a Noetherian henselian local ring with the residue characteristic
p
, and suppose that
T
admits a global chart
P\to\mathcal{M}_{T}
such that the induced map
\mathsf{P}\to\mathcal{M}_{T,\overline{t}}/\mathcal{O}_{T,\overline{t}}^{\times}
is an isomorphism at the geometric point
\overline{t}
of
T
. Let
(\mathrm{fin}/T)_{d}
be the category of finite Kummer flat log group schemes over
T
defined as in
[
43
, 1.6]
; see also
[
84
, Def. 2.3, 2.6]
for details. For
\mathcal{G}\in(\mathrm{fin}/T)_{d}
, there is a unique exact sequence
0\to\mathcal{G}^{\circ}\to\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}^{\text{{\'{e}}t}}\to 0
, which restricts to the classical connected-étale sequence over any finite Kummer flat cover where
\mathcal{G}
becomes a classical
p
-divisible group. Moreover,
\mathcal{G}^{\circ}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
are classical finite flat group schemes. From Kato’s classification, any
\mathcal{G}\in(\mathrm{fin}/T)_{d}
that is
p
-power torsion determines, and is uniquely determined by, a pair
(\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{cl}},\beta)
, where
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{cl}}
is a classical extension of
\mathcal{G}^{\circ}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
, and
\beta\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{G}^{\text{{\'{e}}t}}(1),\mathcal{G}^{\circ})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathsf{P}^{\mathrm{gp}}
.

By definition,
\mathcal{H}=\varinjlim\mathcal{H}_{n}
, where
\mathcal{H}_{n}=\operatorname{ker}(\mathcal{H}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{n}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{H})
is an object in
(\mathrm{fin}/S_{0})_{d}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{H}_{n}
determines and is determined by
(\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\mathrm{cl}},\beta_{n})
. Since
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\text{{\'{e}}t}}(1),\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\circ})
is killed by
p^{n}
,
\beta\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\text{{\'{e}}t}}(1),\mathcal{H}_{n}^{\circ})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathsf{P}^{\mathrm{gp}}/p^{n}\mathsf{P}^{\mathrm{gp}}
. In particular, after passing to
S_{0,\infty}=(\operatorname{Spec}k,0^{\mathbb{N}[\frac{1}{p}]})
,
\beta
is trivialized and
\mathcal{H}_{n}
becomes a classical
p
-divisible group. Therefore,
\mathcal{H}
is classical over
(\operatorname{Spec}k,0^{\mathbb{N}[\frac{1}{p}]})
.
∎

3.
Local systems and shtukas on mixed Shimura varieties

In this section, we work with the theory in characteristic
0
.

3.1.
Group-theoretic lemmas

We collect the results used later regarding various group-theoretic constructions in compactification theory, Bruhat-Tits theory, and their intersection.

3.1.1.

Let
P
be any linear algebraic group. Define
P^{c}:=P/Z(P)_{ac}
, where
Z(P)_{ac}
is the anti-cuspidal part of the multiplicative connected center
Z(P)^{\circ}
of
P
.

Given a mixed Shimura datum
(P,\mathcal{X})
, let
G
be the Levi quotient of
P
.
Then the natural projection
P\to G
induces an embedding
Z(P)\hookrightarrow Z(G)
, and
Z(P)_{ac}
is sent to
Z(G)_{ac}
by definition. Note that
P^{c}\to G^{c}
factors through the Levi quotient
G^{\prime,c}
of
P^{c}
, and
G^{\prime,c}\to G^{c}
is a homomorphism with central kernel.

Lemma 3.1
.

For a mixed Shimura datum
(P,\mathcal{X})
, we have
G^{\prime,c}=G^{c}
.

Proof.

Recall that
(P,\mathcal{X})
satisfies
[
69
, 2.1 (viii)]
. That is, we require that, for any Levi subgroup
\widetilde{G}\subset P
lifting
G
, the adjoint action of the center
Z(\widetilde{G})
of
\widetilde{G}
on
\lie W
factors through a cuspidal quotient;
W
denotes the unipotent radical of
P
. Note that the definition of
Z(\widetilde{G})
is independent of the choice of lifting
\widetilde{G}
.
From this condition, we see that

Z(G)_{ac}\xrightarrow{\sim}Z(\widetilde{G})_{ac}\subset\mathrm{Cent}_{Z(\widetilde{G})}(\lie W)=Z(P).

So the embedding
Z(P)_{ac}\hookrightarrow Z(G)_{ac}
admits a section by the displayed expression above. We then have
Z(P)_{ac}=Z(G)_{ac}
, and the lemma follows.
∎

3.1.2.

Now let
(G,X)
be a Shimura datum.
Let
\Phi=(Q_{\Phi},X_{\Phi}^{+},g_{\Phi})
be a cusp label representative of
(G,X)
(see
[
58
, 2.1.7]
). Recall that one associates to
\Phi
a mixed Shimura datum
(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
. Recall that
Q_{\Phi}\subset G
is an admissible
\mathbb{Q}
-parabolic that contains a normal subgroup
P_{\Phi}
and is equipped with a
Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant morphism

\tau:X\to\pi_{0}(X)\times\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathbb{C}},P_{\Phi,\mathbb{C}}),\quad x\mapsto([x],u_{x}^{Q_{\Phi}}\circ h_{\infty}).

Moreover,
D_{\Phi}
is defined as the
P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{C})
-orbit of a fixed point
([x],u_{x}^{Q_{\Phi}}\circ h_{\infty})
such that
x\in X^{+}_{\Phi}
, where
X_{\Phi}^{+}
is a connected component of
X
; it depends only on
X^{+}_{\Phi}
, not on the choice of
x
in
X^{+}_{\Phi}
. Denote by
W_{\Phi}
the unipotent radical of
P_{\Phi}
and
U_{\Phi}
the center of
W_{\Phi}
. Let
K
be an open compact subgroup of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Define
K_{\Phi}=P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}
; define
K_{\Phi,p}
and
K_{\Phi}^{p}
similarly.

Lemma 3.2
.

The natural embedding
P_{\Phi}\to G
induces a finite map
P^{c}_{\Phi}\to G^{c}
. Let
ZP_{\Phi}:=(Z_{G}\cdot P_{\Phi})^{\circ}
. Then there is an injective map
ZP_{\Phi}^{c}\to G^{c}
.

Proof.

Since the center of
ZP_{\Phi}/ZW_{\Phi}
is isogenous to a product of split tori and
\mathbb{R}
-anisotropic tori (cf.
[
69
, Cor. 4.10]
and
[
83
, Lem. 1.14]
), we know that
Z(P_{\Phi})_{ac}\subset Z(ZP_{\Phi})_{ac}=Z(ZW_{\Phi})_{ac}=Z(G)_{ac}
. So there is a well-defined quotient map
P_{\Phi}^{c}=P_{\Phi}/Z(P_{\Phi})_{ac}\to G^{c}=G/Z(G)_{ac}
.
For
ZP_{\Phi}
, by the last paragraph, we see that the embedding
ZP_{\Phi}\hookrightarrow G
induces an embedding
ZP^{c}_{\Phi}\hookrightarrow G^{c}
.
∎

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a quasi-parahoric group scheme of
G
, and let
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
be the
g_{\Phi}
-conjugate of
\mathcal{G}
(i.e.
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=g_{\Phi}\mathcal{G}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})g_{\Phi}^{-1}
). Let
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
(resp.
\mathcal{W}_{\Phi}
) be the closure of
Q_{\Phi}
(resp.
W_{\Phi}
) in
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
, and let
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}
be the smoothing of the closure of
P_{\Phi}
in
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
.

(1)

When
\mathcal{G}
is stabilizer quasi-parahoric (resp. quasi-parahoric, parahoric), then
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
is stabilizer quasi-parahoric (resp. quasi-parahoric, parahoric) in the sense of
1.18
. Moreover, when
\mathcal{G}
is parahoric, then
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
is a parabolic embedding in the sense of
1.19
.

(2)

When
\mathcal{G}
is stabilizer quasi-parahoric (resp. quasi-parahoric, parahoric), then
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}
is stabilizer quasi-parahoric (resp. quasi-parahoric, quasi-parahoric). Moreover,
(\mathcal{P}_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi})
comes from boundary in the sense of
1.21
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be quasi-parahoric. Let
\mathcal{U}_{\Phi}
be the closure of
U_{\Phi}
in
\mathcal{W}_{\Phi}
, and let
\mathcal{V}_{\Phi}
be the quotient
\mathcal{W}_{\Phi}/\mathcal{U}_{\Phi}
. Then
\mathcal{U}_{\Phi}
and
\mathcal{V}_{\Phi}
are affine smooth group schemes with connected fibers, and we have the decomposition
\mathcal{W}_{\Phi}=\mathcal{U}_{\Phi}\rtimes\mathcal{V}_{\Phi}
.

Proof.

This is essentially
[
60
, Prop. 2.67]
. We start from
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
and omit the index
\Phi
for simplicity (we won’t mention the initial
\mathcal{G}
). First of all, assume
K_{p}:=K_{\Phi,p}
and
Q:=Q_{\Phi}
are in good position, i.e. the associated (generic) point
x\in B_{\mathrm{red}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
of the facet
\mathcal{F}\subset B_{\mathrm{red}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(
\mathcal{G}^{\circ}=\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{F}}
) is contained in an apartment
A_{\mathrm{red}}(G,T)
, where
T
is a maximal
\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-split torus of
G
defined over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
such that
T\subset L\subset Q
. By the arguments below Definition
1.18
, the arguments above Definition
1.21
, and by
[
60
, Prop. 2.67]
with the help of Lemma
3.4
, we see that both statements
(1)
and
(2)
are true. In this case,
\mathcal{L}
(resp.
\mathcal{G}_{h}
) is the smoothening of the closure of
L
(resp.
G_{h}
) in
\mathcal{G}
.

In general, we first show
(1)
: apply arguments in
[
60
, Rmk. 2.45]
: we can always find an element
g\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
such that
K^{\prime}:=g^{-1}Kg
and
Q
are in good position,
K=gK^{\prime}g^{-1}
. We factor
g=qnk
,
q\in Q(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
,
n\in N(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
,
k\in P_{x}^{0}
, where
N
is the normalizer of
T
over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and
P_{x}^{0}=\mathcal{G}_{x}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=(K^{\prime})^{\circ}
. The action
k
fixes
x
,
n
moves
x
to
nx\in A_{\mathrm{red}}(G,T)
, and
q
shifts the apartment
A_{\mathrm{red}}(G,T)
in
B_{\mathrm{red}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Let
K_{1}=nkK^{\prime}(nk)^{-1}
, then
K_{1}
and
Q
are in good position,
Q(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap K_{1}=(W(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap K_{1})\rtimes(L(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap K_{1})
by the first paragraph. Since
W
is normal in
Q
,

Q(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap K=q(Q(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap K_{1})q^{-1}=(W(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap K)\rtimes(qL(\mathbb{Q}_{p})q^{-1}\cap K),

we still have
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{W}\rtimes\mathcal{L}
, and
\mathcal{W}
is smooth with connected fibers since it is conjugated to the one cut out by
K_{1}
. But in this case
\mathcal{L}
is no longer the closure of
L
in
\mathcal{G}
, we need to conjugate the section
L\to Q
by
q
. When we study the levels of mixed Shimura varieties coming from boundary, this is exactly what we do: we define the level
K_{\Phi,L}\subset L_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
as the image of
K_{\Phi,Q}\subset Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
under the projection
Q_{\Phi}\to L_{\Phi}
, and we do not prescribe (and do not need) a section
L_{\Phi}\to Q_{\Phi}
.

For the second statement
(2)
, in the arguments above Definition
1.21
, we use
K_{1}
in place of
K
, and note that the conjugation by
q
shifts apartments but does not change the correspondence between
x_{h}
and
x_{L}
.

For the last statement, when
K_{p}
and
Q
are in good position, fix a splitting
W=U\times V
where both
U
and
V
are products of root groups, by Bruhat-Tits theory, we have
\mathcal{W}=\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{V}
, where both
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{V}
are the closures of
U
and
V
in
\mathcal{W}
respectively.
\mathcal{W}
,
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{V}
are affine smooth group schemes with connected fibers. In general, since
U
is the center of
W
, then
Q
normalizes
U
, as in second paragraph one can conjugate
1\to\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{V}\to 1
by elements in
Q
and still get the wanted exact sequence.
∎

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
x\in A_{\mathrm{red}}(G,T)
,
T\subset Q\subset G
,
\breve{K}^{\circ}=\mathcal{G}_{x}^{\circ}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
,
\breve{K}=\mathcal{G}_{x}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. Let
\breve{K}_{1}
be a quasi-parahoric group controlled by
x
, i.e.,
\breve{K}^{\circ}\subset\breve{K}_{1}\subset\breve{K}
. Then

Q(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\cap\breve{K}_{1}=(W(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\cap\breve{K}_{1})\rtimes(L(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\cap\breve{K}_{1}).

Proof.

We omit the notation
(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. Since

Q\cap\breve{K}^{\circ}=(W\cap\breve{K}^{\circ})\rtimes(L\cap\breve{K}^{\circ}),\quad Q\cap\breve{K}=(W\cap\breve{K})\rtimes(L\cap\breve{K}),

and
W\cap\breve{K}^{\circ}=W\cap\breve{K}
(by the triviality of
\pi_{1}(W)
), we have
W\cap\breve{K}_{1}=W\cap\breve{K}^{\circ}
. On the other hand,

(Q\cap\breve{K}_{1})/(Q\cap\breve{K}^{\circ})=\pi(\breve{K}_{1})/\pi(\breve{K}^{\circ}),\quad(Q\cap\breve{K})/(Q\cap\breve{K}_{1})=\pi(\breve{K})/\pi(\breve{K}_{1}).

Here
\pi:Q\to L
is the projection. Since
\pi(\breve{K})/\pi(\breve{K}^{\circ})=(L\cap\breve{K})/(L\cap\breve{K}^{\circ})\subset\pi_{1}(L)_{I,\mathrm{tor}}
is finite abelian, there is a unique subgroup of
Q\cap\breve{K}
containing
Q\cap\breve{K}^{\circ}
with image
\pi(\breve{K}_{1})/\pi(\breve{K}^{\circ})
in
\pi(\breve{K})/\pi(\breve{K}^{\circ})
, this forces
Q\cap\breve{K}_{1}=(W\cap\breve{K}_{1})\rtimes(L\cap\breve{K}_{1})
and
L\cap\breve{K}_{1}=\pi(\breve{K}_{1})
.
∎

This proof also shows the following.

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
\mathcal{P}_{x}=\mathcal{U}\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{x}
be a stabilizer quasi-parahoric group scheme, and
\mathcal{P}_{x}^{\circ}=\mathcal{U}\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{x}^{\circ}
. Let
\breve{K}=\mathcal{P}_{x}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
,
\breve{K}_{G}=\mathcal{G}_{x}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
,
\breve{K}^{\circ}=\mathcal{P}_{x}^{\circ}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
,
\breve{K}_{G}^{\circ}=\mathcal{G}_{x}^{\circ}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. Given a
\sigma
-invariant closed subgroup
\breve{K}_{1}\subset\breve{K}
that contains
\breve{K}^{\circ}
, let
\breve{K}_{1,G}\subset G(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
be the image of
\breve{K}_{1}
, then
\breve{K}_{1,G}
and
\breve{K}_{1}
are quasi-parahoric and there exist unique quasi-parahoric models
\mathcal{P}_{1}=\mathcal{U}\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{1}
with
\mathcal{P}_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\breve{K}_{1}
,
\mathcal{G}_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\breve{K}_{1,G}
.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
P_{1}=U_{1}\rtimes G_{1}
,
P_{2}=U_{2}\rtimes G_{2}
,
P_{1}\to P_{2}
be an embedding that is compatible with
U_{1}\rtimes G_{1}\to U_{2}\rtimes G_{2}
in the sense of
1.33
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{i}=\mathcal{U}_{i}\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{i}
be quasi-parahoric group schemes of
P_{i}
such that

\mathcal{P}_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\mathcal{P}_{2}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\cap\mathcal{P}_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}),\quad\mathcal{G}_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\mathcal{G}_{2}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\cap\mathcal{G}_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}),

then the induced morphism
\mathcal{P}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}
is compatible with
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
in the sense of
1.35
.

Proof.

The decomposition
\mathcal{P}_{2}=\mathcal{U}_{2}\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{2}
fixes a section
G_{2}\to P_{2}
. In characteristic
0
, we can choose a section
G_{1}\to P_{1}
such that
G_{1}\to P_{1}\to P_{2}
factors through
G_{2}
. By assumption,

\mathcal{G}_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=G_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\cap\mathcal{G}_{2}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=G_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\cap\mathcal{P}_{2}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=G_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\cap\mathcal{P}_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),

this gives a section
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{1}
that induces
\mathcal{P}_{1}=\mathcal{U}_{1}\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{1}
.
∎

3.1.3.
Associating quasi-parahoric group schemes

Recall:

G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{0}=\ker(G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tilde{\kappa}_{G}}}{{\to}}\pi_{1}(G)_{I}),\quad G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{1}=\operatorname{ker}(G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tilde{\nu}_{G}}}{{\to}}\pi_{1}(G)_{I}\otimes\mathbb{Q}),

Given any quasi-parahoric group scheme
\mathcal{G}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, one can find
x\in\operatorname{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathrm{red}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
such that

(3.1)

\mathrm{Stab}_{G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}(x)\cap G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{0}\subset\mathcal{G}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\subset\mathrm{Stab}_{G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}(x)\cap G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{1},

moreover,
x
is in the generic position of the facet
\mathcal{F}\subset\operatorname{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathrm{red}}(G,\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
determined by the parahoric group scheme
\mathcal{G}^{\circ}
. We have
\mathcal{G}^{\circ}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\mathrm{Stab}_{G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}(x)\cap G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{0}
.

Also, recall that, if
G\to G^{\prime}
is a surjection with a central kernel, there is a canonical (
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\to G^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
)-equivariant bijection
\operatorname{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathrm{red}}(G,\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\to\operatorname{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathrm{red}}(G^{\prime},\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. Here we use reduced buildings instead of extended buildings.

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
G
be a reductive group,
Z\subset G
be a central torus, and
G^{\prime}=G/Z
. Then the surjection
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\to G^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
induces a surjection
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{0}\to G^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{0}
, a surjection
\mathrm{Stab}_{G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}(x)\to\mathrm{Stab}_{G^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}(x^{\prime})
, and a morphism
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{1}\to G^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{1}
where
x^{\prime}\in\operatorname{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathrm{red}}(G^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is the image of some
x\in\operatorname{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathrm{red}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. In particular, given a quasi-parahoric (resp. parahoric) group scheme
\mathcal{G}
of
G
associated with a point
x\in\operatorname{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathrm{red}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
in the sense of
(\ref{eq: cut-off quasi-parahoric})
, then the image of
\mathcal{G}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
contains (resp. is) the parahoric group
(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{x^{\prime}})^{\circ}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
and is contained in the stabilizer quasi-parahoric group
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{x^{\prime}}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.

Proof.

Since
Z
is connected,
H^{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p},Z)
is trivial by Steinberg’s theorem,
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\to G^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
is a surjection. Consider the commutative diagram with exact rows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Z(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{1}(Z)_{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{1}(G)_{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{1}(G^{\prime})_{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{1,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\kappa}_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\kappa}_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\kappa}_{G^{\prime}}}

here vertical maps are Kottwitz maps and are surjective (
[
50
, §7.1]
). Chasing the diagram, we have a surjective
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{0}\to G^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{0}
.

Let
g\in\mathrm{Stab}_{G^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}(x^{\prime})
and
g^{\prime}\in G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
be a point in its preimage. Since
\operatorname{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathrm{red}}(G,\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\operatorname{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathrm{red}}(G^{\prime},\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
is
G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-
G^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-equivariant,
g^{\prime}
fixes
x
; thus,
\mathrm{Stab}_{G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}(x)\to\mathrm{Stab}_{G^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}(x^{\prime})
is surjective and moreover,
\mathrm{Stab}_{G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}(x)
is the preimage of
\mathrm{Stab}_{G^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}(x^{\prime})
under
\pi:G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\to G^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. This forces

\pi(\mathrm{Stab}_{G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}(x)\cap G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{0})=\pi(\mathrm{Stab}_{G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}(x))\cap\pi(G(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{0})=\mathrm{Stab}_{G^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})}(x^{\prime})\cap G^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})^{0}

.
∎

By Lemmas
3.7
and
3.5
, we make the following definitions:

Definition/Lemma 3.8
.

Let
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{U}\rtimes\mathcal{G}
be a quasi-parahoric group scheme of
P
, and let
\breve{K}_{p}=\mathcal{P}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. Then
\mathcal{G}^{\circ}
is the parahoric group scheme
\mathcal{G}_{x}^{\circ}=\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{F}}
for some
x\in\mathcal{F}\subset\operatorname{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathrm{red}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

(1)

Let
\breve{K}^{c}_{p}\subset P^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
be the image of
\breve{K}_{p}
under the projection
P\to P^{c}
. Then
\breve{K}^{c}_{p}
is a quasi-parahoric group, and there is a unique quasi-parahoric group scheme
\mathcal{P}^{c}=\mathcal{U}\rtimes\mathcal{G}^{c}
of
P^{c}
with
\mathcal{P}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\breve{K}^{c}_{p}
, where
(\mathcal{G}^{c})^{\circ}
is the parahoric group scheme associated with
x\in\operatorname{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathrm{red}}(G^{c},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. We call
K_{p}^{c}:=\mathcal{P}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
\mathcal{P}^{c}
the quasi-parahoric group (scheme)
associated with

K_{p}
, and
P\to P^{c}
extends to
\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{P}^{c}
.

(2)

In general, let
P\to P^{\prime}
be a quotient with central multiplicative kernel. Let
\mathcal{P}^{\prime,\circ}_{x}=\mathcal{U}\rtimes\mathcal{G}^{\prime,\circ}_{x}
(resp.
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{x}=\mathcal{U}\rtimes\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{x}
) be the parahoric (resp. stabilizer quasi-parahoric) group scheme associated with
x\in\operatorname{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathrm{red}}(G^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Let
\breve{K}^{\prime}_{p}\subset\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{x}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
be a
\sigma
-invariant subgroup that contains
\im(\breve{K}_{p})
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime,\circ}_{x}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
and that stabilizes
\mathcal{U}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. Then it is a quasi-parahoric group, and there is a unique quasi-parahoric group scheme
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=\mathcal{U}\rtimes\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
with
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\breve{K}^{\prime}_{p}
and
(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})^{\circ}=\mathcal{G}^{\prime,\circ}_{x}
; moreover,
P\to P^{\prime}
extends to
\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
.

Remark 3.9
.

In fact, the subgroup generated by
\im(\breve{K}_{p})
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime,\circ}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is just
\im(\breve{K}_{p})\cdot\mathcal{P}^{\prime,\circ}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
and is open compact:
\im(\breve{K}_{p})\subset\mathcal{P}_{x}^{\prime,\circ}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, thus its conjugation stabilizes
\mathcal{P}^{\prime,\circ}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.

Now let
(G,X)
be a Shimura datum, and let
\Phi
be a cusp label representative.

Definition 3.10
.

Let
Y_{\Phi}
be a connected subgroup satisfying
P_{\Phi}\subset Y_{\Phi}\subset ZP_{\Phi}
. Denote
Y_{\Phi}^{*}:=Z(G)_{ac}\cdot Y_{\Phi}/Z(G)_{ac}
.

By definition, we have a central isogeny
Y_{\Phi}^{c}\to Y_{\Phi}^{*}\subset G^{c}
; in addition,
ZP_{\Phi}^{*}=ZP_{\Phi}^{c}
.

Note that
P_{\Phi}^{*}
can also be obtained as follows. Pick
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
. This cusp label representative maps to a cusp label representative
\Phi^{*}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G^{c},X^{c})
. Then
P^{*}_{\Phi}=P_{\Phi^{*}}
.

Definition 3.11
.

Let
\breve{K}_{\Phi,p}^{*}=\breve{K}_{p}^{c}\cap P_{\Phi}^{*}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
and let
\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}
be the quasi-parahoric group scheme whose group of
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-points is
\breve{K}_{\Phi,p}^{*}
(see Lemma
3.3
). The group
\breve{K}_{p}^{c}=\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
is defined in
3.8
, viewing
G
here as the
P
there.

Then there is, by definition, a map
\breve{K}_{\Phi,p}^{c}\to\breve{K}_{\Phi,p}^{*}
; the first group is defined by Definition
3.8
with input
(P,\breve{K}_{p})=(P_{\Phi},\breve{K}_{\Phi,p})
. We have
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{c}\to\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*}
.

Definition 3.12
.

The mixed Shimura datum
(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
and the map
P_{\Phi}\to P_{\Phi}^{*}
induce a mixed Shimura datum
(P_{\Phi}^{*},D_{\Phi}^{*})
. We denote the corresponding tower with respect to the weight filtration by

(P_{\Phi}^{*},D_{\Phi}^{*})\to({\overline{P}_{\Phi}^{*}},{\overline{D}_{\Phi}^{*}})\to(G_{\Phi,h}^{*},D^{*}_{\Phi,h})
. Now, the images of
\breve{K}^{*}_{\Phi,p}
in
\overline{P}^{*}_{\Phi}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
and
G_{\Phi,h}^{*}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
are denoted by
\overline{\breve{K}}^{*}_{\Phi,p}
and
\breve{K}^{*}_{\Phi,h,p}
, respectively.

By Lemma
3.3
, we have quasi-parahoric group schemes
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{*}_{\Phi}
and
\mathcal{G}^{*}_{\Phi,h}
such that

\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}=\mathcal{W}_{\Phi}\rtimes\mathcal{G}^{*}_{\Phi,h},\quad\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{*}_{\Phi}=\mathcal{V}_{\Phi}\rtimes\mathcal{G}^{*}_{\Phi,h},\quad\mathcal{G}^{*}_{\Phi,h}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\breve{K}^{*}_{\Phi,h,p},\quad\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{*}_{\Phi}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\overline{\breve{K}}^{*}_{\Phi,p}.

Moreover, the natural morphisms
\overline{P}_{\Phi}^{c}\to\overline{P}_{\Phi}^{*}
,
G_{\Phi,h}^{c}\to G_{\Phi,h}^{*}
induce morphisms of quasi-parahoric group schemes
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\Phi}^{c}\to\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\Phi}^{*}
,
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{c}\to\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*}
. We denote
K^{*}_{\Phi,h,p}=\mathcal{G}^{*}_{\Phi,h}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
,
\overline{K}^{*}_{\Phi,p}=\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{*}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
as usual.

3.2.
Mixed Shimura varieties

In this subsection, we quickly recall some facts about mixed Shimura varieties.

Let
(P,\mathcal{X})
be a mixed Shimura datum in the sense of
[
69
]
. According to the weight filtration, there is a tower of mixed Shimura data
(P,\mathcal{X})\to(\overline{P},\overline{\mathcal{X}})\to(G_{h},X_{h})
, where
G_{h}
is the Levi quotient of
P
and
\overline{P}=P/U
, where
U
is a normal subgroup contained in the unipotent radical of
P
.

Let
K
be a neat open compact subgroup of
P(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Define
\overline{K}
(resp.
K_{h}
) as the image of
K
in
\overline{P}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
(resp.
P_{h}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
). Thus, we have morphisms between mixed Shimura varieties
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}(\overline{P},\overline{\mathcal{X}})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}}(G_{h},\mathcal{X}_{h})
. The reflex fields of these mixed Shimura varieties are the same.

If we assume that
(P,\mathcal{X})=(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
comes from a cusp label representative
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
of a Shimura datum
(G,X)
, we call such mixed Shimura data/varieties the boundary mixed Shimura data/varieties. See
[
83
, §1.1.3]
.
In this case,
U
is the center of the unipotent radical of
P
.
Note that there exist mixed Shimura varieties that do not arise in this manner.

Write the cusp label representative
\Phi
as
(Q_{\Phi},X_{\Phi}^{+},g_{\Phi})
. Consider an open immersion:

(3.2)

U({\Phi})=P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash X^{+}_{\Phi}\times P(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{\Phi}\subset\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})(\mathbb{C}).

Let

\beta_{\Phi}:U({\Phi})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)(\mathbb{C}),\quad[(x,p)]\mapsto[(x,pg)].

By varying
\Phi
, we obtain a covering of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)(\mathbb{C})
via
\beta_{\Phi}
. The (analytic) construction of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)(\mathbb{C})
uses compactifications of
U({\Phi})
.

Note that we can view
\beta_{\Phi}
as the composition of a natural projection

(3.3)

p:P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash X_{\Phi}^{+}\times P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{\Phi}\to G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash X^{+}_{\Phi}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/g_{\Phi}Kg^{-1}_{\Phi}

followed by a right action

(3.4)

[\cdot g_{\Phi}]:G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash X^{+}_{\Phi}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}\to G(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash X^{+}_{\Phi}\times G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K.

Given
x\in X
, denote by
\mu_{x}
the Hodge cocharacter at
x
. From now on, fix homomorphisms
h_{0}
and
h_{\infty}
from
\mathbb{S}
to
H_{0}
such that their pre-compositions with
\mu:\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}\to\mathbb{S}{C}
, sending
z
to
(z,1)
, are identical after conjugation by a matrix
\mathbf{c}\in\mathrm{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
; the choice of
\mathbf{c}
depends only on the choice of
h_{0}
and
h_{\infty}
, but not on the value of
z
. See, for example,
[
69
, 4.3]
or the beginning of
[
70
, 3.6]
.

Therefore,
\mathrm{int}(\mathbf{c})\circ\mu_{x}=u_{x}^{Q_{\Phi}}\circ h_{\infty}\circ\mu
factors through
P_{\Phi,\mathbb{C}}
. We denote
\mu_{\Phi,x}:=\mathrm{int}(\mathbf{c})\circ\mu_{x}
and
\mu_{\Phi,h,x}:\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}\xrightarrow{\mu_{\Phi,x}}P_{\Phi,\mathbb{C}}\to G_{\Phi,h}
. The assignment
\mu_{x}\mapsto\mu_{\Phi,x}
is well defined.

We have the Borel embedding
X\hookrightarrow\check{X}\cong G/P_{\mu_{x}}(\mathbb{C})
sending a point
x
to the Hodge filtration defined by
\mu_{x}
. Here,
P_{\ast}
denotes the subgroup determined by the cocharacter
\ast
.

We then have the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{x\in X^{+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D_{\Phi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D_{\Phi,h}:=W_{\Phi}(\mathbb{R})U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{C})\backslash D_{\Phi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\breve{X}\cong G/P_{\mu_{x}}(\mathbb{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\breve{D}_{\Phi}\cong P_{\Phi}/P_{\mu_{\Phi,x}}(\mathbb{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\breve{D}_{\Phi,h}\cong G_{\Phi,h}/P_{\mu_{\Phi,h,x}}(\mathbb{C}).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{c}^{-1}(-)\mathbf{c}}

3.3.
p-adic local systems

Fix a mixed Shimura datum
(P,\mathcal{X})
in this subsection. The subgroup
K\subset P(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is assumed to be a neat open compact subgroup. If we assume that
K=K_{p}K^{p}
, then this always means that
K_{p}
is open compact in
P(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
K^{p}\subset P(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is neat open compact. Sometimes, we assume that
(P,\mathcal{X})=(G,X)
is a Shimura datum and consider boundary mixed Shimura data.

3.3.1.

Let
(P,\mathcal{X})
be a mixed Shimura datum. By
[
69
, Lem. 3.7(b)]
, for compact subgroups
K^{\prime}\subset K\subset P(\mathbb{A}_{f})
with
K=K_{p}K^{p}\subset P(\mathbb{A}_{f})
neat open compact, we have

\varprojlim_{K^{\prime}\subset K}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}(P,\mathcal{X})=\varprojlim_{K^{\prime}\subset K}P(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathcal{X}\times P(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\prime}=\varprojlim_{K^{\prime}\subset K}P(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathcal{X}\times P(\mathbb{A}_{f})/Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{-}K^{\prime},

where
Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{-}
is the closure of
Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{\circ}:=\{z\in Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})|z|_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathrm{id}\}
in
Z(P)(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Similarly, we denote by
Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{-}_{K}
the closure of
Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{\circ}\cap K
in
K
.
We let
K^{\prime}
run over the neat open compact subgroups in the form
K^{\prime}=K_{p}^{\prime}K^{p}
. Taking the inverse limit induces a natural pro-étale torsor

(3.5)

\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}^{\prime}K^{p}}(P,\mathcal{X})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})

under the group
\mathscr{G}_{p}(P):=K/Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{-}_{K}K^{p}
.

Definition 3.13
.

Let
K_{p}=\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
be a quasi-parahoric subgroup and define

\mathbb{P}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X}):=\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}^{\prime}K^{p}}(P,\mathcal{X})\times^{\underline{K/Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{-}_{K}K^{p}}}\underline{K^{c}_{p}},

where
K_{p}^{c}=\mathcal{P}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is the one defined by Definition
3.8
for
(P,K_{p})
.

Note that
Z(P)_{K}^{\overline{\ }}=Z(P)_{ac}(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}\cap K
as
K
is neat, the quotient map
K\to K_{p}^{c}
factors through
K/Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})_{K}^{\overline{\ }}K^{p}
.

In the situation where
(P,\mathcal{X})=(G,X)
is a Shimura datum, fix a cusp label representative
\Phi=(Q_{\Phi},X^{+}_{\Phi},g_{\Phi})\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
. Then, in this context, (
3.5
) defines a pro-étale torsor

\varprojlim_{K_{\Phi,p}^{\prime}\subset K_{\Phi,p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,p}^{\prime}K^{p}_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})

under
K_{\Phi}/Z(P_{\Phi})(\mathbb{Q})^{-}_{K_{\Phi}}K_{\Phi}^{p}
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}
be the smoothing of the closure of
P_{\Phi}
in
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
, where
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
is the conjugate of
\mathcal{G}
by
g=g_{\Phi,p}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, the
p
-factor of
g_{\Phi}
. Let
\mathcal{P}^{c}_{\Phi}
be the quasi-parahoric group scheme associated with
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}
.

From Definition
3.13
, we can construct a pro-étale torsor
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
under
\underline{\mathcal{P}^{c}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
. Note that we can further push out the torsor to
\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}
; we will in fact do this in the final step.

3.3.2.

Let us go back to the general setup.
Let
\rho:P(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to P^{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\operatorname{GL}(W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
be a finite-dimensional
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-representation. Since
K_{p}
is compact, there exists a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lattice
W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\subset W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
such that
\rho(\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))\subset\operatorname{GL}(W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
.

We construct
\mathbb{L}_{\rho,W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
as follows: Let

K_{p}^{(n)}=K_{p}\cap\rho^{-1}(\{g\in\operatorname{GL}(W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})|\ g\equiv\mathrm{id}\mod p^{n}\}).

We then have an étale
\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system
\mathbb{L}_{\rho,W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}},n}
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
defined as

(3.6)

\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}^{(n)}K^{p}}\times^{K_{p}/K_{p}^{(n)}}W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}/p^{n},

and we have

\mathbb{L}_{\rho,W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}=\varprojlim_{n}\mathbb{L}_{\rho,W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}},n},\quad\mathbb{L}_{\rho,W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}=\mathbb{L}_{\rho,W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}\otimes\mathbb{Q}.

We say
\mathbb{P}_{K}:=\mathbb{P}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})
is
de Rham
if, for any such pair
(\rho,W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
,
\mathbb{L}_{\rho,W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
is de Rham. It suffices to check this on a single faithful representation of
P^{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. This is independent of the choice of
W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\subset W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.

Proposition 3.14

(Liu-Zhu)
.

The pro-étale torsor
\mathbb{P}_{K}
under
\underline{\mathcal{P}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\mathrm{ad}}
is de Rham.

Proof.

By the rigidity theorem in
[
56
, Thm. 1.1]
, it suffices to show that, on every geometrically connected component of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
, there exists a closed point where the torsor is de Rham.
In
[
69
, Def. 11.5]
, for each morphism of mixed Shimura data
\iota:(T,Y)\to(P,\mathcal{X})
(which is also called a special point when
(T,Y)
is a pure Shimura datum such that
T
is a torus embedded into
P
) and for
K_{T}\subset T(\mathbb{A}_{f})
,
K\subset P(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that
\iota(K_{T})\subset K
, the induced morphism
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{T}}(T,Y)_{\mathbb{C}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})_{\mathbb{C}}
descends to a map between canonical models
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{T}}(T,Y)\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\times_{E(P,\mathcal{X})}E(T,Y)
. Then
[
56
, Lem. 4.4]
implies that
\mathbb{L}_{\rho,W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
is de Rham at special points. Note that, although the notion of special points for mixed Shimura varieties is slightly generalized in the sense that
(T,Y)
can be a finite cover of a usual Shimura datum, the argument in
loc. cit.
goes through verbatim since it only involves class field theory. Since we have a special point on each geometrically connected component by
[
69
, Lem. 11.6]
and the transitivity of the action of
P(\mathbb{A}_{f})
on geometrically connected components, the proposition follows.
∎

Replacing
P
by
\overline{P}
(resp. by
G_{h}
), we have a pro-étale torsor
\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{K}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}(\overline{P},\overline{\mathcal{X}})
(resp.
\mathbb{P}_{K_{h}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}}(G_{h},\mathcal{X}_{h})
) under
\underline{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
(resp.
\underline{\mathcal{G}_{h}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
). Since
P\to\overline{P}\to G_{h}
induces
P^{c}\to\overline{P}^{c}\to G_{h}^{c}
by Lemma
3.1
, it follows directly from the construction that we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}_{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}_{K_{h}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}(\overline{P},\overline{\mathcal{X}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}}(G_{h},\mathcal{X}_{h}).}}

Exactly the same argument as Proposition
3.14
shows that
\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{K}
and
\mathbb{P}_{K_{h}}
are also de Rham.

3.3.3.
Hodge-Tate period maps

Recall that the pro-étale torsor
\mathbb{P}_{K}
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})
under
\underline{\mathcal{P}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
is de Rham. Then there is a
\underline{\mathcal{P}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-equivariant Hodge-Tate period map

(3.7)

\mathrm{HT}_{K}:\mathbb{P}_{K}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{P^{c},\mu^{c,-1}}.

Here the superscript “
(-)^{c}
” means the projection to
P^{c}
. Similarly, we have

\mathrm{HT}_{K_{h}}:\mathbb{P}_{\overline{K}}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{\overline{P}^{c},\overline{\mu}^{c,-1}},\quad\mathrm{HT}_{K_{h}}:\mathbb{P}_{K_{h}}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{h}^{c},\mu_{h}^{c,-1}}.

Lemma 3.15
.

We have the following commutative diagram:

(3.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}_{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}_{K_{h}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{P^{c},\mu^{c,-1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{\overline{P}^{c},\overline{\mu}^{c,-1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{G_{h}^{c},\mu_{h}^{c,-1}}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{HT}_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{HT}_{\overline{K}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{HT}_{K_{h}}}

Proof.

We prove the commutativity of the left square, and the right one follows from the same reason.

By construction, the push-out torsor
\mathbb{P}_{K}^{\prime}:=\mathbb{P}_{K}\times^{\underline{\mathcal{P}^{c}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}}\underline{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{c}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
is the pullback of
\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{K}
along
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}(\overline{P},\overline{\mathcal{X}})
.
The Hodge-Tate period map of
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{K}
is given by the composition
\mathbb{P}_{K}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{P^{c},\mu^{c,-1}}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{\overline{P}^{c},\overline{\mu}^{c,-1}}
. Since the Hodge-Tate period maps are defined using the Hodge filtrations associated with the de Rham pro-étale torsors,
\mathbb{P}_{K}^{\prime}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{\overline{P}^{c},\overline{\mu}^{c,-1}}
factors through
\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{K}
.
∎

By
[
56
]
(cf. Proposition
3.14
) and
[
66
, §2.6]
, the de Rham local system
\mathbb{P}_{K}
(resp.
\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{K}
and resp.
\mathbb{P}_{K_{h}}
) canonically induces a shtuka in
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}^{c},\mu^{c},\operatorname{Spd}E}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sht}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{c},\overline{\mu}^{c},\operatorname{Spd}E}
and resp.
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{h}^{c},\mu_{h}^{c},\operatorname{Spd}E}
) on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})^{\Diamond}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}(\overline{P},\overline{\mathcal{X}})^{\Diamond}
and resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}}(G_{h},\mathcal{X}_{h})^{\Diamond}
).
Together with Corollary
1.47
, we know that:

Corollary 3.16
.

We have the following commutative diagram:

(3.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}(\overline{P},\overline{\mathcal{X}})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}}(G_{h},\mathcal{X}_{h})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{c},\bar{\mu}^{c},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{h}^{c},\mu_{h}^{c},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}.}}

3.3.4.
Some sections

Let
(P,\mathcal{X})
be a mixed Shimura datum,
1\to W\to P_{1}\to P\to 1
be an extension where
W
is a unipotent group. Assume there exists a mixed Shimura datum
(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})
such that
(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})/W\cong(P,\mathcal{X})
. Assume
P_{1}=W\rtimes P
, we fix a section
(P,\mathcal{X})\to(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})
(
[
69
, Prop. 2.17(b)]
). Let
K_{1}\subset P_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be a neat open compact subgroup such that
K_{1}=K_{W}\rtimes K
, where
K_{W}=K_{1}\cap W(\mathbb{A}_{f})\subset W(\mathbb{A}_{f})
,
K\subset P(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is the image of
K_{1}
, then the natural projection
p_{K}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})
has a section
e_{K}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})
. Given
K^{\prime}\subset K
such that
K_{1}^{\prime}=K_{W}^{\prime}\rtimes K^{\prime}
, then
e_{K^{\prime}}
and
p_{K^{\prime}}
are compatible with
e_{K}
and
p_{K}
respectively. Passing to
p^{\infty}
-limit, we then have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}_{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}_{K_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}_{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X}).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{K}}

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a quasi-parahoric model of
P
, and let
\mathcal{W}
be a smooth affine model of
W
with connected fibers such that
\mathcal{P}
normalizes
\mathcal{W}
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{1}=\mathcal{W}\rtimes\mathcal{P}
,
K_{W}=\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
,
K_{1}=\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, and
K=\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
; then
K_{1}=K_{W}\rtimes K
. The fixed section
(P,\mathcal{X})\to(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})
also fixes a section of the conjugacy class of Hodge cocharacters
\{\mu\}\to\{\mu_{1}\}
. Define
\mathcal{P}^{c}
,
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{c}
as in Definition
3.8
; then, by Lemma
3.1
,
P_{1}^{c}=W\rtimes P^{c}
, and
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{c}=\mathcal{W}\rtimes\mathcal{P}^{c}
.

Lemma 3.17
.

We have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}_{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}_{K_{1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}_{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{P^{c},\mu^{c,-1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{1}^{c},\mu_{1}^{c,-1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{P^{c},\mu^{c,-1}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{HT}_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{HT}_{K_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{HT}_{K}}

Proof.

The right diagram commutes due to Lemma
3.15
. For the left diagram, fix a representation
\rho:P^{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to P^{c}_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho_{1}}}{{\to}}\operatorname{GL}(W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
and a lattice
W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\subset W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
stabilized by
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. The induced local system
\rho_{*}\mathbb{P}_{K}
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})
is the pullback of the induced local system
\rho_{1*}\mathbb{P}_{K_{1}}
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})
; thus, the Hodge-Tate period maps commute.
∎

Lemma 3.18
.

We have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}^{c},\mu^{c}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{c},\mu_{1}^{c}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}^{c},\mu^{c}}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{K}}

We can apply this result to
(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\to(\overline{P}_{\Phi},\overline{D}_{\Phi})
and to
(\overline{P}_{\Phi},\overline{D}_{\Phi})\to(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})
.

Lemma 3.19
.

Assume
K_{\Phi}=(K_{\Phi}\cap U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\rtimes\overline{K}_{\Phi}
and
\overline{K}_{\Phi}=(\overline{K}_{\Phi}\cap V_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\rtimes K_{\Phi,h}
. Then the reduction (
3.9
) admits a section:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}(\overline{P}_{\Phi},\overline{D}_{\Phi})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{c},\mu_{\Phi}^{c},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\Phi}^{c},\bar{\mu}_{\Phi}^{c},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{c},\mu_{\Phi,h}^{c},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}.}}

3.4.
Torus action on the tower

We re-organize the materials in
[
69
, 3.13-3.16]
and
[
58
, 2.1.11]
, and describe the torus action in an explicit and functorial way. This exposition is helpful in §
3.4.3
, where we compute the torus action on a tower of mixed Shimura varieties.

Throughout the subsection, we pick any mixed Shimura datum
(P,\mathcal{X})
and denote its reflex field by
\mathbb{E}(P,\mathcal{X})
; the symbol
E
denotes its completion at a place
v
over
p
. Sometimes we use the shorthand
\mathbb{E}:=\mathbb{E}(P,\mathcal{X})
.

3.4.1.

Let
U\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}^{n}
be a connected additive group over
\mathbb{Q}
. The group
\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts on
U
by scalar multiplication.
Denote by
(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{H}_{0})
the “Siegel Shimura datum of dimension zero”, where
\mathcal{H}_{0}=\{\pm\mathrm{id}\}
is the two-point set parameterizing the isomorphisms
\mathbb{Z}\cong\mathbb{Z}(1)
.
Let
P_{0}=U\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}
, where
\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}
acts on
U
by this scalar multiplication. We can write
P_{0}
as a blocked mirabolic subgroup

\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{G}_{m}&U\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}.

In what follows, denote
U:=\begin{pmatrix}1&U\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
by abuse of notation.

Let
X_{0}
be
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{H}_{0})\times

(P_{0}(\mathbb{R})U(\mathbb{C})\text{-orbit of the homomorphism }(z_{1},z_{2})\in\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{C})\mapsto\mathrm{diag}\{z_{1}z_{2},1\}\in P_{0}(\mathbb{C})).

Then
(P_{0},X_{0})
is a mixed Shimura datum with a natural projection
\lambda_{0}:(P_{0},X_{0})\to(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{H}_{0})
obtained by the quotient by
U
. See also
[
69
, 2.24]
.

Let
K_{0}
be a neat open compact subgroup of
P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.
By
[
69
, 3.13]
,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}(P_{0},X_{0})(\mathbb{C})
is a torus torsor under
\mathbf{E}_{K_{0}}(\mathbb{C})
, where
\mathbf{E}_{K_{0}}(\mathbb{C})\cong U(\mathbb{C})/\Lambda_{0}
for
\Lambda_{0}:=(U(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{0})(-1)
. More precisely, fix any
p\in P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, the fiber of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}(P_{0},X_{0})(\mathbb{C})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\lambda_{0}(K_{0})}(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{H}_{0})(\mathbb{C}):=\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{>0}\backslash\{1\}\times\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\times}/K_{0}
at
[(1,\lambda_{0}(p))]
determined by
\lambda_{0}(p)\in\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is
U(\mathbb{C})/(U(\mathbb{Q})\cap pK_{0}p^{-1})(-1)
. Note that
U(\mathbb{Q})\cap pK_{0}p^{-1}
and
U(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{0}
differ by homothety; let us explain this in the next paragraph.

Recall that there is a unique factorization of multiplicative groups
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\times}=\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{>0}\times\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}
. For
g\in\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\times}
, denote by
r(g)
the
\mathbb{Q}^{\times}_{>0}
-factor. Denote

r(p):=r\circ\lambda_{0}(p)=\begin{pmatrix}r(p)&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}.

Denote
\Lambda_{0,\mathbb{A}_{f}}:=U(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap K_{0}
. Writing

(3.10)

\mathbf{E}_{K_{0}}(\mathbb{C})\cong U(\mathbb{Q})\backslash U(\mathbb{C})\times U(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\Lambda_{0,\mathbb{A}_{f}},

the homothety is given by the left conjugate by
r(p)
:

\mathbf{t}(p):U(\mathbb{Q})\backslash U(\mathbb{C})\times U(\mathbb{A}_{f})/\Lambda_{0,\mathbb{A}_{f}}\xrightarrow{\sim}U(\mathbb{Q})\backslash U(\mathbb{C})\times U(\mathbb{A}_{f})/p\Lambda_{0,\mathbb{A}_{f}}p^{-1},

sending
[(u,u_{f})]_{\Lambda_{0,\mathbb{A}_{f}}}\mapsto[(r(p)ur(p)^{-1},r(p)u_{f}r(p)^{-1})]_{r(p)\Lambda_{0,\mathbb{A}_{f}}r(p)^{-1}}.
Note that
r(p)\Lambda_{0,\mathbb{A}_{f}}r(p)^{-1}={p\Lambda_{0,\mathbb{A}_{f}}p^{-1}}
. This isomorphism between the affine complex tori canonically descends to an isomorphism between the algebraic tori.

Define an action

\mathbb{E}_{K_{0}}(P_{0},X_{0})(\mathbb{C}):\mathbf{E}_{K_{0}}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}(P_{0},X_{0})(\mathbb{C})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}(P_{0},X_{0})(\mathbb{C})

as follows:

Fix an isomorphism
\mathrm{sgn}:\mathcal{H}_{0}\cong\{\pm 1\}
. Let
\delta(x)=\mathrm{diag}\{\mathrm{sgn}(x),1\}
for
x\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
. Picking any
p\in P_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, set
[(u,u_{f})]_{\Lambda_{0,\mathbb{A}_{f}}}\in\mathbf{E}_{K_{0}}(\mathbb{C})\mapsto

(3.11)

([(x,p)]_{K_{0}}\mapsto[(\mathrm{int}(\delta(x)r(p)ur(p)^{-1}\delta(x)^{-1})x,\delta(x)r(p)u_{f}r(p)^{-1}\delta(x)^{-1}p)]_{K_{0}}).

Lemma 3.20
.

The map
\mathbb{E}_{K_{0}}(P_{0},X_{0})(\mathbb{C})
is well defined and algebraic. Moreover, it descends to
\mathbb{Q}
.

Proof.

Fix any
u_{0}\in\Lambda_{0,\mathbb{A}_{f}}
. There is
u_{0}^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{0,\mathbb{A}_{f}}
such that
pu_{0}^{\prime}p^{-1}=r(p)u_{0}r(p)^{-1}
, because the conjugation of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}
and
U(\mathbb{A}_{f})
stabilizes
\Lambda_{0,\mathbb{A}_{f}}
. Assume that
\mathrm{sgn}(x)=1
without loss of generality. Then the assignment
\mathbb{E}_{K_{0}}(P_{0},X_{0})(\mathbb{C})(u,u_{f}\cdot u_{0})
sends
[(x,p)]_{K_{0}}
to

\begin{split}&[(\mathrm{int}(r(p)ur(p)^{-1})x,r(p)u_{f}u_{0}r(p)^{-1}\cdot p)]_{K_{0}}\\
&=[(\mathrm{int}(r(p)ur(p)^{-1})x,r(p)u_{f}r(p)^{-1}r(p)u_{0}r(p)^{-1}\cdot p)]_{K_{0}}\\
&=[(\mathrm{int}(r(p)ur(p)^{-1})x,r(p)u_{f}r(p)^{-1}pu_{0}^{\prime}p^{-1}\cdot p)]_{K_{0}}\\
&=[(\mathrm{int}(r(p)ur(p)^{-1})x,r(p)u_{f}r(p)^{-1}\cdot p)]_{K_{0}}.\end{split}

It is easy to check that this map does not change when multiplying
(u,u_{f})
to
(u^{\prime}u,u^{\prime}u_{f})
by
u^{\prime}\in U(\mathbb{Q})
, and that the compatibility of this map with the multiplication on
\mathbf{E}_{K_{0}}
. So this is a well-defined action. Since
\mathbf{t}(p)
is algebraic and since it suffices to check the algebraicity on each individual connected component, we find that
\mathbb{E}_{K_{0}}(P_{0},X_{0})(\mathbb{C})
is algebraic.

Now we consider the Galois action. It suffices to check on a dense subset. So we may assume that
u\in U(\mathbb{Q})
,
u_{f}=1
by strong approximation, and
[(x,p)]_{K}
is contained in a special point
(T,\mathcal{Y})\subset(P_{0},X_{0})
such that

\mathcal{Y}\subset\pi_{0}(\mathcal{H}_{0})\times P_{0}(\mathbb{Q})\text{-orbit of the homomorphism }h_{0}:(z_{1},z_{2})\in\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{C})\mapsto\mathrm{diag}\{z_{1}z_{2},1\}\in P_{0}(\mathbb{C}).

Then, by taking a conjugation in
P_{0}(\mathbb{Q})
, we may consider the case where
T=\mathrm{diag}\{\mathbb{G}_{m},1\}
and
\mathcal{Y}=\{\pm h_{0}\}
.
Again, we assume that
\mathrm{sgn}(x)=1
and the case of
x=-1
is similar.
Now, for
\tau\in\mathrm{Gal}(\mathbb{Q}^{ab}/\mathbb{Q})
, we denote by
[(x,p)]\mapsto[(x,r_{(T,\mathcal{Y})}(\tau)p)]
the Galois action on the points of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{T}}(T,\mathcal{Y})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
for a suitable
K_{T}
. Write
d:=r_{(T,\mathcal{Y})}(\tau)\in\pi_{0}(\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{A})/\mathbb{G}_{m}(\mathbb{Q}))
; we choose a representative of it in
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}
. Writing
u=\begin{pmatrix}1&\underline{u}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
, we check that
\tau\cdot[(u,1)]\cdot[(x,p)]

(3.12)

\begin{split}&=\tau\cdot[(\mathrm{int}(r(p)ur(p)^{-1})x,p)]\\
&=\tau\cdot[(x,r(p)u^{-1}r(p)^{-1}\cdot p)]\\
&=\tau\cdot[(x,\begin{pmatrix}p&-r(p)\underline{u}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix})]\\
&=[(x,\begin{pmatrix}p&-r(p)\underline{u}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}d&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix})]\\
&=[(x,\begin{pmatrix}dp&-r(p)\underline{u}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix})],\end{split}

and that
[(u,1)]\cdot\tau\cdot[(x,p)]

(3.13)

\begin{split}&=[(u,1)]\cdot[(x,d\cdot p)]\\
&=[(\mathrm{int}(r(d)r(p)ur(p)^{-1}r(d)^{-1})x,d\cdot p)]\\
&=[(x,r(d)r(p)u^{-1}r(p)^{-1}r(d)^{-1}dp)]\\
&=[(x,r(p)u^{-1}r(p)^{-1}dp)]\\
&=[(x,\begin{pmatrix}dp&-r(p)\underline{u}\\
0&1\end{pmatrix})].\end{split}

Hence, we see that (
3.12
)
=
(
3.13
). Note that the multiplication of
d
induced by the Galois action in (
3.12
) is on the right because one can check that this is the only way such that the Galois action determining the canonical model is compatible with right Hecke actions.
By density, the Galois action commutes with the action of
\mathbf{E}_{K_{0}}(\mathbb{C})
and
\mathbb{E}_{K_{0}}(P_{0},X_{0})(\mathbb{C})
descends to
\mathbb{Q}
.
∎

Denote the algebraic action over
\mathbb{Q}
by
\mathbb{E}_{K_{0}}(P_{0},X_{0})
.

Convention 3.21
.

Note that an isomorphism
\mathrm{sgn}
is fixed. If we change
\mathrm{sgn}
to
-\mathrm{sgn}
, the
\mathbf{E}_{K_{0}}
-action in (
3.11
) will be reversed. Moreover, the isomorphism (
3.10
) is induced by an exponential map, which also depends on a choice of
\sqrt{-1}
, and here we fixed
\mathbb{C}
and chose
\sqrt{-1}
to be
i\in\mathbb{C}
. Nevertheless, the torsor structure given by (
3.11
) is canonical up to unique isomorphism.

From the explicit construction above, we see that

Lemma 3.22
.

The morphism
\mathbb{E}_{K_{0}}(P_{0},X_{0})
is functorial in
(U,K_{0})
. That is, for any homomorphism
f:U_{1}\to U_{2}
between additive groups as in the beginning of §
3.4.1
, we produce
(P_{0}^{1},X_{0}^{1})
(resp.
(P_{0}^{2},X_{0}^{2})
) for
U_{1}
(resp.
U_{2}
) as in the first paragraph. Then there is a morphism between mixed Shimura data
\widetilde{f}:(P_{0}^{1},X_{0}^{1})\to(P_{0}^{2},X_{0}^{2})
induced by
f
. Picking neat open compact subgroups
K_{0}^{1}
and
K_{0}^{2}
such that
\widetilde{f}(K^{1}_{0})\subset K^{2}_{0}
, we obtain a morphism between mixed Shimura varieties
\widetilde{f}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{1}_{0}}(P_{0}^{1},X_{0}^{1})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{2}_{0}}(P_{0}^{2},X_{0}^{2})
over
\mathbb{Q}
that fits into the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}_{K_{0}^{1}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{1}}(P_{0}^{1},X_{0}^{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{1}}(P_{0}^{1},X_{0}^{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}_{K_{0}^{2}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{2}}(P_{0}^{2},X_{0}^{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}^{2}}(P_{0}^{2},X_{0}^{2}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K_{0}^{1}}(P_{0}^{1},X_{0}^{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[f]\times\widetilde{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K_{0}^{2}}(P_{0}^{2},X_{0}^{2})}

The map
[f]
of the homomorphism between tori is induced by
f
and exponential maps.

Proof.

This follows from the explicit formula (
3.11
) above.
∎

3.4.2.

Now, we consider a mixed Shimura datum
(P,\mathcal{X})
. There is a weight filtration on
\lie P
given by the definition of
(P,\mathcal{X})
being a mixed Shimura datum.
Let
U
be the unipotent group whose Lie algebra corresponds to the weight-(
-2
)-part of
\lie P
and
W
be the unipotent radical of
P
. Recall that the adjoint representation of
P
on
\lie U\cong U
factors through
G_{h}:=P/W
.

For our purpose, we assume that this action factors as

\mathrm{Ad}:P\xrightarrow{\lambda}\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathrm{GL}(U),

where
\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts as scalar multiplication.

As in §
3.4.1
, we define
r(p)=r\circ\lambda(p)
for
p\in P(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

Moreover, we can extend
\lambda
to a morphism between mixed Shimura data
\lambda:(P,\mathcal{X})\to(\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}},\mathcal{H}_{0})
; picking any connected component
\mathcal{X}^{+}\subset\mathcal{X}
, this extension depends on the choice of
\mathcal{X}^{+}\to\mathcal{H}_{0}
, which has two options.

By
[
69
, 3.13]
, for any neat open compact subgroup
K\subset P(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, the morphism
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})(\mathbb{C})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}(\overline{P},\overline{\mathcal{X}})
is a torus torsor under
\mathbf{E}_{K}(\mathbb{C}):=U(\mathbb{C})/\Lambda
. Here
\Lambda=[p_{2}(Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{\circ}\times U(\mathbb{Q})\cap K)](-1)
, where
Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{\circ}
is the centralizer in
Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})
of
\mathcal{X}
.

As (
3.11
), we define the action
\mathbb{E}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})(\mathbb{C})
of
[(u,u_{f})]_{\Lambda_{\mathbb{A}_{f}}}
:

(3.14)

([(x,p)]_{K}\mapsto[(\mathrm{int}(\delta(x)r(p)ur(p)^{-1}\delta(x)^{-1})x,\delta(x)r(p)u_{f}r(p)^{-1}\delta(x)^{-1}p)]_{K}).

Lemma 3.23
.

The action (
3.14
) is well defined.

We can write the action as a
4
-step composition:

(3.15)

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})(\mathbb{C}):\mathbf{E}_{K}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})(\mathbb{C})=\\
&\mathbf{E}_{K}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{\lambda(K)}(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{H}_{0})(\mathbb{C})\times_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\lambda(K)}(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{H}_{0})(\mathbb{C})}\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})(\mathbb{C})\to\\
&\mathbf{E}_{K}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}(P_{0},X_{0})(\mathbb{C})\times_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\lambda(K)}(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{H}_{0})(\mathbb{C})}\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})(\mathbb{C})\xrightarrow{\ \mathbb{E}_{K_{0}}(P_{0},X_{0})(\mathbb{C})\ }\\
&\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{0}}(P_{0},X_{0})(\mathbb{C})\times_{\mathrm{Sh}_{\lambda(K)}(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{H}_{0})(\mathbb{C})}\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})(\mathbb{C})\xrightarrow{\ m_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})(\mathbb{C})\ }\\
&\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})(\mathbb{C}).\end{split}

Here,
(P_{0},X_{0})
is the one defined by
U
in §
3.4.1
and
K_{0}=[p_{2}(Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{\circ}\times U(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap K)]\rtimes\lambda(K)
. The first equality is from the definition of fiber products, the second arrow is induced by the diagonal section from
(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{H}_{0})
to
(P_{0},X_{0})
by construction, and the last arrow is given by the multiplication operation in
[
69
, 2.22]
(in which it was denoted by “
\mu
”).

Moreover, the composition
\mathbb{E}_{K}(P,X)(\mathbb{C})
in (
3.15
) descends to an algebraic morphism over
\mathbb{E}(P,\mathcal{X})
.

Proof.

The first paragraph is verified exactly the same way as Lemma
3.20
. The second paragraph is proved in
[
69
, 2.22 and Cor. 3.12]
, combined with Lemma
3.20
. The third paragraph then follows from the second sentence of Lemma
3.20
, the functoriality of canonical models of mixed Shimura varieties induced by morphisms between mixed Shimura data
[
69
, Ch. 11]
and the identification
(P_{0},X_{0})\times_{(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{H}_{0})}(P,\mathcal{X})=(U\rtimes P,\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
for some
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
.
∎

We denote the algebraic morphism above by
\mathbb{E}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})
.

Lemma 3.24
.

The formalism of the action
\mathbb{E}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})
is functorial in
(P,\mathcal{X},K)
.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that all steps in the composition (
3.15
) in the second paragraph of Lemma
3.23
are functorial. See Lemma
3.22
and
[
69
, Ch. 11]
.
∎

Remark 3.25
.

As mentioned above, changing the choice of
\mathcal{X}^{+}\to\mathcal{H}_{0}
will change
\mathbb{E}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})
to
\mathbb{E}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\circ((-\mathrm{id})\times\mathrm{id})
, but the
\mathbf{E}_{K}
-torsor structure of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})
is canonical up to this isomorphism.

3.4.3.

Fix a choice of
\mathcal{X}^{+}\to\mathcal{H}_{0}
.
For any pair of neat open compact subgroups
K^{\prime}\subset K
, we have a commutative diagram over the reflex field
\mathbb{E}
by Lemma
3.24
:

(3.16)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{K^{\prime}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{K}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X}).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K^{\prime}}(P,\mathcal{X})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}

Assume that
K=K_{p}K^{p}
. Taking the limit over all
K^{\prime}=K_{p}^{\prime}K^{p}\subset K
, we denote
\mathbf{E}_{\infty,K}:=\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathbf{E}_{K^{\prime}}
. Note that the Galois group of
\mathbf{E}_{\infty,K,E}
over
\mathbf{E}_{K,E}
is

\mathscr{G}_{p}(\mathbf{E}_{K}):=\varprojlim_{K^{\prime}_{p}\subset K_{p}}\frac{p_{2}(Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{\circ}\times U(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{p}K^{p})}{p_{2}(Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{\circ}\times U(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{p}^{\prime}K^{p})}.

When
P=P^{c}
, from the neatness of
K
, the group
\mathscr{G}_{p}(\mathbf{E}_{K})=

\varprojlim_{K^{\prime}_{p}\subset K_{p}}\frac{U(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{p}K^{p}}{U(\mathbb{Q})\cap K_{p}^{\prime}K^{p}}=\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\frac{(U(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap K_{p}K^{p})\cap U(\mathbb{Q})}{(U(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap K_{p}^{\prime}K^{p})\cap U(\mathbb{Q})}.

The group
U
is unipotent, and
U(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap K_{p}K^{p}=(U(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap K_{p})(U(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\cap K^{p})
. Therefore, the group
\mathscr{G}_{p}(\mathbf{E}_{K})
is pro-
p
, and
\mathbf{E}_{\infty,K}
is the inverse limit of
\cdots\xrightarrow{p}\mathbf{E}_{K}\xrightarrow{p}\mathbf{E}_{K}\xrightarrow{p}\mathbf{E}_{K}
. Taking the inverse limit of (
3.16
) over
K^{\prime}=K_{p}^{\prime}K^{p}
, we have

(3.17)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}_{\infty,K}\times\mathbb{P}_{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}_{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}_{K}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K^{p}}(P,\mathcal{X})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}

where
\mathbb{E}_{K^{p}}(P,\mathcal{X})=\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathbb{E}_{K^{\prime}}(P,\mathcal{X})
.

Now, we consider a general
P
. Let
K_{p}^{c}
be defined as in Definition
3.8
, and let
K^{c,p}
be the image of
K^{p}
in
P^{c}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.
Note that
K_{p}^{c}
is open compact and
K^{c,p}
is neat open compact.

By Lemma
3.24
, there is a commutative diagram induced by the morphism
(P,\mathcal{X},K)\to(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c},K^{c})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{K}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{K^{c}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c}).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K^{c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}

Lemma 3.26
.

Taking the inverse limit with respect to all open compact subgroups
K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}
, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{\infty,K}\times\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{K}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{\infty,K^{c}}\times\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime,c}\subset K_{p}^{c}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime,c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime,c}\subset K_{p}^{c}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime,c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{K^{c}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K^{p}}(P,\mathcal{X})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K^{c,p}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K^{c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}

Moreover, the commutative diagram

(3.18)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{\infty,K}\times\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{\infty,K^{c}}\times\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime,c}\subset K_{p}^{c}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime,c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime,c}\subset K_{p}^{c}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime,c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K^{p}}(P,\mathcal{X})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K^{c,p}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}

is equivariant under the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{1\}\times K/Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}_{K}K^{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{K/Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}_{K}K^{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{1\}\times K^{c}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{c}_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

over

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{K}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{K^{c}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K^{c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}

Proof.

The first diagram follows from Lemma
3.24
.
Let us show the equivariance of (
3.18
). From the functoriality between mixed Shimura varieties, the two vertical arrows are
(K/Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}_{K}K^{p}\to K_{p}^{c})
-equivariant.

We check the
(K/Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})_{K}^{\overline{\ }}K^{p}\to K/Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})_{K}^{\overline{\ }}K^{p})
-equivariance of
\mathbb{E}_{K^{p}}(P,\mathcal{X})
. Fix
K^{\prime}_{p}\subset K_{p}
and assume that it is a normal subgroup. It suffices to check this over
\mathbb{C}
.
Pick
[(u,1)]\in\mathbf{E}_{K^{\prime}}
and
k\in K_{p}
. On the one hand, we compute that

[(u,1)]\cdot k\cdot[(x,p)]_{K^{\prime}}=[(u,1)]\cdot[(x,pk)]_{K^{\prime}}=[(x,\mathrm{int}(\delta(x)r(pk))(u^{-1})pk)]_{K^{\prime}};

and, on the other hand,
k\cdot[(u,1)]\cdot[(x,p)]_{K^{\prime}}=[(x,\mathrm{int}(\delta(x)r(p))(u^{-1})pk)]_{K^{\prime}}
. The two expressions are the same since
r(k)=1
.
∎

Lemma 3.27
.

With the conventions in Lemma
3.26
, the pushout

\mathbb{P}_{K}:=\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}(P,\mathcal{X})\times^{\underline{K/Z(P)(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}_{K}K^{p}}}\underline{K_{p}^{c}}

is the pullback of
\mathbb{P}_{K^{c}}
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})
.
The action
\mathbb{E}_{K^{p}}(P,\mathcal{X})
induces a
(\mathbf{E}_{\infty,K}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{E}_{K})
-equivariant action
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{K^{p}}(P,\mathcal{X})
on
\mathbb{P}_{K}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})
such that
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty,K}
is an inverse limit of
p
-power covers of
\mathbf{E}_{K}
, i.e.,
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty,K}=\varprojlim_{i\geq 0}\mathbf{E}_{K}^{(i)}
, where
\mathbf{E}_{K}^{(0)}=\mathbf{E}_{K}
and
\mathbf{E}_{K}^{(i)}\to\mathbf{E}_{K}
are
p
-power isogenies between tori for positive integers
i
.

In other words, there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{\infty,K}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{P}_{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}_{K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{K}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X}).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K^{p}}^{\prime}(P,\mathcal{X})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})}

Proof.

The first sentence follows from the equivariance statement in Lemma
3.26
.
Fix an open compact normal subgroup
K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}
. Consider the pullback of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{K^{\prime,c}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime,c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime,c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{K^{c}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K^{\prime,c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{K^{c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})}

along
\mathbf{E}_{K}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\to\mathbf{E}_{K^{c}}\times\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})
. It suffices to show that the connected component of the fiber product
\mathbf{E}_{K,\mathbb{E}}\times_{\mathbf{E}_{K^{c},\mathbb{E}}}\mathbf{E}_{K^{\prime,c},\mathbb{E}}
is a torus
\mathbf{E}
such that the morphism
\mathbf{E}\to\mathbf{E}_{K,\mathbb{E}}
induced by the projection to the first factor of this fiber product is a
p
-power isogeny. This is Lemma
3.28
.
∎

Lemma 3.28
.

Let
\mathbf{E}_{0}
,
\mathbf{E}_{1}
and
\mathbf{E}_{2}
be split tori of the same rank over a field
k
of characteristic zero. Let
f:\mathbf{E}_{1}\to\mathbf{E}_{0}
and
g:\mathbf{E}_{2}\to\mathbf{E}_{0}
be isogenies between tori. Assume that
g
is a
p
-power isogeny. Then the identity component
\mathbf{E}_{3}
of
\mathbf{E}_{1}\times_{f,\mathbf{E}_{0},g}\mathbf{E}_{2}
is a split torus equipped with a
p
-power isogeny
\mathbf{E}_{3}\to\mathbf{E}_{1}
.

Proof.

This fiber product is smooth, so irreducible components are exactly the connected components. If we know that
\mathbf{E}_{3}
is a split torus, then
\mathbf{E}_{3}\to\mathbf{E}_{1}
is a
p
-power isogeny by construction.
We show that
\mathbf{E}_{3}
is a split torus.
Write the character groups of
\mathbf{E}_{i}
by
X_{i}
for
i=0,1,2
. Then there are inclusions
X_{0}\subset X_{1}\subset X_{0}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
and
X_{0}\subset X_{2}\subset X_{0}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. Let
X^{\prime}=X_{1}\cap X_{2}\supset X_{0}
be the intersection of the lattices
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
in
X_{0}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. Let
X
be the abelian group generated by
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
in
X_{0}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
.

We have a map
X_{1}\oplus_{X^{\prime}}X_{2}\to X
given by addition in
X_{0}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
. This is surjective by the definition of
X
and is injective by the definition of
X^{\prime}
. Hence, we see that
\mathbf{E}:=\spec k[X]\to\spec k[X_{1}]\times_{\spec k[X^{\prime}]}\spec k[X_{2}]
is an isomorphism. On the other hand, the map
\mathbf{E}\to\spec k[X_{1}]\times_{\spec k[X_{0}]}\spec k[X_{2}]
is a closed embedding by the separatedness of
\spec k[X^{\prime}]\to\spec k[X_{0}]
, and is a morphism between varieties of the same dimension. We then conclude that
\mathbf{E}\cong\mathbf{E}_{3}:=(\mathbf{E}_{1}\times_{\mathbf{E}_{0}}\mathbf{E}_{2})^{\circ}
.
∎

3.4.4.
Action on shtukas

Now, we work over
E
, the completion of
\mathbb{E}
as the beginning of this subsection.
Let
\mathcal{P}
be a quasi-parahoric model of
P
and
K_{p}=\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Fix a neat level
K^{p}\subset P(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and write
K=K_{p}K^{p}
. Let
E_{K}=\mathbf{E}_{K}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and
E_{\infty,K}^{\prime}=\varprojlim(\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}E_{K}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}E_{K})
.

Note that
(E^{\prime}_{\infty,K})^{\Diamond}
is represented by a perfectoid space in the sense that, when base-changed to
\operatorname{Spa}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
,
(E_{\infty,K}^{\prime})^{\Diamond}_{C}
is the
\Diamond
of
E_{\infty,K,C}^{\prime}\sim\varprojlim(\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}E_{K,C}^{\mathrm{ad}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\to}}E_{K,C}^{\mathrm{ad}})
; see
[
10
, Lem. 2.14]
.

Let
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
be the
\mathcal{P}^{c}
-shtuka on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})^{\Diamond}
associated with
(\mathbb{P}_{K},\mathrm{HT}_{K})
. Let
F
be a finite field extension of
E
or
\breve{E}
, and
C
be a non-archimedean perfectoid field containing
F
. Given
\gamma\in E_{K}(F)
, by taking
p^{\infty}
-roots in
C
, we can lift
\gamma
to some
\tilde{\gamma}\in E_{\infty,K}^{\prime}(C)
.

Lemma 3.29
.

\tilde{\gamma}
induces an isomorphism of
\mathcal{P}^{c}
-shtukas
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
and
\gamma^{*}(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})_{F}
.

Proof.

In Lemma
3.27
, we see that the
\tilde{\gamma}
-action on
\mathbb{P}_{K,C}
is equivariant with the
\gamma
-action on
