\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})_{C}
. This gives an isomorphism of pro-étale torsors
\tilde{\gamma}_{*}:\mathbb{P}_{K,C}\to\gamma^{*}\mathbb{P}_{K,C}
. We need to show that
\tilde{\gamma}_{*}
is compatible with the Hodge–Tate period maps (i.e.
\mathrm{HT}_{K}(\tilde{\gamma}(x))=\mathrm{HT}_{K}(x)
for any
x\in\mathbb{P}_{K,C}
). The action
E_{\infty,K,C}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{P}_{K,C}\to\mathbb{P}_{K,C}
is the base change of
E_{\infty,K}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{P}_{K}\to\mathbb{P}_{K}
. Since the action of elements in
E_{\infty,K}^{\prime}
is compatible with the Hodge–Tate period maps (see the proof of Proposition
5.8
),
\tilde{\gamma}
is compatible with the Hodge–Tate period maps.
∎

Remark 3.30
.

We do not have a canonical lifting of
\gamma\in E_{K}
to
E_{\infty,K}^{\prime}
, and there is no equivariant action of
E_{K}
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}^{c},\mu^{c}}
. Thus, the
\mathcal{P}^{c}
-shtuka on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})
does not descend to
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}(\overline{P},\overline{\mathcal{X}})
. The reader can compare this situation with Proposition
5.8
later, where the action induces descent data.

3.5.
Abelian scheme action on the tower

We focus on the abelian-scheme torsor
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}(\overline{P},\overline{\mathcal{X}})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}}(G_{h},\mathcal{X}_{h})
. Here
G_{h}:=P/W=\overline{P}/V
,
K_{h}=\pi(\overline{K})
, and
\pi:\overline{P}\to G_{h}
is the projection.

Recall that the fiber of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}(\mathbb{C})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}}(\mathbb{C})
at
(x,q)\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}}(\mathbb{C})
can be computed explicitly (see
[
69
, §3.13]
). Let
e:(G_{h},X_{h})\to(\overline{P},\overline{\mathcal{X}})
be a splitting. Then the map
[w]\mapsto[w\cdot e(x),q]
gives an isomorphism from the fiber over
(x,q)
to
\Gamma_{\overline{K}}\backslash V(\mathbb{R})
, where
\Gamma_{\overline{K}}
is the image of
(\{z\in Z(\overline{P})(\mathbb{Q})|\ z|_{\overline{\mathcal{X}}}=\mathrm{id}\}\rtimes V(\mathbb{Q}))\cap q\overline{K}q^{-1}
under the projection
Z(\overline{P})\times V\to V
. When we fix the away-from-
p
part, the embedding
\Gamma_{\overline{K}(p^{n})}\to\Gamma_{\overline{K}(p^{m})}
(
m\leq n
) has
p
-torsion cokernel and gives the projection

(3.19)

p_{n,m}:\Gamma_{\overline{K}(p^{n})}\backslash V(\mathbb{R})\to\Gamma_{\overline{K}(p^{m})}\backslash V(\mathbb{R}).

By
[
69
, §3.22]
,
\Gamma_{\overline{K}}\backslash V(\mathbb{R})=\Gamma_{\overline{K}}\backslash V(\mathbb{C})/\exp(\mathrm{Fil}^{0}(\operatorname{Lie}(V))_{\mathbb{C}})
is an abelian variety, and (
3.19
) is a
p
-isogeny of abelian varieties. This picture descends over the reflex field.

Let
K_{V}
be the image of
(\{z\in Z(\overline{P})(\mathbb{Q})|\ z|_{\overline{\mathcal{X}}}=\mathrm{id}\}\rtimes V(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\cap\overline{K}
under the projection
Z(\overline{P})\times V\to V
, and let
K_{h}^{*}\subset G_{h}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be an open compact subgroup that contains
K_{h}
and normalizes
K_{V}
; let
\widetilde{K}^{*}=K_{V}\rtimes K_{h}^{*}
. By
[
69
, Cor. 3.12]
and
[
69
, §10]
,
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}}
is canonically a torsor under the family of abelian varieties
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{*}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}^{*}}
.

To simplify the calculation, we assume that
K_{h}
normalizes
K_{V}
(and we can, and will, always do this in later sections), and take
K_{h}^{*}=K_{h}
. In particular, for any pair of neat subgroups
\overline{K}^{\prime}\subset\overline{K}
such that
K_{h}^{\prime}
normalizes
K_{V}^{\prime}
, we have a commutative diagram over the reflex field:

(3.20)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{\prime}}\times_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}^{\prime}}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}}\times_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\overline{K}}}

For our purpose, we directly work over
E
rather than
\mathbb{E}
. We fix a system of normal subgroups
\overline{K}^{\prime}\subset\overline{K}
such that
\overline{K}^{\prime p}=\overline{K}^{p}
; then
\widetilde{K}^{\prime}\subset\widetilde{K}
are normal and
\widetilde{K}^{\prime p}=\widetilde{K}^{p}
. Fix
x:=x_{\overline{K}}\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}}(F)
, where
F
is a finite field extension of
E
or
\breve{E}
. Lift
x
to
\tilde{x}\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}^{p}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}):=\varprojlim_{K_{h,p}^{\prime}\subset K_{h,p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h,p}^{\prime}K_{h}^{p}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, and let
x_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}^{\prime}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
be its image. Denote the abelian scheme
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}^{\prime}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}^{\prime}}
by
A_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}^{\prime}}
, the fiber at
x_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}
by
A_{x_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}}
, and
A_{x_{\overline{K}}}
by
A_{x}
. Also denote the fiber of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}^{\prime}}
at
x_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}
by
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{\prime},x_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}}
, and
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K},x_{\overline{K}}}
by
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K},x}
. The projection
A_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}\to A_{\overline{K}}
induces a
p
-isogeny
A_{x_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}}\to A_{x}
, by (
3.19
).

Passing to the
p^{\infty}
-level, denote

\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{p}}=\varprojlim_{\widetilde{K}_{p}^{\prime}\subset\widetilde{K}_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{p}^{\prime}\widetilde{K}^{p}},\quad\mathcal{A}_{\overline{K}^{p}}=\varprojlim_{\overline{K}_{p}^{\prime}\subset\overline{K}_{p}}A_{\overline{K}_{p}^{\prime}\overline{K}^{p}},\quad\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{p}}=\mathcal{A}_{\overline{K}^{p}}.

\mathcal{S}_{\overline{K}^{p}}=\varprojlim_{\overline{K}_{p}^{\prime}\subset\overline{K}_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}_{p}^{\prime}\overline{K}^{p}},\quad\mathcal{S}_{K_{h}^{p}}=\varprojlim_{K_{h,p}^{\prime}\subset K_{h,p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h,p}^{\prime}K_{h}^{p}},\quad\mathcal{A}_{\infty,\tilde{x}}=\varprojlim_{\overline{K}_{p}^{\prime}\subset\overline{K}_{p}}A_{x_{\overline{K}^{\prime}_{p}\overline{K}^{p}}}.

Remark 3.31
.

In abelian-type case,
\mathcal{S}_{K_{h}^{p}}^{\Diamond}
is represented by a perfectoid space (see
[
79
]
), one can show that
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{p}}^{\Diamond}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\overline{K}^{p}}^{\Diamond}
are represented by perfectoid spaces using the fact that the fibers
\mathcal{A}_{\infty,\tilde{x}}^{\Diamond}
are represented by perfectoid spaces (see
[
10
, Thm. 1]
). We do not need this result, so we do not spell out the proof.

Let
\tilde{x}^{c}
be the image of
\tilde{x}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}^{c,p}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}):=\varprojlim_{K_{h,p}^{c,\prime}\subset K_{h,p}^{c}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h,p}^{c,\prime}K_{h}^{c,p}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. We can similarly define these objects using
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{c}}(\overline{P}^{c},\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{c})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}^{c}}(G_{h}^{c},X_{h}^{c})
. Then diagram (
3.20
) induces a commutative diagram:

(3.21)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{x_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}}\times_{x_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{\prime},x_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{\prime},x_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{x_{\overline{K}^{c,\prime}}}\times_{x_{\overline{K}^{c,\prime}}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{c,\prime},x_{\overline{K}^{c,\prime}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{c,\prime},x_{\overline{K}^{c,\prime}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{x}\times_{x}\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K},x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K},x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{x^{c}}\times_{x^{c}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{c},x^{c}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{c},x^{c}}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{x_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{x_{\overline{K}^{c,\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{x^{c}}}

By passing to
p^{\infty}
-level, we have a commutative diagram:

(3.22)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}_{\infty,\tilde{x}}\times_{\tilde{x}}\mathcal{S}_{\overline{K}^{p},\tilde{x}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}_{\overline{K}^{p},\tilde{x}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}_{\infty,\tilde{x}^{c}}\times_{\tilde{x}^{c}}\mathcal{S}_{\overline{K}^{c,p},\tilde{x}^{c}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}_{\overline{K}^{c,p},\tilde{x}^{c}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{x}\times_{x}\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K},x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K},x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A_{x^{c}}\times_{x^{c}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{c},x^{c}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{c},x^{c}},}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\tilde{x}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{\tilde{x}^{c}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{x^{c}}}

where
\mathcal{S}_{\overline{K}^{p},\tilde{x}}
is the fiber of
\mathcal{S}_{\overline{K}^{p}}\to\mathcal{S}_{K_{h}^{p}}
at
\tilde{x}
. Similarly for
\mathcal{S}_{\overline{K}^{c,p},\tilde{x}^{c}}
.

We introduce lemmas analogous to Lemma
3.27
and Lemma
3.28
.

Lemma 3.32
.

With the above conventions, the pushout

\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{K}:=\varprojlim_{\overline{K}^{\prime}_{p}\subset\overline{K}_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}(\overline{P},\overline{\mathcal{X}})\times^{\underline{\overline{K}/Z(\overline{P})(\mathbb{Q})^{-}_{\overline{K}}\overline{K}^{p}}}\underline{\overline{K}^{c}_{p}}

is the pullback of
\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{K^{c}}
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{c}}(\overline{P}^{c},\overline{\mathcal{X}}^{c})
. The action
\mu_{\tilde{x}}
induces a
(\mathcal{A}_{\infty,\tilde{x}}^{\prime}\to A_{x})
-equivariant action
\mu_{\tilde{x}}^{\prime}
on
\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{K,\tilde{x}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}(\overline{P},\overline{\mathcal{X}})_{x}
, where
\mathcal{A}_{\infty,\tilde{x}}^{\prime}
is an inverse limit of
p
-power covers of
\mathcal{A}_{x}
, and
\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{K,\tilde{x}}
is the fiber of
\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{K}\to\mathbb{P}_{K_{h}}
at
\tilde{x}
.

Proof.

Same as the proof of Lemma
3.27
(using Lemma
3.33
).
∎

Lemma 3.33
.

Let
A_{0},A_{1},A_{2}
be abelian varieties over a field of characteristic
0
, and let
f:A_{1}\to A_{0}
and
g:A_{2}\to A_{0}
be isogenies. Assume that
g
is a
p
-power isogeny. Then the identity component
A_{3}
of
A_{1}\times_{f,A_{0},g}A_{2}
is an abelian variety equipped with a
p
-power isogeny
A_{3}\to A_{1}
.

Proof.

A_{1}\times_{f,A_{0},g}A_{2}
is a closed subgroup of
A_{1}\times A_{2}
, hence projective; its identity component
A_{3}
is therefore an abelian variety by Cartier’s theorem since all varieties are defined over a field of characteristic zero. By construction,
A_{3}\to A_{1}
is a
p
-power isogeny.
∎

Let
(\overline{\mathscr{P}},\phi_{\overline{\mathscr{P}}})
be the
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{c}
-shtuka on
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}}^{\Diamond}
associated with
(\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{K},\overline{\mathrm{HT}}_{K})
.

Lemma 3.34
.

Let
x\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}}(F)
. Given
\gamma\in A_{x}(F)
, there is an isomorphism of
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{c}
-shtukas
(\overline{\mathscr{P}},\phi_{\overline{\mathscr{P}}})
and
\gamma^{*}(\overline{\mathscr{P}},\phi_{\overline{\mathscr{P}}})
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K},x}^{\Diamond}
.

Proof.

This follows similarly from the proof of Lemma
3.29
. Let
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{\prime},x}
(resp.
A_{\overline{K}^{\prime},x}^{\prime}
) be the fiber of
x
along
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}}
(resp.
A_{\overline{K}^{\prime}}^{\prime}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}^{\prime}}
; here we use the identity component of
A_{\overline{K}}\times_{A_{\overline{K}^{c}}}A_{\overline{K}^{c,\prime}}
, where we pull back these abelian schemes over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}^{\prime}}
). Let
S:=\mathcal{S}_{K_{h}^{p},x}
. Let
\mathcal{A}_{\infty,x}^{\prime}=\varprojlim_{\overline{K}_{p}^{\prime}\subset\overline{K}_{p}}A^{\prime}_{\overline{K}^{\prime}_{p}\overline{K}^{p},x}
; it is a group object over
S
. Given any
x^{\prime}\in S
, the fiber of
\mathcal{A}_{\infty,x}^{\prime}
at
x^{\prime}
is
\mathcal{A}_{\infty,\tilde{x}^{\prime}}^{\prime}
for some
\tilde{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{\overline{K}^{p}}
lifting
x^{\prime}
. Consider the projection
\mathcal{A}_{\infty,x}^{\prime}\to A_{x}\times_{x}S
; this is a morphism of group objects over
S
. Let
T_{p}(A_{S})
be its kernel; it is a
v
-sheaf over
S
. Recall that for any
x^{\prime}\in S
, the fiber
T_{p}(A_{S})_{x^{\prime}}
is the kernel of
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\infty,\tilde{x}^{\prime}}\to A_{x}
, which is isomorphic to
T_{p}(A_{x})
. In other words,
T_{p}(A_{S})
is a torsor under
T_{p}(A_{x})_{S}
.

Given
\gamma\in A_{x}(F)
, it has image
x\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{h}}(F)
. We lift
\gamma
to
\gamma_{S}\in A_{x}(S)
. Take a
v
-cover
T\to S
that trivializes
T_{p}(A_{S})
; we can lift
\gamma_{S}
to
\tilde{\gamma}_{T}\in(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\infty,x})_{T}
and obtain an action
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\infty,x})_{T}\times_{T}(\mathbb{P}_{\overline{K},x})_{T}\to(\mathbb{P}_{\overline{K},x})_{T}
lifting the group action
A_{x}\times_{x}\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K},x}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K},x}
. In particular, the
\tilde{\gamma}_{T}
-action on
(\mathbb{P}_{\overline{K},x})_{T}
lifts the
\gamma
-action on
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K},x}
. Moreover, the action of
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\infty,x})_{T}
on
(\mathbb{P}_{\overline{K},x})_{T}
essentially comes from the action of
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{\infty,x}
on
\mathbb{P}_{\overline{K},x}
over
S
; the
\tilde{\gamma}_{T}
-action is a global lifting of
\gamma
and is compatible with the Hodge–Tate period map, thus it induces an isomorphism of
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{c}
-shtukas
(\overline{\mathscr{P}},\phi_{\overline{\mathscr{P}}})
and
\gamma^{*}(\overline{\mathscr{P}},\phi_{\overline{\mathscr{P}}})
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K},x}^{\Diamond}
.

∎

4.
Canonicity and functoriality

4.1.
Canonical integral models

Mimicking the axioms in
[
66
]
, we define canonical integral models for mixed Shimura varieties coming from the boundary.

Let
(G,X)
be a pure Shimura datum, let
\mathcal{G}
be a quasi-parahoric group scheme, and let
K_{p}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. We fix a cusp label
[\Phi]=[(Q_{\Phi},X_{\Phi}^{+},g_{\Phi})]\in\operatorname{Cusp}_{K}(G,X)
. Here we can vary
K^{p}
, and do not distinguish
\Phi
for different levels
K^{p}
once we have prescribed
g=g_{\Phi}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Recall that, given
\mathcal{G}
, we can attach quasi-parahoric group schemes
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}
(resp.
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{c}
,
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*}
) to
P_{\Phi}
(resp.
P_{\Phi}^{c}
,
P_{\Phi}^{*}
) as in Section
3.1
.

Recall the Pappas–Rapoport integral models:

Axiom 4.1

(
[
66
, Conj. 4.2.2]
,
[
15
, Def. 4.1.2]
,
[
16
, Def. 4.3]
)
.

Consider a system
\{\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)\}_{K^{p}}
of normal flat schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
with generic fiber
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)\}_{K^{p}}
, with
K^{p}
varying over all sufficiently small compact open subgroups of
G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. We say
\{\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)\}_{K^{p}}
is a canonical integral model of
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)\}_{K^{p}}
if the following properties are satisfied.

(1)

For every discrete valuation ring
R
of mixed characteristic over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
, we have

\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)(R[1/p])=(\varprojlim_{K^{p}}\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X))(R).

(2)

For every
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
K^{\prime p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and an
h\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
with
h^{-1}K^{\prime p}h\subset K^{p}
, there are finite étale morphisms

t_{K^{\prime},K}(h):\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime}}(G,X)\to\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)

extending the generic fiber.

(3)

The
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{K,E}
extends to a
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{K}
on
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\Diamond/}
for every sufficiently small
K^{p}
.

(4)

Consider
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)(k)
, with corresponding
b_{x}\in G^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
. Let
x_{0}
be the base point in
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},b_{x},\mu^{c}}(k)
. Then there exists an isomorphism of
v
-sheaves:

\Theta_{x}:(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},b_{x},\mu^{c}})^{\wedge}_{/x_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\wedge}_{/x})^{\Diamond},

such that
\Theta_{x}^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{K})
is the universal
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka on
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},b_{x},\mu^{c}}
.

We mimic the above definition:

Axiom 4.2
.

Consider a system
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\}_{K_{\Phi}^{p}}
of normal flat schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
with generic fiber
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\}_{K_{\Phi}^{p}}
, with
K_{\Phi}^{p}
varying over all sufficiently small compact open subgroups of
P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. We say
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\}_{K_{\Phi}^{p}}
is a canonical integral model of
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\}_{K_{\Phi}^{p}}
if the following properties are satisfied.

(1)

For every discrete valuation ring
R
of mixed characteristic over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
, we have

\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,p}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})(R[1/p])=(\varprojlim_{K_{\Phi}^{p}}\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,p}K_{\Phi}^{p}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi}))(R).

(2)

For every
K_{\Phi}^{p}\subset P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
K_{\Phi}^{\prime p}\subset P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and an
h\in P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
with
h^{-1}K_{\Phi}^{\prime p}h\subset K_{\Phi}^{p}
, there are finite étale morphisms

t_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi},K_{\Phi}}(h):\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})

extending the generic fiber.

(3)

The
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{K_{\Phi},E}
extends to a
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{K_{\Phi}}
on
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\Diamond/}
for every sufficiently small
K_{\Phi}^{p}
.

(4)

Consider
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})(k)
, with corresponding
b_{\Phi,x}\in P_{\Phi}^{*}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\hookrightarrow G^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
(see the first paragraph of subsection
7.1
). Let
x_{0}
be the base point in
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\Phi},b_{\Phi,x},\mu^{c}_{\Phi}}(k)
. Then there exists an isomorphism of
v
-sheaves:

\Theta_{x}:(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\Phi},b_{\Phi,x},\mu^{c}_{\Phi}})^{\wedge}_{/x_{0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\wedge}_{/x})^{\Diamond},

such that
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}^{c}\times^{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*}}\Theta_{x}^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{K_{\Phi}})
is the universal
\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\Phi}
-shtuka on
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\Phi},b_{\Phi,x},\mu^{c}_{\Phi}}
.

In general, let
Y_{\Phi}
be a connected subgroup of
G
satisfying
P_{\Phi}\subset Y_{\Phi}\subset ZP_{\Phi}
, define
Y_{\Phi}^{*}
as in Definition
3.10
, we set up the axioms for the canonical integral model
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}^{Y}}(Y_{\Phi},D_{\Phi}^{Y})\}_{K_{\Phi}^{Y,p}}
of
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{Y}}(Y_{\Phi},D_{\Phi}^{Y})\}_{K_{\Phi}^{Y,p}}
in exactly same way.

Remark 4.3
.

We use the convention that when
(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})=(G,X)
,
P_{\Phi}^{c}=P_{\Phi}^{*}=G^{c}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{c}=\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*}=\mathcal{G}^{c}
, so Axiom
4.2
generalizes Axiom
4.1
.

Remark 4.4
.

We can consider the following condition
(3)^{\prime}
(resp.
(4)^{\prime}
) compared with
(3)
(resp.
(4)
): in
(3)
(resp.
(4)
), replace
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*}
with
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{c}
in the statement.

By devissage
1.39
, since
\mathcal{P}^{c}_{\Phi}\to\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\Phi}
factors through
\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\Phi}
,
(4)
and
(4)^{\prime}
are equivalent. However,
(3)^{\prime}
is stronger than
(3)
. In the rest of the article, we use
(3)
instead of
(3)^{\prime}
, mainly due to two reasons:

(1)

The
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}
-action on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
fixes the
\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}
-shtuka rather than the
\mathcal{P}^{c}_{\Phi}
-shtuka,

(2)

P_{\Phi}^{c}\to G^{c}
might not be an embedding, in practice, we need an embedding
P_{\Phi}^{*}\to G^{c}
to apply the funtoriality result Proposition
4.9
(which is needed in proving the canonicity of integral models of abelian-type, see Theorem
6.26
).

In order to prove functoriality for such canonical integral models, we need further assumptions when
G^{c}\neq G
.

Definition 4.5
.

Let
(P,\mathcal{X})
be a Shimura datum, let
Z^{\prime}
be a central multiplicative group in
P
that contains
Z(P)_{ac}
, and let
(P^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime}):=(P,\mathcal{X})/Z^{\prime}
. Let
\mathcal{P}
be a quasi-parahoric group scheme of
P
, and let
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
be a quasi-parahoric group scheme of
P^{\prime}
defined in Definition
3.8
. Let
K_{p}=\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
K_{p}^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Consider a system
\{\mathscr{S}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\}_{K^{p}}
(resp.
\{\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime}}(P^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\}_{K^{\prime,p}}
) of normal flat schemes over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
with generic fiber
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\}_{K^{p}}
(resp.
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}(P^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\}_{K^{\prime,p}}
), with
K^{p}
(resp.
K^{\prime,p}
) varying over all sufficiently small compact open subgroups of
P(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
(resp.
P^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
).

Let
\pi:(P,\mathcal{X})\to(P^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
be the projection. We say
\{\mathscr{S}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\}_{K^{p}}
and
\{\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime}}(P^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\}_{K^{\prime,p}}
are
adapted
if, for any pair
(K^{p},K^{\prime,p})
such that
\pi(K^{p})\subset K^{\prime,p}
, there is a finite morphism

(4.1)

\mathscr{S}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\to\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime}}(P^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})

extending the generic fiber
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}(P^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})
induced by
\pi
.

We say
\{\mathscr{S}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\}_{K^{p}}
is
adapted with respect to
P\to P^{\prime}
if there exists
\{\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime}}(P^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\}_{K^{\prime,p}}
such that
\{\mathscr{S}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\}_{K^{p}}
and
\{\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime}}(P^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\}_{K^{\prime,p}}
are adapted.

Definition 4.6
.

Keep notation in Definition
4.5
. Let
(G,X):=(P,\mathcal{X})/W
. We say a system of normal flat models
\{\mathscr{S}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\}_{K^{p}}
of
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\}_{K^{p}}
adapted with respect to
P\to P^{\prime}
is moreover
adapted with respect to
P\to G
if there exists a system of normal flat models
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{G}}(G,X)\}_{K^{p}_{G}}
of
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{G}}(G,X)\}_{K^{p}_{G}}
that is adapted with
G\to G^{\prime}
, where
G^{\prime}=G/Z^{\prime}
(by Lemma
3.1
,
Z(G)_{ac}=Z(P)_{ac}\subset Z^{\prime}
) and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=\mathcal{U}\rtimes\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, such that when
K^{p}_{G}
and
K^{\prime,p}_{G}
contain the image of
K^{p}
and
K^{\prime,p}
respectively, the induced morphisms on generic fibers

\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{G}}(G,X),\quad\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}(P^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{G}^{\prime}}(G^{\prime},X^{\prime})

extend to morphisms on integral models

\mathscr{S}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{G}}(G,X),\quad\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime}}(P^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{G}^{\prime}}(G^{\prime},X^{\prime}),

respectively.

Recall the following fact:

Lemma 4.7
.

Given a morphism between Shimura data
\iota:(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})\to(P_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2})
, then
\iota(W_{1})\subset W_{2}
and
\iota(U_{1})\subset U_{2}
, and
\iota
induces
G_{1}\to G_{2}
and
V_{1}\to V_{2}
. In particular, the morphism
P_{1}\to P_{2}
is compatible with
U_{1}\rtimes G_{1}\to U_{2}\rtimes G_{2}
in the sense of
1.33
.

Proof.

Let
h_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{1}
, and set
h_{2}=h_{1}\circ\iota\in\mathcal{X}_{2}
. By definition, the weight filtration on
\operatorname{Lie}P_{i}
induced by
\mathrm{Ad}_{P_{i}}\circ h_{i}
is
W_{-1}(\operatorname{Lie}P_{i})=\operatorname{Lie}W_{i}
,
W_{-2}(\operatorname{Lie}P_{i})=\operatorname{Lie}U_{i}
. By construction,
\operatorname{Lie}P_{1}\to\operatorname{Lie}P_{2}
is a morphism between mixed Hodge structures, and it is strict; thus it maps
\operatorname{Lie}W_{1}
to
\operatorname{Lie}W_{2}
and
\operatorname{Lie}U_{1}
to
\operatorname{Lie}U_{2}
.
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

Consider one of the following two cases:

(1)

Let
\iota:(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})\to(P_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2})
be an embedding of mixed Shimura data, let
(G_{i},X_{i})=(P_{i},\mathcal{X}_{i})/W
be the induced pure Shimura datum. Let
E_{1}
,
E_{2}
be the completion of reflex fields. Let
\mathcal{P}_{1},\mathcal{P}_{2}
be quasi-parahoric group schemes of
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
respectively, such that

(4.2)

\mathcal{P}_{2}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\cap P_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})=\mathcal{P}_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),\quad\mathcal{G}_{2}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\cap G_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})=\mathcal{G}_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}).

Let
K_{2}^{p}\subset P_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
be a sufficiently small subgroup. One can always find
K_{1}^{p}\subset P_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that the induced morphisms
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(P_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2})\otimes_{E_{2}}E_{1}
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{G_{1}}}(G_{1},X_{1})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{G_{2}}}(G_{2},X_{2})\otimes_{E_{2}}E_{1}
are closed embeddings (such levels exist; see
[
83
, Lem. 1.22]
), where
K_{i}=\mathcal{P}_{i}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})K^{p}_{i}
, and
K_{G_{i}}=\mathcal{G}_{i}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})K_{G_{i}}^{p}
, where
K_{G_{i}}^{p}\subset G_{i}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
are the images of
K_{i}^{p}
.

Let
\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime}
be a quasi-parahoric group scheme of
P_{i}^{\prime}
as in Definition
3.8
, such that
P_{1}^{\prime}\to P_{2}^{\prime}
induces
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}
. We
assume

P_{1}^{\prime}\to P_{2}^{\prime}
is a closed embedding and moreover the smoothing
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1}^{\prime}
of the closure of
P_{1}^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}
is a quasi-parahoric group scheme with
(\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1}^{\prime})^{\circ}=\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime\circ}
.

(2)

Let
(P,\mathcal{X}):=(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})=(P_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2})
be a mixed Shimura datum and
(G,X)=(G_{i},X_{i})=(P_{i},\mathcal{X}_{i})/W_{i}
be the induced pure Shimura datum, let
E_{1}=E_{2}
be the completion of the reflex field. Let
\mathcal{P}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}
be quasi-parahoric group schemes of
P
such that
\mathcal{U}_{1}=\mathcal{U}_{2}
and
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\circ}=\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\circ}
(i.e.
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\circ}=\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\circ}
). Define
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}
as in Definition
3.8
such that we have a morphism
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}
(which automatically induces
(\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime})^{\circ}=(\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime})^{\circ}
by construction). Let
K_{i}=\mathcal{P}_{i}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})K^{p}
for some neat
K^{p}\subset P(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and
K_{G_{i}}=\mathcal{G}_{i}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})K_{G}^{p}
, where
K_{G}^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
are the images of
K^{p}
. Define
K_{i}^{\prime}
,
K_{G_{i}}^{\prime}
similarly, for some
K^{\prime,p}\subset P^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.

Assume

\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(P_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2})\}_{K_{2}^{p}},\quad\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{G_{2}}}(G_{2},X_{2})\}_{K_{G_{2}}^{p}},\quad\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}^{\prime}}(P^{\prime}_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\prime})\}_{K_{2}^{\prime,p}},\quad\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{G_{2}}^{\prime}}(G^{\prime}_{2},X_{2}^{\prime})\}_{K_{G_{2}}^{\prime,p}}

have integral models

(4.3)

\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}}(P_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2})\}_{K_{2}^{p}},\quad\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{G_{2}}}(G_{2},X_{2})\}_{K_{G_{2}}^{p}},\quad\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}^{\prime}}(P^{\prime}_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\prime})\}_{K_{2}^{\prime,p}},\quad\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{G_{2}}^{\prime}}(G^{\prime}_{2},X_{2}^{\prime})\}_{K_{G_{2}}^{\prime,p}}

respectively that are normal and flat over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{2}}
and are adapted with
P_{2}\to P_{2}^{\prime}
and
P_{2}\to G_{2}
in the sense of Definitions
4.5
and
4.6
, such that the shtukas on generic fibers extend over these integral models, i.e., for
(P_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2})
(and similarly for
(P_{2}^{\prime},\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\prime})
), given the commutative diagram on the right, we have horizontal morphisms on the left diagram that make the diagram commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}}(P_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(P_{2},\mathcal{X}_{2})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K_{G_{2}}}(G_{2},X_{2})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1},}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{G_{2}}}(G_{2},X_{2})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}.}}

Let

\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})\}_{K_{1}^{p}},\quad\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{G_{1}}}(G_{1},X_{1})\}_{K_{G_{1}}^{p}},\quad\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}(P^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\prime})\}_{K_{1}^{\prime,p}},\quad\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{G_{1}}^{\prime}}(G^{\prime}_{1},X_{1}^{\prime})\}_{K_{G_{1}}^{\prime,p}}

be the relative normalizations of ((
4.3
)
\otimes\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}
) in

\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})\}_{K_{1}^{p}},\quad\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{G_{1}}}(G_{1},X_{1})\}_{K_{G_{1}}^{p}},\quad\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}(P^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\prime})\}_{K_{1}^{\prime,p}},\quad\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{G_{1}}^{\prime}}(G^{\prime}_{1},X_{1}^{\prime})\}_{K_{G_{1}}^{\prime,p}},

then these integral models are normal and flat over
\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}
and are adapted with
P_{1}\to P_{1}^{\prime}
and
P_{1}\to G_{1}
, such that the shtukas on generic fibers extend over these integral models, i.e., for
(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})
(and similarly for
(P_{1}^{\prime},\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\prime})
), given the commutative diagram on the right, we have horizontal morphisms on the left diagram that make the diagram commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K_{G_{1}}}(G_{1},X_{1})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1},}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{G_{1}}}(G_{1},X_{1})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}.}}

Proof.

We prove these two cases separately.

(1)

Since
P_{1}^{\prime}\to P_{2}^{\prime}
is a closed embedding,
G_{1}^{\prime}\to G_{2}^{\prime}
is also a closed embedding. Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{\prime}
be the smoothing of the closure of
G_{1}^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\prime}
. We claim that
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{\prime}
is a quasi-parahoric group scheme and
(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{\prime})^{\circ}=(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime})^{\circ}
: condition (
4.2
) shows that
\mathcal{P}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}
is compatible with
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
by Lemma
3.6
; in particular, we have compatible sections
G_{1}\to P_{1}
and
G_{2}\to P_{2}
by taking generic fibers. Consider the induced compatible sections
G_{1}^{\prime}\to P_{1}^{\prime}
and
G_{2}^{\prime}\to P_{2}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}
is compatible with
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\prime}
under such sections. Then the closure of
G_{1}^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\prime}
is the same as the closure of
G_{1}^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}
, hence also the closure of
G_{1}^{\prime}
in
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1}^{\prime}
. Since
(\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1}^{\prime})^{\circ}=\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime\circ}
, we have
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{U}_{1}\rtimes\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{\prime}
, and
(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{\prime})^{\circ}=(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime})^{\circ}
.

Let
\widetilde{K}_{G_{1},p}^{\prime}=\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
\widetilde{K}_{G_{1}}^{\prime}=\widetilde{K}_{G_{1},p}^{\prime}K_{G_{1}}^{p}
. Then
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{G_{1}}^{\prime}}(G_{1}^{\prime},X_{1}^{\prime})^{\Diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}
extends to
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{G_{1}}^{\prime}}(G^{\prime}_{1},X_{1}^{\prime})^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}
, by
[
66
, Thm. 4.3.1, 4.5.2]
(with modifications in the quasi-parahoric case using
[
15
, Thm. 4.1.8]
). Since
(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{\prime})^{\circ}=(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime})^{\circ}
, we pass from
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{\prime}
to
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime}
using
[
15
, Thm. 4.1.15]
;
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{G_{1}}^{\prime}}(G_{1}^{\prime},X_{1}^{\prime})^{\Diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}
extends to
\mathscr{S}_{K_{G_{1}}^{\prime}}(G^{\prime}_{1},X_{1}^{\prime})^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}
.

Let
P_{3}=U_{2}\rtimes G_{1}
, where
G_{1}
acts on
U_{2}
via the embedding
G_{1}\to G_{2}
, and let
\mathcal{P}_{3}=\mathcal{U}_{2}\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{1}
. Moreover, let
P_{3}^{\prime}=U_{2}\rtimes G_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{P}_{3}^{\prime}=\mathcal{U}_{2}\rtimes\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime}
. We can always do this since
\mathcal{P}_{1}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}
(resp.
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}
) is compatible with
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
(resp.
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\prime}
). Consider
\mathcal{P}_{3}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}
that is compatible with
\mathcal{G}_{3}^{\prime}=\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\prime}
, by Corollary
1.49
, we have a dashed morphism that makes the diagrams commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}(P_{1}^{\prime},\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\prime})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}^{\prime}}(P_{2}^{\prime},\mathcal{X}_{2}^{\prime})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{3}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K_{G_{1}}^{\prime}}(G_{1}^{\prime},X_{1}^{\prime})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

Apply Proposition
1.43
to the exact sequence
1\to\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{P}_{3}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{U}_{2}/\mathcal{U}_{1}\to 1
(the Part
(2)
in the proof of Proposition
1.43
does not need
U_{1}
being normal in
U_{3}
). Then the dashed morphism
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}(P_{1}^{\prime},\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\prime})^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{3}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}
factors through
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}}
, hence through
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}
; see Corollary
1.47
. Compose with

\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}(P_{1}^{\prime},\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\prime})\quad(\textit{resp.}\ \mathscr{S}_{K_{G_{1}}}(G_{1},X_{1})\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{G_{1}}^{\prime}}(G_{1}^{\prime},X_{1}^{\prime})),

we have

\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(P_{1},\mathcal{X}_{1})^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}\quad(\textit{resp.}\ \mathscr{S}_{K_{G_{1}}}(G_{1},X_{1})^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1})

extending the one on the generic fiber. The commutativity of the diagram follows from
[
66
, Cor. 2.7.10]
.

(2)

First of all, since
(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime})^{\circ}=(\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\prime})^{\circ}
by construction,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{G_{1}}^{\prime}}(G^{\prime},X^{\prime})^{\Diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}
extends to
\mathscr{S}_{K_{G_{1}}^{\prime}}(G^{\prime},X^{\prime})^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}
, by
[
15
, Thm. 4.1.15]
. Composing with
\mathscr{S}_{K_{G_{1}}}(G,X)\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{G_{1}}^{\prime}}(G^{\prime},X^{\prime})
, we obtain
\mathscr{S}_{K_{G_{1}}}(G,X)^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}
.

By Lemma
3.1
,
P^{\prime}=U\rtimes G^{\prime}
. By Corollary
1.49
, we obtain the desired dashed morphism that extends the one on the generic fiber and makes the diagrams commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}(P^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime}_{2}}(P^{\prime},\mathcal{X}^{\prime})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime}_{G_{1}}}(G^{\prime},X^{\prime})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

Similarly for
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(P,\mathcal{X})^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\delta=1}
. The commutativity of the diagram follows from
[
66
, Cor. 2.7.10]
.

∎

Proposition 4.9

(
[
66
, Thm. 4.3.1, 4.5.2]
,
[
15
, Thm. 4.1.15]
)
.

Consider one of the following two cases:

(1)

Let
\iota:(G_{1},X_{1})\to(G_{2},X_{2})
be an embedding of pure Shimura data, and let
E_{1}
,
E_{2}
be completions of reflex fields. Let
\mathcal{G}_{1}
,
\mathcal{G}_{2}
be quasi-parahoric group schemes of
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
respectively, such that
\mathcal{G}_{2}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\cap G_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})=\mathcal{G}_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. Let
K_{2}^{p}\subset G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
be any sufficiently small subgroup; one can always find
K_{1}^{p}\subset G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that the induced morphism
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}(G_{1},X_{1})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})
is a closed embedding, where
K_{i}=\mathcal{G}_{i}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})K_{i}^{p}
.
Let
[\Phi_{1}]=[(Q_{\Phi_{1}},X_{\Phi_{1}}^{+},g_{\Phi_{1}})]\in\operatorname{Cusp}_{K_{1}}(G_{1},X_{1})
, and
[\Phi_{2}]=[\iota_{*}\Phi_{1}]=[(Q_{\Phi_{2}},X_{\Phi_{2}}^{+},g_{\Phi_{2}})]\in\operatorname{Cusp}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})
(with
g_{\Phi_{1}}=g_{\Phi_{2}}
). Here we can vary
K_{1}^{p}
(resp.
K_{2}^{p}
), and do not distinguish
\Phi_{1}
(resp.
\Phi_{2}
) for different levels
K_{1}^{p}
(resp.
K_{2}^{p}
) once we have prescribed
g=g_{\Phi_{1}}=g_{\Phi_{2}}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. Assume

(a)

G_{1}^{c}\to G_{2}^{c}
is an embedding, and the smoothing
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{c}
of the closure of
G_{1}^{c}
in
\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c}
is a quasi-parahoric group scheme with
(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}^{c})^{\circ}=(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{c})^{\circ}
.

(b)

\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{2}}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\cap P_{\Phi_{1}}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})=\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{1}}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
induces
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi_{2},h}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\cap G_{\Phi_{1},h}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})=\mathcal{G}_{\Phi_{1},h}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
.

(2)

Let
(G,X):=(G_{1},X_{1})=(G_{2},X_{2})
be a pure Shimura datum, and let
E_{1}=E_{2}
be the completion of the reflex field. Let
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
be quasi-parahoric group schemes of
G
such that
\mathcal{G}^{\circ}_{1}=\mathcal{G}^{\circ}_{2}
. Fix
(Q_{\Phi},X_{\Phi}^{+},g_{\Phi}):=(Q_{\Phi_{1}},X_{\Phi_{1}}^{+},g_{\Phi_{1}})=(Q_{\Phi_{2}},X_{\Phi_{2}}^{+},g_{\Phi_{2}})
as above. Let
K_{i}=\mathcal{G}_{i}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})K^{p}
for some neat
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.

Assume
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}\}_{K^{p}_{2}}
and
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}^{c}}\}_{K^{c,p}_{2}}
have canonical integral models
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}}\}_{K^{p}_{2}}
and
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}^{c}}\}_{K^{c,p}_{2}}
respectively that are adapted with
G_{2}\to G_{2}^{c}
. Let
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}\}_{K^{p}_{1}}
and
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{c}}\}_{K^{c,p}_{1}}
be the relative normalizations of
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}}\}_{K^{p}_{2}}
and
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}^{c}}\}_{K^{c,p}_{2}}
in
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}\}_{K^{p}_{1}}
and
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}^{c}}\}_{K^{c,p}_{1}}
respectively. In case
(2)
, we
further assume
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{c}}\}_{K^{p}_{1}}
are étale
. Then
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}\}_{K^{p}_{1}}
and
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{c}}\}_{K^{c,p}_{1}}
are canonical integral models of
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}\}_{K^{p}_{1}}
and
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}^{c}}\}_{K^{c,p}_{1}}
respectively, adapted with
G_{1}\to G_{1}^{c}
.

Moreover, assume

(4.4)

\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p}},\quad\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{*}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{*,p}},\quad\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}^{p}},\quad\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}^{*}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}^{*,p}}

have canonical integral models

(4.5)

\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p}},\quad\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{*}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{*,p}},\quad\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}^{p}},\quad\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}^{*}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}^{*,p}}

respectively, adapted with
P_{\Phi_{2}}\to P_{\Phi_{2}}^{*}
and
P_{\Phi_{2}}\to G_{\Phi_{2},h}
. Let

(4.6)

\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{p}},\quad\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{*}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{*,p}},\quad\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1},h}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{1},h}^{p}},\quad\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1},h}^{*}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{1},h}^{*,p}}

be the relative normalizations of (
4.5
) in

(4.7)

\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{p}},\quad\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{*}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{*,p}},\quad\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1},h}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{1},h}^{p}},\quad\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1},h}^{*}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{1},h}^{*,p}}

respectively. In case
(2)
, we
further assume
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{*}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{p}}
and
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1},h}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1},h}^{*}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{1},h}^{p}}
are étale
. Then (
4.6
) are canonical integral models of (
4.7
) respectively, adapted with
P_{\Phi_{1}}\to P_{\Phi_{1}}^{*}
and
P_{\Phi_{1}}\to G_{\Phi_{1},h}
.

Proof.

Axiom
(1)
is automatic; it comes from the construction of the relative normalization, see the first paragraph in the proof of
[
15
, Thm. 4.1.15]
. Axiom
(2)
can be verified using axiom
(4)
, since
\Diamond
is full-faithful on the category of flat and normal formal schemes locally formally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
; see
[
75
, Prop. 18.4.1]
. We only need to consider axioms
(3)
and
(4)
.

For
(G_{1},X_{1})
:

(1)

We first verify that
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{c}}(G_{1}^{c},X_{1}^{c})\}_{K^{c,p}_{1}}
is canonical in the sense of the Pappas–Rapoport conjectural framework; this is
[
66
, Thm. 4.3.1, 4.5.2]
(with quasi-parahoric modifications using
[
15
, Thm. 4.1.8]
, and with modifications in the
G\neq G^{c}
case using the second paragraph of the proof of Lemma
4.8
, case
(1)
). Next, we show
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(G_{1},X_{1})\}_{K^{p}_{1}}
is canonical. The extension of shtukas is given by composing
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{c}}
with
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{c}}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{c},\mu_{1}^{c},\delta=1}
. Let
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(k)
and
\bar{x}\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{c}}(k)
be its image. The existence of an isomorphism
\Theta_{x}:((\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}})^{\wedge}_{/x})^{\Diamond}\cong(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}_{1}^{c},b_{x},\mu_{1}^{c}})^{\wedge}_{/x_{0}}
follows from the same arguments as in
[
66
, §4.7, 4.8]
, and
\Theta_{x}
is compatible with
\Theta_{\bar{x}}
by construction. This in turn shows that the finite morphism
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{c}}
is étale, and
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}\}_{K^{p}_{1}}
is adapted with
G_{1}\to G_{1}^{c}
.

(2)

The canonicity of
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{c}}(G_{1}^{c},X_{1}^{c})\}_{K^{c,p}_{1}}
comes from
[
15
, Thm. 4.1.15]
. Let us show the canonicity of
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}(G_{1},X_{1})\}_{K^{p}_{1}}
. Note that
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}}
is finite étale since
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{c}}
and
\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}^{c}}
are finite étale by assumption, and
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{c}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}^{c}}
is finite étale by axiom
(4)
; this implies axiom
(4)
. The extension of shtukas comes from Lemma
4.8
, case
(2)
.

For
(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})
and
(G_{\Phi_{1},h},D_{\Phi_{1},h})
, apply Lemma
4.8
, and let
P_{i}^{\prime}=P_{\Phi_{i}}^{*}
,
\mathcal{P}_{i}^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{i}}^{*}
. Verify the conditions in Lemma
4.8
:

(1)

We need to show that
P_{1}^{\prime}\to P_{2}^{\prime}
is a closed embedding and that the smoothing
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1}^{\prime}
of the closure of
P_{1}^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}
is a quasi-parahoric group scheme with
(\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1}^{\prime})^{\circ}=(\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime})^{\circ}
. Since
G_{1}^{c}\hookrightarrow G_{2}^{c}
and
P_{i}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow G_{i}^{c}
, we have
P_{1}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow P_{2}^{\prime}
. We have

\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1}^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=P_{1}^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\cap\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=P_{1}^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\cap\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=P_{1}^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\cap\mathcal{G}_{1}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}).

Since a smooth affine group scheme of a given group is uniquely determined by its set of
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-points, we have
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime}
.

(2)

We need to show that
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
induces
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{1}}\to\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{2}}
such that
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{1}}^{\circ}=\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{2}}^{\circ}
. This is standard, since
(\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{i}},\mu_{\Phi_{i}})
comes from the boundary in the sense of Definition
1.21
; see Lemma
3.3
. On the other hand,
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{c}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c}
induces
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}
such that
(\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime})^{\circ}=(\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime})^{\circ}
.

Now let us show canonicity of integral models. For
(G_{\Phi_{1},h},D_{\Phi_{1},h})
:

(1)

This is similar to
(G_{1},X_{1})
, but we need to handle the case where
G_{\Phi_{1},h}\to G_{\Phi_{1},h}^{*}
has non-connected kernel. By assumption, the smoothing
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\Phi_{1},h}^{*}
of the closure of
G_{\Phi_{1},h}^{*}
in
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi_{2},h}^{*}
is a quasi-parahoric group scheme and
(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\Phi_{1},h}^{*})^{\circ}=(\mathcal{G}_{\Phi_{1},h}^{*})^{\circ}
; see the first paragraph of the proof of Lemma
4.8
. For the extension of shtukas, we apply Lemma
4.8
, case
(1)
. For axiom
(4)
, we apply results
(1)
and
(2)
successively from the first two paragraphs of this proof.

(2)

This is similar to
(G_{1},X_{1})
.

For
(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})
:

(1)

For extension of shtukas, we apply Lemma
4.8
Case
(1)
. Note that we can adjust level away from
p
such that
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}
,
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1},h}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}}
are all closed embeddings by
[
83
, Lem. 1.22]
.
Axiom
(4)
follows from the same arguments as in
[
66
, §4.7, 4.8]
; we only explain where the arguments need to be adjusted or verified in our case.
We use notation from
[
66
, §4.7, 4.8]
. The base point
x_{0}
in
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\Phi_{2}},b_{\Phi_{2},x},\mu_{\Phi_{2}}^{c}}
is the image of the base point in
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\Phi_{1}},b_{\Phi_{1},x},\mu_{\Phi_{1}}^{c}}
(with
b_{\Phi_{2},x}=b_{\Phi_{1},x}
). The arguments in
[
66
, §4.7.2]
(with modifications in
[
66
, §4.8]
) imply that there is a
\operatorname{Spd}\hat{R}_{x}
-point (where
\operatorname{Spf}\hat{R}_{x}=\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})^{\wedge}_{/x}
) of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\Phi_{1}},b_{\Phi_{1},x},\mu_{\Phi_{1}}^{c}}
lifting the base point
x_{0}
, such that the corresponding
\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\Phi_{1}}
-shtuka is equal to the pushout
\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\Phi_{1}}\times^{\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi_{1}}}
of the
\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi_{1}}
-shtuka coming from
\mathscr{P}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}
. In the arguments below
[
66
, Prop. 4.7.1]
, note that
\operatorname{Spd}(\hat{R}_{x})_{\eta}
and
(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\Phi_{1}},b_{\Phi_{1},x},\mu^{c}_{\Phi_{1}}})^{\wedge}_{/x_{0}}
have the same dimension (see Remark
1.16
). Similarly for
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{*}}(P_{\Phi_{1}}^{*},D_{\Phi_{1}}^{*})
, and
\Theta_{\bar{x}}
is compatible with
\Theta_{x}
, where
\bar{x}\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{*}}(k)
is the projection of
x
. This in particular shows that the finite morphism
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{*}}
is étale, and
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}\}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{p}}
is adapted with
P_{\Phi_{1}}\to P_{\Phi_{1}}^{*}
.

(2)

For the extension of shtukas, we apply Lemma
4.8
, case
(2)
. Axiom
(4)
for
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{*}}
: we need to show
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{*}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{*}}
is étale, and this follows from the same process as the proof of
[
15
, Thm. 4.1.15]
: Note that
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{*}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{*}}
is a finite étale torsor under the finite abelian group
K_{\Phi_{2},p}^{*}/K_{\Phi_{1},p}^{*}=\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{2}}^{*})^{\phi}/\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{1}}^{*})^{\phi}
since
(P_{i}^{*})^{c}=P_{i}^{*}
, and by
[
15
, Prop. 2.3.1]
and Corollary
1.49
, we have a cartesian diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{*}}(P_{\Phi_{1}}^{*},D_{\Phi_{1}}^{*})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{1}}^{*},\mu_{1}^{*},\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{*}}(P_{\Phi_{2}}^{*},D_{\Phi_{2}}^{*})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{2}}^{*},\mu_{2}^{*},\delta=1},}}

where
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{1}}^{*},\mu_{1}^{*},\delta=1,E}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{2}}^{*},\mu_{2}^{*},\delta=1,E}
is a finite étale torsor under
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{2}}^{*})^{\phi}/\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{1}}^{*})^{\phi}
, and
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{*}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{*}}
defined via relative normalization is also a a finite étale torsor under
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{2}}^{*})^{\phi}/\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{1}}^{*})^{\phi}
. Axiom
(4)
for
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}
then follows from the étaleness of
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{*}}
(which also implies
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
is étale since
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{*}}
and
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{*}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{*}}
are étale).

∎

Proposition 4.10

(
[
66
, Thm. 4.2.4]
and
[
15
, Prop. 4.1.10]
)
.

Let
\iota:(G_{1},X_{1})\to(G_{2},X_{2})
be a morphism of Shimura data (not necessarily an embedding), and let
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
be completions of reflex fields. Let
\mathcal{G}_{1}
,
\mathcal{G}_{2}
be quasi-parahoric group schemes of
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
respectively, such that
G_{1}\to G_{2}
induces
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
. Let
[\Phi_{1}]=[(Q_{\Phi_{1}},X_{\Phi_{1}}^{+},g_{\Phi_{1}})]\in\operatorname{Cusp}_{K_{1}}(G_{1},X_{1})
, and
[\Phi_{2}]=[\iota_{*}\Phi_{1}]=[(Q_{\Phi_{2}},X_{\Phi_{2}}^{+},g_{\Phi_{2}})]\in\operatorname{Cusp}_{K_{2}}(G_{2},X_{2})
. Assume
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{i}}}(P_{\Phi_{i}},D_{\Phi_{i}})\}_{K_{i}^{p}}
have canonical models
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{i}}}(P_{\Phi_{i}},D_{\Phi_{i}})\}_{K_{\Phi_{i}}^{p}}
that are adapted with
P_{\Phi_{i}}\to P_{\Phi_{i}}^{*}
and
P_{\Phi_{i}}\to G_{\Phi_{i},h}
for
i=1,2
. Then the induced morphism
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}},D_{\Phi_{2}})\otimes_{E_{1}}E_{2}
extends canonically to

\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}},D_{\Phi_{2}})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}\mathcal{O}_{E_{2}}.

Proof.

Consider the diagonal embedding of Shimura data
(G_{1},X_{1})\hookrightarrow(G_{1}\times G_{2},X_{1}\times X_{2})
. Then
[\Phi_{1}]=[(Q_{\Phi_{1}},X_{\Phi_{1}}^{+},g_{\Phi_{1}})]\in\operatorname{Cusp}_{K_{1}}(G_{1},X_{1})
induces
[\Phi_{1}\times\Phi_{2}]=[(Q_{\Phi_{1}}\times Q_{\Phi_{2}},X_{\Phi_{1}}^{+}\times X_{\Phi_{2}}^{+},g_{\Phi_{1}}\times g_{\Phi_{2}})]\in\operatorname{Cusp}_{K_{1}\times K_{2}}(G_{1}\times G_{2},X_{1}\times X_{2})
. Note that
\mathcal{G}_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=G_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\cap(\mathcal{G}_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\times\mathcal{G}_{2}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))
,
(G_{1}\times G_{2})^{c}=G_{1}^{c}\times G_{2}^{c}
, the closure of
G_{1}^{c}
in
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{c}\times\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c}
is exactly
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{c}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi_{1},h}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})=G_{\Phi_{1},h}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\cap(\mathcal{G}_{\Phi_{1},h}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\times\mathcal{G}_{\Phi_{2},h}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}))
. Then we apply Proposition
4.9
case
(1)
, and get a canonical model
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})^{\prime}\}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{p}}
of
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})\}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}^{p}}
. We need to show that the projection to the first factor
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})^{\prime}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})
is an isomorphism. We apply arguments from the proof of
[
15
, Prop. 4.1.10]
, and use the pushout
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi_{i}}^{c}
-shtukas instead of
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{i}}^{*}
-shtukas.
∎

Corollary 4.11

(
[
66
, Thm. 4.2.4]
and
[
15
, Cor. 4.1.13]
)
.

A canonical integral model
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\}_{K_{\Phi}^{p}}
of
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\}_{K_{\Phi}^{p}}
that is adapted with
P_{\Phi}\to P_{\Phi}^{*}
and
P_{\Phi}\to G_{\Phi,h}
is unique up to a unique isomorphism, if it exists.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
4.10
(see the proof of
[
15
, Cor. 4.1.13]
).
∎

5.
Canonical extensions on toroidal compactifications

5.1.
Axiomatic setup of good compactifications

Let
(G,X)
be a Shimura datum.
Fix an open compact subgroup
K\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, and assume that
K=K_{p}K^{p}
, where
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is neat open compact and
K_{p}
is open compact.
Choose an admissible (rational polyhedral) smooth projective cone decomposition
\Sigma
(without self-intersections).
Denote the toroidal compactification by
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)
and the minimal compactification by
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}(G,X)
; they are defined over the reflex field
\mathbb{E}:=\mathbb{E}(G,X)
.
There is a proper morphism from
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}
to
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}
that is compatible with the stratifications on the source and the target.

For integral models, a similar story is expected.
The properties of these integral models are summarized as
[
52
, Prop. 2.1.2]
and
[
58
, Thm. 4.1.5]
:

Axiom 5.1

(Qualitative descriptions of good compactifications;
[
53
, Prop. 2.2]
and
[
58
, Thm. 4.1.5 and Thm. 5.2.11]
)
.

Fix a prime number
p
and a place
v
of
\mathbb{E}
over
p
. Set
E:=\mathbb{E}_{v}
.
For the cone decomposition
\Sigma
above
3
3

3

In practice, it is usually harmless to refine the cone decompositions, as long as the refinements are still smooth and projective or are refinements of some smooth projective cone decompositions.
, there is a normal, proper, flat model
\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
for
\mathrm{Sh}^{\Sigma}_{K,E}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
, and also a normal, projective, flat model
\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}
for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K,E}^{\mathrm{min}}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
. The following properties hold for
\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
and
\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}
:

(
5.1
.1)

There is a proper surjective morphism
\oint_{K}^{\Sigma}:\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}\to\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}
with geometrically connected fibers. The map
\oint_{K}^{\Sigma}
extends the one constructed in the characteristic zero theory. There are open dense embeddings
J^{\Sigma}:\mathscr{S}_{K}\to\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
and
J^{\mathrm{min}}:\mathscr{S}_{K}\to\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}
such that
\oint^{\Sigma}_{K}\circ J^{\Sigma}=J^{\mathrm{min}}
.

(
5.1
.2)

There is a stratification of locally closed subschemes for
\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
,

\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}:=\coprod_{\Upsilon:=[(\Phi,\sigma)]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)}\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K},

and a stratification of locally closed subschemes for
\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}
,

\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}:=\coprod_{[\Phi]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X)}\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi],K}

extending the stratifications for
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}
and
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}
, respectively. Each stratum is normal and is flat over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
. The same partial orders among strata and the same expressions of closures of strata as the characteristic zero theory hold for
\{\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K}\}
and
\{\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi],K}\}
. The preimage
\oint^{\Sigma}_{K}(\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi],K})
is the union of the strata labeled by preimage of
[\Phi]
in
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)
under the projection
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)\to\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X)
. For the definitions of
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X)
and
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)
, see, e.g.,
[
83
, §1.1 and §1.3]
.

For any cusp label representative
\Phi
, there is a cone decomposition
\Sigma^{+}(\Phi)
associated, which decomposes an open self-adjoint nondegenerated cone
\mathbf{P}^{+}_{\Phi}
; the
\sigma
’s in
[(\Phi,\sigma)]
are taken from
\Sigma^{+}(\Phi)
.

(
5.1
.3)

There is a partial-ordered set
\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
of cusp label representatives from characteristic zero theory; the quotient of
\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
by the equivalence relations induces
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X)
. For each
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
, one can associate a mixed Shimura datum
(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
in the sense of
[
69
, Def. 2.1]
. The mixed Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
admits a tower

\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\to{\mathrm{Sh}}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}(\overline{P}_{\Phi},\overline{D}_{\Phi})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h}),

where the first map is a torsor under a split torus
\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi}}
and the second map is a torsor under an abelian scheme over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,h}}
. The variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,h}}
is a pure Shimura variety over
E
.

There is a tower

(5.1)

\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}

of normal flat schemes of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
, such that the tower extends the one displayed above. The first map is also a torsor under the same split torus. The second map is proper and surjective.

For any equivalence
\Phi\xrightarrow{\sim}\Phi^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
, the corresponding towers of integral models as in (
5.1
) are canonically isomorphic.

(
5.1
.4)

There is a commutative diagram

(5.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}^{\Sigma}_{K}\supset\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathscr{S}}_{K_{\Phi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}^{\mathrm{min}}_{K}\supset\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi],K}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces/\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces/\Delta_{\Phi,K}}

In the diagram above, there are groups
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\triangleleft\Delta_{\Phi,K}
acting on
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
and
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}
, respectively. Their actions factor through finite quotients, and the quotient schemes have canonical isomorphisms
\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K}\cong\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{[\Phi],K}\cong\Delta_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}
. The scheme
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}
is the
\sigma
-stratum of the (relative) affine toric embedding
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
with respect to the cone
\sigma
.

For the definitions of
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}
and
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
, see
[
83
, §1.3]
. In fact, there is an equivariant action of
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
on (
5.1
), and
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}
is the normal subgroup fixing the induced action on the cone decomposition (which is independent of the choice of cones).

Moreover, the quotient
\mathscr{S}^{*}_{K_{\Phi}}:=\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\overline{\mathscr{S}}^{*}_{K_{\Phi}}:=\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\overline{\mathscr{S}}_{K_{\Phi}}
is also a torsor under a split torus
{\mathbf{E}}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
.

(
5.1
.5)

There is a strata-preserving isomorphism

\mathfrak{X}_{\Upsilon,K}:=\mathfrak{X}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K}:={(\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K}}\cong\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash{(\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma))}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}}.

(
5.1
.6)

There is a stronger strata-preserving isomorphism

\mathfrak{X}_{\Upsilon,K}^{\circ}\cong\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash{(\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma))}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}^{+}}.

The scheme
\mathscr{S}^{+}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}
denotes
\cup_{\tau\subset\sigma,\tau\in\Sigma^{+}(\Phi)}\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi},\tau}
and
\mathfrak{X}^{\circ}_{\Upsilon,K}:={(\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{Z}^{+}_{\Upsilon,K}}
, where
\mathcal{Z}^{+}_{\Upsilon,K}:=\cup_{\tau\subset\sigma,\tau\in\Sigma^{+}(\Phi)}\mathcal{Z}_{[(\Phi,\tau)],K}
.

Convention 5.2
.

Denote
\mathscr{S}^{+,*}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}:=\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathscr{S}^{+}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}
. When
\Phi
and
K
are clear, we denote
\mathfrak{X}_{\Upsilon,K}
,
\mathfrak{X}^{\circ}_{\Upsilon,K}
,
\mathscr{S}^{+,(*)}_{K_{\Phi},\sigma}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{+}_{\Upsilon,K}
by
\mathfrak{X}_{\sigma}
,
\mathfrak{X}_{\sigma}^{\circ}
,
\mathscr{S}^{+,(*)}_{\sigma}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{+}_{\sigma}
, respectively.

Over the generic fiber, good compactifications satisfying the above axioms was proved by Pink
[
69
]
based on
[
6
]
. The notation system for the generic fiber can be obtained by replacing
\mathscr{S}
with
\mathrm{Sh}
and replacing
\mathcal{Z}
with
\mathrm{Z}
.

Remark 5.3
.

The main difference between Axiom
5.1
and
[
53
, Prop. 2.2]
is that we need to treat the case where
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}
is nontrivial. The item (9) in
loc. cit.
is in fact a consequence of having a strata-preserving isomorphism between completions as in
(.5)
(see, e.g.,
[
83
, Prop. 4.53]
).

Axiom
(.6)
was proved in the Hodge-type case in
[
52
, Prop. 2.1.3]
.
In fact, Axiom
(.6)
is also true in the abelian-type case. The proof is the same as
[
83
, Prop. 4.32 and Lem. 4.49]
with only some symbols changed; let us record it below.

Proposition 5.4
.

Axiom
(.6)
is true for integral models
\mathscr{S}^{\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}}_{K_{2}}
stated in
[
83
, Thm. 4.39]
.

Proof.

In this proof, we will use some conventions in
[
83
]
without explanation; the readers are recommended to consult the list of symbols there.

By replacing “
\mathcal{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}
” with
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma}^{+}:=\cup_{\tau\subset\sigma,\tau\in\Sigma^{+}(\Phi)}\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\tau}
and replacing “
\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
” with
\mathcal{Z}^{+}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K}:=\cup_{\tau\subset\sigma,\tau\in\Sigma^{+}(\Phi)}\mathcal{Z}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\tau)],K}
, the argument in
[
83
, Prop. 4.32]
goes through verbatim with the following exception: We need to see that
[\sigma]_{ZP}
and
[\tau]_{ZP}
are of the same cardinality. But
[\tau]_{ZP}
is defined to be the
\Delta
-orbit in
\Delta_{ZP,K}
-orbit of
\tau
, where
\Delta\subset\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}
is some subgroup independent of the choice of
\tau
. Also, the stabilizer
\Delta^{ZP,\circ}_{\Phi,K}
of the
\Delta^{ZP}_{\Phi,K}
-action is independent of the choice of
\tau
. We now have

{(\mathscr{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}^{\Sigma})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathcal{Z}^{+}_{[ZP^{b}(\Phi,\sigma)],K,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}\cong{(\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(\sigma)_{\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}})}{}^{\wedge{\ }}_{\mathscr{S}^{+}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\sigma,\mathcal{O}_{K_{Z}}}}.

Now, with the same replacements, the proof of
[
83
, Lem. 4.49]
goes through verbatim.
∎

In summary,

Theorem 5.5

(
[
20
]
,
[
55
]
,
[
58
]
, and
[
83
]
)
.

For abelian-type Shimura data, there are integral models satisfying Axiom
5.1
for all
K
at the beginning of §
5.1
.

Remark 5.6
.

In §
5.2
, we work over the generic fiber, so all arguments work without assumptions. In §
5.3
, we assume Axiom
5.1
holds for certain compactifications; however, the only condition we will need is a stratification with formal completions described by
toric schemes
. In §
5.4
, we will further impose Axiom
4.2
to define canonical integral models of compactifications; as we just mentioned, one does not have to require the stronger conditions in §
5.4
to show the canonical extension of shtukas to integral models of toroidal compactifications.

5.2.
Canonical extensions on generic fiber

Let
(G,X)
be a Shimura datum and
\Sigma
be smooth and projective with respect to
K
. On
\mathrm{Sh}^{\Sigma}_{K}(G,X)
, we will explain that the pro-Kummer-étale
\underline{\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-torsor induces a log shtuka with a good description at the boundary.

5.2.1.

Let
\mathbb{P}_{K}:=\mathbb{P}_{K}(G,X)
and
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}}:=\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
be the pro-étale torsor defined as in Definition
3.13
.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
\Phi_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\gamma,q_{2})_{K}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Phi_{2}
be an equivalence between cusp label representatives in
\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
(see
[
58
, §2.1.14]
). Then we have a canonical isomorphism
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}\to\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
that makes the diagram commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}},D_{\Phi_{2}}).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

Similar statements hold for
\mathbb{P}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi_{i}}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi_{i}}}(\overline{P}_{\Phi_{i}},\overline{D}_{\Phi_{i}})
and
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi_{i},h}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{i},h}}(G_{\Phi_{i},h},D_{\Phi_{i},h})
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that, at each normal subgroup
K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}
, we have a canonical dashed morphism fitting into the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{1}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{2}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}},D_{\Phi_{2}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}},D_{\Phi_{2}}),}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

where
K^{\prime}=K_{p}^{\prime}K^{p}
and
K=K_{p}K^{p}
. Recall that
\gamma g_{1}=q_{2}g_{2}k
for some
k\in K
, we decompose
(\gamma,q_{2})_{K}
as

(5.3)

\Phi_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\gamma,1)_{K}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Phi_{1}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1,q_{2})_{K}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Phi_{2}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1,k)_{K}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Phi_{2},

where
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}=(Q_{\Phi_{2}},X_{\Phi_{2}}^{+},\gamma g_{1})
,
\Phi_{2}^{\prime}=(Q_{\Phi_{2}},X_{\Phi_{2}}^{+},g_{2}k)
.

(\gamma,1)_{K}
: Conjugation by
\gamma
gives an isomorphism
[\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(\gamma)]:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1}^{\prime}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}^{\prime}},D_{\Phi_{1}^{\prime}})
, similarly at
K^{\prime}
-level.

(1,q_{2})_{K}
: Since
(\gamma g_{1})=(q_{2})(g_{2}k)
,
K_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}}=q_{2}^{-1}K_{\Phi_{1}^{\prime}}q_{2}
, right multiplication by
q_{2}
gives an isomorphism
[\cdot q_{2}]:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1}^{\prime}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}^{\prime}},D_{\Phi_{1}^{\prime}})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}},D_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}})
, similarly at
K^{\prime}
-level since
K_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}}^{\prime}=q_{2}^{-1}K_{\Phi_{1}^{\prime}}^{\prime}q_{2}
.

(1,k)_{K}
: Note that
K_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}}=K_{\Phi_{2}}
. The identity morphism induces
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}},D_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}})\cong\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}},D_{\Phi_{2}})
, similarly at
K^{\prime}
-level since
K^{\prime}
is normal in
K
and
K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}}=K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{2}}
.

To summarize, we have a commutative diagram induced by (
5.3
):

(5.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{1}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{1}^{\prime}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}^{\prime}},D_{\Phi_{1}^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}},D_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi_{2}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}},D_{\Phi_{2}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}},D_{\Phi_{1}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{1}^{\prime}}}(P_{\Phi_{1}^{\prime}},D_{\Phi_{1}^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}},D_{\Phi_{2}^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}},D_{\Phi_{2}}).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(\gamma)]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\cdot q_{2}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(\gamma)]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\cdot q_{2}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

The statements for
\mathbb{P}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi_{i}}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi_{i}}}(\overline{P}_{\Phi_{i}},\overline{D}_{\Phi_{i}})
and
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi_{i},h}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{i},h}}(G_{\Phi_{i},h},D_{\Phi_{i},h})
are proved in the same way.
∎

Take
\Phi_{1}=\Phi_{2}=\Phi
. Then
\gamma
belongs to

\Delta_{\Phi,K}:=(\mathrm{Stab}_{Q(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\cap P(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1})/P(\mathbb{Q}).

For each
\gamma\in\Delta_{\Phi,K}
, we find a
q_{2}\in P(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that
\Phi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\gamma,q_{2})_{K}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Phi
. In particular,
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
naturally acts on the tower

(5.5)

\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}(\overline{P}_{\Phi},\overline{D}_{\Phi})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h}),

and by Lemma
5.7
,
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
-action naturally lifts to the tower

(5.6)

\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\mathbb{P}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}\to\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi,h}}.

Proposition 5.8
.

The commutative diagrams (
3.8
) and (
3.9
) in Lemma
3.15
and Corollary
3.16
are equivariant under the
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
-action.

Proof.

We work with
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
; the other two follow in the same way. This essentially follows from Lemma
5.7
, but we spell out the details for completeness. We abbreviate
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
to
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}
. Let
x\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}
be a closed point. The supported shtuka
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}^{\Diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}^{c}_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}^{c},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}
at
x
is determined by the Hodge–Tate period map
\mathrm{HT}_{\Phi,x}:\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi},x}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{P^{c}_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}^{c}}
. This map is determined by the de Rham
\underline{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-torsor
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi},x}
itself, which in turn is determined by the Galois representation
\rho_{x}:\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{k(x)}|k(x))\to\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. We now recall the construction of
\rho_{x}
, following
[
48
, §3.1]
.

Fix a lifting
x\in\mathrm{Sh}_{\varnothing_{\Phi}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}):=\mathrm{Sh}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, with image
xK_{\Phi}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
at each level
K_{\Phi}^{\prime}\subset K_{\Phi}
. Let
S_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime},x}
be the geometrically connected component of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}
containing
xK_{\Phi}^{\prime}
; it is defined over some abelian extension
E_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime},x}
of
E
. For an open normal subgroup
K_{\Phi}^{\prime}\subset K_{\Phi}
, we have Cartesian squares of Galois coverings under the right action of
K_{\Phi}/Z(P_{\Phi})(\mathbb{Q})^{-}_{K_{\Phi}}K_{\Phi}^{\prime}
:

(5.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime}}\otimes_{E}E_{K_{\Phi},x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}}S_{K_{\Phi},x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widetilde{S}_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime},x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}\otimes_{E}E_{K_{\Phi},x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{S_{K_{\Phi},x},}}

where
\widetilde{S}_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime},x}\subset\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime}}\otimes_{E}E_{K_{\Phi},x}
is the connected component that contains
xK_{\Phi}^{\prime}
. Let
\overline{x}
be a geometric point over
x
, then the above diagram induces a morphism

\pi_{1}(S_{K_{\Phi},x},\bar{x})\to\mathrm{Aut}(\widetilde{S}_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime},x}/S_{K_{\Phi},x})^{\mathrm{op}}\subset K_{\Phi}/Z(P_{\Phi})(\mathbb{Q})^{-}_{K_{\Phi}}K_{\Phi}^{\prime}.

Taking the inverse limit, and specializing it to
(\operatorname{Spec}E_{K_{\Phi},x},\bar{s})
, we have

\widetilde{\rho}_{x}:\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{k(x)}|k(x))\to\pi_{1}(S_{K_{\Phi},x},\bar{x})\to K_{\Phi}/Z(P_{\Phi})(\mathbb{Q})^{-}_{K_{\Phi}}.

The projection of
\widetilde{\rho}_{x}
to
p
-factor
\mathcal{P}^{c}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is
\rho_{x}
.

Let
\gamma\in\Delta_{\Phi,K}
. Let
y=\gamma x\in\mathrm{Sh}_{\varnothing_{\Phi}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, as in Lemma
5.7
,
[\gamma]
acts compatibly on the diagram (
5.7
), thus
\widetilde{\rho}_{x}
and
\widetilde{\rho}_{y}
are conjugated by
[\gamma]
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{\rho}_{x}:\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{k(x)}|k(x))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{1}(S_{K_{\Phi},x},\bar{x})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Aut}(\widetilde{S}_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime},x}/S_{K_{\Phi},x})^{\mathrm{op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{K_{\Phi}/Z(P_{\Phi})(\mathbb{Q})^{-}_{K_{\Phi}}K_{\Phi}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{\rho}_{y}:\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{k(y)}|k(y))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{1}(S_{K_{\Phi},y},\bar{y})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Aut}(\widetilde{S}_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime},y}/S_{K_{\Phi},y})^{\mathrm{op}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{K_{\Phi}/Z(P_{\Phi})(\mathbb{Q})^{-}_{K_{\Phi}}K_{\Phi}^{\prime}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\gamma]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\gamma]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(q_{2}^{-1}\gamma)}

We fix a representation
\rho_{\Phi}:P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to P_{\Phi}^{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\operatorname{GL}(W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
, and a lattice
W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\subset W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
such that
\rho_{\Phi}(\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))\subset\operatorname{GL}(W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
, this produces a de Rham local system
\mathbb{L}_{\rho_{\Phi},W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}
as in (
3.6
). For simplicity, we denote
\mathbb{L}:=\mathbb{L}_{\rho_{\Phi},W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}
. The canonical isomorphism
\gamma:\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi},x}\to\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi},y}
induces an isomorphism
\gamma:\mathbb{L}_{x}\to\mathbb{L}_{y}
. We identify the underlying
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local systems
\mathbb{L}_{x}\to\mathbb{L}_{y}
, then the Galois action on
\mathbb{L}_{y}
is twisted by
[\gamma]
-conjugation from the one on
\mathbb{L}_{x}
since
\widetilde{\rho}_{x}
and
\widetilde{\rho}_{y}
are conjugated by
[\gamma]
. By the Cartan-Leray spectral sequence, the identification
\mathbb{L}_{x}\cong\mathbb{L}_{y}
induces a canonical identification
(D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{L}_{x}),\nabla_{\mathbb{L}_{x}},\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathbb{L}_{x}})\cong(D_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathbb{L}_{y}),\nabla_{\mathbb{L}_{y}},\mathrm{Fil}_{\mathbb{L}_{y}})
. In particular, in the construction
[
66
, Prop. 2.6.3]
,
[\gamma]
-action identifies
\mathrm{DRT}(\mathbb{L}_{x})
and
\mathrm{DRT}(\mathbb{L}_{y})
. Therefore,
[\gamma]
induces an equivariant action on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\Diamond}\to[\operatorname{Gr}_{P^{c}_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}^{c,-1}}/\underline{\mathcal{P}^{c}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}]
where it acts trivially on the target. Finally, since
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
-action on
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}
lifts canonically to a
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
-action on
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}}
as in Lemma
5.7
, we have a cocycle condition for the identifications among
\{\mathrm{DRT}(\mathbb{L}_{\gamma(x)})\}_{\gamma\in\Delta_{\Phi,K}}
,
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
acts on the diagram
(\ref{eq: reduction, generic fiber, general})
.
∎

5.2.2.

Recall that we have

\mathfrak{X}_{\sigma,\eta}^{\circ}:=\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash(\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma))_{\underset{\tau\in\Sigma^{+}(\Phi),\ \tau\subset\sigma}{\cup}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi},\tau}}^{\wedge}\cong(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma})_{\underset{\tau\in\Sigma^{+}(\Phi),\ \tau\subset\sigma}{\cup}\mathrm{Z}_{[(\Phi,\tau)]}}^{\wedge}.

Here
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\subset\Delta_{\Phi,K}
is the subgroup that stabilizes
\sigma
; when
K
is neat, it is independent of the choice of
\sigma
(see
[
69
, Thm. 6.19]
and also
[
58
, §2.1.19]
).

We first consider the torsor defined by the tower
\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}}\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K^{\prime}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi}}
.
Denote by
ZP_{\Phi}
the connected component of
Z_{G}\cdot P_{\Phi}
, and by
ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})
. When
K
is neat, by
[
83
, Lem. 1.11]
, we have that

\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(\mathbb{C})=ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}\backslash D_{\Phi}\times ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}.

Then the Galois group is
\mathscr{G}_{p}^{\circ}(\Phi,K):=\operatorname{Gal}(\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K^{\prime}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime}}/\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}})

(5.8)
