\begin{split}&=\operatorname{Gal}(\frac{\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}\backslash D_{\Phi}\times ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}K^{\prime}g_{\Phi}^{-1}}{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}\backslash D_{\Phi}\times ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1}})\\
&=\frac{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}K_{p}g_{\Phi}^{-1}}{(Z(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{\ }}\cap g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1})(ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}K^{p}g_{\Phi}^{-1})}\\
&=\frac{ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}K^{p}g_{\Phi}^{-1}\cap g_{\Phi}K_{p}g_{\Phi}^{-1}}{(Z(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{}}\cap g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1})g_{\Phi}K^{p}g_{\Phi}^{-1}\cap g_{\Phi}K_{p}g_{\Phi}^{-1}}\\
&=g_{\Phi}\frac{(g_{\Phi}^{-1}ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi})K^{p}\cap K_{p}}{(Z(\mathbb{Q})^{\overline{}}\cap K)K^{p}\cap K_{p}}g_{\Phi}^{-1}.\end{split}

The image of the numerator of
\mathscr{G}_{p}^{\circ}(\Phi,K)
in
ZP^{c}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=(ZP_{\Phi})^{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
lies in
P^{*}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
; this is also due to neatness and the fact that
ZP^{c}_{\Phi}/P_{\Phi}^{*}
is a cuspidal torus.

Definition 5.9
.

Denote
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}}^{*}:=\varprojlim_{K_{\Phi,p}^{\prime}}\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K^{\prime}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}_{\Phi}}\times^{\mathscr{G}_{p}^{\circ}(\Phi,K)}\underline{\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
.

Then, by Proposition
5.8
, the quotient induced by the action of
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}
descends
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}}\times^{\underline{\mathcal{P}^{c}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}}\underline{\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
to
\mathbb{P}^{*}_{K_{\Phi}}
; the Hodge-Tate map
\mathrm{HT}_{K_{\Phi}}
descends to

\mathrm{HT}_{K_{\Phi}}^{*}:\mathbb{P}^{*}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\operatorname{Gr}_{P^{*}_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}},

where
\mu_{\Phi}^{*}
is the projection of
\mu_{\Phi}
to
P^{*}_{\Phi}
.
More precisely,

Lemma 5.10
.

The commutative diagram (
3.9
) descends to the commutative diagram:

(5.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}(\overline{P}_{\Phi},\overline{D}_{\Phi})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\mu_{\Phi}^{*},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\Phi}^{*},\bar{\mu}_{\Phi}^{*},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*},\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*},\delta=1,\operatorname{Spd}E}.}}

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
5.8
, Lemma
1.48
and Proposition
1.45
.
∎

Let
\mathfrak{W}=\operatorname{Spf}(R,I)\subset\mathfrak{X}_{\sigma,\eta}^{\circ}
be an affine open formal subscheme, we can consider the flat morphisms

W=\operatorname{Spec}R\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}
and
W\to\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
.
Let
W^{0}\subset W
be the common open subscheme associated with
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}\subset\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}
and
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}\subset\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma)
. We have flat morphisms
W^{0}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)
and
W^{0}\to\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
.

We pull
\mathbb{P}_{K}
and
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}}^{*}
back to
W^{0}
, and denote them by
\mathbb{P}_{K,W^{0}}
and
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi},W^{0}}
, respectively. Now we compare these two pro-étale torsors.

Lemma 5.11
.

\mathbb{P}_{K,W^{0}}\cong\underline{\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\times^{\underline{\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}}\mathbb{P}^{*}_{K_{\Phi},W^{0}}
, where
\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is the composition of
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g^{-1}_{\Phi})}g_{\Phi}^{-1}\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})g_{\Phi}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Proof.

Note that
g_{\Phi}^{-1}\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})g_{\Phi}\to\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is injective by Definition
3.11
.
Let
K^{\prime}\subset K
. The cone decomposition of the toroidal compactification at level
K^{\prime}
is the cone decomposition induced by
\Sigma
, which is denoted by the same symbol.
There is a transition map
\pi_{K^{\prime},K}^{\Sigma}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}^{\Sigma}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}
. Pick a stratum
\mathrm{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
. The preimage of
\mathrm{Z}_{[(\Phi,\sigma)],K}
under
\pi^{\Sigma}_{K^{\prime},K}
is the disjoint union

\coprod_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\to[(\Phi,\sigma)]}\mathrm{Z}_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})],K^{\prime}},

where
[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]
are cusp labels with cones in
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K^{\prime}}(G,X,\Sigma)
that map to
[(\Phi,\sigma)]
in
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)
.

Let
\mathfrak{W}=\operatorname{Spf}R\subset\mathfrak{X}^{\circ}_{\sigma,\eta}
be an open formal subscheme. Let
\mathfrak{W}^{\prime}=\operatorname{Spf}R^{\prime}
be the pullback via
\pi^{\Sigma}_{K^{\prime},K}
. Denote
W:=\spec R
and
W^{\prime}:=\spec R^{\prime}
. We then have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}^{\Sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\coprod\limits_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\to[(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime},K^{\prime}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}(\sigma^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma),}

where both squares are Cartesian.

Recall that, fixing
Q_{\Phi}
, the set
I_{K}(Q_{\Phi},\Sigma)
(see
[
83
, Def. 1.32]
) consists of the cusp labels with cones
[(\Phi_{1},\sigma_{1})]
equivalent to
[(Q_{\Phi},X_{\Phi}^{+,\prime},g_{\Phi}^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]
for some
(X_{\Phi}^{+,\prime},g_{\Phi}^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
in
\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)
; we add subscript
K
here to emphasize the role of
K
in the equivalence relation. By
[
83
, Prop. 1.4]
and the paragraph above
[
83
, Def. 1.16]
,

I_{K}(Q_{\Phi},\Sigma)\cong\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi})\backslash(\Sigma^{+}(\Phi)\times P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\backslash G(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K).

From this, we see that
K
permutes all
[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]
mapping to
[(\Phi,\sigma)]
.
Restricting to
W^{0}
, the diagram above gives

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\prime,0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\coprod\limits_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\to[(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime},K^{\prime}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sh}_{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}},}

where both squares are Cartesian.
Now, we shrink
K_{p}
and take the inverse limit. The pushout of the inverse limit of
[(\Phi,\sigma)]\to[(\Phi,\sigma)]
on the right vertical arrow gives rise to
\mathbb{P}^{*}_{K_{\Phi}}
.

We need to see that on
W^{0}
,
\mathbb{P}_{K,W^{0}}\cong\underline{\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\times^{\underline{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}}\mathbb{P}^{*}_{K_{\Phi},W^{0}}
.
For this, we compute that
K_{p}
acts transitively on
\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}}I_{K^{\prime}}(Q_{\Phi},\Sigma)
with stabilizer
(g_{\Phi}^{-1}\mathrm{Stab}_{Q_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})}(D_{\Phi},\sigma)P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}K^{p})\cap K_{p}
at
[(\Phi,\sigma)]
. When
K
is neat, this intersection is independent of the choice of
\sigma\in\Sigma^{+}(\Phi)
and is equal to
(g_{\Phi}^{-1}ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})_{1}P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})g_{\Phi}K^{p})\cap K_{p}
.

We finally compute that on
W^{0}
:
[\varprojlim\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}]\times^{\mathscr{G}^{\circ}_{p}(\Phi,K)}\underline{\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}

\begin{split}&=[\varprojlim\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}]\times^{\mathscr{G}^{\circ}_{p}(\Phi,K)}\underline{K_{p}/(K_{p}\cap K^{p}Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K}^{\overline{\ }})}\times^{\underline{K_{p}/(K_{p}\cap K^{p}Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K}^{\overline{\ }})}}\underline{\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\\
&=[\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\coprod_{[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]\to[(\Phi,\sigma)]}\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi^{\prime},K^{\prime}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}]\times^{\underline{K_{p}/(K_{p}\cap K^{p}Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K}^{\overline{\ }})}}\underline{\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\\
&=\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\prime}}\times^{\underline{K_{p}/(K_{p}\cap K^{p}Z(\mathbb{Q})_{K}^{\overline{\ }})}}\underline{\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}.\end{split}

The only nontrivial part is the computation from the second line to the third line. This is done by comparing (
5.8
) with the stabilizer in the last paragraph; note that there is a
g_{\Phi}
-conjugation difference in the construction.
∎

Lemma 5.12
.

We have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi},W^{0}}^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{P}_{K,W^{0}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{P_{\Phi}^{*},\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}_{G^{c},\mu^{c,-1}}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{HT}_{K_{\Phi}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{HT}_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Int}(g_{\Phi}^{-1})}

Proof.

Recall that, in (
3.6
), for any representation
\rho:G^{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\operatorname{GL}(V_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
and a lattice
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\subset V_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
such that
\rho(\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))\subset\operatorname{GL}(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
,
\rho
induces a morphism
\rho_{\Phi}:P_{\Phi}^{*}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\operatorname{GL}(V_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}})
such that
\rho_{\Phi}(\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))=\rho(g_{\Phi}^{-1}(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}))g_{\Phi})\subset\operatorname{GL}(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
. Since
\mathbb{L}_{{\rho_{\Phi}},V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}},W^{0}}=\mathbb{L}_{\rho(g_{\Phi}^{-1}(-)g_{\Phi}),V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}},W^{0}}
is de Rham by
[
56
]
, the association of Hodge-Tate period map is intrinsic (see
[
66
, Prop. 2.6.3]
). This gives the desired commutative diagram.
∎

Corollary 5.13
.

We have the following commutative diagram

(5.10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W^{0,\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\mu^{*}_{\Phi}}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Int}(g_{\Phi}^{-1})}

Corollary 5.14
.

For any representation
(\rho,V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
of
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
where
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
is a finite free module over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, the pro-Kummer étale local system
J^{\Sigma}_{*}\mathbb{L}_{\rho,V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}},W^{0}}
has unipotent geometric monodromy in the sense of Remark
2.44
(cf.
[
18
, Def. 6.3.7]
). Hence, by
[
56
]
and Corollary
2.53
(2) and (3), there is a log shtuka
\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{can}}_{\eta}
on
(\mathrm{Sh}^{\Sigma}_{K})^{\log\Diamond}
uniquely extending the shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\eta}
on
(\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X))^{\Diamond}
associated with
\mathbb{P}_{K}
under
[
66
, Prop. 2.5.3]
.

Proof.

Note that it suffices to consider the mixed Shimura varieties in the form of

\varprojlim_{K_{p}^{\prime}\subset K_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\prime}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi}),

where
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\prime}:=ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}K^{\prime}g_{\Phi}^{-1}
. Indeed, this follows from the description of the boundary using
ZP
-cusps (see
[
83
, (1.32)]
), and from the open and closed immersion
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K^{\prime}}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\prime}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi})
for all
K^{\prime}_{p}
by
[
83
, Lem. 1.42 (1)]
and
[
83
, Lem. 1.11]
.
For unipotence, combine Lemma
5.11
and Lemma
3.27
(the mixed Shimura variety in the lemmas is
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi})
).
Then the action of the geometric Kummer étale fundamental group factors through
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
on the pro-Kummer étale torsor
J(\sigma)_{*}\mathbb{P}^{*}_{K_{\Phi}}
on
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})(\sigma)\subset\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi})(\sigma)
. Here
J(\sigma):\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\hookrightarrow\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})(\sigma)
is the twisted toric embedding.
By the proof of Lemma
3.27
and since we are considering
P^{*}_{\Phi}
, which is a quotient from
P^{c}_{\Phi}
, we can further reduce the problem to the case where
P=P^{c}
. The torus action is defined by (
3.14
) and (
3.15
). When
K_{p}
varies, the action of the torus part is given by the action of a conjugate of a unipotent element
u_{f}\in U(\mathbb{A}_{f})
as explained at the beginning of §
3.4.3
.
The other statement is self-explanatory.
∎

5.3.
Canonical extensions on integral models

We formulate and prove two general extension results for log shtukas. The techniques we use are consequences of toric charts but not the geometry of integral models of (open) Shimura varieties; this, in particular, enables us to show the main theorems in satisfactory generality.

5.3.1.
Gluing lemma

The rough idea of the following lemma is to glue the log shtukas using toric charts. In particular, we do not use the geometry of
\mathscr{S}_{K}
. Let
E
be the completion of the reflex field of the Shimura datum
(G,X)
that we consider.

Lemma 5.15
.

We still assume that
\Sigma
is smooth projective.
Suppose that there is an integral model
\mathscr{S}^{\Sigma}_{K}
satisfying Axiom
5.1
for
(G,X,K)
. (In fact, we only need the assumptions for toroidal compactifications.)

Choose any affine formal open subscheme
\mathfrak{W}=\operatorname{Spf}(A,I)\subset\mathfrak{X}_{\Upsilon}
. Let
{W}:=\operatorname{Spec}A
. The open embedding
j:\mathscr{S}_{K}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
induces an open embedding
j_{W}:{W}^{0}:=\mathscr{S}_{K}\times_{j,\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}}W\hookrightarrow W
, which fits into the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W}^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{W}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

Denote
\mathscr{S}:=\mathscr{S}_{K}
and
\mathcal{X}:=\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
for simplicity. We further assume:

•

There is a
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{S}}
on
\mathscr{S}^{\Diamond/}
extending the one
\mathscr{P}_{\eta}:=\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{S}}|_{\mathscr{S}_{E}}
on the generic fiber. Assume that
\mathscr{P}_{\eta}
extends (uniquely) to a log shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\eta}^{\mathrm{can}}
on
\mathcal{X}_{E}^{\log\Diamond}
.
Then there is a canonical isomorphism
\theta_{\eta}:i^{0,*}_{E}\mathscr{P}_{\eta}\cong i^{0,*}_{E}j^{*}_{E}\mathscr{P}_{\eta}^{\mathrm{can}}\xrightarrow{\sim}j_{W,E}^{*}i^{*}_{E}\mathscr{P}_{\eta}^{\mathrm{can}}
on
W^{0,\Diamond}_{E}
.

•

For any
\mathfrak{W}
, there is a log
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{W}
on
{W}^{\log\diamond}
extending
i^{*}_{E}\mathscr{P}_{\eta}^{\mathrm{can}}
, such that there is a unique isomorphism

\theta:i^{0,*}\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{S}}\xrightarrow{\sim}j^{*}_{W}\mathscr{P}_{W}

on
W^{0,\diamond}
extending
\theta_{\eta}
.

Then there is a unique log
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{can}}
on
\mathcal{X}^{\log\diamond}=\mathcal{X}^{\log\Diamond}
extending both
\mathscr{P}_{\eta}^{\mathrm{can}}
and
\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{S}}
.

Proof.

Induction on the dimension of
\sigma
in the pair
\Upsilon=[(\Phi,\sigma)]
. Note that
\dim\sigma=\mathrm{codim}\ \mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon,K}
. When
\sigma
is trivial, by assumption, there is a shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\mathscr{S}}
.

Assume that there is an extension of log shtuka
\mathscr{P}^{k}
on
\mathcal{X}^{k,\log\Diamond/}
, whose underlying scheme
\mathcal{X}^{k}
is set-theoretically the union of all strata
\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon}
of codimension
\leq k
. The strata of codimension exactly
k+1
are disjoint in
\mathcal{X}
. So we add one more stratum
\Upsilon^{\prime}:=[(\Phi^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})]
to
\mathcal{X}^{k}
and show that there is an extension of the log shtuka on
(\mathcal{X}^{k}\cup\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}})^{\log\Diamond/}
or
(\mathcal{X}^{k}\cup\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}})^{\log\diamond}
. (Here
\mathcal{X}^{k}\cup\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}}
denotes the normal subscheme in
\mathcal{X}
.)

Pick any affine open subscheme
V
of
\mathcal{X}
that is contained in
\mathcal{X}^{k}\cup\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}}
and intersects with
\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}}
.
After taking a suitable étale cover and taking an affine open subscheme again, there is an affine scheme
U=\spec B
and étale morphisms
\mathsf{e}_{1}:U\to V=\spec A
and
\mathsf{e}_{2}:U\to T=\spec R\times\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi^{\prime}}}(\sigma^{\prime})
for some normal flat
R
, such that the pullbacks of the stratifications on
V
and
T
via
\mathsf{e}_{1}
and
\mathsf{e}_{2}
coincide on
U
. Indeed, this follows from a refinement of Artin’s approximation theorem (see
[
83
, Prop. 4.53]
). We can choose finitely many such
U
covering
\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}}
.

Denote by
\tau_{1}
, …,
\tau_{k+1}
the
1
-dimensional faces of
\sigma^{\prime}
. Denote by
D_{i}
the divisor on
U
defined by
\mathsf{e}_{2}^{-1}(T_{\tau_{i}})
for
1\leq i\leq(k+1)
; denote by
U_{i}
the complement
U\backslash D_{i}
. Denote
U^{\prime}:=\bigcup_{i=1}^{k+1}U_{i}
.

By construction,
D_{i}
is defined by a principal ideal
I_{i}=(s_{i})\subset B
. Denote by
I
the ideal generated by
I_{i}
for all
i
. Denote by
\mathbf{V}
the completion of
V
along the closed stratum
Z\subset V
corresponding to
T_{\sigma^{\prime}}
; the stratum
Z
corresponds to an ideal
J
. Then
\spec\widehat{A}_{J}\times_{V}U=\spec\widehat{B}_{I}
and
\mathsf{e}_{1}^{-1}V(J)=V(I)=\mathsf{e}_{2}^{-1}T_{\sigma^{\prime}}
. Denote
\mathbf{U}:=\spec\widehat{B}_{I}
and
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}:=\spec\widehat{B}_{I}\backslash V(I)
.

We now claim that there is an isomorphism between
v
-sheaves

(5.11)

(U^{\prime})^{\log\diamond}\coprod_{(\mathbf{U}^{\prime})^{\log\diamond}}\mathbf{U}^{\log\diamond}\cong U^{\log\diamond}.

Here, the LHS is defined as the
v
-sheaf gluing
(U^{\prime})^{\log\diamond}
and
\mathbf{U}^{\log\diamond}
. The map from left to right is injective. Since both side are
v
-sheaves by Theorem
2.18
, for any affinoid
S^{\sharp}\in\operatorname{Perfd}
, it suffices to check surjectivity over a
v
-cover.
For this, let
Z=\operatorname{Spa}(\prod_{t\in\mathcal{T}}C_{t}^{+}[1/\varpi_{t}],\prod_{t\in\mathcal{T}}C_{t}^{+})\to S^{\sharp}
be a
v
-cover constructed by a product of points, where
C_{t}^{+}[1/\varpi_{t}]
are complete algebraically closed non-archimedean fields and
C_{t}^{+}
are open and bounded valuation rings (see
[
25
, Ex. 1.1, Def. 1.2]
). This cover is a strictly totally disconnected perfectoid space (see
[
25
, Prop. 1.6]
), so all points are analytic.

For each
C_{t}^{+}
, if there is a map
f:\spec C_{t}^{+}\to U
, then the map factors through either
\mathbf{U}
or
U^{\prime}
according to the valuations of
\{s_{i}\}
are all in the maximal ideal of
C^{+}_{t}
or not: Indeed, for any
(C_{t}^{+}[1/\varpi_{t}],C^{+}_{t})
, the valuation
v_{t}:C_{t}^{+}[1/\varpi_{t}]\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
is given by the
\varpi_{t}
-topology by
[
75
, Lem. 4.2.2, Def. 4.2.3]
. Therefore, for any
s_{i}\in C^{+}_{t}
,
s_{i}
lies in the maximal ideal if and only if
v_{t}(s_{i})<1
if and only if the whole
\widehat{B}_{(s_{i})}
maps into
C_{t}^{+}
. If
v_{t}(s_{i})=1
then
s_{i}^{-1}\in C^{+}_{t}
.

For any
(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}})\in U^{\log\diamond}
, we pullback the log structure to
Z
and denote it by
\mathcal{M}
. Then the log structure will also factor through
U^{\prime}
or
\mathbf{U}
, since the log structures on
U^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{U}
are pulled back from the one on
U
. The claim is proved.

Note that there is a log shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\mathbf{U}}
on
(\mathbf{U})^{\log\diamond}
given by pulling back the log shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\mathbf{V}}
on
(\mathbf{V})^{\log\diamond}
and a log shtuka
\mathscr{P}^{k}_{U^{\prime}}
given by pulling back
\mathscr{P}^{k}
. The restrictions of both
\mathscr{P}^{k}_{U^{\prime}}
and
\mathscr{P}_{\mathbf{U}}
to
(\mathbf{U}^{\prime})^{\log\diamond}
both extend the pullback of
\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{can}}_{\eta}
to
(\mathbf{U}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\prime})^{\log\Diamond}
. Now it follows from Theorem
2.55
that there is a canonical isomorphism between
\mathscr{P}^{k}_{U^{\prime}}
and
\mathscr{P}_{\mathbf{U}}
on
(\mathbf{U}^{\prime})^{\log\diamond}
. (Here we can use Theorem
2.55
because
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
is a finite union of spectra of excellent Noetherian normal flat domains; for the fact that the completion is still excellent in this case, see
[
51
, Appendix A]
, especially the table in the end.)
Combining (
5.11
) in the last paragraph with Proposition
2.34
, we extend the log shtuka to
U
. Taking an étale cover of
U
’s constructed as the third paragraph and by Theorem
2.55
and the étale descent of shtukas, we obtain an extension
\mathscr{P}^{k,\Upsilon^{\prime}}
on
(\mathcal{X}^{k}\cup\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon^{\prime}})^{\log\diamond}
.

Now, we choose another stratum
\Upsilon^{\prime\prime}=[(\Phi^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime})]
with codimension
(k+1)
and repeat the construction above. Since there are only finitely many such strata and finitely many choice of
k
, we finally construct an extension on
(\mathcal{X}^{k+1})^{\log\diamond}
, and we are done by induction.
∎

5.3.2.
Extension along an affine toric embedding

Fix
\mathbf{E}
a split torus over
\mathbb{Z}
. Let
N=\mathbf{X}_{*}(\mathbf{E})
and
M=\mathbf{X}^{*}(\mathbf{E})
. Let
\sigma\subset N\otimes\mathbb{R}
be a smooth convex polyhedral cone, and
\sigma^{\vee}\subset M\otimes\mathbb{R}
be its dual. We consider the toric embedding
\mathbf{E}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{E}(\sigma)=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[\sigma^{\vee}\cap M]
.
We can naturally endow
\mathbf{E}(\sigma)
with a log structure associated with
D_{\sigma}=\mathbf{E}(\sigma)\backslash\mathbf{E}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{E}(\sigma)
, and we denote it by
(\mathbf{E}(\sigma),\mathcal{M}_{\sigma})
, with chart
\mathsf{P}_{\sigma}\to\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}
associated with the monoid
\sigma^{\vee}\cap M/(\sigma^{\vee}\cap M)^{\times}
.

Let
Y
be a normal scheme that is separated, flat, and of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
; we also assume that the generic fiber of
Y
is smooth.

Let
X
be an
\mathbf{E}
-torsor over
Y
. The twisted toric embedding
X\hookrightarrow X(\sigma):=X\times^{\mathbf{E}}\mathbf{E}(\sigma)
is equipped with a log structure associated with the chart
\mathsf{P}_{\sigma}
.

Let
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}:=\varprojlim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbf{E}=\varprojlim\mathbf{E}_{n}
, where
\mathbf{E}_{n}
denotes
\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[\frac{1}{p^{n}}M]
. Write
\mathbf{E}_{n}(\sigma)=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[\sigma^{\vee}\cap\frac{1}{p^{n}}M]
and
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\sigma)=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}[\sigma^{\vee}\cap M[\frac{1}{p}]]
.

Note that
X\hookrightarrow X(\sigma)
is equipped with an equivariant
\mathbf{E}
-action over
Y
. We write this action as
\mathbb{E}:\mathbf{E}\times X\to X
and
\mathbb{E}(\sigma):\mathbf{E}\times X(\sigma)\to X(\sigma)
. Pre-composing with
p_{\infty}\times\mathrm{id}:\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times X\to\mathbf{E}\times X
(resp.
p_{\infty}\times\mathrm{id}:\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times X(\sigma)\to\mathbf{E}\times X(\sigma)
), where
p_{\infty}:\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\to\mathbf{E}
is the canonical projection, we obtain an
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}
-action
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}:\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\times X\to X
(resp.
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}(\sigma):\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\times X(\sigma)\to X(\sigma)
); the actions
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
and
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}(\sigma)
are equivariant with respect to
X\hookrightarrow X(\sigma)
.

Construction 5.16
.

The cover
X\to Y
is faithfully flat. There is a canonical isomorphism between schemes
i:\mathbf{E}\times X\xrightarrow{\sim}X\times_{Y}X
; for any
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebra
R
,
e\in\mathbf{E}(R)
,
x\in X(R)
, and
y:=e\cdot x
,
i
sends
(e,x)
to
(x,y)
.
The descent datum
\varphi:X\times_{Y}X\xrightarrow{\sim}X\times_{Y}X
for the cover
X\to Y
is an isomorphism
\varphi=((-\mathrm{id})\circ p_{1}\times\mathbb{E}):\mathbf{E}\times X\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{E}\times X
after pulled back via
i
.

Let
i_{\infty}:=i\circ(p_{\infty}\times\mathrm{id})
. We also pull back
\varphi
to an isomorphism of
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times X
; i.e., we define
\varphi_{\infty}:=((-\mathrm{id})\circ p_{1}\times\mathbb{E}_{\infty}):\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times X\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times X
.

For toric embeddings, we can also define

\varphi_{\infty}(\sigma):=((-\mathrm{id})\circ p_{1}\times\mathbb{E}_{\infty}(\sigma)):\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\times X(\sigma)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\times X(\sigma).

Similarly, we define
\varphi(\sigma)
by projection to
\mathbf{E}
.
The action of
\mathbf{E}
on
X(\sigma)
is not free if
\sigma^{\vee}\cap M/(\sigma^{\vee}\cap M)^{\times}
is nontrivial; in this case
\varphi(\sigma)
is not an fpqc descent datum.
\square

Definition 5.17
.

Let
(\mathcal{G},\mu)
be as in §
1.3
, where
\mathcal{G}
is a quasi-parahoric model of a possibly non-reductive linear algebraic group (it was denoted as
\mathcal{P}
in §
1.3
).
Let
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
be a
\mathcal{G}
-shtuka (bounded by
\mu
) on
X^{\diamond}
. We say that there is an (equivariant)
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
-action on
\mathscr{P}
if there is an isomorphism

\varphi(\mathscr{P}):p_{2}^{*}(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{*}(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})

on
(\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times X)^{\diamond}
that satisfies the following conditions:

(1)

Let
s:X\to\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\times X
be the identity section of
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}
. Then
s^{*}\varphi(\mathscr{P})=\mathrm{id}_{(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})}
;

(2)

There is a commutative diagram (we omit
\phi_{\mathscr{P}}
below):

(5.12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{23}^{*}p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{23}^{*}\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{*}\mathscr{P}=(\mathrm{id}\times\mathbb{E}_{\infty})^{*}p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id}\times\mathbb{E}_{\infty})^{*}\mathbb{E}^{*}_{\infty}\mathscr{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(m\times\mathrm{id})^{*}p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(m\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{*}\mathscr{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{23}^{*}\varphi(\mathscr{P})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id}\times\mathbb{E}_{\infty})^{*}\varphi(\mathscr{P})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(m\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\varphi(\mathscr{P})}

over the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\times\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\times X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id}\times\mathbb{E}_{\infty})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{\infty}}

Definition 5.18
.

Let
(\mathscr{P}(\sigma),\phi_{\mathscr{P}(\sigma)})
be a
\mathcal{G}
-shtuka (bounded by
\mu
) on
X(\sigma)^{\log\diamond}
. We say that there is an
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
-action on
\mathscr{P}(\sigma)
if there is an isomorphism

\varphi(\mathscr{P}(\sigma)):p_{2}^{*}(\mathscr{P}(\sigma),\phi_{\mathscr{P}(\sigma)})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{E}_{\infty}(\sigma)^{*}(\mathscr{P}(\sigma),\phi_{\mathscr{P}(\sigma)})

on
(\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times X(\sigma))^{\log\diamond}
that satisfies similar conditions above.

The following proposition was inspired by
[
32
, 4.1.1]
(cf.
[
31
, Prop. 1.3.5]
).

Theorem 5.19
.

Let
(\mathcal{G},\mu)
be as in §
1.3
. With the conventions above, there is a natural equivalence of categories between

•

The category
\mathrm{Sht}^{\diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu,\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}}(X)
of
\mathcal{G}
-shtukas
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
with one leg bounded by
\mu
on
X^{\diamond}
that are equipped with equivariant
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
-actions;

•

The category
\mathrm{Sht}^{\diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu,\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}}(X(\sigma),\mathcal{M}_{\sigma})
of log
\mathcal{G}
-shtukas
(\mathscr{P}(\sigma),\phi_{\mathscr{P}(\sigma)})
with one leg bounded by
\mu
on
X^{\log\diamond}
that are equipped with equivariant
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
-actions.

The morphisms in the two categories are those preserving the
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
-actions. In fact, we show that every object in the first category can be uniquely extended to the second one.

Note that the last statement in the theorem is stronger than saying the restriction functor is essentially surjective.

We prove this theorem in §
5.3.3
. An immediate corollary is:

Corollary 5.20
.

Let
(G,X)
be any Shimura datum. If the
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{E}
determined by
\mathbb{P}^{*}_{K_{\Phi}}
extends to a
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}
on
(\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}})^{\diamond}
, it uniquely extends to a log shtuka
\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{can}}
on
(\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma))^{\log\diamond}
.

Proof.

There is a map between
\underline{\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-torsors

\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}}^{*}\to\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi^{*}}}(P_{\Phi}^{*},D_{\Phi^{*}})

over a map

\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{*}}}(P_{\Phi}^{*},D_{\Phi^{*}}),

where
\Phi^{*}
is the one defined below Definition
3.10
.

The first scheme is a torus torsor under
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
, which corresponds to a lattice (see
[
83
, 1.3.3]
)

\bm{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi}}:=p_{2}(Z(\mathbb{Q})\times U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}Kg_{\Phi}^{-1});

since
K
is neat, this lattice maps to

\bm{\Lambda}_{K_{\Phi}}^{*}:=p_{2}((1\times U_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\cap g_{\Phi}K^{c}g_{\Phi}^{-1}).

Thus, the map

\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{*}}}(P_{\Phi}^{*},D_{\Phi^{*}})

is equivariant under the isogeny between tori
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\to\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi^{*}}}
.

Now we use the proof of Lemma
3.27
verbatim, replacing
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(P,\mathcal{X})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{c}}(P^{c},\mathcal{X}^{c})
with
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{*}}}(P_{\Phi}^{*},D_{\Phi^{*}})
. We then see that there is an
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\infty}:=\varprojlim_{x\mapsto x^{p}}\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
-action. That is, there is a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\infty}\times\mathbb{P}^{*}_{K_{\Phi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{*}_{K_{\Phi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}\times\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}.}

Thus, by
[
66
, Thm. 2.7.7]
and the Tannakian formalism,
\mathscr{P}
is equipped with an
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\infty}^{\prime}
-action. Note that although
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\infty}\times\mathscr{S}
is not of finite type, the argument in
loc. cit.
still works. Indeed, denote
\mathscr{S}:=\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}
. It suffices to work with an affine open
\operatorname{Spec}A\subset\mathscr{S}
. By the proof of
loc. cit.
, we find a perfectoid cover
\operatorname{Spa}(\widetilde{R},\widetilde{R}^{+})\to\operatorname{Spa}(\widehat{A}[1/p],\widehat{A})
by taking the completion of finite étale extensions of
\widehat{A}[1/p]
in its fraction field. Then we can replace
\operatorname{Spa}(\widetilde{R},\widetilde{R}^{+})
with the perfectoid space
\operatorname{Spa}(\widetilde{R}\langle\mathsf{R}\rangle,\widetilde{R}^{+}\langle\mathsf{R}\rangle)
(see
[
18
, Lem. 2.2.15]
), where
\mathsf{R}
is the character group of
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\infty}
; and the rest of the proof in
loc. cit.
works for
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi},\infty}\times\operatorname{Spec}A
.

We then obtain
\mathscr{P}(\sigma)
extending
\mathscr{P}
by applying Theorem
5.19
. The uniqueness follows from Corollary
2.56
.
∎

5.3.3.
Proof of Theorem
5.19

There is a natural restriction functor

\mathrm{Res}:\mathrm{Sht}^{\diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu,\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}}(X(\sigma),\mathcal{M}_{\sigma})\to\mathrm{Sht}^{\diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu,\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}}(X).

It is fully faithful by Corollary
2.56
. We show that the last statement in the theorem is true; in particular, the restriction functor is essentially surjective.

Let
\mathsf{P}:=\mathsf{P}_{\sigma}
and
\mathsf{Q}:=\sigma^{\vee}\cap M
. Let
\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}:=\mathsf{Q}[\frac{1}{p}]
.
There is a natural injective homomorphism between monoids
j:\mathsf{Q}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}
. Also, there is an injective homomorphism

\psi:\mathsf{Q}\to\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}
given by the embeddings
\mathsf{Q}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}
and
j
.

Construction 5.21
.

There is a natural coaction of
\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}_{\infty}
on
\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}
.

In fact, the action of
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
on
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\sigma)
induces a coaction of
\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}_{\infty}
on
\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}
. Explicitly, this is given by

\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\to\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}};\ x\mapsto(x,x).

Similarly, the action of
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
on
\mathbf{E}
given by
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{E}\xrightarrow{p_{\infty}\times\mathrm{id}}\mathbf{E}\times\mathbf{E}\xrightarrow{\text{multiplication}}\mathbf{E}
also induces a comultiplication
\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\to\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}}
.

Combining the two (co)actions, we obtain a map between monoids

d_{\infty}(\sigma):\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\to\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}};\ (x,y)\mapsto(x,y,-x+y).

This corresponds to an action
d_{\infty}(\sigma):\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{E}\times\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\sigma)\to\mathbf{E}\times\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\sigma)
that pointwisely sends
(g,a,b)
to
(g^{-1}a,gb)
. Note that the quotient of
\mathbf{E}\times\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\sigma)
by this action is
\mathbf{E}\times^{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\sigma)\cong\mathbf{E}(\sigma)
.

Denote by
d_{\infty}
the
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}
-action
d_{\infty}(\sigma)
restricted to
\mathbf{E}\times\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\sigma)
.
\square

We need a lemma on saturated fiber products.

Lemma 5.22
.

We have that
(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus_{j,\mathsf{Q},\psi}(\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}))^{\mathrm{sat}}\cong\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}}
. Moreover, the
\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}}
-coaction on the second factor
\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}
induces the comultiplication on the right-hand side on the second factor of
\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}}
.

Proof.

We first compute
(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus_{j,\mathsf{Q},\psi}(\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}))^{\mathrm{int}}
.
Since
\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}
is integral and
(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty})^{\mathrm{gp}}=\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}}
, by
[
62
, I. Prop. 1.3.4]
,
(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus_{j,\mathsf{Q},\psi}(\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}))^{\mathrm{int}}
is the image of
\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}
in
\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus_{j^{\mathrm{gp}},\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}},\psi^{\mathrm{gp}}}(\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}})
.
Therefore,

(5.13)

(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus_{j,\mathsf{Q},\psi}(\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}))^{\mathrm{int}}\subset(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}+\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}})\oplus_{j^{\mathrm{gp}},\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}},\psi^{\mathrm{gp}}}(\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}+\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}})),

where
(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}+\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}})
denotes the monoid in
\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}}
generated by
\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}
and
\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}
.

Denote by
(a,b,c)
an element in
\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}
. Then
(a,b,c)\sim(a+b,0,c-b)
in the RHS of (
5.13
) since
(0,b,b)\sim(b,0,0)
by amalgamated product.
Hence,
(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus_{j,\mathsf{Q},\psi}(\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}))^{\mathrm{int}}
is generated by
(a,0,0)
,
(0,0,c)
, where
a,c\in\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}
, and
(b,0,-b)
, where
b\in\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}
.

Thus,
(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus_{j,\mathsf{Q},\psi}(\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}))^{\mathrm{sat}}=\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}}
. Indeed, for any
(x,0,y)\in(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}},0,\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}})
such that
(nx,0,ny)=(a+b,0,c-b)
, by enlarging
n
, we assume that
nx
and
ny
are in
\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}
; and therefore,
a
and
c
are in
\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\cap\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}=\mathsf{Q}
.
We then write
(a+b,0,c-b)=(a+b+c-c,0,c-b)=(a+c,0,0)+(b-c,0,c-b)
. Hence,
y
can be any element in
\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}}
and
x+y
can be any element in
\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}
.
Note that the generators of
(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}\oplus_{j,\mathsf{Q},\psi}(\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}))^{\mathrm{sat}}
are
(u,0,0)\in(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty},0,0)
and
v\in\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}_{\infty}\xrightarrow{(-v,v)}(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}},0,\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}_{\infty})
.

Now we check the last sentence. The induced
\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}_{\infty}
-coaction on the RHS of (
5.13
) sends
(u,v,w)\in(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}+\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}})\oplus_{j^{\mathrm{gp}},\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}},\psi^{\mathrm{gp}}}(\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus(\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}+\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}))
to
(u,v,w,-v+w)
; note that this is compatible with the amalgamated product as the coactions on the last two factors are canceled and therefore coincide with the trivial action on the first factor. Hence, the induced coaction on
(-v,0,v)
is the comultiplication on the third factor, while the coaction on
(u,0,0)
is trivial.
∎

We now go back to the proof.
Note that

Lemma 5.23
.

It suffices to show the last statement of Theorem
5.19
over an étale cover
\widetilde{Y}
of
Y
.

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{X}:=\widetilde{Y}\times_{Y}X
and
\widetilde{X}(\sigma):=\widetilde{Y}\times_{Y}X(\sigma)
.
Suppose that there is a log shtuka
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}(\sigma)
in
\mathrm{Sht}^{\diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}}(\widetilde{X}(\sigma),\mathcal{M}_{\widetilde{X}(\sigma)})
that extends some
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
in
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}}^{\diamond}(X)
to
\widetilde{X}(\sigma)^{\log\diamond}
.
Since
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
is the pullback of some
\mathscr{P}
in
\mathrm{Sht}^{\diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}}(X)
.
By Corollary
2.56
, the descent datum of
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
from
\widetilde{X}
to
X
extends uniquely to a strict étale descent datum of
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}(\sigma)
from
\widetilde{X}(\sigma)
to
X(\sigma)
, as
\sigma
is a smooth cone decomposition and the generic fiber of
X
is smooth.
By strict étale descent of log shtukas (see Lemma
2.36
), there is a log shtuka
\mathscr{P}(\sigma)
over
(X(\sigma))^{\log\diamond}
descending
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}(\sigma)
.
∎

By Lemma
5.23
, we can assume that
X
is a trivial torsor over
Y
, and that
X\times\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\sigma)\to X(\sigma)
admits a chart
\mathsf{Q}\to\mathsf{Q}^{\mathrm{gp}}\oplus\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}
.

Let
(S^{\sharp}=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+}),\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}},f)\in(X(\sigma),\mathcal{M}_{\sigma})^{\log\diamond}(S)
, where

f:(S^{\sharp},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}})\to(S^{\sharp,+}:=\operatorname{Spec}R^{+},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp,+}})\xrightarrow{f^{+}}(X(\sigma),\mathcal{M}_{\sigma}).

We assume that
\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}=\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp}}^{\mathrm{can}}
by Proposition
2.38
. And
f
corresponds to an
R^{+}
-point of
X(\sigma)
denoted by
f^{+}:(\operatorname{Spec}R^{+},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp,+}})\to(X(\sigma),\mathcal{M}_{\sigma})
that admits a chart
\mathsf{Q}\to\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}
by Lemma
2.40
.

Consider the fiber product in the category of saturated log schemes

(5.14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{T}^{\sharp,+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X\times\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\sigma)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Spec}R^{+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X(\sigma).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{\infty}}

Lemma 5.24
.

The
p
-adic completion of
\widetilde{T}^{\sharp,+}
is the spectrum of the integral perfectoid ring
R^{+}\langle\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}}\rangle
equipped with an
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
-action.

Proof.

Recall that we can work étale locally over
Y
by Lemma
5.23
, and assume that
X=Y\times\mathbf{E}
and that
Y
is affinoid. Set
X_{\infty}(\sigma):=X(\sigma)\times_{\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathsf{Q}]}\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}].

Then

\begin{split}&(S^{\sharp,+},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp,+}})\times^{\mathrm{sat}}_{X(\sigma)}(X\times\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\sigma))\\
&=(\operatorname{Spec}R^{+},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp,+}})\times_{X_{\infty}(\sigma)}X_{\infty}(\sigma)\times^{\mathrm{sat}}_{X(\sigma)}(X\times\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\sigma))\\
&=(\operatorname{Spec}R^{+},\mathcal{M}_{S^{\sharp,+}})\times_{X_{\infty}(\sigma)}X_{\infty}(\sigma)[\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}}].\end{split}

The second line follows from Lemma
2.40
and the third line follows from Lemma
5.22
.
∎

We now finish the proof. We omit
\phi_{\mathscr{P}}
in the proof. Suppose that we are given
\mathscr{P}
in
\mathrm{Sht}^{\diamond}_{\mathcal{G},\mu,\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}}(X)
.
Set
\widetilde{T}^{\sharp}:=\operatorname{Spa}(R\langle\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}}\rangle,R^{+}\langle\mathsf{Q}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{gp}}\rangle)
, which is affinoid perfectoid by
[
18
, Lem. 2.2.5]
.

Note that there is a commutative diagram

(5.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\infty}^{\prime}:=d_{\infty}\circ(i\times\mathrm{id}\times\mathrm{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{23}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times\varphi_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{23}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}

Here,
d_{\infty}
also denotes the action of the first factor
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
on
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times X
that is a (positive) multiplication on the first factor
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
and
-\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
on the second factor
X
;
m
(resp.
i
) denotes the multiplication (resp. inverse) on
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
;
d_{\infty}^{\prime}
maps
(a,b,x)
to
(a^{-1}b,ax)
.

Let
\mathscr{P}_{2}
be the pullback of
\mathscr{P}
to
(\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\times X)^{\diamond}
along the projection
p_{2}
to the second factor. Note that
p_{2}\circ d_{\infty}^{\prime}=p_{2}\circ\varphi_{\infty}\circ p_{13}=\mathbb{E}_{\infty}\circ p_{13}
. From this, we obtain a descent datum
\varphi(\mathscr{P}_{2}):p_{23}^{*}\mathscr{P}_{2}\xrightarrow{\sim}d_{\infty}^{\prime*}\mathscr{P}_{2}
by pulling back
\varphi(\mathscr{P}):p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\varphi_{\infty}^{*}p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}=\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{*}\mathscr{P}
along
p_{13}
. The cocycle condition is given by the group action
\varphi(\mathscr{P})
.
Therefore,
\mathscr{P}_{2}
descends to
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}
on
X
with this descent datum.

On the other hand, denote by
\mathscr{P}_{3}:=\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{*}\mathscr{P}=\varphi_{\infty}^{*}p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}
, we have a standard descent datum
\varphi(\mathscr{P}_{3}):p_{23}^{*}\mathscr{P}_{3}\xrightarrow{\sim}d_{\infty}^{\prime*}\mathscr{P}_{3}
given by the pullback of
\mathscr{P}
along
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
.
Note that
\varphi(\mathscr{P})
gives an isomorphism between descent data
(\mathscr{P}_{2},\varphi(\mathscr{P}_{2}))\to(\mathscr{P}_{3},\varphi(\mathscr{P}_{3}))
, and it descends to an isomorphism
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{P}
. Indeed, define
\bm{\alpha}:\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\times\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\times X\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\times\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\times X
by the assignment
(a,b,x)\mapsto(a^{-1}b,a,x)
. Then
p_{23}\circ\bm{\alpha}=p_{13}
,
(\mathrm{id}\times\mathbb{E}_{\infty})\circ\bm{\alpha}=d^{\prime}_{\infty}
and
(m\times\mathrm{id})\circ\bm{\alpha}=p_{23}
. Pulling back (
5.12
) along
\bm{\alpha}
, we obtain a commutative diagram

(5.16)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{13}^{*}p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{13}^{*}\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{*}\mathscr{P}=d^{\prime,*}_{\infty}p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{\prime,*}_{\infty}\mathbb{E}^{*}_{\infty}\mathscr{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{23}^{*}p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{23}^{*}\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{*}\mathscr{P}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{13}^{*}\varphi(\mathscr{P})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{\prime,*}_{\infty}\varphi(\mathscr{P})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{23}^{*}\varphi(\mathscr{P})}

We write (
5.16
) above in the following form:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{*}_{3}\mathscr{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{13}^{*}\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{*}\mathscr{P}=d^{\prime,*}_{\infty}p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{23}^{*}\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{*}\mathscr{P}=(\mathrm{id}\times\varphi_{\infty})^{*}p_{3}^{*}\mathscr{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id}\times\varphi_{\infty})^{*}(m\times\mathrm{id})^{*}p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}=d^{\prime,*}_{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{*}\mathscr{P};}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{13}^{*}\varphi(\mathscr{P})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{23}^{*}\varphi(\mathscr{P})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{\prime,*}_{\infty}\varphi(\mathscr{P})}

this implies the claim that
\varphi(\mathscr{P})
is an isomorphism between the descent data of
\mathscr{P}_{2}
and
\mathscr{P}_{3}
.

Moreover, we show that
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{P}
is an automorphism of
\mathscr{P}
. Consider the canonical section
s:X\to\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times X
sending
x
to
(e,x)
, it is a section of both
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
and
p_{2}
. Then,

\mathscr{P}^{\prime}=s^{*}\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{*}\mathscr{P}^{\prime}=s^{*}\mathscr{P}_{2}=s^{*}p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{P}=\mathscr{P}.

Also, the descended automorphism
\mathscr{P}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{P}
is an identity, as it can be realized as the pullback
s^{*}\varphi(\mathscr{P}):\mathscr{P}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{P}
of the morphism
\varphi(\mathscr{P})
between the descent data; here we use the fact that the following composition is the identity:

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\to}}\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi_{\infty}}}{{\to}}\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p_{2}}}{{\to}}X.

This automorphism is an identity by the first condition in Definition
5.17
.

Next, we extend the first row of (
5.15
) to

(5.17)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}\times\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\sigma)\times X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\sigma)\times X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X(\sigma).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\infty}^{\prime}(\sigma):=d_{\infty}(\sigma)\circ(i\times\mathrm{id}\times\mathrm{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{23}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}_{\infty}(\sigma)^{\prime}}

Here,
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}(\sigma)^{\prime}
is defined by
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\sigma)\times X\to\mathbf{E}(\sigma)\times X\to\mathbf{E}(\sigma)\times^{\mathbf{E}}X=X(\sigma)
and it extends the map
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
.
Again, we equip
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}(\sigma)
with the positive multiplication of
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
and
X
with the negative action. Thus,
d_{\infty}(\sigma)
extends
d_{\infty}
(resp.
d_{\infty}^{\prime}(\sigma)
extends
d_{\infty}^{\prime}
).

Denote by
\mathscr{Q}
the pullback of
\mathscr{P}
to
(\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\sigma)\times X)^{\log\diamond}
via the projection to the second factor
X
.

For any
f^{+}:(\operatorname{Spec}R^{+},\mathcal{M}_{R^{+}})\to X(\sigma)
as above, the evaluation
\mathscr{Q}_{\widetilde{T}^{\sharp}}
of
\mathscr{Q}
at
\widetilde{T}^{\sharp}\to\widetilde{T}^{\sharp,+}\to X\times\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\sigma)
is equipped with an
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
-action given by the pullback of
d_{\infty}^{\prime}(\sigma)
by construction and by Lemma
5.24
.
By
v
-descent of shtukas (see
[
75
, Prop. 19.5.3]
), the shtuka
\mathscr{Q}_{\widetilde{T}^{\sharp}}
descends to a shtuka
\mathscr{Q}_{S^{\sharp}}
.

This assignment can be easily checked to be functorial, and we denote the obtained shtuka on
X(\sigma)^{\log\diamond}
by
\mathscr{P}(\sigma)
; the
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
-action on
\mathscr{P}(\sigma)
is descended from that of
\mathscr{Q}
by the multiplication action of
\mathbf{E}_{\infty}^{\prime}
on
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}(\sigma)
. Moreover, when we restrict this construction to
(\mathbf{E}^{\prime}_{\infty}\times X)^{\diamond}
, we have seen that we obtain a shtuka
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}=\mathscr{P}
.
Now we have completed the proof of last statement in Theorem
5.19
.
\square

5.4.
Canonical integral models of compactifications

5.4.1.
Assumptions

As mentioned in Remark
5.6
, the extension of shtukas to toroidal compactifications
\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
can be achieved without understanding the geometry of the interior
\mathscr{S}_{K}
.
To highlight this point, we present a sequence of assumptions and definitions at slightly different levels of generality.

Let
(G,X)
be a Shimura datum, let
\mathcal{G}
be a quasi-parahoric model of
G=G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, and let
K_{p}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Let
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}}(G,X)\}_{K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})}
be a family of integral models of
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}(G,X)\}_{K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})}
.

To study the boundary stratification of the integral model of the toroidal compactification and to construct log shtukas on it, we assume:

Assumption 5.25
.

For each neat
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
,

(1)

\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)
has a good compactification theory as in Axiom
5.1
. (In fact, we only need the assumptions for toroidal compactifications.)

(2)

For any
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
, the
\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,E},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,E}})
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\Diamond}
with one leg bounded by
\mu^{*}_{\Phi}
associated with the push-out of the de Rham pro-étale
\underline{\mathcal{P}^{c}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-torsor
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
via
\mathcal{P}^{c}_{\Phi}\to\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}
extends to a
\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi}})
over
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\Diamond/}
.

To consider the boundary stratification on the integral model of the minimal compactification and prove the well-positionedness of various strata on the special fiber, we further assume:

Assumption 5.26
.

For each neat
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, assumptions
5.25
hold. Moreover, for each
[\Phi]\in\mathcal{CLR}_{K}(G,X)
,
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}(\overline{P}_{\Phi},\overline{D}_{\Phi})\to\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})
is an abelian scheme torsor.

To consider functoriality and uniqueness of integral models of toroidal (resp. minimal compactifications), we make the following definition:

Definition 5.27
.

Fix
K^{\prime}=K_{p}K^{\prime,p}
for
K_{p}
a quasi-parahoric subgroup and
K^{\prime,p}
neat open compact. Assume
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime}}:=\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime}}(G,X)
has a good compactification theory as in Axiom
5.1
and let
\Sigma
be a projective smooth cone decomposition for
(G,X,K^{\prime})
.

We say that
\{\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}\}_{K^{p}}
(resp.
\{\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\min}\}_{K^{p}}
) is a system of
canonical integral models
(in the sense of Pappas-Rapoport) of toroidal compactifications
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\Sigma}\}_{K^{p}}
(resp. minimal compactifications
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}^{\min}\}_{K^{p}}
) if, for any
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
, the integral model of mixed Shimura variety
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}\}_{K^{p}}
(resp.
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}\}_{K^{p}}
) is a system of canonical integral models in the sense of Axiom
4.2
(resp. Axiom
4.1
).
The inverse system runs over a cofinal collection of neat open compact subgroups
K^{p}\subset K^{\prime,p}
with the cone decomposition the induced one (see
[
83
, Def. 1.18(2)]
).

Theorem 5.28
.

Let
(G,X)
be any abelian-type Shimura datum, let
p>0
be any prime, and let
\mathcal{G}
be any quasi-parahoric model with
K_{p}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Then
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}(G,X)\}_{K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})}
has a
canonical integral model

\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}}\}_{K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})}
in the sense of Axiom
4.1
that satisfies Assumption
5.26
. Moreover,
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}K^{p}}\}_{K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})}
has canonical integral models of toroidal compactifications and minimal compactifications for any given neat open compact
K^{\prime,p}
.

Proof.

In Theorem
6.26
, we will check Assumption
5.25
(2) and the second assertion using the construction in
[
83
]
(specializing to Case (
\mathrm{STB}_{1}
) and its normalization models with corresponding quasi-parahoric levels; see
loc. cit.
Sec. 4.2). Assumption
5.25
(1) (i.e., Axiom
5.1
) is Theorem
5.5
. The second statement of Assumption
5.26
is
[
83
, Prop. 4.59]
; as one will see later, this, in fact, requires Proposition
6.21
.
∎

5.4.2.

We mention some important consequences. The following proposition means that the shtukas on the strata
automatically
glue together.

Proposition 5.29
.

Under Assumption
5.25
, there is a unique morphism

(\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma})^{\log\Diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}

extending
\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}
. Moreover, there is a commutative diagram

(5.18)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma})^{\log\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\log\Diamond/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma))^{\log\Diamond/}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\mu_{\Phi}^{*},\delta=1}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g_{\Phi}^{-1})}

By Lemma
2.31
, there is no difference of using
\log\Diamond
and
\log\Diamond/
on the top-left corner of (
5.18
) since
\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
is proper.

Proof.

Let
\mathscr{S}_{K}
be an integral model satisfying the Assumption
5.25
. By Corollary
5.13
and Theorem
2.55
, there is a unique
\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
\mathscr{P}_{\Phi}^{*}
on
\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}^{\Diamond/}
such that the commutative diagram (
5.10
) extends integrally. Then the first sentence follows from applying Corollary
5.20
and Lemma
5.15
in order. The diagram (
5.18
) follows from the proof of Lemma
5.15
.
∎

Combing Proposition
5.29
and Corollary
2.58
, we see that

Corollary 5.30
.

The log shtuka in Proposition
5.29
induces a non-log shtuka on the special fiber

\mathscr{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{E}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}}^{\Sigma,\Diamond}=\mathscr{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{E}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}}^{\Sigma,\diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}

with a commutative diagram

(5.19)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathscr{S}_{K,\mathcal{O}_{E}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}}^{\Sigma})^{\textrm{perf}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W^{\textrm{perf}}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma))^{\textrm{perf}}_{\mathcal{O}_{E}/\mathfrak{p}_{v}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}^{W}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\mu_{\Phi}^{*},\delta=1}^{W}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g_{\Phi}^{-1})}

Corollary 5.31
.

Under Assumption
5.25
, for any
\Upsilon=[(\Phi,\sigma)]\in\mathrm{Cusp}_{K}(G,X,\Sigma)
, there is a map

(\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon,K})^{\log?}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1},

where
?=\Diamond,\diamond
or
\Diamond/
and the log structure on
\mathcal{Z}_{\Upsilon,K}
is defined by pulling back the log structure on
\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}
.
In particular, there is a morphism

\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}

extending
\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}.

Proof.

Since
\mathscr{S}^{\Sigma}_{K}
is proper,
(\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma})^{\log\Diamond}=(\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma})^{\log\Diamond/}=(\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma})^{\log\diamond}
(see Lemma
2.31
). For a map between fs log schemes
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})\to(Y,\mathcal{M}_{Y})
that are separated and of finite type, by definition, there is a map between log diamonds
(X,\mathcal{M}_{X})^{\log?}\to(Y,\mathcal{M}_{Y})^{\log?}
. For the last sentence, note that the pullback log structure to the interior is the trivial log structure. Then it follows from Corollary
2.39
.
∎

5.4.3.

Lemma 5.32
.

Under Assumption
5.26
, for any
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
, the
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{*}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi,E},\phi_{\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi,E}})
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}^{\Diamond}
with one leg bounded by
\overline{\mu}^{*}_{\Phi}
associated with the push-out of the de Rham pro-étale
\underline{\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{c}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
-torsor
\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}
via
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{c}_{\Phi}\to\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{*}_{\Phi}
extends to a
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{*}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi},\phi_{\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi}})
over
\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}^{\Diamond/}
. In other words, we have a morphism
(\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}})^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\Phi}^{*},\bar{\mu}_{\Phi}^{*}}
extending the one on generic fiber. Similarly, we have a morphism
(\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}})^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*},\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*}}
extending the one on generic fiber.

Proof.

Zariski locally over
\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}=:C(\Phi)
,
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}
is the projection
\mathbf{E}(\Phi)\times_{\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}}C(\Phi)\to C(\Phi)
. It admits the zero section
C(\Phi)\to\mathbf{E}(\Phi)\times C(\Phi)
. We have the following commutative diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbf{E}(\Phi)_{\eta}\times C(\Phi)_{\eta})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\mu_{\Phi}^{*}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbf{E}(\Phi)\times C(\Phi))^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\mu_{\Phi}^{*}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C(\Phi)_{\eta}^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\Phi}^{*},\bar{\mu}_{\Phi}^{*}},}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C(\Phi)^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\Phi}^{*},\bar{\mu}_{\Phi}^{*}}.}}

The composition
C(\Phi)^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\Phi}^{*},\bar{\mu}_{\Phi}^{*}}
exists Zariski locally on
C(\Phi)
. Since, over generic fiber, we have a globally defined morphism
C(\Phi)_{\eta}^{\Diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\Phi}^{*},\bar{\mu}_{\Phi}^{*}}
, and the commutativity of the left square implies that the morphism is independent of the choice of (Zariski local) section
C(\Phi)\to\mathbf{E}(\Phi)\times C(\Phi)
, we obtain a globally defined morphism
C(\Phi)^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\Phi}^{*},\bar{\mu}_{\Phi}^{*}}
using
[
66
, Cor. 2.7.10]
. This further descends to
(\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}})^{\Diamond/}
using the descent data coming from the generic fiber. We can apply this argument to
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}
since
\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}
is an abelian scheme torsor by assumption.
∎

In particular, we have

Proposition 5.33
.

Under Assumption
5.26
, the commutative diagram (
5.9
) in Lemma
5.10
extends over integral base. That is to say, for any
[\Phi]
, we have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}(\overline{P}_{\Phi},\overline{D}_{\Phi})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\mu_{\Phi}^{*},\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\Phi}^{*},\bar{\mu}_{\Phi}^{*},\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*},\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*},\delta=1}.}}

Proposition 5.34
.

Under Assumption
5.26
, the commutative diagram (
5.10
) in Corollary
5.13
extends over the integral base. That is to say, for all such coverings
W^{0}
(where
\mathfrak{W}
are open affine coverings of
\mathfrak{X}^{\circ}_{[\Phi,\sigma],K}
, here for each
\Phi
, we let
\sigma
run over all
\Sigma(\Phi)^{+}
), we have following commutative diagram

(5.20)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W^{0,\diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})^{\diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\mu_{\Phi}^{*},\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*},\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*},\delta=1}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g_{\Phi}^{-1})}

Taking the special fibers (apply the reduction functor), we have

(5.21)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\textrm{perf}}_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W^{0,\textrm{perf}}_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\textrm{perf}}_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})^{\textrm{perf}}_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}^{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\mu_{\Phi}^{*},\delta=1}^{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*},\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*},\delta=1}^{W}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g_{\Phi}^{-1})}

Note that
W^{0}
is not of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
; nevertheless, since
W^{0}
is affine, noetherian and flat over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, one can still apply
[
66
, Cor. 2.7.10]
(see the first paragraph of the proof of Theorem
2.55
).

5.4.4.

The compactifications satisfying Definition
5.27
are fully functorial.

Proposition 5.35
.

Let
f:(G_{1},X_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1},K_{1}^{p},\Sigma_{1})\to(G_{2},X_{2},\mathcal{G}_{2},K_{2}^{p},\Sigma_{2})
be a morphism. Here, the meaning of a morphism is: (1)
f:(G_{1},X_{1})\to(G_{2},X_{2})
is a morphism between Shimura data; (2)
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
is a morphism between quasi-parahoric group schemes whose generic fiber is
f:G_{1}\to G_{2}
; (3)
K_{1}^{p}\to K_{2}^{p}
is a morphism between neat open compact subgroups compatible with
f:G_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
; (4)
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{2}
are smooth projective and are compatible in the sense of
[
83
, Def. 1.18(3)]
.

Let
\mathscr{S}_{K_{i}}^{\Sigma_{i}}(G_{i},X_{i})
be a canonical integral model of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{i}}^{\Sigma_{i}}(G_{i},X_{i})
for
i=1,2
. Then there is a unique morphism
f_{\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}}:\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}^{\Sigma_{1}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}}
extending the one
f:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}
over the generic fiber by functoriality between Shimura data, and makes the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}^{\Sigma_{1}})^{\log\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c}_{1},\mu^{c}_{1},\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}}^{\Sigma_{2}})^{\log\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c}_{2},\mu^{c}_{2},\delta=1}.}}

Proof.

By Proposition
4.10
, for any cusp label representative
\Phi_{1}
mapping to
\Phi_{2}
, there is a morphism
f_{\Phi_{1}}:\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
extending the one on the generic fiber. Note that this morphism is automatically a morphism between torus torsors equivariant under
(\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}\to\mathbf{E}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}})
by separatedness and normality. Hence, we obtain a morphism of integral models
f_{\Phi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}):\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}(\sigma_{1})\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
for
\sigma_{1}
mapping to
\sigma_{2}
; we also have a morphism between closed strata
f_{\Phi_{1},\sigma_{1}}
. Axiom
(.4)
requires that
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}
acts on
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}
. By separatedness and normality again, there are morphisms
f^{\circ}_{\Phi_{1}}:\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi_{1},K_{1}}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}\to\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
,
f^{\circ}_{\Phi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}):\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi_{1},K_{1}}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{1}}}(\sigma_{1})\to\Delta^{\circ}_{\Phi_{2},K_{2}}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(\sigma_{2})
and also a morphism between their corresponding closed strata. We have the desired morphism between compactifications by
[
58
, Lem. A.3.4]
. The commutativity of the diagram is guaranteed by Theorem
2.55
, and by the commutativity of the diagram on the generic fiber.
∎

In particular,

Corollary 5.36
.

Fix
(G,X,\Sigma)
. The inverse system of canonical integral models
\{\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}\}_{K^{p}}
is unique up to a unique isomorphism.

Upon restricting the maps to the open dense strata, we obtain that

Corollary 5.37
.

Fix
(G,X)
. Let
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}_{2}
be a morphism of quasi-parahoric group schemes, then there exists a
proper
morphism
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}}
extending
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}
.

Similarly, one has canonicity and functoriality of canonical integral models of minimal compactifications.

Proposition 5.38
.

Let
f:(G_{1},X_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1},K_{1}^{p})\to(G_{2},X_{2},\mathcal{G}_{2},K_{2}^{p})
be a morphism as in Proposition
5.35
.
Let
\mathscr{S}_{K_{i}}^{\mathrm{min}}(G_{i},X_{i})
be a canonical integral model of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{i}}^{\mathrm{min}}(G_{i},X_{i})
for
i=1,2
. Then there is a unique morphism
f^{\mathrm{min}}:\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}^{\mathrm{min}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}
extending
f:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{1}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{2}}
over the generic fiber between Shimura varieties induced by functoriality between Shimura data.

In particular, the inverse system of canonical integral models of minimal compactifications
\{\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\mathrm{min}}\}_{K^{p}}
is unique up to a unique isomorphism.

Proof.

The argument as in
[
58
, Lem. A.3.4]
still works. See
[
83
, Lem. 5.5]
and
[
60
, 4.1.8-4.1.9]
.
∎

6.
Shtukas on integral models of boundary mixed Shimura varieties of abelian type

In this section, we construct shtukas on integral models of boundary mixed Shimura varieties of abelian type, and verify the axioms
4.2
.

6.1.
Hodge-type case

6.1.1.
Set up

Let
(G,X)
be a Hodge-type Shimura datum, and let
\mathcal{G}
be a stabilizer quasi-parahoric model of
G
, with
K_{p}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.
We fix an adjusted Hodge embedding
(G,X,K_{p})\hookrightarrow(G^{\mathrm{\ddagger}},X^{\mathrm{\ddagger}},K_{p}^{\mathrm{\ddagger}})
in the sense of
[
60
, Def. 3.16, Rmk. 3.17]
, where
G^{\mathrm{\ddagger}}=\operatorname{GSp}(V,\psi)
,
\operatorname{\mathcal{GSP}}=\operatorname{GSp}(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
is a hyperspecial model of
G^{\mathrm{\ddagger}}
,
K_{p}^{\mathrm{\ddagger}}=\operatorname{\mathcal{GSP}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is the stabilizer group of the self-dual lattice
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
, and
K_{p}=G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap K_{p}^{\mathrm{\ddagger}}
. Explicitly, we work with the construction in
[
46
]
, combine the chain of lattices into a single lattice and then apply the Zarhin’s trick to get a self-dual lattice in the Siegel side. We fix such an adjusted Hodge embedding instead of a random one as in
[
66
]
in order to have a good theory of compactifications as in
[
58
]
and to ensure good compatibility of levels at the boundary; see
[
60
, Prop. 1.2]
.
The induced morphism
\mathcal{G}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{GSP}}
factors through a smoothening map
\mathcal{G}\to\overline{\mathcal{G}}
, and
\overline{\mathcal{G}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{\mathcal{GSP}}
is a closed embedding. Let
(s_{\alpha})\in V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\otimes}
be the Hodge tensors that cut out the closed subscheme
\overline{\mathcal{G}}
. Note that
s_{\alpha}
can be defined in
V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}^{\otimes}
.

Remark 6.1
.

In the Hodge-type case, the center
Z(G)^{\circ}
is an almost product of a split
\mathbb{Q}
-torus and a compact-type
\mathbb{Q}
-torus; thus
Z(G)_{ac}
is trivial,
G=G^{c}
,
P_{\Phi}=P^{c}_{\Phi}=P^{*}_{\Phi}
, and
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}
is trivial. See
[
58
, Lem. 2.1.20]
.

On the boundary, since the level group
K_{\Phi,G}=gKg^{-1}
is twisted by
g=g_{\Phi}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, we need to twist the Hodge tensors. Let
(s_{\alpha,\Phi})\in V_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}^{g,\otimes}
(where
V^{g}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}=gV_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\cap V_{\mathbb{Q}}
) be the collection of Hodge tensors that cut out the closed subgroup scheme
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\Phi}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{GSP}}_{\Phi^{\mathrm{\ddagger}}}
; here
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\Phi}
and
\operatorname{\mathcal{GSP}}_{\Phi^{\mathrm{\ddagger}}}
are the left
g
-conjugates of
\overline{\mathcal{G}}
and
\operatorname{\mathcal{GSP}}
, respectively. Indeed, we take
g_{p}
-conjugation, where
g_{p}
is the
p
-component of
g
; we do not distinguish
g
and
g_{p}
when the difference is clear from the context. Let
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}\to\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\Phi}
be the dilated morphism;
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
is a smooth affine model of
G
.

Over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,
(s_{\alpha})
and
(s_{\alpha,\Phi})
are related as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V^{\otimes n}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V^{g,\otimes n}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V^{\otimes m}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V^{g,\otimes m}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{\alpha,\Phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

6.1.2.
Reduction

Recall that over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)
, the family of Hodge tensors
(s_{\alpha})\in V^{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
defines a family of étale tensors
(t_{\alpha,\text{{\'{e}}t}})
on the Tate module
T_{p}(A)
, where
A
is the universal abelian scheme over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)
, pulled back from
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\mathrm{\ddagger}}}(G^{\mathrm{\ddagger}},X^{\mathrm{\ddagger}})
. We denote by
\mathbb{L}_{\rho,V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}
the local system given by
T_{p}(A)
, where
\rho:G\to\operatorname{GSp}(V)\to\operatorname{GL}(V)
. Let
\mathbb{P}_{K}
be the pro-étale torsor under
\underline{K_{p}}
defined by

(6.1)

\operatorname{\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}}_{(t_{\alpha,\text{{\'{e}}t}}),(s_{\alpha})}(\mathbb{L}_{\rho,V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}},V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)}),

this coincides with the pro-étale torsor (
3.5
).

One can repeat the above process with
A
replaced by the universal
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
, pulled back from
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\mathrm{\ddagger}}}}:=\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi^{\mathrm{\ddagger}}}}(P_{\Phi^{\mathrm{\ddagger}}},D_{\Phi^{\mathrm{\ddagger}}})
. Here
\Phi^{\mathrm{\ddagger}}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G^{\mathrm{\ddagger}},X^{\mathrm{\ddagger}})
is the cusp-label representative induced by
\Phi
.

By the work of Brylinski, all Hodge classes on
1
-motives over
\mathbb{C}
are absolute Hodge cycles; thus
(s_{\alpha,\Phi})
induces
(t_{\alpha,\Phi,\text{{\'{e}}t}})\in T_{p}(\mathcal{Q})^{\otimes}
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}
. We denote by
\mathbb{L}_{\rho_{\Phi},K_{\Phi}}
the local system given by the Tate module
T_{p}(\mathcal{Q})
. Consider the following torsor under
\underline{\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}
:

(6.2)

\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi,G}}:=\operatorname{\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}}_{(t_{\alpha,\Phi,\text{{\'{e}}t}}),(s_{\alpha,\Phi})}(\mathbb{L}_{\rho_{\Phi},K_{\Phi}},V^{g}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}}).

Consider the following torsor under
\underline{K_{\Phi,Q,p}}=\underline{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}
:

\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi,W^{\bullet}}}:=\operatorname{\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}}_{(t_{\alpha,\Phi,\text{{\'{e}}t}}),(s_{\alpha,\Phi})}(W_{\bullet}\mathbb{L}_{\rho_{\Phi},K_{\Phi}},W_{\bullet}V^{g}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}}),

where the filtration
W_{\bullet}\mathbb{L}_{\rho_{\Phi},K_{\Phi}}
comes from the weight filtration
W_{\bullet}T_{p}(\mathcal{Q})
on
T_{p}(\mathcal{Q})
. Since the weight filtration is constant, both the Hodge tensors and the weight filtration are trivialized when
T_{p}(\mathcal{Q})
is trivialized. Therefore, we have a canonical embedding
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi,W^{\bullet}}}\to\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi,G}}
, which induces

(6.3)

\underline{\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\times^{\underline{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}}\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi,W_{\bullet}}}=\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi,G}}.

On the other hand, there is a natural pro-étale torsor
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}}
under
K_{\Phi}
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}
, as defined in Definition
3.13
.

Lemma 6.2
.

T_{p}(\mathcal{Q})
is trivialized over
\varprojlim_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime}\subset K_{\Phi}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{\prime}K_{\Phi}^{p}}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that for each
n
,
T_{p}(\mathcal{Q})/p^{n}
is trivialized over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,p}^{(n)}K_{\Phi}^{p}}
. It also suffices to work with the Siegel case, where the result follows from the moduli interpretation; see
[
58
, §2.2.14]
.
∎

Corollary 6.3
.

\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi,G}}
reduces to
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}}
.

Proof.

Along the standard representation
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}\to\operatorname{GL}(V^{g}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}})
, the push-out torsor of
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}}
is the frame bundle associated with
T_{p}(\mathcal{Q})
. By Lemma
6.2
, we have a morphism

\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\operatorname{\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}}(\mathbb{L}_{\rho_{\Phi},K_{\Phi}},V^{g}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}}),

that factors through
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi,G}}
and thus factors through
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi,W_{\bullet}}}
.
∎

6.1.3.
Hodge-Tate period map

The de Rham pro-étale torsor
\mathbb{P}_{K}
under
\underline{K_{p}}
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}
produces a Hodge-Tate period map (
3.7
), which is the sheaf-theoretic version of the Hodge-Tate period map

\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}\sim\varprojlim_{K_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}\to\mathcal{F}l_{G,\mu^{-1}}

from the perfectoid space
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}
to the flag variety
\mathcal{F}l_{G,\mu^{-1}}
. Here
(\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}})^{\Diamond}=\varprojlim_{K_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{p}K^{p}}^{\Diamond}
. Recall that given a geometric point
x\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{p}}(C,C^{+})
, we have an abelian variety
A_{x}
over
C
together with a trivialization
(T_{p}(A_{x}),(t_{\alpha,\text{{\'{e}}t},x}))\cong(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}},(s_{\alpha}))
, then
\mathrm{HT}_{K}(x)\in\mathcal{F}l_{G,\mu^{-1}}(C,C^{+})
corresponds to the Hodge-Tate filtration:

0\to(\operatorname{Lie}(A_{x}))(1)\to T_{p}(A_{x})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}C\to(\operatorname{Lie}A_{x}^{\vee})^{\vee}\to 0,

see
[
13
, Thm. 2.1.3]
.

Similarly, let
G_{\Phi}
be the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-group
gG_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}g^{-1}
. One has a perfectoid space together with a Hodge-Tate period map

\mathrm{HT}_{K_{\Phi,G}}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{p}}\to\mathcal{F}l_{G_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}^{-1}},\quad\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{p}}\sim\varprojlim_{K_{\Phi,p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,p}K_{\Phi}^{p}},\quad\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{p}}^{\Diamond}=\varprojlim_{K_{\Phi,p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,p}K_{\Phi}^{p}}^{\Diamond},

defined as follows: given
x\in\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(C,C^{+})
, by Corollary
6.3
, we have a
1
-motive
\mathcal{Q}_{x}
over
C
together with a trivialization
(T_{p}(\mathcal{Q}_{x}),(t_{\alpha,\Phi,\text{{\'{e}}t},x}))\cong(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{g},(s_{\alpha,\Phi}))
. Then
\mathrm{HT}_{K_{\Phi,G}}(x)\in\mathcal{F}l_{G_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}^{-1}}(C,C^{+})
corresponds to the Hodge-Tate filtration
