0\to(\operatorname{Lie}(\mathcal{Q}_{x}))(1)\to T_{p}(\mathcal{Q}_{x})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}C\to(\operatorname{Lie}\mathcal{Q}_{x}^{\vee})^{\vee}\to 0.

By Corollary
6.3
,
\mathrm{HT}_{K_{\Phi,G}}
factors through
\mathrm{HT}_{K_{\Phi}}:\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{p}}\to\mathcal{F}l_{P_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}^{-1}}
and is the sheaf-theoretic version of the Hodge-Tate period map (
3.7
).

Corollary 6.4
.

Over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}^{\Diamond}
, the
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-shtuka associated with
(\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi,G}},\mathrm{HT}_{K_{\Phi,G}})
with a leg bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
reduces to the
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}
-shtuka associated with
(\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}},\mathrm{HT}_{K_{\Phi}})
with a leg bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
.

6.1.4.
Comparison

Let
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)^{\Diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\Diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}}
) be the shtuka associated with
\mathbb{P}_{K}
(resp.
\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}}
). We use the notation from subsection
5.2
.

Lemma 6.5

(See Corollary
5.13
)
.

We have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W^{0,\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\Diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g_{\Phi}^{-1})}

Remark 6.6
.

This is a special case of Corollary
5.13
; see Remark
6.1
. We give a more direct and intuitive proof in the Hodge-type case.

Proof.

By Corollary
6.4
, it suffices to work with
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\Diamond}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}}
. In
[
58
, Prop. 3.1.6]
(using fpqc descent from
\bigsqcup V\to W^{0}
; see
[
58
, §3.1.5]
for the notation
V
), we have comparisons over
W^{0}
:

\mathcal{Q}[p^{\infty}]\cong A[p^{\infty}],\quad T_{p}(\mathcal{Q})\cong T_{p}(A),\quad(t_{\alpha,\text{{\'{e}}t}})\cong(t_{\alpha,\Phi,\text{{\'{e}}t}}),

the tensor comparisons are compatible with
(s_{\alpha})\cong(s_{\alpha,\Phi})
, in the sense that the diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V^{g,\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T_{p}(\mathcal{Q}_{W^{0}})^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T_{p}(A_{W^{0}})^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V^{g,\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T_{p}(\mathcal{Q}_{W^{0}})^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T_{p}(A_{W^{0}})^{\otimes}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g^{\otimes}}}{{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s_{\alpha})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s_{\alpha,\Phi})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t_{\alpha,\Phi,\text{{\'{e}}t}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(t_{\alpha,\text{{\'{e}}t}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g^{\otimes}}}{{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

This gives the isomorphism
\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g_{\Phi}^{-1}):(\mathbb{P}_{K},\mathrm{HT}_{K})\cong(\mathbb{P}_{K_{\Phi}},\mathrm{HT}_{K_{\Phi}})
.
∎

6.1.5.
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-shtukas

We move on to integral models.

Proposition 6.7
.

The
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G,E},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G,E}})
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}^{\Diamond}\to\operatorname{Spd}(E)
with one leg bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
extends (uniquely) to a
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G}})
over
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}^{\Diamond/}\to\operatorname{Spd}(\mathcal{O}_{E})
with one leg bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
.

This proposition follows almost verbatim from
[
66
, §4.6.3]
. We only need to replace the BKF module of the universal
p
-divisible group
A[p^{\infty}]
over
\mathscr{S}_{K}
by the universal
p
-divisible group
\mathcal{Q}[p^{\infty}]
over
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}
. The uniqueness of the extension follows from
[
66
, Cor. 2.7.10]
. Let us write down some key steps in the construction in order to fix notation.

Proof.

By Tannakian formalism, under

\rho_{\Phi}:\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}\to\operatorname{\mathcal{GSP}}(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{g})\to\operatorname{GL}(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{g}),

the
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G,E},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G,E}})
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}^{\Diamond}\to\operatorname{Spd}(E)
gives a vector shtuka
(\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,E},\phi_{\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,E}})
over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}^{\Diamond}
, associated with the de Rham local system
\mathbb{L}_{\rho_{\Phi},K_{\Phi}}
as in (
6.2
). We have a family of tensors
(t_{\alpha,\Phi,E})\in(\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,E},\phi_{\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,E}})^{\otimes}
, which can be viewed as shtukas homomorphisms over
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}}^{\Diamond}
:

(6.4)

t_{\alpha,\Phi,E}:(\oplus_{i}\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,E}^{\otimes m_{i}},\phi_{\oplus_{i}\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,E}^{\otimes m_{i}}})\to(\oplus_{i}\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,E}^{\otimes n_{i}},\phi_{\oplus_{i}\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,E}^{\otimes n_{i}}}).

Given
S=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})\in\operatorname{Perf}
, a map
S\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}^{\diamond}
is given by an untilt
S^{\sharp}=\operatorname{Spa}(R^{\sharp},R^{\sharp+})\to(\widehat{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}})^{\mathrm{ad}}
. We pull back the universal
p
-divisible group
\mathcal{Q}[p^{\infty}]
to
\operatorname{Spec}R^{\sharp+}
, and let
M_{\inf,\Phi}(R^{\sharp+})
be the BKF-module of
\mathcal{Q}[p^{\infty}]
with one leg at
\phi(\xi)=0
. Denote
(\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,S},\phi_{\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,S}})
by the corresponding minuscule vector shtuka with height
2n
and dimension
n
over
S
with one leg at
S^{\sharp}
, given by the restriction of
(\phi^{-1})^{*}M_{\inf,\Phi}(R^{\sharp+})
to
\operatorname{Spa}(W(R^{+}))\backslash\{[\varpi]=0\}
. By
[
66
, Thm. 2.7.7]
, the tensors
(t_{\alpha,\Phi,E})
in (
6.4
) extend uniquely to tensors
(t_{\alpha,\Phi})\in(\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,S},\phi_{\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,S}})^{\otimes}
.

By exactly the same arguments as in Steps
1,2,3
in
[
66
, §4.6.3]
, the
v
-sheaf over
S
defined by

(6.5)

\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi,G,S}:=\operatorname{\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}}_{(t_{\alpha,\Phi}),(s_{\alpha,\Phi})\otimes\mathrm{id}}(\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,S},V^{g}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}})

is induced by a
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-torsor
\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G,S}
over
S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
via
\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi,G,S}=\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{\Phi}\times^{\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}}\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G,S}
. The Frobenius structure
\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G,S}}
comes from the Frobenius structure
\phi_{\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,S}}
of
\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,S}
. Then
(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G,S},\phi_{\Phi,G,S})
gives a
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-shtuka over
S
. It has a leg bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
by its nature over the generic fiber: one can reduce it to
(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
-points and then apply arguments in
[
66
, §3.3.7]
. By varying
S
, and using descent data from the generic fiber, we finish the proof.
∎

6.1.6.
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-shtukas

We follow the notation from the proof of Proposition
6.7
. The BKF module
M_{\inf,\Phi}(R^{\sharp+})
associated with the universal
p
-divisible group
\mathcal{Q}[p^{\infty}]
has a canonical filtration coming from the weight filtration on
\mathcal{Q}[p^{\infty}]
; therefore, the associated vector shtuka
(\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,S},\phi_{\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,S}})
is equipped with a canonical weight filtration
W_{\bullet}(\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,S},\phi_{\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,S}})
. Consider the
v
-sheaf over
S
defined by

\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet},S}:=\operatorname{\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}}_{(t_{\alpha,\Phi}),(s_{\alpha,\Phi})\otimes\mathrm{id}}(W_{\bullet}\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,S},W_{\bullet}V^{g}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}}).

Note that
\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet},S}
has a natural
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-action factoring through
\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Phi}
.

Lemma 6.8
.

The
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-torsor
\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G,S}
reduces to a
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-torsor
\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet},S}
(i.e.
\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G,S}=\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}\times^{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}}\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet},S}
), and
\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet},S}=\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Phi}\times^{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}}\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet},S}
. Moreover, the
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-torsor isomorphism
\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G,S}}
reduces to a
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-torsor isomorphism

\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet},S}}:\operatorname{Frob}_{S}^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet},S})|_{S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\setminus S^{\sharp}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet},S}|_{S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\setminus S^{\sharp}},

Proof.

This follows from the fact that the weight filtration on
\mathcal{Q}
is constant. To be more precise, in the construction
[
66
, §4.6.3]
, one first deals with geometric points, and then deals with products of geometric points. Let
T\to S
be a
v
-cover such that
T
is a product of geometric points. By
[
75
, Prop. 19.5.3]
, the category of
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-torsors (resp.
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-torsors) on
S\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is equivalent to the category of
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-torsors (resp.
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-torsors) on
T\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with suitable descent data (resp. descent data for
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-torsors plus descent data for the weight filtration).

Denote
T=\operatorname{Spa}(B,B^{+})
, where
B^{+}=\prod_{j}C_{j}^{+}
. The
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-torsor on
T\operatorname{\dot{\times}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
comes from the restriction of the (trivial)
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-torsor on
A_{\inf}(B^{+})=W(B^{+})
given by the (trivial)
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-BKF-module
\mathcal{T}_{\Phi,G}
constructed in
[
66
, Lem. 4.6.6]
. Recall that the weight filtration on
M_{\inf,\Phi}
is the filtration of BKF modules of
0\subset T[p^{\infty}]\subset G[p^{\infty}]\subset\mathcal{Q}[p^{\infty}]
. Over
W(B^{+})=\prod_{j}W(C_{j}^{+})
, this filtration becomes constant, thus the
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-torsor
\mathcal{T}_{\Phi,G}
reduces to a
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-torsor
\mathcal{T}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet}}
. We have descent data of the weight filtration since the weight filtration over
T
is the one pulled back from
S
, the
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-torsor
\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G,S}
reduces to a
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}
-torsor
\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet},S}
, and
\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet},S}=\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{\Phi}\times^{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}}\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet},S}
.

The Frobenius structure
\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G,S}}
comes from the Frobenius structure
\phi_{\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,S}}
on
\mathscr{V}_{\Phi,S}
, which comes from the restriction of the Frobenius structure
\phi_{M_{\inf,\Phi}}
on the BKF module
M_{\inf,\Phi}(R^{\sharp+})
of
\mathcal{Q}[p^{\infty}]
. Since the Frobenius structure on
M_{\inf,\Phi}(R^{\sharp+})
preserves the weight filtration,
\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G,S}}
reduces to the
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-torsor isomorphism
\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet},S}}
.
∎

Lemma 6.9
.

The
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G}})
over
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}^{\Diamond/}\to\operatorname{Spd}(\mathcal{O}_{E})
with one leg bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
reduces to a
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W^{\bullet}},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W^{\bullet}}})
with one leg bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.8
, it suffices to show that the leg of the
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W^{\bullet}},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W^{\bullet}}})
is bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
. It suffices to check the geometric points. Over generic fiber, this follows from Corollary
6.4
. Over special fiber, let
D
be an algebraically closed field, and
x:\operatorname{Spec}D\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}
be a geometric point on the special fiber, we need to show the pullback
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
x^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet}},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet}}})
is bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
. As in
[
66
, §4.6.3, Step(3)]
, we lift
\operatorname{Spa}D
to a point
\tilde{x}:\operatorname{Spa}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})\to(\widehat{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}})^{\mathrm{ad}}
, where
C
is a complete non-archimedean algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
, such that
\mathcal{O}_{C}/\mathfrak{m}_{C}=D
. By
[
66
, §4.6.3, Step(2)]
, the
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
\tilde{x}^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G}})
on
\operatorname{Spa}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
extends to a
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-BKF module
(\mathcal{T}_{\Phi,G},\phi_{\mathcal{T}_{\Phi,G}})
on
W(\mathcal{O}_{C^{\flat}})
and then to a
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-BKF module
(\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\Phi,G},\phi_{\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\Phi,G}})
on
W(D)
. By the correspondence of
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-BKF modules on
W(D)
and
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-shtukas on
\operatorname{Spec}D
(
[
66
, Thm. 2.3.8]
), and by
[
66
, Prop. 2.4.6]
, the
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
x^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G}})
on
\operatorname{Spec}D
is the specialization of the
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
\tilde{x}^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G}})
on
\operatorname{Spa}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
. In the proof of Lemma
6.8
, we see that the
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-torsor
\mathcal{T}_{\Phi,G}
on
W(\mathcal{O}_{C^{\flat}})
reduces to a
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-torsor
\mathcal{T}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet}}
, thus the
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-torsor
\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\Phi,G}
on
W(D)
reduces to a
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-torsor
\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet}}
. In particular, the
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
x^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet}},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet}}})
on
\operatorname{Spec}D
is the specialization of the
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
\tilde{x}^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet}},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet}}})
on
\operatorname{Spa}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
.

In particular, the relative position of
\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W^{\bullet}}}(\operatorname{Frob}^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W^{\bullet}}))
and
\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W^{\bullet}}
at
\tilde{x}
gives a
\operatorname{Spa}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})
-point in
\operatorname{Gr}_{Q_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}^{-1}}
, which lifts to a
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathcal{O}_{C})
-point in
\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}}
whose specialization gives the relative position of
\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W^{\bullet}}}(\operatorname{Frob}^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W^{\bullet}}))
and
\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W^{\bullet}}
at
x
.

By construction,
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}}=\underline{|\operatorname{Gr}_{Q_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}^{-1}}|^{wgc}}\times_{\underline{|\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{E}}|}}\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{E}}
(where
|\operatorname{Gr}_{Q_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}^{-1}}|^{wgc}
is the weakly generalizing closure of
|\operatorname{Gr}_{Q_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}^{-1}}|
in
|\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{E}}|
). Since
|\operatorname{Spa}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})|
is open and dense in
|\operatorname{Spa}(\mathcal{O}_{C})|
and the image of
|\operatorname{Spa}(\mathcal{O}_{C})|
in
|\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi},\mathcal{O}_{E}}|
is weakly generalizing, then
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathcal{O}_{C})\to\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}}
factors through
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi},\mu_{\Phi}}
, the
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
x^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet}},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W_{\bullet}}})
on
\operatorname{Spec}D
is bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
.
∎

6.1.7.
\mathscr{P}_{\Phi}
-shtukas

Proposition 6.10
.

The
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G}})
over
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}^{\Diamond/}\to\operatorname{Spd}(\mathcal{O}_{E})
with one leg bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
reduces to a
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi}})
with one leg bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
. This extends Corollary
6.4
over the integral base.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.8
and
6.9
, the
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,G}})
with one leg bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
reduces to the
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W^{\bullet}},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W^{\bullet}}})
with one leg bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
. Corollary
6.4
says that the
\mathcal{Q}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W^{\bullet},E},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,W^{\bullet},E}})
with one leg bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
reduces to a
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,E},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{\Phi,E}})
with one leg bounded by
\mu_{\Phi}
. Then the result follows from Proposition
1.43
.
∎

6.1.8.
Rest of axioms

Proposition 6.11
.

Let
(G,X)
be a Hodge-type Shimura datum, and let
\mathcal{G}
be any quasi-parahoric model of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. Then there exists a family of integral models
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\}_{K_{\Phi}^{p}}
that is canonical in the sense of Definition
4.2
, and is adapted with
P_{\Phi}\to G_{\Phi,h}
in the sense of Definition
4.6
.

As in Remark
6.1
,
P_{\Phi}=P_{\Phi}^{c}
, so it is trivially true that the integral model is adapted with
P_{\Phi}\to P_{\Phi}^{c}
in the sense of
4.5
.

Proof.

It suffices to deal with stabilizer quasi-parahoric
\mathcal{G}
by the functoriality result (Proposition
4.9
, case
(2)
). Let
\mathcal{G}
be a stabilizer quasi-parahoric. We show that the family of integral models
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\}_{K_{\Phi}^{p}}
defined via relative normalizations is canonical. This follows from the Siegel case (Lemma
6.12
) and the functoriality result (Proposition
4.9
, case
(1)
). Note that the condition
(b)
is not automatic, we verified it in
[
60
, Prop. 1.2(2)]
under a fixed adjusted Hodge embedding. Also note that, for the integral canonical model
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)
of a Siegel-type Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)
with
K_{p}
being hyperspecial,
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})
is again the integral canonical model of the Siegel-type Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})
with
K_{\Phi,h}
being hyperspecial, for each
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
, see
[
60
, Thm. 3.58(2)]
.
∎

Lemma 6.12
.

Proposition
6.11
holds when
(G,X)=(G^{\mathrm{\ddagger}},X^{\mathrm{\ddagger}})
is a Siegel Shimura datum and when
\mathcal{G}
is hyperspecial.

Proof.

In this case, we have moduli interpretations for the smooth integral models
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
and
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})
(
[
58
, §2.2.14, 2.2.15]
), and a projection
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})
defined via the forgetful functor (
[
58
, §2.2.16]
); then
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
is adapted with
P_{\Phi}\to G_{\Phi,h}
. Now we verify those axioms in Definition
4.2
.

Axiom
(1)
follows from the Néron-Ogg-Shafarevich criterion for
1
-motives; see
[
58
, Appendix, Lem. A.3.5]
. Indeed, recall that
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})
represents the following moduli functor: given a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme
S
,
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})(S)
is the set of isomorphism classes of tuples
(\mathcal{Q},\lambda,\alpha,\alpha^{\vee},\epsilon)
over
S
, where
(\mathcal{Q},\lambda)
is a polarized
1
-motive over
S
, equipped with isomorphisms of sheaves of
\mathbb{Z}
-modules over
S
:

\alpha:\gr_{0}^{W}\underline{V}^{g}(\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathcal{Q}^{\text{{\'{e}}t}},\quad\alpha^{\vee}:\gr_{0}^{W}\underline{V}^{\vee,g}(\mathbb{Z})(\nu)\cong\mathcal{Q}^{\vee,\text{{\'{e}}t}}=\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{mult}},

where
V^{\vee}(\nu)
is the dual representation
V^{\vee}
twisted by the similitude character
\nu:\operatorname{GSp}(V,\psi)\to\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}
, and
\epsilon\in H^{0}(S,\operatorname{\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}}(V^{g}(\hat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p}),\hat{T}^{p}(\mathcal{Q}))/K_{\Phi,G}^{p})
is a
K_{\Phi,G}^{p}
-level structure, where
K_{\Phi,G}^{p}=g^{p}K^{p}g^{p,-1}
. See
[
58
, §2.2.14]
for conventions.

Let
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,p}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})(R[1/p])
, it gives a tuple
(\mathcal{Q}_{\eta},\lambda_{\eta},\alpha_{\eta},\alpha^{-1}_{\eta},\epsilon_{\eta})
over
\operatorname{Spec}R[1/p]
. By
[
58
, Appendix, Lem. A.3.5]
,
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}
(resp.
\mathcal{Q}_{\eta}^{\vee}
) extends to a
1
-motif
\mathcal{Q}
(resp.
\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
) over
\operatorname{Spec}R
. Note that
\alpha_{\eta}
,
\alpha^{-1}_{\eta}
,
\epsilon_{\eta}
extend to
\alpha
,
\alpha^{-1}
,
\epsilon
respectively by the étaleness, and
\lambda_{\eta}
extends to
\lambda
using the rigidity theorem for homomorphisms of semi-abelian schemes (thus of 1-motives), see
[
20
, I, Prop. 2.7]
. The tuple
(\mathcal{Q},\lambda,\alpha,\alpha^{-1},\epsilon)
over
\operatorname{Spec}R
gives the wanted lifting.

Axiom
(2)
: This follows from moduli interpretations. Also see
[
83
, Lem. 4.25]
.

Axiom
(3)
: This is a special case of Proposition
6.10
.

Axiom
(4)
: We replace the Serre-Tate theorem for abelian schemes with the Serre-Tate theorem for
1
-motives. Fix a
k
-point
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})(k)
. We have a tuple
(\mathcal{Q}_{0},\lambda_{0},\alpha_{0},\alpha^{\vee}_{0},\epsilon_{0})
over
\operatorname{Spec}k
, the formal completion
\hat{S}_{x}:=(\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi}))^{\wedge}_{/x}
represents the functor
\mathrm{Def}:\mathrm{Art}_{W(k)}\to\mathrm{Set}
sending
S
to isomorphism classes of deformations over
S
of the tuple
(\mathcal{Q}_{0},\lambda_{0},\alpha_{0},\alpha^{\vee}_{0},\epsilon_{0})
.

On the other hand,
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}:=\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,b_{\Phi,x},\mu_{\Phi}}}
is a Rapoport-Zink space, the deformation space of
p
-divisible groups, for example, see
[
75
, Thm. 25.1.2]
. Let
(X_{1},\lambda_{1})
be a polarized
p
-divisible group in the isogeny class determined by
[b_{\Phi,x}]\in B(G,\{\mu^{-1}\})=B(G_{\Phi},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{-1}\})
, given a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme
S
,
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}(S)
is the set of isomorphism classes of
(X,\lambda,\rho)
over
S
, where
(X,\lambda)
is a polarized
p
-divisible group over
S
and
\rho:(X,\lambda)\times_{S}S/p\to(X_{1},\lambda_{1})\times_{k}S/p
is a quasi-isogeny. Let
x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}(k)
be the base-point associated with
(X_{1},\lambda_{1},\mathrm{id})
. Since the quasi-isogeny
\rho
over
S/p
is rigid in the sense that there is a unique
\rho
lifting the isomorphism
\rho\otimes S/\mathfrak{m}_{S}
, for example, see
[
45
, §2.3]
. Therefore,
(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}})^{\wedge}_{/x_{0}}
represents the functor
\mathrm{Def}_{(X_{1},\lambda_{1})}:\mathrm{Art}_{W(k)}\to\mathrm{Set}
sending
S
to isomorphism classes of deformations over
S
of the polarized
p
-divisible group
(X_{1},\lambda_{1})
.

Let
(X_{0},\lambda_{0})
be the
p
-divisible group
(\mathcal{Q}_{0},\lambda_{0})[p^{\infty}]
, then we have an isogeny
\rho_{01}:(X_{0},\lambda_{0})\to(X_{1},\lambda_{1})
.
(X_{0},\lambda_{0},\rho)
gives a
k
-point in
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}
, which we denote by
x_{1}
.

By the main theorem of
[
7
]
, the Serre-Tate theorem holds for
1
-motives: let
R\in\mathrm{Art}_{W(k)}
,
\mathcal{M}_{1}(R)
be the category of
1
-motives over
R
. Let
M
(resp.
B
) be a
1
-motive (resp. a
p
-divisible group) over
R
,
M_{0}
(resp.
B_{0}
) be its reduction over
\operatorname{Spec}k
. Let
\mathrm{Def}(R,k)
be the category of triples
(M_{0},B,e)
, where
M_{0}
is a
1
-motive over
\operatorname{Spec}k
,
B
is a
p
-divisible group over
R
, and
e:B_{0}\to M_{0}[p^{\infty}]
is an isomorphism of
p
-divisible groups. There is a natural equivalence of categories:

\Delta_{R}:\mathcal{M}_{1}(R)\to\mathrm{Def}(R,k),\quad M\to(M_{0},M[p^{\infty}],\textrm{natural}\ e).

Consider the morphism
\hat{S}_{x}\to(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}})^{\wedge}_{/x_{1}}
defined by sending
(\mathcal{Q},\lambda,\alpha,\alpha^{\vee},\epsilon)
to
((\mathcal{Q},\lambda)[p^{\infty}],\mathrm{id})
. We claim this is an equivalence of categories. By Serre-Tate for
1
-motives, and by the fact that
(\alpha,\alpha^{\vee})
and
\epsilon
are rigid by the étaleness (thus determined by their reductions
(\alpha_{0},\alpha_{0}^{\vee})
and
\epsilon_{0}
over
\operatorname{Spec}k
respectively), it suffices to show that, given a polarization of
p
-divisible groups
\lambda[p^{\infty}]:\mathcal{Q}[p^{\infty}]\to\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}[p^{\infty}]
, we can lift it canonically to a polarization of
1
-motives
\lambda:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
. Recall that a polarization
\lambda:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
is a morphism between
1
-motives such that
\lambda^{\mathrm{ab}}:\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{ab}}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\vee,\mathrm{ab}}
is a polarization of abelian schemes,
\lambda^{\text{{\'{e}}t}}:\mathcal{Q}^{\text{{\'{e}}t}}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{mult}}
is injective, and
(\lambda^{\text{{\'{e}}t}})^{\vee}=\lambda^{\mathrm{mult}}
. The full-faithfulness of
\Delta_{R}
gives a unique morphism
\lambda:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\vee}
and its proof also shows that
\lambda^{\mathrm{ab}}
is a polarization,
\lambda^{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
and
\lambda^{\mathrm{mult}}
satisfy the properties since they are étale in nature and
\lambda_{0}^{\text{{\'{e}}t}}
and
\lambda_{0}^{\mathrm{mult}}
do satisfy the properties.

Therefore, we have an isomorphism
\hat{S}_{x}\cong(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}})^{\wedge}_{/x_{1}}
. We show that the isomorphism
\hat{S}_{x}\cong(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}})^{\wedge}_{/x_{1}}
matches the supported shtukas. By
[
75
, Thm. 25.1.2]
, the identification of the Rapoport-Zink space with
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}
is to evaluate the universal
p
-divisible group at perfectoid space
S=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})\in\operatorname{Perf}
to get a BKF module over
A_{\inf}(R,R^{+})
, and then restrict the BKF module to
S\dot{\times}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
to get the desired shtuka; also, the quasi-isogeny
\rho
provides a framing. Under this identification, since
\hat{S}_{x}\cong(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}})^{\wedge}_{/x_{1}}
matches the universal
p
-divisible groups, it matches the supported shtukas. Finally, after changing the base point,
(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}})^{\wedge}_{x_{1}}\cong(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}})^{\wedge}_{x_{0}}
, we are done.
∎

Finally, to prove the axiom
(1)
in abelian-type case, we need following enhanced result:

Lemma 6.13
.

Keep the notation from Proposition
6.11
. Let
l
be a prime
\neq p
,
K_{\Phi}^{l,p}=K_{\Phi}^{p}\cap P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p,l})
,
K_{\Phi}^{l}=K_{\Phi,p}K_{\Phi}^{p,l}
. For every discrete valuation ring
R
of mixed characteristic over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
, we have

\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi}^{l}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})(R[1/p])=\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}^{l}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})(R).

Proof.

This follows from the proof of the Siegel case (Lemma
6.12
) and the proof of the functoriality result (Proposition
4.9
, case
(1)
). Note that in
[
58
, Appendix, Lem. A.3.5]
, one only needs to trivialize
T^{l}(\mathcal{Q}_{\eta})
, rather than the whole
\hat{T}^{p}(\mathcal{Q}_{\eta})
.
∎

6.2.
Abelian-type case

6.2.1.
Construction

Let us recall the construction in
[
83
]
. The key steps are listed in Construction
6.14
,
6.15
, and
6.16
for convenience.

Construction 6.14
.

Let
(G_{2},X_{2})
be an abelian-type Shimura datum with an associated Hodge-type Shimura datum
(G,X)
; note that, in
[
83
]
, it was denoted by
(G_{0},X_{0})
. There is a central isogeny
\pi^{\mathrm{der}}:G^{\mathrm{der}}\to G^{\mathrm{der}}_{2}
with kernel
C^{\mathrm{der}}
.

Let
G^{\prime}:=G_{2}\times^{G_{2}^{\mathrm{der}}}(G/C^{\mathrm{der}})
.
There is an embedding
\pi^{a}:G_{2}\to G^{\prime}
and a map
\pi^{b}:G\to G^{\prime}
such that they induce morphisms between Shimura data
\pi^{a}:(G_{2},X_{2})\to(G^{\prime},X_{a})
and
\pi^{b}:(G,X)\to(G^{\prime},X_{b})
.
By replacing
(G,X)
with a conjugate by
g\in G^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbb{Q})
, we assume that the images of
X_{2}
and
X
in
X^{\mathrm{ad}}
have nontrivial intersection. We then have that the images of
X_{a}
and
X_{b}
in
X^{\mathrm{ad}}
coincide.
Then
\pi^{a}
and
\pi^{b}
satisfy the conditions in
[
83
, §1.4.3]
. See
[
83
, §1.4.3 and Const. 1.46]
.

We recall more group-theoretic preparations.

Construction 6.15
.

We start with
K_{2}=K_{2,p}K^{p}_{2}\subset G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and an admissible cone decomposition
\Sigma_{2}
for
(G_{2},X_{2},K_{2})
.

•

By
[
83
, Prop. 1.35, Prop. 1.37 and Prop. 1.47]
, we can find
K^{\prime}:=K_{p}^{\prime}K^{\prime,p}\subset G^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
containing
K_{2}
, and an admissible smooth projective
ZP
-invariant cone decomposition
\Sigma
for
(G^{\prime},X_{a},K^{\prime})
and
(G^{\prime},X_{b},K^{\prime})
inducing a cone decomposition
\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}
refining
\Sigma_{2}
for
(G_{2},X_{2},K_{2})
, such that the strata between toroidal (with cones chosen as above) and minimal compactifications of
(G_{2},X_{2})
and
(G^{\prime},X_{a})
are all open and closed embeddings, and such that the map between mixed Shimura varieties for any cusp label representative
\Phi_{2}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G_{2},X_{2})
mapping to a cusp label representative
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G^{\prime},X_{a})
is an isomorphism. (Note that
K^{\prime}_{p}
is chosen such that
K^{\prime}_{p}\cap G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=K_{2,p}
.) There is an identification
\mathcal{CLR}(G^{\prime},X_{a})=\mathcal{CLR}(G^{\prime},X_{b})
by definition and construction.

•

Denote
I_{G^{\prime}/G,K^{\prime}}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q})}(X)\pi^{b}(G(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\backslash G^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\prime}
.
Choose a complete system
\{g_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I_{G^{\prime}/G,K^{\prime}}}
of representatives of
I_{G^{\prime}/G,K^{\prime}}
in
G^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.
Let
K^{\alpha}_{p}:=\pi^{b,-1}(g_{\alpha}K^{\prime}_{p}g_{\alpha}^{-1})\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Choose suitable neat open compact
K^{\alpha,p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and denote
K^{\alpha}:=K^{\alpha}_{p}K^{\alpha,p}
.
We obtain the induced cone decompositions
\Sigma^{\alpha}:=\pi^{b,*}(\Sigma)
.

•

In our case, we assume that
K_{p}^{\prime}
is a quasi-parahoric subgroup, which is the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-points of a quasi-parahoric group scheme
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
corresponding to
{x}\in\mathcal{B}(G^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Following the construction in Case (STB) of
[
83
, §4.2]
, there is a Siegel-type Shimura datum
(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})=(\mathrm{GSp}(V,\psi),X^{\ddagger})
with an embedding
\rho:(G,X)\hookrightarrow(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
obtained as follows:

For any
\alpha
, we choose
K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\alpha}\subset G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and neat open compact
K^{{\ddagger},\alpha,p}\subset G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that

\rho^{\alpha}:(G,X,K_{p}^{\alpha})\hookrightarrow(G^{{\ddagger},\alpha},X^{{\ddagger},\alpha},K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\alpha})

is an adjusted Hodge embedding satisfying the setup in both
[
58
, §3.1]
and
[
15
]
.

In fact,
G^{{\ddagger},\alpha}:=\mathrm{GSp}(W^{\alpha},\psi^{\alpha})
,
K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\alpha,\sharp}=\mathrm{Stab}_{G^{{\ddagger},\alpha}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}(W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\alpha})
for some self-dual
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lattice
W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\alpha}
in
W_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}^{\alpha}
. Moreover,
W_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\alpha}
comes from a self-dual
\mathbb{Z}
-lattice
W_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\alpha}
.

Furthermore, there are positive integers
n_{\alpha}
such that
(W^{\alpha},\psi^{\alpha})^{\perp n_{\alpha}}=(V,\psi)
for a symplectic space
(V,\psi)
for all
\alpha
. By defining
V^{\alpha}_{?}:=W_{?}^{\alpha,\perp n_{\alpha}}
for
?=\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,
(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger})
as the Siegel Shimura datum defined by
(V,\psi)
, and
K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\alpha}:=\mathrm{Stab}_{G^{\ddagger}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\alpha})
, we obtain from
\rho^{\alpha}
the Hodge embeddings

\iota^{\alpha}:(G,X,K_{p}^{\alpha})\hookrightarrow(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger},K_{p}^{{\ddagger},\alpha}).

After this slight modification,
[
60
, Thm. 3.58]
still holds.

•

By
[
83
, Prop. 1.47]
again, cone decompositions
\Sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}
can be chosen for
(G^{\ddagger},X^{\ddagger},K^{{\ddagger},\alpha})
such that, for any
\alpha
,
\Sigma^{\alpha}
is induced by both
\Sigma
via
\pi^{b}(g_{\alpha})
and
\Sigma^{{\ddagger},\alpha}
via
\iota^{\alpha}
, and such that
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\alpha}}^{\Sigma^{\alpha}}
can be constructed satisfying Axiom
5.1
according to
[
58
]
.

We now recall some schematic constructions in
[
83
]
.

Construction 6.16
.

We recall the construction of integral models of boundary mixed Shimura varieties
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}
for
\Phi_{2}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G_{2},X_{2})
following
[
83
, Sec. 4.2]
in Case (STB) and its normalization (DL) case to parahoric levels.

(1)

We introduce more group-theoretic setup related to Bruhat-Tits theory. In general, there are maps between groups
\pi^{a}:K_{2,p}\hookrightarrow K_{p}^{\prime}
and
\pi^{b}(g_{\alpha}):K_{p}^{\alpha}\to g_{\alpha}K_{p}^{\prime}g_{\alpha}^{-1}
. Starting with
\Phi_{2}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G_{2},X_{2})
, let
\Phi
be the image of
\Phi_{2}
in
\mathcal{CLR}(G^{\prime},X_{a})\cong\mathcal{CLR}(G^{\prime},X_{b})
.

Fix a point in the reduced building
x\in\mathcal{B}(G_{2},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. We denote by
K_{2,p}^{\circ}
(resp.
K_{2,p}^{\mathrm{stb}}
and
K_{2,p}
) the parahoric (resp. the stabilizer quasi-parahoric and resp. a general quasi-parahoric) subgroup of
G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
corresponding to
x
. For
\Phi_{2}
, define
K_{\Phi_{2},p}^{\mathrm{min}}:=P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap g_{\Phi_{2}}K_{2,p}^{\circ}g_{\Phi_{2}}^{-1}
(resp.
K_{\Phi_{2},p}^{\mathrm{stb}}:=P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap g_{\Phi_{2}}K_{2,p}^{\mathrm{stb}}g_{\Phi_{2}}^{-1}
and resp.
K_{\Phi_{2},p}:=P_{\Phi_{2}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap g_{\Phi_{2}}K_{2,p}g_{\Phi_{2}}^{-1}
); note that
K_{\Phi_{2},p}^{\mathrm{stb}}
is still a stabilizer quasi-parahoric subgroup. Let
K_{p}^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}
be the stabilizer quasi-parahoric subgroup in
G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
corresponding to
x
. Similarly, define
K_{\Phi,p}^{\mathrm{stb}}:=P_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap g_{\Phi_{2}}K_{p}^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}g_{\Phi_{2}}^{-1}
and
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}^{\mathrm{stb}}:=ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap g_{\Phi_{2}}K_{p}^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}g_{\Phi_{2}}^{-1}
. Note that we can still use Definition
1.13
and Lemma
3.3
to get that
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}^{\mathrm{stb}}
is a stabilizer quasi-parahoric subgroup. The prime-to-
p
neat open compact subgroups are chosen and written in an obvious pattern.

(2)

Denote by
(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi}^{b})
(resp.
(P_{\Phi},D^{a}_{\Phi})
) the mixed Shimura datum associated with
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X_{b})
. We construct
\{\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{\mathrm{stb}}_{\Phi}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})\}_{K^{\prime,p}}
and
\{\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{\mathrm{stb}}_{\Phi}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{a}_{\Phi})\}_{K^{\prime,p}}
.
Note that the Shimura datum
(G,X)
is chosen such that any place
v^{\prime}
of
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}:=\mathbb{E}(G,X)\cdot\mathbb{E}(G_{2},X_{2})
over a place
v_{2}
of
\mathbb{E}_{2}:=\mathbb{E}(G_{2},X_{2})
over
p
splits completely over
v_{2}
.

We define
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})
over
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{E}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{E},(v)}
for any place
v
of
\mathbb{E}:=\mathbb{E}(G,X)
.
For any cusp label
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G^{\prime},X_{b})
extending to a
ZP
-cusp label
[ZP^{b}(\Phi)]
, by
[
83
, (4.22)]
, we construct

(6.6)

\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi}):=\coprod_{\alpha\in I_{G^{\prime}/G,K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}}}\coprod_{\pi^{b}(g_{0}^{\alpha})\alpha\sim g^{b}}\coprod_{[\sigma^{\alpha}]\in[\sigma]_{ZP}}\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}/\Delta_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi}).

The group
\Delta_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha},K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi})
is a group that acts on
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}}
through a finite group; this group is defined and functorial in
all
neat
K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}
; its quotient is finite étale by
[
83
, Lem. 4.25]
. Writing in this form a priori depends on the choice of
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\alpha}}^{\Sigma^{\alpha}}
and, in particular, on the choice of
\Sigma^{\alpha}
. But we can also re-label the disjoint union in (
6.6
) as

(6.7)

\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi}):=\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi,K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}}}\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}}}/\Delta_{\Phi_{\beta},K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi}),

where
\Phi_{\beta}
are cusp label representatives in
\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
that occurred in (
6.6
) and the index set
I_{\Phi,K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}}
is defined in
[
83
, 4.2.6]
; it depends only on
\Phi
,
K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}
, the Shimura data
(G,X)
and the group
G^{\prime}
, satisfying obvious functoriality for varying
K^{\prime}\subset G^{\prime}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. The level group
K_{\Phi_{\beta}}
is defined as
P_{\Phi_{\beta}}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi_{\beta}}K^{\alpha}g^{-1}_{\Phi_{\beta}}
, where
K_{p}^{\alpha}=\pi^{b,-1}(g_{\alpha}K_{p}^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}g_{\alpha}^{-1})
.

Replacing
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})
with
\mathscr{S}_{\overline{\widetilde{K}}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})
(resp.
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,h}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})
), the integral model is constructed by replacing the Hodge-type mixed Shimura varieties
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}}}
in (
6.7
) with
\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi_{\beta}}}
(resp.
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta},h}}
). Again, the group action of
\Delta_{\Phi_{\beta}}
factors through a finite group that acts freely on
\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi_{\beta}}}
(resp.
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta},h}}
). Moreover, there is a sequence of morphisms

(6.8)

\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})\to\mathscr{S}_{\overline{\widetilde{K}}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})\to\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,h}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi}).

The first morphism is a torsor under a split torus
\mathbf{E}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
, and the second morphism is an abelian scheme torsor by
[
83
, Prop. 4.30]
.

To construct
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{a}_{\Phi})
, we first base change
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})
to
\mathcal{O}^{ur}
such that
\mathcal{O}^{ur}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{E}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{E},(v)}
where
\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime}
is finite extension of
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}
that is unramified over
p
, and
that the generic fiber of the base change
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})_{\mathcal{O}^{ur}}
is
\mathrm{Sh}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})_{\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime}}
. Thus, the integral model
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{a}_{\Phi})
is obtained from desending the model
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})_{\mathcal{O}^{ur}}
with the descend datum given by the Shimura datum
(G,X_{a})
. There is also a sequence of morphisms as (
6.8
) replacing “
b
” with “
a
” having the same property.

(3)

Let
K_{p}^{\prime}
be a quasi-parahoric subgroup associated with
K_{p}^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}
. Denote
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}^{\prime}=ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\cap g_{\Phi}K^{\prime}_{p}g_{\Phi}^{-1}
. We construct
\{\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})\}_{K^{\prime,p}}
as the normalization from the tower
\{\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})\}_{K^{\prime,p}}
.
More explicitly, we also construct
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})
as
\coprod_{\beta^{\prime}\in I_{\Phi,K^{\prime}}}\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}/\Delta_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime},K^{\prime}}}(ZP_{\Phi})
. Each
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
is constructed as follows: For Any
\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
, the integral model
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\mathrm{stb}}_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
at stabilizer quasi-parahoric level is constructed as before. Then
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}
, the integral model assciated with the same cusp label representative but at the quasi-parahoric level, is defined by taking relative normalization from
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{\mathrm{stb}}_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})
. There is a finite map

\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}}/\Delta_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}},K^{\prime}}(ZP_{\Phi})\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}}}^{\mathrm{stb}}}/\Delta_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}},K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi}).

There is also a sequence as (
6.8
) for
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})
.

We can also construct
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{a}_{\Phi})
with the same method as above. See
[
83
, Prop. 4.30 and Const. 4.33]
.

(4)

We construct
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}}\}_{K_{2}^{p}}
,
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\}_{K_{2}^{p}}
and
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{stb}}}\}_{K_{2}^{p}}
. In fact, they are constructed by taking the relative normalization of
\{\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{\mathrm{stb}}_{\Phi}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{a}_{\Phi})\}_{K^{\prime,p}}
in
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}}\}_{K_{2}^{p}}
,
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\}_{K_{2}^{p}}
and
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{stb}}}\}_{K_{2}^{p}}
, respectively.

\square

6.2.2.
Choosing accessible Hodge-type liftings

Definition 6.17
.

Fix a prime number
p
.
Let
(G,X)
and
(G_{1},X_{1})
be Shimura data with a central isogeny
\pi:G^{\mathrm{der}}\to G^{\mathrm{der}}_{1}
such that
\pi
induces an isomorphism between the two adjoint Shimura data. Let
\mathcal{G}^{\circ}_{1,x}
be a parahoric group scheme corresponding to
x
in the building
\mathcal{B}(G_{1},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, and
\mathcal{G}^{\circ}_{x}
be the corresponding parahoric group scheme of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.

We say that
\breve{K}_{p}^{\circ}:=\mathcal{G}_{x}^{\circ}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

is accessible to

\breve{K}^{\circ}_{1,p}:=\mathcal{G}_{1,x}^{\circ}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
if the map
\pi:G^{\mathrm{der}}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\to G_{1}^{\mathrm{der}}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
restricts to a map

(6.9)

\breve{K}_{p}^{\circ}\cap G^{\mathrm{der}}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})\to\breve{K}_{1,p}^{\circ}\cap G^{\mathrm{der}}_{1}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}).

We say that
(G,X)

is accessible to

(G_{1},X_{1})
(at
p
with respect to
\pi
) if, for any point
x
in the reduced building of
G_{1,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
,
\breve{K}_{p}^{\circ}
is always accessible to
\breve{K}^{\circ}_{1,p}
.

Convention 6.18
.

Let
H
and
G
be two connected reductive groups over
\mathbb{Q}
or
\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with a map
f:H\to G
. Denote
\ker(f:\pi_{1}(H_{\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})_{I}\to\pi_{1}(G_{\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})_{I})
by
\pi(H,G)
.

Given
f:H\to G
and
g:G\to G^{\prime}
, we have, by definition, an inclusion

\pi(H,G)\subset\pi(H,G^{\prime})
.

It follows from the definition of (quasi-)parahoric subgroups that

Lemma 6.19
.

To show that
(G,X)
is accessible to
(G_{1},X_{1})
, it suffices to show that

\pi(G^{\mathrm{der}},G)\subset\pi(G^{\mathrm{der}},G_{1}).

Proposition 6.20
.

Fix a prime
p
. Let
(G_{2},X_{2})
be an abelian-type Shimura datum. We assume that there is always a Hodge-type lifting
(G,X)
of
(G_{2},X_{2})
such that

(1)

For any place
v^{\prime}
of
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}
over a place
v_{2}
of
\mathbb{E}_{2}
over
p
,
v^{\prime}
splits completely over
v_{2}
;

(2)

(G,X)
is accessible to
(G_{2},X_{2})
at
p
.

Then, for any cusp label representative
\Phi_{2}\in\mathcal{CLR}(G_{2},X_{2})
and any quasi-parahoric subgroup
K_{\Phi_{2},p}
as in Construction
6.16
(1), the maps

\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{stb}}}
,

\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}^{\mathrm{stb}}_{\Phi_{2}}}

and

\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}^{\mathrm{stb}}}

are finite étale.

Proof.

With these two assumptions, the equality
\pi^{b}(K_{p}^{\circ})\pi^{a}(K_{2,p})\cap G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=K_{2,p}
holds.
Indeed, it suffices to show that
\pi^{b}(K_{p}^{\circ})\cap G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset K_{2,p}
. Since
\pi^{b}(G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))\cap G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset G_{2}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, we show that
\pi^{b}(K_{p}^{\circ})\cap G^{\mathrm{der}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset K_{2,p}\cap G^{\mathrm{der}}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Since the kernel of
\pi^{b}
is in the center of
G^{\mathrm{der}}
, we know that if
x\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
maps to
G^{\prime,\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=G_{2}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, then
x\in G^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Thus,
\pi^{b}(K_{p}^{\circ}\cap G^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))=\pi^{b}(K_{p}^{\circ})\cap G^{\prime,\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Finally, we know from the assumption that
\pi^{b}(K_{p}^{\circ}\cap G^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}))=\pi^{b}(K_{p}^{\circ})\cap G^{\prime,\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset K_{2,p}\cap G_{2}^{\mathrm{der}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

When this equality holds, for any quasi-parahoric
K_{2,p}\subset G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, we can choose
K_{p}^{\prime}\subset G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
such that
K_{p}^{\prime}\cap G_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=K_{2,p}
. Moreover,
\pi^{b,-1}(K_{p}^{\prime})
are quasi-parahoric subgroups. See
[
83
, Lem. 4.57]
.

Now,
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{stb}}}
can be constructed by choosing the corresponding stabilizer quasi-parahoric subgroup
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}^{\mathrm{stb}}
of
ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, and
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{min}}}
can be constructed from
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}}
such that
\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,p}
is an open compact subgroup whose preimage in
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
contains the corresponding parahoric subgroups of
P_{\Phi_{0}^{\alpha}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Then, by the statement that the quotient of each
\Delta_{\Phi_{\beta^{\prime}},K^{\prime}}(ZP_{\Phi})
is finite étale proved in
[
83
, Lem. 4.25]
, we are reduced to showing the corresponding statement for Hodge-type Shimura data. This follows from Proposition
4.9
(2), noting that the assumption there is trivial by
[
83
, Cor. 1.9]
.
∎

The crucial part is to show that

Proposition 6.21
.

There always exists a Hodge-type lifting
(G,X)
such that the assumptions in Proposition
6.20
are true.

Construction 6.22
.

For an abelian-type
(G_{2},X_{2})
, we associate a Hodge-type
(G,X)
such that it satisfies (1) of Proposition
6.20
; this can be done by the construction as in
[
17
, Prop. 2.3.10]
and
[
46
]
for any prime
p
. We now modify the Shimura datum to make it to satisfy (2) of Proposition
6.20
.

Let
(T,h)\subset(G,X)
be a special point with
T
a maximal torus. We first consider
G^{db}:=G\times_{G^{ab}}G
where the map
G\to G^{ab}=G/G^{\mathrm{der}}
is the natural one. Denote
T^{ab}:=T/(T\cap G^{\mathrm{der}})
. Define a refined construction
G^{rf}:=G\times_{G^{ab}}T
.

Since
(G,X)
is of Hodge-type, we can decompose
G
to an almost-direct product
G=G^{\mathrm{der}}\cdot Z^{c}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{m}
, where
Z^{c}
is an
\mathbb{R}
-anisotropic torus. Then
G^{db}\cong(G^{\mathrm{der}}\times G^{\mathrm{der}})\cdot Z^{c}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{m}\subset G\times G
.

In the second expression of the above line,
Z^{c}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{m}
maps to
G\times G
diagonally into
(Z^{c}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{m})\times(Z^{c}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{m})
. Alternatively,
G^{db}\cong((G^{\mathrm{der}}\times G^{\mathrm{der}})\times Z^{c}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{m})/Z^{c}\cdot\mathbb{G}_{m}\cap G^{\mathrm{der}}
, where the last intersection maps to the first factor diagonally and to the second factor naturally.

Denote the corresponding Shimura data by
(G^{db},X^{db})
and
(G^{rf},X^{rf})
, respectively. There is a natural map
(G^{rf},X^{rf})\to(G^{db},X^{db})
. Then both of them are of Hodge type because they are both contained in the Hodge-type Shimura datum defined by
G\times_{\eta,\mathbb{G}_{m},\eta}G
.

Proposition 6.23
.

Proposition
6.21
is true for any prime
p
. Given
(G_{2},X_{2})
of abelian type, the accessible Hodge-type lifting is
(G^{rf},X^{rf})
, as given by Construction
6.22
.

More precisely,
\pi(G^{rf,\mathrm{der}},G^{rf})
is trivial. In particular, the intersection of any parahoric subgroup
\breve{K}_{p}
of
G^{rf}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
with
G^{rf,\mathrm{der}}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
is parahoric.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
\pi(G^{rf,\mathrm{der}},G^{rf})
is trivial.
We first consider
\pi(G^{db,\mathrm{der}},G^{db})
. By Lemma
6.24
below, we have that

\pi(G^{db,\mathrm{der}},G^{db})=\operatorname{diag}_{\{1,2\}}\{\pi(G^{\mathrm{der}},G)\},

where the index
\{1,2\}
labels the two factors of
G^{db,\mathrm{der}}=G^{\mathrm{der}}\times G^{\mathrm{der}}
.

Note that
G^{rf,\mathrm{der}}=G^{\mathrm{der}}\times\{1\}\subset G^{db,\mathrm{der}}
. Then
\pi(G^{\mathrm{der}}\times\{1\},G^{db})\subset\pi(G^{db,\mathrm{der}},G^{db})
is trivial. So
\pi(G^{rf,\mathrm{der}},G^{rf})\subset\pi(G^{rf,\mathrm{der}},G^{db})
is trivial.
∎

Lemma 6.24
.

Let
G
be any reductive group,
\pi:G\to G^{ab}
,
G^{db}=G\times_{G^{ab}}G
. Then

\pi(G^{db,\mathrm{der}},G^{db})=\operatorname{diag}_{\{1,2\}}\pi(G^{\mathrm{der}},G)\subset\pi(G^{\mathrm{der}}\times G^{\mathrm{der}},G\times G).

Proof.

Consider the commutative diagram of exact sequences:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{\mathrm{der}}\times G^{\mathrm{der}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{\mathrm{der}}\times G^{\mathrm{der}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{db}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G\times G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{ab}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{ab}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{ab}\times G^{ab}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{ab}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{0.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi,\pi^{-1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id}^{-1})}

Take the long exact sequences of it,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{1}(I,\pi_{1}(G^{ab})_{I})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{1}(I,\pi_{1}(G^{ab})_{I})\times H_{1}(I,\pi_{1}(G^{ab})_{I})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{1}(G^{\mathrm{der}})_{I}\times\pi_{1}(G^{\mathrm{der}})_{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{1}(G^{\mathrm{der}})_{I}\times\pi_{1}(G^{\mathrm{der}})_{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{1}(G^{db})_{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{1}(G)_{I}\times\pi_{1}(G)_{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta(\delta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\delta,\delta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

From the diagram, we see that
\pi(G^{db,\mathrm{der}},G^{db})=\operatorname{im}\Delta(\delta)
, and
\pi(G^{\mathrm{der}}\times G^{\mathrm{der}},G\times G)=\operatorname{im}\delta\times\delta
. It follows from definition that
\operatorname{im}\Delta(\delta)\subset\operatorname{im}\delta\times\delta
is exactly
\operatorname{diag}_{\{1,2\}}\operatorname{im}\delta=\operatorname{diag}_{\{1,2\}}\pi(G^{\mathrm{der}},G)
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.21
.

Now let
(G,X)
be any Hodge lifting of
(G_{2},X_{2})
. Since
G^{db}\subset G\times_{\eta,\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}},\eta}G
, where
\eta:G\to\mathbb{G}_{\mathrm{m}}
is the similitude character (here
\eta
factors through
G^{ab}
),
(G^{db},X^{db})
is a Hodge-type Shimura datum. Therefore,
(G^{rf},X^{rf})
is a Hodge-lifting of
(G_{2},X_{2})
and satisfies the second condition of Proposition
6.20
.

\square

Remark 6.25
.

We remark that, in our construction,
G=G^{rf}
might not be
R
-smooth when
p=2
, and that
Z_{G}
might not be connected. Some supplementary examples are provided in a note
[
59
]
.

6.2.3.
Main theorem for boundary mixed Shimura varieties of abelian type

Theorem 6.26
.

Let
(G_{2},X_{2})
be an abelian-type Shimura datum, and let
\mathcal{G}_{2}
be any quasi-parahoric model of
G_{2,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. Then the construction above gives a family of canonical integral models

\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}(P_{\Phi_{2}},D_{\Phi_{2}})\}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{p}}

that is adapted with
P_{\Phi_{2}}\to G_{\Phi_{2},h}
and
G_{2}\to G_{2}^{c}
. Here we can take
\{K_{\Phi}^{p}\}
to be the collection of all neat open compact subgroups in
G_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.

Proof.

As the first step, we show that
\{\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}:=\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})\}_{K^{\prime,p}}
is a canonical integral model adapted with
ZP_{\Phi}\to ZG_{\Phi,h}:=ZP_{\Phi}/W_{\Phi}
and
ZP_{\Phi}\to ZP_{\Phi}^{c}
in the sense of Definition
4.6
and Definition
4.5
, respectively.

Note that there is a morphism
\pi^{c}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}:\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb},c}}(ZP_{\Phi}^{c},(ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})^{c})
by construction. Indeed, in Construction
6.16
, we replace
G^{\prime}
in
(G^{\prime},X_{b})
with
G^{\prime,c}
. Since
G^{\prime}=G_{2}\times^{G^{\mathrm{der}}_{2}}(G/C^{\mathrm{der}})
and
G=G^{c}
, we see that
\pi^{b,c}:(G,X)\to(G^{\prime,c},X_{b}^{c})
also satisfies the setup in Construction
6.14
.
The morphism
\pi^{c}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}
is finite étale since we can write the map as

\coprod_{\beta\in I_{\Phi,K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}}}\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}}}/\Delta_{\Phi_{\beta},K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi})\to\coprod_{\beta^{c}\in I_{\Phi^{c},K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb},c}}}\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{c}}}}/\Delta_{\Phi_{\beta^{c}},K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb},c}}(ZP_{\Phi}^{c}),

and for each
\beta
mapping to
\beta^{\prime}
, there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{c}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}}}/\Delta_{\Phi_{\beta},K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta^{c}}}}/\Delta_{\Phi_{\beta^{c}},K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb},c}}(ZP_{\Phi}^{c}),}

such that all but the bottom arrow are already known to be finite étale.

We check that the models
\{\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}\}_{K^{\prime,p}}
are Pappas-Rapoport canonical integral models in the sense of Axiom
4.2
. For (2), it follows from the construction and
[
83
, Lem. 4.25]
. For (1), we choose any discrete valuation ring
R
with fraction field
F
.
Choose any prime
l\neq p
. Choose a
F
-point
x
of
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb},l}}
.
Then it follows from Construction
6.16
(2) (and (3)) and the proof of
[
83
, Prop. 4.30]
that the action of
\varprojlim_{K_{l}^{\prime}}\Delta_{\Phi_{\beta},K^{\prime,\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi})
on
\varprojlim_{K_{l}}\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}}}
factors through a
finite
group. Thus, by Lemma
6.13
, there is a finite field extension
F^{\prime}
of
F
, such that
x
lifts to an
F^{\prime}
-point
\widetilde{x}
of some
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}}^{l}}
.

Denote by
R^{\prime}
the normalization of
R
in
F^{\prime}
; this is still a
discrete
valuation ring. Then we can apply Lemma
6.13
in the Hodge-type case to get a unique extension of
\widetilde{x}
to an
R^{\prime}
-point
\widetilde{y}
of
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}}^{l}}
. This point maps to an
R^{\prime}
-point
y
of
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb},l}}
. The uniqueness of this lifting follows from separatedness. Now (a stronger version of) (1) follows because
R^{\prime}\cap F=R
.

We now check (3) and (4). Denote by
(\mathscr{P}_{\beta},\phi_{\beta})
the
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{\beta}}
-shtuka on
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{\beta}}}^{\diamond}
given by Proposition
6.11
.
We push out
\mathscr{P}_{\beta}
via
\mathcal{P}_{\Phi_{\beta}}\to g_{\Phi_{\beta}}\mathcal{ZP}_{\Phi}^{c}g_{\Phi_{\beta}}^{-1}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{ZP}_{\Phi}^{c}
; we denote by
(\mathscr{Q}_{\beta},\psi_{\beta})
the pushout.
By checking over the generic fiber, the
\Delta_{\Phi_{\beta}}
-action lifts to an action on
(\mathscr{Q}_{\beta},\psi_{\beta})
. By étale descent, there is a
\mathcal{ZP}_{\Phi}^{c}
-shtuka
(\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},\widetilde{\phi})
on
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}^{\diamond}
.
This checks (3). For (4), it follows again from corresponding (4) in the Hodge-type case and
[
83
, Lem. 4.25]
and from the dévissage of integral local Shimura varieties by
[
67
, Prop. 5.3.1]
.

We can also verify Axiom
4.2
for
\{\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}^{\mathrm{stb}}_{\Phi,h}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})\}
,
\{\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb},c}}(ZP_{\Phi}^{c},(ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})^{c})\}
and
\{\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,h}^{\mathrm{stb},c}}(ZP_{\Phi}^{c},(ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{b}_{\Phi})^{c})\}
in exactly the same way. We now have finished the first step.

For the second step, we show that above properties for integral models associated with
(G^{\prime},X_{b},\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}})
can be transferred to the integral models associated with
(G^{\prime},X_{a},\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}})
: All geometric properties required here can be checked over an étale cover. We base change to
\mathcal{O}^{ur}
and descend the schemes to
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{a}_{\Phi})\to\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi,h}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{a}_{\Phi})
and
\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{a}_{\Phi})\to\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi}^{c},(ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{a}_{\Phi})^{c})
. The latter map is again finite étale by étale descent. Note that the Weil descent datum of shtukas extends from the generic fiber by
[
66
, Cor. 2.7.10]
.

For the third step, we show that
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{stb}}}\}
,
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}^{\mathrm{stb}}}\}
,
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{stb},c}}\}
,
\{\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}^{\mathrm{stb},c}}\}
satisfy Axiom
4.2
and has the desired properties. We apply Lemma
4.8
(1) to the embedding of mixed Shimura data
(P_{\Phi_{2}},D_{\Phi_{2}})=(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})\to(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D_{\Phi}^{a})
, to verify axiom
(3)
(and also axiom
(1)
,
(2)
, as in the proof of Proposition
4.9
(1)). Note that, since the whole tower
\{\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{a}_{\Phi})\}_{K^{\prime,p}}
is constructed, we can adjust prime-to-
p
levels so that the cited lemma can be applied. In fact, we adjust
K^{\prime,p}
so that both
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{stb}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb}}}(ZP_{\Phi},ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{a}_{\Phi})
and
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{stb},c}}\to\mathscr{S}_{\widetilde{K}_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{stb},c}}(ZP_{\Phi}^{c},(ZP_{\Phi}(\mathbb{Q})D^{a}_{\Phi})^{c})
are open and closed embeddings. We see from this that
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{stb}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{stb},c}}
is finite étale for any
K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{stb},p}
. In particular, axiom
(4)
follows easily, using the
dévissage
of integral local Shimura varieties (
[
67
, Prop. 5.3.1]
).

Now, we consider a general quasi-parahoric level
K_{2,p}
and finish by applying Proposition
4.9
(2) to
K_{2,p}\to K_{2,p}^{\mathrm{stb}}
. We just need to verify the condition highlighted there. We adopt the conventions in Construction
6.16
. In fact,
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{c}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{stb},c}}
is finite étale by applying Proposition
4.9
(2), whose condition is trivial for this case. Now, it suffices to show that
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2}}^{\mathrm{stb}}}
,

\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi_{2}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}^{\mathrm{stb}}_{\Phi_{2}}}

and

\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi_{2},h}^{\mathrm{stb}}}

are finite étale. This is done by Proposition
6.21
and Proposition
6.20
.
∎

7.
Well-positioned subschemes

Fix a Shimura datum
(G,X)
and a quasi-parahoric model
\mathcal{G}
of
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. For each
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
, we denote
G_{\Phi}=gG_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}g^{-1}
, where
g=g_{\Phi,p}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. In this subsection, we generalize the results in
[
11
]
,
[
52
]
and
[
61
]
. In order to present the nature of those well-positioned subschemes,
we work under Assumption
5.26
.

Let us recall the definition of a well-positioned subscheme.

Definition/Proposition 7.1

(
[
52
, Def. 2.2.1 and Lem. 2.2.2]
)
.

Let
T
be a locally noetherian scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
. A locally closed subset (resp. subscheme)
Y
of
\mathscr{S}_{K,T}:=(\mathscr{S}_{K})_{T}
is called
well positioned
, if, for each
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
, there exists a locally closed subset (resp. subscheme)
Y^{\natural}(\Phi)\subset\mathrm{Z}(\Phi)_{T}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{Z}([\Phi])_{T}
such that for some (thus for all) cone decompositions
\Sigma
, and for each
\sigma\in\Sigma(\Phi)^{+}
, there are some (thus for all) open affine coverings
\mathfrak{W}
of
\mathfrak{X}^{\circ}_{\sigma}
satisfying the following property: the pullback of
Y^{\natural}(\Phi)
along

W^{0}_{T}\to\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\Xi(\Phi)_{T}\to\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash C(\Phi)_{T}\to\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)_{T}\to\mathrm{Z}(\Phi)_{T}

coincides with the pullback of
Y
along
W^{0}_{T}\to\mathscr{S}_{K,T}
as a subset (resp. subscheme). If this is the case, we say
Y
is well positioned with respect to
Y^{\natural}:=\{Y^{\natural}(\Phi)\}_{\Phi}
, and
Y^{\natural}
is associated with
Y
.

In practice, we usually take
T=s=\operatorname{Spec}k_{E}
or
T=\bar{s}=\operatorname{Spec}\bar{k}_{E}
.

Remark 7.2
.

Compared with
[
52
, Def. 2.2.1 and Lem. 2.2.2]
, we need to consider the
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}
-action. Note that
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}
is trivial in the Hodge-type case, and thus was not considered in
[
52
]
. With this modification, if we replace everything

(7.1)

\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi),\ C(\Phi),\ \Xi(\Phi),\ \Xi(\Phi)(\sigma),\ \Xi(\Phi)_{\sigma},\ \overline{\Xi}_{\Sigma},\ \mathfrak{X}_{[\Phi,\sigma]},\ \mathfrak{X}_{[\Phi,\sigma]}^{\circ},\ \mathfrak{X}_{\Sigma}

by their quotients under the
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}
-action, then the statements and proofs in
[
52
]
and
[
61
]
work without change.

Remark 7.3
.

The quotient map
\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)\to\mathrm{Z}(\Phi)
is finite étale by
[
83
, Cor. 4.27]
. It follows that
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)\to\mathrm{Z}(\Phi)
is also finite étale. Under Assumption
5.26
,
\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash C(\Phi)\to\mathrm{Z}(\Phi)
is flat with geometrically reduced fibers, which verifies
[
61
, Assumption 2.7]
. In this situation, well-positioned subschemes enjoy more satisfactory properties.

Definition 7.4

(
[
52
, Def. 2.3.1]
)
.

Let
Y
be a locally closed subscheme of
\mathscr{S}_{K,T}
. Let
\overline{Y}
be the closure of
Y
in
\mathscr{S}_{K,T}
,
Y_{0}=\overline{Y}\backslash Y
the complement of
Y
in
\overline{Y}
. Let
\overline{Y}^{\min}
and
Y_{0}^{\min}
be the closure of
\overline{Y}
and
Y_{0}
in
\mathscr{S}_{K,T}^{\min}
respectively,
Y^{\min}
be the complement of
Y_{0}^{\min}
in
\overline{Y}^{\min}
. Similarly we define
\overline{Y}^{\Sigma}
,
Y_{0}^{\Sigma}
,
Y^{\Sigma}
. We call
Y^{\min}
(resp.
Y^{\Sigma}
) the
partial minimal compactification
(resp. the
partial toroidal compactification
with respect to
\Sigma
) of
Y
.

If
Y\subset\mathscr{S}_{K,T}
is well positioned with respect to
Y^{\natural}:=\{Y^{\natural}(\Phi)\}_{\Phi}
, then
\oint_{K,T}^{\Sigma}:\mathscr{S}_{K,T}^{\Sigma}\to\mathscr{S}_{K,T}^{\min}
induces a morphism
\oint_{Y}^{\Sigma}:Y^{\Sigma}\to Y^{\min}
. Moreover,
(\oint_{K,T}^{\Sigma})^{-1}(Y^{\min})=Y^{\Sigma}
, and the compactifications
Y^{\Sigma}\to Y^{\min}
of
Y
satisfy Axiom
5.1
except for the flatness and normality of
Y^{\Sigma}
, of
Y^{\min}
, and of their boundary strata. See
[
52
, Thm. 2.3.2]
. Note that, in Axiom
5.1
, for any scheme (or formal scheme) in (
7.1
), we replace
(?)
by
Y_{(?)}^{\natural}
, which is defined as the pullback of
Y^{\natural}(\Phi)
along
(?)\to\mathrm{Z}(\Phi)
. The identification
\mathrm{Z}(\Phi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{Z}([\Phi])
induces a canonical morphism
Y^{\natural}(\Phi)\to Y_{\mathcal{Z}([\Phi])}:=(Y^{\min}\cap\mathcal{Z}([\Phi]))
, which induces a bijection on the underlying sets.

7.1.
Newton strata and central leaves

We define Newton strata and central leaves following
[
66
]
. Let
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)(k)
. Pulling back the universal
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
over
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\Diamond/}
, we get a
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P}_{x},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{x}}):=x^{*}(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
over
\operatorname{Spec}(k)
, which is associated with a
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-torsor on
\operatorname{Spec}W(k)
together with an isomorphism
\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{x}}:\phi^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{x})[1/p]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{P}_{x}[1/p]
. A choice of trivialization of
\mathscr{P}_{x}
defines an element
b_{x}\in G^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
; a different choice of trivialization gives
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
-
\sigma
-conjugation of
b_{x}
. Let
C(\mathcal{G}^{c})=G^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})_{\sigma}
and
B(G^{c})=G^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/G^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})_{\sigma}
; we have

(7.2)

\Upsilon_{K}(k):\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)(k)\to C(\mathcal{G}^{c}),\quad\delta_{K}(k):\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)(k)\to B(G^{c}).

Since
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
is bounded by
\mu^{c}
, the
\sigma
-conjugation class of
b_{x}
sits inside
B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})\subset B(G^{c})
.

One can define
\Upsilon_{K}
,
\delta_{K}
globally, without referring to
k
-points. In fact, in subsection
1.3.3
, we recall a natural projection
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}^{W}\to G^{c}\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}_{\mu^{c,-1}}
. By construction,
\Upsilon_{K}(k)
and
\delta_{K}(k)
in (
7.2
) are the
k
-points of
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}^{\textrm{perf}}\to\mathrm{Sht}^{W}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}
and
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}^{\textrm{perf}}\to G^{c}\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}_{\mu^{c,-1}}
, respectively. Note that perfection does not change the underlying topological space, so we have a morphism

\delta_{K}:\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}\to B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})

whose
k
-points are
\delta_{K}(k)
. Given
[b]\in B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
with
b\in G^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, let
\mathcal{N}^{[b]}\subset\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}
be the preimage
\delta_{K}^{-1}([b])
; we call it the
Newton stratum
associated with
[b]
.
[
71
, Thm. 3.6]
says that
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}\to B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
is semi-lower continuous; therefore, Newton strata are locally closed. We endow them with the unique reduced subscheme structures.

Now we define
\Upsilon_{K}
globally. Given
[[b]]\in C(\mathcal{G}^{c})
with
b\in G^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, let
\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}(k)\subset\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)(k)
(resp.
\mathcal{N}^{[b]}(k)\subset\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)(k)
) be the preimage
\Upsilon_{K}(k)^{-1}([[b]])
(resp.
\delta_{K}(k)^{-1}([b])
).

Lemma 7.5
.

\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}(k)\subset\mathcal{N}^{[b]}(k)
is closed.

Proof.

Consider the diagram

(7.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ}}(G,X)^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\prime\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{\circ,c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square}

where
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\prime\Diamond/}
is the fiber product, and
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ}}(G,X)
is the relative normalization of
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{K^{\circ}}(G,X)
. Since
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c,\circ},\mu^{c},\delta=1}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}
is a finite étale torsor under the abelian group
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{G}^{c})^{\phi}
,
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\prime\Diamond/}
is represented by a normal flat scheme
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\prime}
, and
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\prime}\to\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)
is a finite étale torsor under
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{G}^{c})^{\phi}
, thanks to
[
15
, Prop. 2.3.1]
. We take the special fiber of the diagram (
7.3
), and denote the Newton strata and central leaves in
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\prime}(k)
by
\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}(k)
and
\mathcal{N}^{[b]}(k)
, respectively, using
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c,\circ},\mu^{c},\delta=1}^{W}
. Since
\mathcal{N}^{[b]}(k)
is locally closed in
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\prime}(k)
by
[
71
]
, we claim that
\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}(k)
is closed in
\mathcal{N}^{[b]}(k)
. If this is true, by using torsors, a central leaf in
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)(k)
is the image of some
\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}(k)
under
f
, and its preimage under
f
is a topologically disjoint union of some
\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{i}]]}(k)
; then the lemma follows from the fact that
f
is finite étale.

Let
x\in\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}(k)
. In
[
29
, §2.14]
, given a perfect scheme
S
and a
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-shtuka
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
on
S
, the authors define

\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}_{(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})}:=\left\{s\in S|\ \bar{s}^{*}(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})\cong(\mathscr{P}_{x},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{x}})|_{\overline{\kappa(s)}}\right\},

and similarly define
\mathcal{N}^{[b]}_{(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})}
using isocrystals.
[
29
, Prop. 2.15(3)]
says that
\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}_{(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})}\subset\mathcal{N}^{[b]}_{(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})}
is closed. Now let
S
be the perfection of
\mathcal{N}^{[b]}
; then
\mathcal{N}^{[b]}_{(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})}
is the whole space
\mathcal{N}^{[b],\textrm{perf}}
. Thus,
\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}(k)=\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}_{(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})}(k)\subset\mathcal{N}^{[b]}(k)
is closed.
∎

By some standard arguments on Jacobson schemes, (e.g.,
[
60
, §3.2]
), there exists a unique closed subscheme
\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}\subset\mathcal{N}^{[b]}
with induced reduced subscheme structure such that the set of
k
-points of
\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}
is
\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}(k)
. Finally, let

C(\mathcal{G}^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\}):=\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}^{W}(k),

then we obtain a globally defined
\Upsilon_{K}
, which is determined by its values on
k
-points
\Upsilon_{K}(k)
:

\Upsilon_{K}:\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}\to C(\mathcal{G}^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\}),

and we call the fibers
central leaves
. In the proof of Lemma
7.5
, fix a
k
-point
s\in\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}
, we see that

\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}=\left\{x\in\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}|\ (\mathscr{P}_{s},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{s}})|_{\overline{\kappa(x)}}\cong(\mathscr{P}_{x},\phi_{\mathscr{P}_{x}})|_{\overline{\kappa(x)}}\right\}.

Remark 7.6
.

The above arguments and definitions also apply to
(G^{*}_{\Phi,h},\mathcal{G}^{*}_{\Phi,h})
. The central leaves on
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})_{\bar{s}}
, defined using
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\Phi,h},\mu^{c}_{\Phi,h},\delta=1}^{W}
, are finer than those defined using
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{*}_{\Phi,h},\mu^{*}_{\Phi,h},\delta=1}^{W}
. Nevertheless, the index set of Newton strata (and of KR strata, of EKOR strata, recalled in the next subsection) depends only on the adjoint group, so there is no difference in whether one uses
\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\Phi,h}
or
\mathcal{G}^{*}_{\Phi,h}
.

Proposition 7.7
.

Newton strata are well positioned. Moreover, let
\mathcal{N}^{[b]}
be a Newton stratum on
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}
with some
[b]\in B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
. Then, for each
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
,
(\mathcal{N}^{[b]})_{\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)}^{\natural}
is either empty or a Newton stratum
\mathcal{N}^{[b_{\Phi,h}]}
on
\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)_{\bar{s}}\cong\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})_{\bar{s}}
, for some
[b_{\Phi,h}]\in B(G_{\Phi,h}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\})
. The relation between
[b]
and
[b_{\Phi,h}]
is given in the proof.

Proof.

We pass the commutative diagram (
5.21
) to isocrystals:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\textrm{perf}}_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W^{0,\textrm{perf}}_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\textrm{perf}}_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})^{\textrm{perf}}_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{c}\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}_{\mu^{c,-1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P^{*}_{\Phi}\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}_{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G_{\Phi,h}^{*}\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}_{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g_{\Phi}^{-1})}

We further take the underlying topological spaces and get the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W^{0}_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B(P_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B(G_{\Phi,h}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\}).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g_{\Phi}^{-1})}

Let
[b]\in B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
. If
\mathcal{N}^{[b]}\subset\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}
is non-empty over
W_{\bar{s}}^{0}
, then
[b]
is in the image of
\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g_{\Phi}^{-1}):B(P_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})\to B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
for some
[b_{\Phi}]\in B(P_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})
.

On the other hand, let
[b_{\Phi,h}]\in B(G_{\Phi,h}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\})
be the image of
[b_{\Phi}]
. Since
B(P_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}B(G_{\Phi,h}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\})
is a bijection, the preimage of
\mathcal{N}^{[b]}
over
W_{\bar{s}}^{0}
is the finite union of preimages of those Newton strata
\mathcal{N}^{[b_{\Phi,h}]}
on
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})_{\bar{s}}
, where
[b_{\Phi,h}]
are the preimages of
[b]\in B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
under
B(G_{\Phi,h}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}B(P_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})\to B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
.

We further claim that
B(P_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})\to B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
is injective; then there is a unique
[b_{\Phi,h}]
associated with
[b]
. By
[
33
, Lem. 2.1]
, we have injectivity:

B(L_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})=B(Q_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})\to B(G_{\Phi}^{c},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{c,-1}\})=B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\}).

Also, note that
B(P_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})\to B(Q_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})
is bijective:

B(P_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})=B(G_{\Phi,h}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\})=B(G_{\Phi,h}^{\mathrm{ad}},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{\mathrm{ad},-1}\}),

B(Q_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})=B(L_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\})=B(L_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{\mathrm{ad},-1}\}),

and

B(L_{\Phi}^{\mathrm{ad}},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{\mathrm{ad},-1}\})=B(G_{\Phi,h}^{\mathrm{ad}},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{\mathrm{ad},-1}\})\times B(G_{\Phi,l}^{\mathrm{ad}},\{\mathrm{id}\})=B(G_{\Phi,h}^{\mathrm{ad}},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{\mathrm{ad},-1}\}).

Finally, to show that Newton strata are well positioned, we pass to
\mathrm{Z}(\Phi)=\Delta_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)
. It suffices to show that
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
stabilizes each Newton stratum; then there is a unique subscheme
(\mathcal{N}^{[b]})^{\natural}\subset\mathrm{Z}(\Phi)_{\bar{s}}
whose preimage in
\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)_{\bar{s}}
is
(\mathcal{N}^{[b]})^{\natural}_{\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)}=\mathcal{N}^{[b_{\Phi,h}]}
. Such
(\mathcal{N}^{[b]})^{\natural}\subset\mathrm{Z}(\Phi)_{\bar{s}}
is automatically locally closed if it exists; see
[
52
, Lem. 2.3.10]
. Consider the
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
-action on Newton strata; now we apply Proposition
5.33
.
∎

Remark 7.8
.

As explained in
[
61
, Lem. 2.16, 2.20, Prop. 2.30]
, the connected components and closures of Newton strata, as well as
\mathcal{N}^{\leq[b]}
for any
[b]\in B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
, are all well-positioned. The same applies to central leaves, KR strata, and EKOR strata proved in later sections, and we do not repeat the arguments there.

The following proof was explained to us by Sian Nie.

Lemma 7.9
.

Let
G
be any reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
,
M
be any proper Levi subgroup of
G
,
\mu
be a cocharacter of
M
that is non-central in
G
. The image of
B(M,\{\mu\})\to B(G,\{\mu\})
does not contain the basic element of
B(G,\{\mu\})
.

Proof.

Given
[b_{1}]\leq[b_{2}]
in
B(M,\{\mu\})
, then
[b_{1}]\leq[b_{2}]
in
B(G,\{\mu\})
. Since basic elements are minimal, it suffices to show that given the basic element
[b]\in B(M,\{\mu\})
, its image in
B(G,\{\mu\})
can not be basic.

We fix a pinning and assume
\mu
is dominant in
G
. Let
\mu^{\diamond}
be the
\sigma
-average of
\mu
, recall that

B(M,\{\mu\})=\left\{[b]\in B(M)|\ \kappa([b])\leq\kappa(\mu),\nu_{b}\leq\mu^{\diamond}\right\}

Let
[b]\in B(M,\{\mu\})
be basic, then
\nu_{b}=\mu^{\diamond}-h
is central in
M
, where
h
is a non-negative linear combination of positive coroots of
M
. Recall that
[b]
is basic in
B(G,\{\mu\})
if and only if
\nu_{b}
is central in
G
if and only if
\langle\alpha,\nu_{b}\rangle=0
for all simple roots in
G
. For those simple roots
\alpha
not in
M
,
\langle\alpha,\mu^{\diamond}\rangle\geq 0
since
\mu^{\diamond}
is dominant in
G
, and
\langle\alpha,h\rangle\leq 0
by the Cartan matrix, then

\langle\alpha,\nu_{b}\rangle=\langle\alpha,\mu^{\diamond}\rangle-\langle\alpha,h\rangle\geq 0,

it takes equality if and only if
\langle\alpha,\mu^{\diamond}\rangle=\langle\alpha,h\rangle=0
. This implies that
h=0
. Since
\nu_{b}=\mu^{\diamond}
is central in
M
, then
\mu^{\diamond}
is central in
G
, we get a contradiction.
∎

Corollary 7.10
.

Basic Newton stratum has no boundary.

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
7.7
, it suffices to show that the basic element
[b_{0}]\in B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
does not come from any Levi subgroup; this is proved in Lemma
7.9
. Note that the Hodge cocharacter
\mu
is central in
G
if and only if
G=T
is a torus, and the Shimura variety associated with
(T,\{h\})
has no boundary.
∎

Definition 7.11
.

Let
Y
be a scheme and
\{Y_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be subschemes of
Y
. We say that
Y
is a
topologically disjoint union
of
\{Y_{i}\}_{i\in I}
if
Y=\bigsqcup_{i\in I}Y_{i}
and all
Y_{i}\subset Y
are open and closed.

Proposition 7.12
.

Central leaves are well positioned. Moreover, let
\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}
