be a central leaf on
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}
with some
[[b]]\in C(\mathcal{G}^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
. Then, for each
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
,
(\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]})_{\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)}^{\natural}
is either empty or a topologically disjoint union of central leaves
\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi,h}]]}
on
\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)_{\bar{s}}\cong\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})_{\bar{s}}
, for some collection of
[[b_{\Phi,h}]]\in C(\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\})
. The relation between
[[b]]
and the collection of
[[b_{\Phi,h}]]
is given in the proof.

Proof.

Let
[[b]]\in C(\mathcal{G}^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
. If
\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}\subset\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}
is non-empty over
W_{\bar{s}}^{0}
, then
[[b]]
is in the image of some
[[b_{\Phi}]]
under
\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g_{\Phi}^{-1}):C(\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})\to C(\mathcal{G}^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
; here
C(\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\}):=\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\mu_{\Phi}^{*}}^{W}(k)
. We let
[[b_{\Phi,h}]]\in C(\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\})
be the image of
[[b_{\Phi}]]
.

We claim that there is at most one
[[b_{\Phi}]]\in C(\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})
in the image of
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(k)\to C(\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})
that has fixed image
[[b_{\Phi,h}]]\in C(\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\})
. If this is the case, the preimage of
\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}
over
W_{\bar{s}}^{0}
is the finite union of preimages of those central leaves
\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi,h}]]}
on
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h},\bar{s}}
, where
[[b_{\Phi,h}]]
are the preimages of
[[b]]\in C(\mathcal{G}^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
under

C(\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\})\leftarrow C(\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g^{-1}_{\Phi})}}{{\to}}C(\mathcal{G}^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\}).

Let us show the claim. We first work with abelian scheme torsor part. Let
x\in\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi,h}]]}(k)\subset\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(k)
,
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}(k)
be in the preimage of
x
. Since
\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}
is a torsor under the abelian scheme
\mathcal{A}_{K}(\Phi)\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}
, there exists
\gamma\in\mathcal{A}_{K}(\Phi)(k)
with image
x
such that
\gamma y_{1}=y_{2}
. We could lift
y_{1}
,
y_{2}
,
x
,
\gamma
to some
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-points
\tilde{y}_{1}
,
\tilde{y}_{2}
,
\tilde{x}
,
\tilde{\gamma}
respectively, where
F
is a finite extension of
\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, such that
\tilde{\gamma}\tilde{y}_{1}=\tilde{y}_{2}
with image
\tilde{x}
. By Lemma
3.34
and
[
66
, Corollary 2.7.10]
, there exists an isomorphism of
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\Phi}^{*}
-shtukas
(\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi},\phi_{\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi}})
and
\tilde{\gamma}^{*}(\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi},\phi_{\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi}})
over
\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi},\tilde{x}}
, thus there exists an isomorphism of
\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\Phi}^{*}
-shtukas
y_{1}^{*}(\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi},\phi_{\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi}})
and
y_{2}^{*}(\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi},\phi_{\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi}})
. Next, we show that given different
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi,h}]]}(k)
, one can take some points in the fiber
y,y^{\prime}\in\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}(k)
respectively such that
y^{*}(\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi},\phi_{\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi}})
is isomorphic to
y^{\prime*}(\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi},\phi_{\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi}})
. Étale locally
T\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}
, there is a section
T\to\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi},T}\cong\mathcal{A}_{K}(\Phi)_{T}
, we can take it to be the zero section of the abelian scheme, such that its generic fiber
T_{\eta}\to\mathcal{A}_{K}(\Phi)_{T_{\eta}}
coincides with the base change of the zero section
\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,h}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{K_{\Phi,V}\rtimes K_{\Phi,h}}
. This can be done using the group structure of
\mathcal{A}_{K}(\Phi)_{T}
. By Lemma
3.19
and
[
66
, Cor. 2.7.10]
, we have a unique extension of morphism of shtukas:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi},T}^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*},\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{\Phi}^{*},\bar{\mu}_{\Phi}^{*}}.}}

In particular, for any
x\in\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi,h}]]}(k)
, we can find a preimage
y\in\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}(k)
such that the
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{*}_{\Phi}
-shtuka
y^{*}(\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi},\phi_{\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi}})
is isomorphic to the pushforward of the
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*}
-shtuka
x^{*}(\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi,h},\phi_{\overline{\mathscr{P}}_{\Phi,h}})
. This finishes the claim for the abelian scheme torsor part. Similar arguments can be made for the torus torsor part
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}\to\mathscr{S}_{\overline{K}_{\Phi}}
, with Lemma
3.34
replaced by Lemma
3.29
.

To show central leaves are well positioned, we need to pass to
\mathrm{Z}(\Phi)=\Delta_{\Phi,K}\backslash\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)
and show that
\Delta_{\Phi,K}
stabilizes each central leaf. See the last paragraph of the proof of Proposition
7.7
.

Finally, to show
topological disjointness
, note that central leaves are closed in Newton strata; this follows from Proposition
7.7
and the fact that if
Y_{1}\subset Y_{2}
is a closed embedding of well-positioned subschemes, then
Y^{\natural}_{1,\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)}\subset Y^{\natural}_{2,\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)}
are closed embeddings for all
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
. See
[
61
, Lem. 2.17, 2.18, 2.19]
.
∎

7.1.1.
Toroidal compactifications

Recall that we have globally defined shtukas on the special fiber of the integral model of the toroidal compactification by Proposition
5.29
and Corollary
5.30
:

(7.4)

\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}^{\textrm{perf}}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}^{W}\to G^{c}\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}_{\mu^{c,-1}},

which induces

\Upsilon_{K}^{\Sigma}(k):\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)(k)\to C(\mathcal{G}^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\}),\quad\delta_{K}^{\Sigma}:\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)\to B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\}).

Proposition 7.13
.

Let
[b]\in B(G^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
, and let
\mathcal{N}^{[b]}\subset\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}
be the Newton stratum. Then its partial toroidal compactification
(\mathcal{N}^{[b]})^{\Sigma}
(see
[
52
, Definition 2.3.1]
) is the fiber
\delta_{K}^{\Sigma,-1}([b])
.

Proof.

By restricting the diagram (
5.18
) to the special fiber, we have a commutative diagram:

(7.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)^{\textrm{perf}}_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W^{\textrm{perf}}_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})(\sigma)^{\textrm{perf}}_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})^{\textrm{perf}}_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c},\delta=1}^{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\mu_{\Phi}^{*},\delta=1}^{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*},\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*},\delta=1}^{W}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G^{c}\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}_{\mu^{c,-1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P^{*}_{\Phi}\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}_{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G_{\Phi,h}^{*}\textit{-}\operatorname{Isoc}_{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g_{\Phi}^{-1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g_{\Phi}^{-1})}

By Proposition
7.7
,
\mathcal{N}^{[b]}
is well positioned, and its preimage in
W^{0}_{\bar{s}}
is the preimage of some
(\mathcal{N}^{[b]})^{\natural}_{\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)}\subset\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)_{\bar{s}}=\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h},\bar{s}}
. The preimage of its partial toroidal compactification
(\mathcal{N}^{[b]})^{\Sigma}
in
W_{\bar{s}}
should also be the preimage of the same
(\mathcal{N}^{[b]})^{\natural}_{\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)}
; thus
(\mathcal{N}^{[b]})^{\Sigma}
is contained in the fiber
(\delta^{\Sigma}_{K})^{-1}([b])
. On the other hand, Newton strata form a (weak) stratification
\mathscr{S}_{K,\bar{s}}=\bigsqcup_{[b]}\mathcal{N}^{[b]}
; thus
\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}=\bigsqcup_{[b]}(\mathcal{N}^{[b]})^{\Sigma}
(see
[
61
, Lem. 2.20]
). This forces
(\mathcal{N}^{[b]})^{\Sigma}=(\delta^{\Sigma}_{K})^{-1}([b])
.
∎

Proposition 7.14
.

Let
[[b]]\in C(\mathcal{G}^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
, and let
\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}\subset\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}
be the central leaf. Then its partial toroidal compactification
(\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]})^{\Sigma}
has the set of
k
-points
\Upsilon_{K}^{\Sigma}(k)^{-1}([[b]])
. In particular, we can upgrade
\Upsilon_{K}^{\Sigma}(k)
to
\Upsilon_{K}^{\Sigma}:\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}\to C(\mathcal{G}^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
, and
(\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]})^{\Sigma}=\Upsilon_{K}^{\Sigma,-1}([[b]])
.

Proof.

We cannot directly apply the proof of Proposition
7.13
, since

(7.6)

C(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\}):=\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\mu_{\Phi}^{*},\delta=1}^{W}(k)\to C(\mathcal{G}^{*}_{\Phi,h},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\}):=\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*},\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*},\delta=1}^{W}(k)

is not injective.

Taking the set of
k
-points of the diagram (
7.5
), we have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)(k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W(k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})(\sigma)(k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})(k)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C(\mathcal{G}^{c},\{\mu^{c,-1}\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{\Phi},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C(\mathcal{G}^{*}_{\Phi,h},\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\}).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g_{\Phi}^{-1})}

By Proposition
7.12
,
\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]}(k)
is well positioned, and its preimage in
W(k)
is the preimage of a union of central leaves
(\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]})^{\natural}_{\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)}(k)\subset\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)(k)=\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(k)
. The preimage of
(\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]})^{\Sigma}(k)
in
W(k)
should also be the preimage of
(\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]})^{\natural}_{\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)}(k)
. Let
\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi,h}]]}\subset(\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]})^{\natural}_{\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)}
be a central leaf. The arguments in the proof of Proposition
7.12
show that the preimage of
\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi,h}]]}(k)
in
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(k)
is a central leaf
\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi}]]}(k)
with a unique
[[b_{\Phi}]]\in C(\mathcal{P}^{c}_{\Phi},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{c,-1}\})
.

Let us denote by
\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi}]]}(\sigma)(k)
(resp.
\mathcal{N}^{[b_{\Phi}]}(\sigma)(k)
) the preimage of
\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi,h}]]}(k)
(resp.
\mathcal{N}^{[b_{\Phi,h}]}(\sigma)(k)
) in
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma)(k)
. By Lemma
7.15
,
\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi}]]}(k)\subset\mathcal{N}^{[b_{\Phi}]}(k)
and
\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi,h}]]}(k)\subset\mathcal{N}^{[b_{\Phi,h}]}(k)
are closed. Then
\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi}]]}(\sigma)(k)
is closed in
\mathcal{N}^{[b_{\Phi}]}(\sigma)(k)
and has to be the closure of
\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi}]]}(k)
in
\mathcal{N}^{[b_{\Phi}]}(\sigma)(k)
. Applying Lemma
7.15
again, the central leaf in
\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(\sigma)(k)
associated with
[[b_{\Phi}]]
is closed in the Newton stratum and contains
\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi}]]}(k)
; therefore, it must contain
\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi}]]}(\sigma)(k)
. In particular, the preimages of
\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{\Phi,h}]]}(k)
in
W(k)
and in
W^{0}(k)
have the same image
[[b_{\Phi}]]\in C(\mathcal{P}^{c}_{\Phi},\{\mu_{\Phi}^{c,-1}\})
. Therefore,
(\mathcal{C}^{[[b]]})^{\Sigma}(k)
is contained in
(\Upsilon^{\Sigma}_{K}(k))^{-1}([[b]])
. Then we apply the last paragraph of the proof of Proposition
7.13
.
∎

Lemma 7.15
.

Let
S
be a perfect scheme and let
\mathcal{P}
be a quasi-parahoric group scheme. Given
p:S\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}}^{W}\to\operatorname{Isoc}_{P}
, we define (the set of
k
-points of) central leaves to be the fibers of
S(k)\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P}}^{W}(k)
, and define Newton strata to be the fibers of
S\to B(P)
. Then Newton strata are locally closed in
S
, and central leaves are closed in the (set of
k
-points of) Newton strata.

Proof.

When
\mathcal{P}
is parahoric, this follows from
[
29
, Prop. 2.15]
. Note that in
[
29
, §2]
, it is not necessary to assume that the generic fiber of
\mathcal{P}
is reductive; the setup applies to any flat affine group scheme of finite type over the base with connected fibers. The references
[
4
]
,
[
5
]
, and
[
38
, Thm. A.14]
also work in this general setting.

When
\mathcal{P}
is quasi-parahoric, we instead apply the arguments in the proof of Lemma
7.5
, using
[
15
, Prop. 2.3.1]
with
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{P}^{c})^{\phi}
in place of
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{G}^{c})^{\phi}
.
∎

7.2.
KR strata and EKOR strata

7.2.1.
Algebraicity

Let us recall the setting in
[
25
, §3]
. Given a morphism
f\colon S\to T
of affine perfect schemes,
f
is a universally subtrusive cover if and only if the induced morphism
f^{\diamond}\colon S^{\diamond}\to T^{\diamond}
is a
v
-cover.

Lemma 7.16
.

Let
S
be a perfect scheme, and let
H
be the perfection of an affine group scheme of finite type. Then
H^{1}_{uv}(S,H)=H^{1}_{v}(S^{\diamond},H^{\diamond})
, where
H^{1}_{uv}
is defined under the universally subtrusive topology.

Proof.

Since
\diamond
is fully faithful on the category of perfect schemes and preserves surjections, and by definition preserves fiber products, we have a morphism
H^{1}_{uv}(S,H)\to H^{1}_{v}(S^{\diamond},H^{\diamond})
. On the other hand, given an affinoid perfectoid space
U=\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\to S^{\diamond}
that trivializes the given
H^{\diamond}
-torsor
\mathcal{F}
on
S^{\diamond}
,
U_{\mathrm{red}}
is represented by
\operatorname{Spec}A_{\mathrm{red}}
(
A_{\mathrm{red}}=(A/A\cdot A^{\circ\circ})^{\textrm{perf}}
, see
[
25
, Prop. 3.18]
), and
\operatorname{Spec}A_{\mathrm{red}}\to S=\operatorname{Spec}R
is surjective (indeed, we have the surjective specialization map
\operatorname{sp}_{U}:\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\to\operatorname{Spec}A^{+}_{\mathrm{red}}
). We claim that
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{red}}
is represented by a perfect scheme. Following this, since the reduction functor preserves finite limits and
\diamond
is fully faithful, taking reduction gives a section
H^{1}_{v}(S^{\diamond},H^{\diamond})\to H^{1}_{uv}(S,H)
. Moreover, by adjointness,
(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{red}})^{\diamond}\to\mathcal{F}
is a morphism of
H^{\diamond}
-torsors, which is automatically an isomorphism; then
H^{1}_{v}(S^{\diamond},H^{\diamond})\cong H^{1}_{uv}(S,H)
.

Let
H_{0}
be a linear algebraic group such that
H=H_{0}^{\textrm{perf}}
. We claim that a
H_{0}
-torsor on
S
is the same as a
H
-torsor on
S
: recall that a
H
-torsor over
S
can be viewed as a trivialization
U\to S
with a section
H(U\times_{S}U)
that satisfies cocycle conditions. Since for any perfect scheme
T
,
H(T)=H_{0}(T)
, we have
H^{1}_{uv}(S,H_{0})=H^{1}_{uv}(S,H)
.

Now we work in the topos
\widetilde{\operatorname{SchPerf}}
. Assume
H_{0}=\operatorname{GL}_{n}
. A
\operatorname{GL}_{n}
-torsor
\mathcal{P}
on
S
can be viewed as a vector bundle on
S
via
\mathcal{P}\times^{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}\mathcal{O}_{S}^{n}
. By
[
9
, Thm. 4.1]
, vector bundles over
S
form a
v
-stack, thus
\mathcal{P}\times^{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}\mathcal{O}_{S}^{n}
is representable by the full-faithfulness of
\diamond
. By taking the framing,
\mathcal{P}=\operatorname{\underline{\mathrm{Isom}}}_{S}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{P}\times^{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}\mathcal{O}_{S}^{n})
is representable. In general, take a closed embedding
H_{0}\to\operatorname{GL}_{n}
such that the quotient is quasi-affine (see
[
65
, Prop. 1.3]
), then
\operatorname{GL}_{n}^{\textrm{perf}}/H_{0}^{\textrm{perf}}
is a perfect quasi-affine scheme. Let
G=\operatorname{GL}_{n}^{\textrm{perf}}
,
H=H_{0}^{\textrm{perf}}
. Consider the push-out torsor
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{P}\times^{H}G
,
\mathcal{Q}
is representable, in particular,
\mathcal{Q}\to S
is fpqc. Consider the quotient sheaf (under fpqc topology)
\mathcal{Q}/H=\mathcal{Q}\times^{G}G/H=\mathcal{P}\times^{H}G/H
, it is trivialized over the fpqc cover
\mathcal{Q}\to S
, thus
\mathcal{Q}/H
itself is a perfect quasi-affine scheme by the fpqc descent. Since the
v
-topology on the category of perfect qcqs schemes is subcanonical (see
[
9
, Remark 4.2]
), then
\mathcal{Q}/H=\mathcal{P}\times^{H}G/H
(viewed as a representable
v
-sheaf) has a section over
S
, thus
\mathcal{P}
is representable.
∎

7.2.2.
Kottwitz-Rapoport strata

Recall that in
[
66
, §4.9]
, given a quasi-parahoric group scheme
\mathcal{G}
, the authors constructed a
v
-sheaf theoretical local model diagram:

(7.7)

\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\to[\mathcal{G}^{\Diamond}\backslash\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}],

where
\mathcal{G}^{\Diamond}(S)
(
S=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})\in\operatorname{Perf}
) consists of pairs
(S^{\sharp},g)
,
S^{\sharp}=\operatorname{Spa}(R^{\sharp},R^{\sharp+})
is an untilt of
S
and
g\in\mathcal{G}(R^{\sharp})
. Such constructions are functorial.

On the other hand, we have a local model diagram

(7.8)

\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W}\to[\mathcal{G}_{0}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}]

constructed as follows: let
\operatorname{Spec}R\in\operatorname{PCAlg^{op}}
,
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W,\square}(R)
classifies tuples
((\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}}),\alpha)
, where
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})\in\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W}(R)
and
\alpha:\mathscr{P}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\phi^{*}(\mathscr{P})
is a trivialization of the
\mathcal{G}
-torsor
\phi^{*}(\mathscr{P})
over
W(R)
. Then
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W,\square}(R)\to M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(R)
that maps
((\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}}),\alpha)
to
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}}\circ\alpha)
is
L^{+}\mathcal{G}
-equivariant. Note that the
L^{+}\mathcal{G}
-action on
M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
factors through
\mathcal{G}_{0}
, and the trivialization
\alpha
is uniquely determined by its reduction over
R
by the smoothness of
\mathcal{G}
, then we get (
7.8
).

Remark 7.17
.

Let us also compare (
7.8
) with the one constructed in
[
85
, §7.2.3]
(cf.
[
78
, §4.2.2]
). The local Hecke stack
\mathrm{Hk}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W}(R)
(resp.
M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(R)\subset\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathcal{G}}^{W}(R)
) classifies the modifications
\gamma:\overleftarrow{\mathscr{P}}\dashrightarrow\overrightarrow{\mathscr{P}}
(resp.
\mathscr{P}_{0}\dashrightarrow\mathscr{P}
) of
\mathcal{G}
-torsors over
W(R)
of type
\mu
. By choosing a trivialization
\alpha:\mathcal{P}_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\overleftarrow{\mathscr{P}}
, we have
\mathrm{Hk}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W}=[L^{+}\mathcal{G}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}]
. Also recall the truncated
\mathrm{Hk}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W,(1)}=[\mathcal{G}_{0}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}]
. We have a morphism
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W}\to\mathrm{Hk}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W}
that maps
(\mathscr{P},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
to
(\overleftarrow{\mathscr{P}}=\phi^{*}\mathscr{P},\overrightarrow{\mathscr{P}}=\mathscr{P},\gamma=\phi_{\mathscr{P}})
. Compose with
\mathrm{Hk}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W}\to\mathrm{Hk}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W,(1)}
, we get (
7.8
).

Lemma 7.18
.

Let
\mathcal{G}_{0}
be the special fiber of
\mathcal{G}
. We have a projection
[\mathcal{G}^{\Diamond}\backslash\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}]_{\mathrm{red}}\to[\mathcal{G}_{0}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}]
. In particular, from (
7.7
), we further have
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W}\to[\mathcal{G}_{0}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}]
which coincides with (
7.8
).

Proof.

Take the reduction of (
7.7
). Let
S\in\operatorname{PCAlg^{op}}
be an affine perfect scheme,

[\mathcal{G}^{\Diamond}\backslash\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}]_{\mathrm{red}}(S)=\mathrm{Hom}(S^{\diamond},[\mathcal{G}^{\Diamond}\backslash\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}])

gives
S^{\diamond}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\leftarrow}}\widetilde{S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle q}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}
, where
p
is a
\mathcal{G}^{\Diamond}
-torsor, and
q
is
\mathcal{G}^{\Diamond}
-equivariant. Take reduction again, we have
(S^{\diamond})_{\mathrm{red}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bar{p}}}{{\leftarrow}}\widetilde{S}_{\mathrm{red}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle q}}{{\rightarrow}}M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
, where
\bar{p}
is a
(\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\diamond})_{\mathrm{red}}
-torsor, and
q
is
(\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\diamond})_{\mathrm{red}}
-equivariant: given a perfect algebra
R
, by
[
25
, Theorem 2]
,

(\mathcal{G}^{\Diamond})_{\mathrm{red}}(\operatorname{Spec}R)=\mathcal{G}^{\Diamond}(\operatorname{Spd}R)=\mathcal{G}(R)=\mathcal{G}_{0}(R)=(\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\diamond})_{\mathrm{red}}(\operatorname{Spec}R).

Since
S\cong(S^{\diamond})_{\mathrm{red}}
,
(\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\diamond})_{\mathrm{red}}
is represented by
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\textrm{perf}}
, Lemma
7.16
shows that
\widetilde{S}_{\mathrm{red}}
is represented by a perfect scheme
\widetilde{S}_{0}
and
\widetilde{S}_{0}\to S
is a
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\textrm{perf}}
-torsor. On the other hand, since
\diamond
is fully faithful and
M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
is represented by a perfect scheme (which we still denote by
M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
),
\widetilde{S}_{\mathrm{red}}\to M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
is represented by a
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\textrm{perf}}
-equivariant morphism
\widetilde{S}_{0}\to M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
between perfect schemes, thus we have a point in
[\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\textrm{perf}}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}](S)
. This gives a morphism
[\mathcal{G}^{\Diamond}\backslash\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}]_{\mathrm{red}}\to[\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\textrm{perf}}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}]=[\mathcal{G}_{0}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}]
. By constructions, these two
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W}\to[\mathcal{G}_{0}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}]
coincide.
∎

We apply this to
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\Diamond/}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}
, and use Lemma
7.16
again. We then have a morphism:

(7.9)

\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}^{\textrm{perf}}\to[\mathcal{G}_{0}^{c}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}].

When
\mathcal{G}^{c}
is parahoric, it is homeomorphic to

\displaystyle\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})_{\breve{K}^{c}}

\displaystyle:=\breve{K}^{c}\backslash\breve{K}^{c}\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})\breve{K}^{c}/\breve{K}^{c}

\displaystyle=W_{K^{c}}\backslash W_{K^{c}}\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})W_{K^{c}}/W_{K^{c}}\subset W_{K^{c}}\backslash\widetilde{W}^{c}/W_{K^{c}},

where we use the induced Bruhat order (from the affine-Weyl group
\widetilde{W}^{c}
of
G^{c}
) on
W_{K^{c}}\backslash\widetilde{W}^{c}/W_{K^{c}}
, thus the morphism (
7.9
) induces a continuous morphism of underlying topological spaces

l_{K}:\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}\to\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})_{\breve{K}^{c}}.

When
\mathcal{G}^{c}
is quasi-parahoric, since
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{c,\circ},\mu^{c}}\cong\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}
, we identify

M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}(k)=\breve{K}^{c,\circ}\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})\breve{K}^{c}/\breve{K}^{c}.

It is equal to
\breve{K}^{c}\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})\breve{K}^{c}/\breve{K}^{c}
since the conjugation of
\breve{K}^{c}/\breve{K}^{c,\circ}\hookrightarrow\pi_{1}(G^{c})_{I}
acts trivially on
\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})
.. Thus

|[\mathcal{G}_{0}^{c,\circ,\textrm{perf}}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c,\circ},\mu^{c}}]|=\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})_{\breve{K}^{c,\circ}}=\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})_{\breve{K}^{c}}=|[\mathcal{G}_{0}^{c}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}]|.

We define the fibers of
l_{K}
as
Kottwitz-Rapoport strata
. In particular, KR strata are locally closed.

By applying the exact same arguments, given a quasi-parahoric group scheme
\mathcal{P}
such that
(\mathcal{P},\mu)
comes from boundary (see Definition
1.21
), and by Corollary
1.32
, the
L^{+}\mathcal{P}
-action on
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}
factors through
\mathcal{P}^{\Diamond}
, we have morphisms

\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}\to[\mathcal{P}^{\Diamond}\backslash\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{v}],\quad\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}^{W}\to[\mathcal{P}_{0}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{P},\mu}].

Proposition 7.19
.

KR strata are well positioned. Moreover, let
\mathrm{KR}_{w}
be a KR stratum on
\mathscr{S}_{K,\bar{s}}
with some
w\in\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})_{\breve{K}^{c}}
. Then, for each
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
,
(\mathrm{KR}_{w})_{\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)}^{\natural}
is either empty or a finite union of KR strata
\mathrm{KR}_{w_{\Phi,h}}
on
\mathrm{Z}^{\star}(\Phi)_{\bar{s}}\cong\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h},\bar{s}}
, for some collection of
w_{\Phi,h}\in\operatorname{Adm}_{G_{\Phi,h}^{*}}(\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\})_{\breve{K}_{\Phi,h}^{*}}
. The relation between
w
and the collection of
w_{\Phi,h}
is given in the proof.

Proof.

KR strata are locally closed and are unions of central leaves. Since central leaves are well positioned by Proposition
7.12
, KR strata are well positioned; see
[
61
, Lem. 2.20]
. We describe their boundaries. Using the main diagram (
5.20
), we have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W^{0,\diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})^{\diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})^{\diamond}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathcal{G}^{c,\Diamond}\backslash\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c,-1}}^{v}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*,\Diamond}\backslash\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{P}_{\Phi}^{*},\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}}^{v}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*,\Diamond}\backslash\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}^{*},\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}}^{v}].}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g_{\Phi}^{-1})}

Applying the above arguments, we have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{W^{0}_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}(P_{\Phi},D_{\Phi})_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{\Phi,K}^{\circ}\backslash\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi,h}}(G_{\Phi,h},D_{\Phi,h})_{\bar{s}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})_{\breve{K}^{c}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Adm}_{P_{\Phi}^{*}}(\{\mu_{\Phi}^{*,-1}\})_{\breve{K}_{\Phi}^{*}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Adm}_{G_{\Phi,h}^{*}}(\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\})_{\breve{K}^{*}_{\Phi,h}}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathrm{Int}}(g_{\Phi}^{-1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=(\ref{eq: P-grassmanian factorization})}

The arguments in the proof of Proposition
7.7
can be adapted here without much change.
∎

Remark 7.20
.

We expect that the boundary of a KR stratum should be a single KR stratum, as proved in
[
52
]
for PEL types and in
[
61
]
for Hodge types. In other words, we expect that

\operatorname{Adm}_{G_{\Phi,h}^{*}}(\{\mu_{\Phi,h}^{*,-1}\})_{\breve{K}^{*}_{\Phi,h}}\cong\operatorname{Adm}_{L_{\Phi}^{*}}(\{\mu_{\Phi,L}^{*,-1}\})_{\breve{K}_{\Phi,L}^{*}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})_{\breve{K}^{c}}

is an injection.

Next, we work with partial toroidal compactifications of KR strata. Equations (
7.4
) and (
7.9
) give

(7.10)

\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}^{\textrm{perf}}\to[\mathcal{G}_{0}^{c}\backslash M^{\mathrm{loc}}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}],\quad l_{K}^{\Sigma}:\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}\to\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})_{\breve{K}^{c}}.

Proposition 7.21
.

Let
w\in\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})_{\breve{K}^{c}}
, and let
\mathrm{KR}_{w}\subset\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}
be the KR stratum. Then its partial toroidal compactification
\mathrm{KR}_{w}^{\Sigma}
is the fiber
l_{K}^{\Sigma,-1}(w)
.

Proof.

Similar to the proof of Proposition
7.13
.
∎

7.2.3.
Relation with schematic local model diagram

In
[
1
]
and
[
24
]
, it was proved that
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{v}
is represented by a normal scheme
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
that is flat and of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
. In
[
66
, §4.9.1]
, it is conjectured that there exists a schematic local model diagram; that is to say, there exists a smooth morphism

\pi_{\mathrm{dR},\mathcal{G}^{c}}:\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)\to[\mathcal{G}^{c}\backslash\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}],

such that we have a (
2
-)commutative diagram

(7.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathcal{G}^{c,\Diamond/}\backslash\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}^{v}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathcal{G}^{c,\Diamond}\backslash\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}^{v}].}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathrm{dR},\mathcal{G}^{c}}^{\Diamond/}}

Here
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}^{\Diamond/}=\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}^{\Diamond}=\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}^{v}
by properness. In most cases of Hodge type, the Kisin-Pappas-Zhou integral models have schematic local model diagrams; see
[
46
, Thm. 7.1.3]
and
[
15
, Appendix A]
. In these cases, by the (perfect) smoothness of the morphism, we have a closure relation

(7.12)

\overline{\mathrm{KR}_{w}}=\bigsqcup_{w^{\prime}\leq w,w^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})_{\breve{K}}}\mathrm{KR}_{w^{\prime}},

Here the KR strata are defined using the fibers of
\pi_{\mathrm{dR},\mathcal{G}^{c}}
, and this coincides with the fibers of
l_{K}
(
7.9
), by the commutativity of the above diagram (
7.11
).

We focus on the abelian-type case. Assume
\mathcal{G}
is parahoric. In
[
46
, Thm. 7.2.20 and Rmk. 7.2.22]
(with supplements in
[
15
]
), the authors showed that, given an abelian-type Shimura datum
(G,X)
, when
p>2
(we use
(G,X)
instead of
(G_{2},X_{2})
to keep consistency of the notation in this subsection), there exists an integral model
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{K}(G,X)
that has a list of good properties (see
[
46
, Thm. 7.2.20]
). Among these properties, we have a smooth morphism

(7.13)

\pi_{\mathrm{dR},\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}}:\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)\to[\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}\backslash\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},\mu^{\mathrm{ad}}}].

Since
\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})_{\breve{K}^{c}}\cong\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{\mathrm{ad}}}(\{\mu^{\mathrm{ad},-1}\})_{\breve{K}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}}
(see
[
78
, Lem. 5.1.4]
), we have a stratification (
7.12
), here the KR strata are defined using the fibers of
\pi_{\mathrm{dR},\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}}
.

Proposition 7.22
.

When
\mathcal{G}
is parahoric and
p>2
, we have a (
2
-)commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ,\Diamond/}\backslash\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},\mu^{\mathrm{ad}}}^{v}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ,\Diamond}\backslash\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},\mu^{\mathrm{ad}}}^{v}].}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\mathrm{dR},\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}}^{\Diamond/}}

Proof.

When
(G,X)
is Hodge-type, this is essentially
[
15
, Thm. A.3.3, Prop. 4.3.3]
. We generalize this result to the abelian-type case. We argue as follows: we first show the proposition when
G=G^{\mathrm{ad}}
, and then show it for a general abelian-type Shimura datum.

When
G=G^{\mathrm{ad}}
, by the construction in
[
46
]
, there is a Hodge-type Shimura datum
(G^{\prime},X^{\prime})
and a parahoric group scheme
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
lifting
(G^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad}},\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ})
that satisfy Conditions (A)–(E) in §7.1 of
loc. cit.
. Consider the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime}}(G^{\prime},X^{\prime})^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K^{\mathrm{ad}}}{(G^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad}})}^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},\mu^{\mathrm{ad}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ,\Diamond/}\backslash\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},\mu^{\mathrm{ad}}}^{v}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ,\Diamond}\backslash\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},\mu^{\mathrm{ad}}}^{v}].}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\Diamond/}_{\mathrm{dR},\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}}(G^{\prime},X^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\Diamond/}_{\mathrm{dR},\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}}(G^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad}})}

The left triangle is commutative by the construction of the
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}
local model diagram from the Hodge-type case in
[
46
]
. The right triangle is canonically commutative. Since we can cover
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\mathrm{ad}}}(G^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad}})
by a disjoint union of
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime,\alpha}}(G^{\prime},X^{\prime})
satisfying (A)-(E) (where
K^{\prime,\alpha}
is
K_{p}^{\prime,\alpha}K^{\prime,\alpha,p}
for
K^{\prime,\alpha}_{p}
the conjugation of
K^{\prime}_{p}=\mathcal{G}^{\prime}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
by an element
\alpha\in G^{\prime}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
K^{\prime,\alpha,p}
neat open compact), it suffices to show the commutativity of the bottom square by composition with
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime}}^{\Diamond/}\to\mathscr{S}_{K^{\mathrm{ad}}}^{\Diamond/}
. Then the desired commutativity for adjoint Shimura data follows from the Hodge-type case and diagram-chasing.

For a general
(G,X)
, the assertion follows from the last paragraph and the commutativity of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{K^{\mathrm{ad}}}{(G^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad}})}^{\Diamond/}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},\mu^{\mathrm{ad}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\mathrm{ad},\Diamond/}}

by functoriality of canonical integral models. Note that, since the local model diagram of an abelian-type integral model
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)
in
[
46
]
is constructed by first pushing out the
\mathcal{G}^{\prime,\circ}
-local model diagram of
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime}}(G^{\prime},X^{\prime})
to a
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}
-local model diagram and then passing to
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)
as in
[
47
, Cor. 4.6.18]
, we still have
\pi^{\Diamond/}_{\mathrm{dR},\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}}(G^{\mathrm{ad}},X^{\mathrm{ad}})\circ\pi^{\mathrm{ad},\Diamond/}=\pi^{\Diamond/}_{\mathrm{dR},\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}}(G,X)
. The proposition is now proved.
∎

Corollary 7.23
.

Such constructed KR strata (using
\pi_{\mathrm{dR},\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}}
) coincide with the ones defined in the last subsection (using
l_{K}
).

Corollary 7.24
.

Under the setting of Proposition
7.22
, we have a closure relation:

\overline{\mathrm{KR}_{w}^{\Sigma}}=\bigsqcup_{w^{\prime}\leq w,w^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})_{\breve{K}}}\mathrm{KR}_{w^{\prime}}^{\Sigma}.

Proof.

This follows from (
7.12
) and the compatibility in Corollary
7.23
(see
[
61
, Prop. 7.1]
).
∎

Remark 7.25
.

In
[
16
, Thm. A]
, the authors constructed a shtuka map on Kisin-Pappas-Zhou integral models when
p>2
with parahoric level structures; the construction of the integral model and the shtuka map
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}
in this paper coincides with
loc. cit.
under the condition therein by the uniqueness of canonical integral models (see Theorem
6.26
and
[
16
, Thm. B]
) and by the unique extension of shtukas from generic fibers to integral models (
[
66
, Cor. 2.7.10]
). We also remark that the existence of integral models at parahoric levels with
schematic local model diagrams
is still not known for all abelian-type Shimura data when
p=2
; cf.
[
86
]
for recent progress.

7.2.4.
Ekedahl-Kottwitz-Oort-Rapoport strata

It is difficult to study EKOR strata only using shtukas; nevertheless, let us mention some easily deduced properties. By the constructions in
[
78
, §4.1]
, for parahoric
\mathcal{G}
, from
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}^{\textrm{perf}}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}^{W}
, one can define

\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}^{\textrm{perf}}\to(\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}^{W})^{\mathrm{loc}(m,1)},\quad\nu_{K}:\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}\to\prescript{\breve{K}^{c}}{}{\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})},

where
(\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}^{W})^{\mathrm{loc}(m,1)}
is the stack of
(m,1)
-truncated shtukas. It is an algebraic stack, with underlying topological space homeomorphic to
\prescript{\breve{K}^{c}}{}{\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})}
. We define the preimages of
\nu_{K}
as
EKOR strata
. EKOR strata are locally closed.

Similarly, we can consider the partial toroidal compactifications of EKOR strata. (
7.4
) gives

\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}^{\textrm{perf}}\to(\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}^{W})^{\mathrm{loc}(m,1)},\quad\nu_{K}^{\Sigma}:\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}\to\prescript{\breve{K}^{c}}{}{\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})}.

In the abelian-type case, when
\mathcal{G}
is quasi-parahoric, we further define the EKOR strata on
\mathscr{S}_{K,\bar{s}}
(resp.
\mathscr{S}_{K,\bar{s}}^{\Sigma}
) as the images of EKOR strata on
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ},\bar{s}}
(resp.
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ},\bar{s}}^{\Sigma}
) under the finite étale surjection
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ},\bar{s}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K,\bar{s}}
(resp.
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ},\bar{s}}^{\Sigma}\to\mathscr{S}_{K,\bar{s}}^{\Sigma}
; the étaleness can be easily seen using the étaleness of
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ}_{\Phi}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{\Phi}}
for any
\Phi\in\mathcal{CLR}(G,X)
).

Proposition 7.26
.

EKOR strata are well positioned. Let
w\in\prescript{\breve{K}^{c}}{}{\operatorname{Adm}_{G^{c}}(\{\mu^{c,-1}\})}
,
\mathrm{EKOR}_{w}\subset\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}
be the EKOR strata; then its partial toroidal compactification
\mathrm{EKOR}_{w}^{\Sigma}
is the fiber
\nu_{K}^{\Sigma,-1}(w)
.

Proof.

EKOR strata are locally closed and are unions of central leaves. Since central leaves are well positioned by Proposition
7.12
, EKOR strata are well positioned (see
[
61
, Lem. 2.20]
). Write
\mathrm{EKOR}_{w}=\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{i}]]}
as a union of central leaves; then
\mathrm{EKOR}_{w}^{\Sigma}=\bigsqcup_{i\in I}(\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{i}]]})^{\Sigma}
by
[
61
, Lem. 2.20]
. By Proposition
7.14
,
(\mathcal{C}^{[[b_{i}]]})^{\Sigma}\subset\nu_{K}^{\Sigma,-1}(w)
; then
\mathrm{EKOR}_{w}^{\Sigma}\subset\nu_{K}^{\Sigma,-1}(w)
. Since
\mathscr{S}_{K}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}=\bigsqcup_{w}\mathrm{EKOR}_{w}
,
\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\Sigma}(G,X)_{\bar{s}}=\bigsqcup_{w}\mathrm{EKOR}_{w}^{\Sigma}
by
[
61
, Lem. 2.20]
. This forces
\mathrm{EKOR}_{w}^{\Sigma}=\nu_{K}^{\Sigma,-1}(w)
.
∎

Remark 7.27
.

Finally, let us briefly discuss the Ekedahl–Oort strata. Assume that
\mathcal{G}
is hyperspecial. Then, for any
[\Phi]\in\operatorname{Cusp}_{K}(G,X)
,
\mathcal{G}_{\Phi,h}
is hyperspecial. In
[
85
, §5.3]
, there is a natural perfectly smooth morphism

(\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}^{W})^{\mathrm{loc}(m,1)}\to G\textit{-}\mathrm{Zip}_{\mu^{-1}}^{\textrm{perf}},

where
G\textit{-}\mathrm{Zip}^{\textrm{perf}}
is the perfection of
G\textit{-}\mathrm{Zip}
. By composing with
(\mathscr{S}_{K,\bar{s}})^{\textrm{perf}}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}^{W}
and
(\mathscr{S}_{K,\bar{s}}^{\Sigma})^{\textrm{perf}}\to\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}}^{W}
, we obtain

\epsilon_{K}:(\mathscr{S}_{K,\bar{s}})^{\textrm{perf}}\to G^{c}\textit{-}\mathrm{Zip}_{\mu^{c,-1}}^{\textrm{perf}},\quad\epsilon_{K}^{\Sigma}:(\mathscr{S}_{K,\bar{s}}^{\Sigma})^{\textrm{perf}}\to G^{c}\textit{-}\mathrm{Zip}_{\mu^{c,-1}}^{\textrm{perf}}.

We can define the EO strata as the fibers of
\epsilon_{K}
, endowed with induced reduced subscheme structures, and similarly show that the EO strata are well positioned.

Thus, we obtain a similar diagram for
\epsilon_{K}
and
\epsilon_{K}^{\Sigma}
as those in Proposition
7.19
, so that we deduce that, when
\Sigma
is smooth,
\epsilon_{K}
is perfectly smooth if and only if
\epsilon_{K}^{\Sigma}
is so (cf.
[
83
, Cor. 4.51]
).

Moreover, the partial toroidal compactification of the EO strata coincides with the EO strata defined using
\epsilon_{K}^{\Sigma}
. Also, when
\mathcal{G}
is hyperspecial, EKOR strata are the same as EO strata; thus, we omit the proof for EO strata here.

Remark 7.28
.

For the KR strata, we expect the existence of
schematic
local model diagram. For EO strata, one expects that these morphisms arise from a prismatic or syntomic approach, where the coherent and infinitesimal structures can be directly seen; that is, the morphisms
\epsilon_{K}
and
\epsilon_{K}^{\Sigma}
should respectively be the perfections of smooth morphisms

\mathscr{S}_{K,s}\to G^{c}\textit{-}\mathrm{Zip}_{\mu^{c,-1}},\quad\mathscr{S}_{K,s}^{\Sigma}\to G^{c}\textit{-}\mathrm{Zip}_{\mu^{c,-1}}.

For the interior morphism
\epsilon_{K}
, the most general result currently available is given by
[
57
]
; let us also mention the previous works
[
64
]
,
[
81
]
,
[
68
]
,
[
88
]
,
[
37
]
, etc. Indeed, when
(G,X)
is of abelian type or when
p
is large enough, Madapusi and Youcis constructed a formally étale map of
p
-adic formal stacks over
\operatorname{Spf}\mathcal{O}_{E}
:
\widehat{\mathscr{S}_{K}}\to\mathrm{BT}_{\infty}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu^{c}}
, where
\mathrm{BT}_{\infty}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu^{c}}
serves as the stack of ’
p
-divisible groups with
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-structures’ without actually working with
p
-divisible groups. The compositions
\widehat{\mathscr{S}_{K}}\to\mathrm{BT}_{n}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu^{c}}
are smooth and surjective, for all
n>0
. There is a smooth surjection
\mathrm{BT}_{1}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu^{c}}\otimes k_{E}\to G^{c}\textit{-}\mathrm{Zip}_{\mu^{c,-1}}
. The composition gives the deperfection of
\epsilon_{K}
.

Appendix A
Some category theory

This appendix serves as a complement to the categorical language employed throughout the paper, offering a direct reference applicable to the context considered herein.

A.1.
Morphisms between presheaves in categories

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a (
1
-)category. Let
\mathsf{Categories}
(resp.
\mathsf{Groupoids}
) be the
2
-category (resp.
(2,1)
-category) of categories (resp. groupoids). Let
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
be two (
2
-)functors (or presheaves)
F_{1},F_{2}:\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathsf{Categories}
.

As in
[
80
,
Ex. 02XV
]
, there are fibered categories
p_{1}:\mathcal{X}_{1}\to\mathcal{C}
and
p_{2}:\mathcal{X}_{2}\to\mathcal{C}
corresponding to
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
, respectively. More precisely, a category
\mathcal{X}
with a functor
p
to
\mathcal{C}
is called a fibered category if, for any
x\in\mathcal{X}
with
f:V\to p(x)\in\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(V,p(x))
, there exists a strongly Cartesian lift
y\to x
over
f
(cf.
[
80
,
Def. 02XM
]
).

A
(
1
-)morphism

\mathscr{F}:(\mathcal{X}_{1},p_{1})\to(\mathcal{X}_{2},p_{2})
is a
1
-morphism in the
2
-category of fibered categories over
\mathcal{C}
. That is,
\mathscr{F}
is a functor from
\mathcal{X}_{1}
to
\mathcal{X}_{2}
such that
p_{2}\circ\mathscr{F}=p_{1}
, and
\mathscr{F}
sends a strongly Cartesian morphism to a strongly Cartesian morphism (see
[
80
,
Def. 02XP
]
).

On the other hand, we define
1
-morphisms between presheaves in categories.

Definition A.1
.

Define a (
1
-)morphism (or a natural transformation)
\mathbf{F}:F_{1}\to F_{2}
to be the collection
\{\mathbf{F}(U);\phi(U_{1},U_{2},f)\}_{U,U_{1},U_{2}\in\ob\mathcal{C},f\in\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(U_{1},U_{2})}
as follows:

(1)

For any
U\in\ob\mathcal{C}
,
\mathbf{F}(U):F_{1}(U)\to F_{2}(U)
is a
1
-morphism (i.e., a functor) between categories.

(2)

For
f\in\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(U_{1},U_{2})
with
U_{1},U_{2}\in\ob\mathcal{C}
,

\phi(U_{1},U_{2},f):\mathbf{F}(U_{1})F_{1}(f)(F_{1}(U_{2}))\to F_{2}(f)\mathbf{F}(U_{2})(F_{1}(U_{2}))

is a natural transformation between functors
\mathbf{F}(U_{1})F_{1}(f)
and
F_{2}(f)\mathbf{F}(U_{2})
mapping from
F_{1}(U_{2})
to
F_{2}(U_{1})
such that,

(a)

\phi(U,U,\mathrm{id}_{U})(x)=\mathrm{id}_{\mathbf{F}(U)x}
for any
U\in\ob\mathcal{C}
and
x\in F_{1}(U)
.

(b)

For any morphisms in
\mathcal{C}
,
f:U_{1}\to U_{2}
and
g:U_{2}\to U_{3}
, and any
x_{3}\in\ob F_{1}(U_{3})
, we have
F_{2}(f)(\phi(U_{2},U_{3},g))\circ\phi(U_{1},U_{2},f)(F_{1}(g)x_{3})=\phi(U_{1},U_{3},g\circ f)(x_{3})
.

(c)

For any pair
(U_{1},U_{2})
and
f:U_{1}\to U_{2}
, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}(U_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}(U_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}(U_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}(U_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{F}(U_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{F}(U_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}(f)}

is commutative up to composing
\phi(U_{1},U_{2},f)
.

In fact, the definitions of
1
-morphisms in the two contexts correspond to each other.

Lemma A.2
.

\mathscr{F}
determines and is determined by a morphism
\mathbf{F}
from
F_{1}
to
F_{2}
.

Proof.

Recall that the fibered category
p_{1}:\mathcal{X}_{1}\to\mathcal{C}
is defined as follows: the objects are
(U,x)
such that
U\in\ob\mathcal{C}
and
x\in\ob F_{1}(U)
; the morphisms are
\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}((U_{1},x_{1}),(U_{2},x_{2}))=\{(f,\phi)|f\in\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(U_{1},U_{2}),\phi\in\mathrm{Mor}_{F_{1}(U_{1})}(x_{1},F_{1}(f)x_{2})\}
. The composition is defined as
(g,\psi)\circ(f,\phi)=(g\circ f,F_{1}(f)(\psi)\circ\phi)
.

The
1
-morphism
\mathscr{F}
maps
(U,x)
to
(V,y)\in\ob\mathcal{X}_{2}
. Since
p_{2}\circ\mathscr{F}=p_{1}
, we have that
V=U
and
y\in\ob F_{2}(U)
. This determines an assignment
\mathscr{F}(U)
from
\ob F_{1}(U)
to
\ob F_{2}(U)
.

Hence, the functor
\mathscr{F}
maps
\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}((U_{1},x_{1}),(U_{2},x_{2}))
to
\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}((U_{1},\mathscr{F}(U_{1})x_{1}),(U_{2},\mathscr{F}(U_{2})x_{2}))
. More precisely, a pair
(f,\phi)
as above is sent to
(\mathscr{F}(f),\mathscr{F}(\phi))
. But
\mathscr{F}(f)=f
since
p_{2}\circ\mathscr{F}=p_{1}
. So
\mathscr{F}(\phi):\mathscr{F}(U_{1})x_{1}\to F_{2}(f)(\mathscr{F}(U_{2})x_{2})
is a uniquely determined morphism in
F_{2}(U_{1})
induced by
\mathscr{F}
.

Now let
(U_{1},x_{1})=(U_{1},F_{1}(f)x_{2})
and let
\phi=\mathrm{id}
. Then
\mathscr{F}(\phi)=:\phi_{\mathscr{F}}(U_{1},U_{2},f)(x_{2})
is a functor
\mathscr{F}(\phi):\mathscr{F}(U_{1})(F_{1}(f)x_{2})\to F_{2}(f)(\mathscr{F}(U_{2})x_{2})
.
Moreover, fix any
(t:x_{2}\to x_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathrm{Mor}_{F_{1}(U_{2})}(x_{2},x_{2}^{\prime})
and consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U_{1},F_{1}(f)x_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U_{2},x_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U_{1},F_{1}(f)x_{2}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U_{2},x_{2}^{\prime}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}(f)(t)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f,\mathrm{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f,\mathrm{id})}

The diagram above is commutative after applying
\mathscr{F}
as
\mathscr{F}
is a functor, which implies the commutativity of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}(U_{1})F_{1}(f)x_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}(f)\mathscr{F}(U_{2})x_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}(U_{1})F_{1}(f)x_{2}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}(f)\mathscr{F}(U_{2})x_{2}^{\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\mathscr{F}}(U_{1},U_{2},f)(x_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}(U_{1})F_{1}(f)t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}(f)\mathscr{F}(t)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\mathscr{F}}(U_{1},U_{2},f)(x_{2}^{\prime})}

This implies that
\phi_{\mathscr{F}}(U_{1},U_{2},f)
is a natural transformation.

Hence, the diagram of functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}(U_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}(U_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}(U_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}(U_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}(U_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{F}(U_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}(f)}

is commutative up to uniquely determined
2
-morphisms. Then it can be checked that
\{\mathscr{F}(U);\phi_{\mathscr{F}}(U_{1},U_{2},f)\}
determines a
1
-morphism
\mathbf{F}:F_{1}\to F_{2}
. The second condition of
\mathbf{F}
listed above follows from the fact that
\mathscr{F}
is a functor that preserves compositions.

Conversely, suppose that there is a
1
-morphism
\mathbf{F}:F_{1}\to F_{2}
given by the collection
\{\mathbf{F}(U);\phi(U_{1},U_{2},f)\}
. Then set
\mathscr{F}(U,x_{1})=(U,\mathbf{F}(U)x_{1})
. Let
(f:U_{1}\to U_{2},\phi:x_{1}\to F_{1}(f)x_{2})\in\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}((U_{1},x_{1}),(U_{2},x_{2}))
. Set
\mathscr{F}(f,\phi)=(f,\phi(U_{1},U_{2},f)(x_{2})\circ\mathbf{F}(U_{1})(\phi))
.

Then it can be checked that
\mathscr{F}
is a functor. We only check that it preserves compositions because the other conditions are easier. Suppose that we are given
U_{1}\xrightarrow{f}U_{2}\xrightarrow{g}U_{3}
,
x_{i}\in F_{1}(U_{i})
for
i=1,2,3
, and
\phi:x_{1}\to F_{1}(f)x_{2}
and
\psi:x_{2}\to F_{1}(g)x_{3}
. We have
\mathscr{F}(g\circ f,F_{1}(f)(\psi)\circ\phi)=

(g\circ f,\phi(U_{1},U_{3},g\circ f)(x_{3})\circ\mathbf{F}(U_{1})(F_{1}(f)(\psi)\circ\phi)).

The last expression is computed as

\begin{split}&(g\circ f,F_{2}(f)(\phi(U_{2},U_{3},g))\circ\phi(U_{1},U_{2},f)(F_{1}(g)x_{3})\circ\mathbf{F}(U_{1})(F_{1}(f)(\psi)\circ\phi))=\\
&(g\circ f,F_{2}(f)(\phi(U_{2},U_{3},g))\circ\phi(U_{1},U_{2},f)(F_{1}(g)x_{3})\circ(\mathbf{F}(U_{1})(F_{1}(f)(\psi))\circ\mathbf{F}(U_{1})(\phi)))=\\
&(g\circ f,F_{2}(f)(\phi(U_{2},U_{3},g))\circ F_{2}(f)\mathbf{F}(U_{2})(\psi)\circ\phi(U_{1},U_{2},f)(x_{2})\circ\mathbf{F}(U_{1})(\phi))=\\
&(g\circ f,F_{2}(f)(\phi(U_{2},U_{3},g)\circ\mathbf{F}(U_{2})(\psi))\circ(\phi(U_{1},U_{2},f)(x_{2})\circ\mathbf{F}(U_{1})(\phi)))=\\
&(g,\phi(U_{2},U_{3},g)\circ\mathbf{F}(U_{2})(\psi))\circ(f,\phi(U_{1},U_{2},f)(x_{2})\circ\mathbf{F}(U_{1})(\phi))=\mathscr{F}(g,\psi)\circ\mathscr{F}(f,\phi).\end{split}

The second line to the third line follows from the fact that
\phi(U_{1},U_{2},f)
is a natural transformation, from which the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{F}(U_{1})F_{1}(f)x_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}(f)\mathbf{F}(U_{2})x_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{F}(U_{1})F_{1}(f)(F_{1}(g)x_{3})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}(f)\mathbf{F}(U_{2})(F_{1}(g)x_{3})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi(U_{1},U_{2},f)(x_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{F}(U_{1})F_{1}(f)(\psi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{2}(f)\mathbf{F}(U_{2})(\psi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi(U_{1},U_{2},f)(F_{1}(g)x_{3})}

commutes.
∎

A.2.
2-limits

A.2.1.

We explain what a
2
-limit is, specialized to our situation.

Definition A.3
.

Let
p:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{C}
be a fibered category in groupoids constructed from a presheaf
F:\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathsf{Groupoids}
(see
[
80
,
Def. 003T
]
and
[
80
,
Ex. 0049
]
). Define

2\text{-}\varprojlim_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{X}

as a groupoid. More precisely,

(1)

The objects of it are of the form

L:=\{(U,x_{U});T_{f_{U,V}}\}_{U\in\ob\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}},f_{U,V}\in\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}(U,V)}

where
x_{U}\in\ob F(U)
,
T_{f_{U,V}}\in\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}}((U,x_{U}),(V,x_{V}))
such that
p^{\mathrm{op}}(T_{f_{U,V}})=f_{U,V}
,
T_{f_{U_{2},U_{1}}}\circ T_{f_{U_{3},U_{2}}}=T_{f_{U_{3},U_{1}}}
for
f_{U_{2},U_{1}}\circ f_{U_{3},U_{2}}=f_{U_{3},U_{1}}
in
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
.

(2)

The morphisms from
L
to
L^{\prime}=\{(U,x^{\prime}_{U});T^{\prime}_{f_{U,V}}\}
are of the form
\{C_{U}\}_{U\in\ob\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}
, where
C_{U}\in\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{op}}}((U,x_{U}),(U,x_{U}^{\prime}))
such that
T^{\prime}_{f_{U,V}}\circ C_{U}=C_{V}\circ T_{f_{U,V}}
.

In Definition
A.3
, an object
L
determines and is determined by a functor
\mathscr{L}:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{X}
sending
U
to
(U,x_{U})
and
f_{U,V}^{\mathrm{op}}
to
T_{f_{U,V}}^{\mathrm{op}}
such that
p\circ\mathscr{L}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}
.

Let
F_{1}
,
F_{2}
,
(\mathcal{X}_{1},p_{1})
,
(\mathcal{X}_{2},p_{2})
,
\mathbf{F}
, and
\mathscr{F}
be as in §
A.1
.
Suppose that there is a
1
-morphism
\pi:\mathcal{X}_{2}\to\mathcal{X}_{1}
(such that
p_{1}\circ\pi=p_{2}
, and) such that
(\mathcal{X}_{2},p)
is isomorphic to the fibered category in groupoids over
\mathcal{X}_{1}
associated with a functor
\pi^{presh}:\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathsf{Groupoids}
. Denote by
\Phi:F_{2}\to F_{1}
the
1
-morphism between presheaves corresponding to
\pi
according to Lemma
A.2
.

Lemma A.4
.

In this setup, an object in
2
-
\varprojlim_{\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{X}_{2}
determines and is determined by a section
\mathscr{S}:\mathcal{X}_{1}\to\mathcal{X}_{2}
of
\pi:\mathcal{X}_{2}\to\mathcal{X}_{1}
in
1
-
\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X}_{1},\mathcal{X}_{2})
, which, from Lemma
A.2
, corresponds to a
1
-morphism
\Psi:F_{1}\to F_{2}
such that
\Phi\circ\Psi=\mathrm{id}_{F_{1}}
. Moreover, the groupoid of such sections
\mathscr{S}
(with morphisms among them defined by the natural transformations between functors from
\mathcal{X}_{1}
to
\mathcal{X}_{2}
) is
isomorphic
to the groupoid
2
-
\varprojlim_{\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{X}_{2}
.

Proof.

In the context of this lemma, we have
p_{2}\circ\mathscr{L}=p_{1}\circ\pi\circ\mathscr{L}=p_{1}
, which means that
\mathscr{L}
is automatically a morphism in
1
-
\mathrm{Mor}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{X}_{1},\mathcal{X}_{2})
; note that every morphism is strongly Cartesian by
[
80
,
Lem. 003V
]
. The first part of the lemma now follows from Lemma
A.2
. The isomorphism of groupoids follows from the construction in Definition
A.3
(2).
∎

Remark A.5
.

By Lemma
A.4
, the three notions “an object in
2
-
\varprojlim_{\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{X}_{2}
”, “a
1
-morphism section of
\pi:\mathcal{X}_{2}\to\mathcal{X}_{1}
”, and “a
1
-morphism section of
\Phi:F_{2}\to F_{1}
” can and should be used interchangeably in this paper.

Assume further that
\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\lim}:=

\operatornamewithlimits{2-lim}\limits_{U\in\ob\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}\varinjlim_{F_{1}(U)}F_{1}(U)

exists. If it exists, this is a groupoid since the inner colimits
\varinjlim_{F_{1}(U)}F_{1}(U)
(that is, the colimit in the category
F_{1}(U)
) are groupoids, as they are unique up to unique isomorphisms by definition.

Denote by
\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\prime}
the full subcategory of
\mathcal{X}_{1}
consisting of objects of the form
(U,\varinjlim_{F_{1}(U)}F_{1}(U))
. Note that there is also a projection
\ell:\mathcal{X}_{1}\to\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\prime}
according to the definition of colimits, which makes natural inclusion
i:\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}_{1}
a section of
\ell
, i.e.,
\ell\circ i=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}
.

Lemma A.6
.

With the assumptions above, there is a natural equivalence of groupoids

2\text{-}\varprojlim_{\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{X}_{2}\cong 2\text{-}\varprojlim_{\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\prime,\mathrm{op}}}\mathcal{X}_{2}.

The second limit is formed for the projection
\ell\circ\pi:\mathcal{X}_{2}\to\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The functor
E_{1}
from the LHS to the RHS is the natural projection; the functor
E_{2}
in the other direction is defined by pulling back objects of
\mathcal{X}_{2}
over
\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{X}_{1}
via the morphisms defined by colimits (since
\pi:\mathcal{X}_{2}\to\mathcal{X}_{1}
is a fibered category by assumption). The fact that
E_{2}\circ E_{1}\simeq\mathrm{id}
(of LHS) is due to the universal property of strongly Cartesian morphisms and the assumption that
\mathcal{X}_{2}
is fibered in groupoids: Write an object in LHS by
L=\{(x_{1},x_{2});T_{f_{x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}}}\}
. Suppose that
x_{1}\in\ob F_{1}(U)
maps to
x_{1}^{*}\in\ob\varinjlim_{F_{1}(U)}F_{1}(U)
. Suppose that
p:x_{2}^{*}\to x_{1}^{*}
is the object in the data of
L
. Then there is a (strongly Cartesian) lift
g:x_{2}^{\prime}\to x_{2}^{*}
of
f:x_{1}\to x_{1}^{*}
. As
\mathcal{X}_{2}\to\mathcal{X}_{1}
is fibered in groupoids and there is a isomorphism
T_{f_{x_{1},x_{1}^{*}}}:x_{2}\to x_{2}^{*}
, there is a unique isomorphism
C_{x_{1}}:x_{2}\to x_{2}^{\prime}
.

The commutativity in Definition
A.3
(2) is obtained in a similar way. In fact, let
y_{1}
,
y_{1}^{*}
,
y_{2}^{*}
,
y_{2}^{\prime}
, and
y_{2}
be the objects constructed in the same way for another
V\to U
in
\mathcal{C}
. We have a diagram

(A.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{2}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{2}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{2}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{1}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{2}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y_{1}^{*}.}

To show the commutativity of the diagram formed by
y_{2}
,
x_{2}
,
x_{2}^{\prime}
and
y_{2}^{\prime}
, it suffices to show the commutativity of
y_{2}
,
x_{2}
,
x_{2}^{*}
and
y_{2}^{*}
. This follows from the definition of
2
-limits.
∎
